# CLAUDE.md

This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.

@AGENTS.md

The rules above (worktrees, Redmine, privacy, gates, commits) are binding. This file adds commands and the architecture you need before touching code.

## Commands

Clone with `--recurse-submodules`. Toolchain: Node 24 + pnpm 10, uv (Python 3.12). GNU Make ≥ 3.82.

```bash
make setup          # build pinned Showdown, uv sync, pnpm install, pre-commit install
make check          # every offline CI gate: python + ts + guards
make showdown       # rebuild vendor/pokemon-showdown (needed before any TS test)
make ts             # pnpm lint && pnpm typecheck && pnpm test (vitest, coverage) + check-rules.ts
make python         # ruff check, ruff format --check, mypy, pytest (coverage)
make contracts      # contract schemas fit their ADR-008 profile; generated TS types and pydantic models have no drift
make contracts-update  # regenerate the TS types and pydantic models after a schema change (commit the result)
make guards         # team guards (PokePaste text, JSON data_class) + production licence allowlist
make db-up db-test  # rules store on a fresh local Postgres 18 (127.0.0.1:55432): roles, migrations, tests
make db-down        # remove that container
scripts/check-evaluation-store.sh --store DIR --owner USER  # owner only: read-only isolation checks of the evaluation store (docs/runbooks/evaluation-store.md)
make db-bootstrap-test  # bootstrap.sql on its own throwaway Postgres: refuses missing/blank passwords, sends none outside its CREATE ROLE
make importer-e2e   # build the importer image, import the recorded releases into a fresh db-up DB twice (commit, then no-op); db-up again before db-test
RULES_DB_PORT=55433 RULES_DB_COMPOSE_PROJECT=areneon-e2e make importer-e2e  # the same beside the default DB (db-up/db-test/db-down honour both too)
```

Single tests. The coverage thresholds apply per file and fail on a subset, so run without coverage:

```bash
pnpm exec vitest run packages/showdown-bridge/test/runner.test.ts        # one file
pnpm exec vitest run packages/showdown-bridge/test/request.test.ts -t "forced switch"
uv run pytest tests/scripts/test_check_licenses.py --no-cov               # add ::test_name for one
```

Reducer sweep (#919; reads a verified dataset, writes only to `--out`; the actor mixes include `uniform-all`, a sampler over every candidate pending ones included, which is what a random-initialised policy is; about 0.15 s per battle after a 30 s pool load; the check takes about 20 ms per request):

```bash
node packages/team-dataset/scripts/reducer-sweep.ts --config RUN_CONFIG.json --mix random-legal|pending-first|rule-vs-random|uniform-all|uniform-vs-random --battles 1100 [--start 0] [--run-key sweep-1] [--only-ability Illusion] [--only-move Transform,Copycat] --out OUT.jsonl
uv run python scripts/reducer_sweep.py OUT.jsonl...   # reducer and encoder at every request; each refusal kind with a count and its shortest example; exit 1 on any refusal (nothing is excluded; the Illusion `|replace|` is reduced, #851) and on an input that checked nothing; the play script exits 2 on an unknown flag, a missing value, an unknown mix or a `--battles` below 1; `--only-ability Illusion` plays only teams that hold the ability and ignores the config's `excluded_abilities` (the way to sweep Illusion battles); `--only-move a,b` plays only teams that hold one of the moves (names or ids; the Transform sweep of #938)
```

Re-capture golden fixtures and team verdicts (only after a deliberate Showdown pin bump or engine-facing change; review the diff):

```bash
node packages/showdown-bridge/scripts/capture-golden.ts
node packages/showdown-bridge/scripts/capture-choices.ts   # golden choice corpus; also after an enumerator change
node packages/showdown-bridge/scripts/capture-choice-channels.ts   # the player channel of each choice-corpus case (`fixtures/showdown/<format>/choice-channels/channels.json`, read by tests/ml/model/test_target.py); after capture-choices.ts or a pin bump
node packages/showdown-bridge/scripts/capture-samples.ts   # shared contract sample corpus; also after a contract change
node packages/showdown-bridge/scripts/export-team-vocabulary.ts   # golden team vocabulary (about 10 s); also after a vocabulary-export change; run before capture-samples.ts, which reads it
node packages/showdown-bridge/scripts/export-role-sets.ts   # golden role-set table (ADR-020); after a pin bump or a role-set export change, once the team vocabulary is current
node packages/showdown-bridge/scripts/export-move-classes.ts   # golden move-class table (the move-use diagnostic); after a pin bump or a class-rule change
node packages/showdown-bridge/scripts/export-generator-prior.ts --commit <40 hex>   # config/opponent-model/<format>/generator-matched.<config_id>.json (ADR-025 §10); after a generator config change or a deliberate change of the proposal's draw rules (the commit of that code)
node packages/showdown-bridge/scripts/export-role-sets-prior.ts   # config/opponent-model/<format>/role-sets.fill-<config_id>.json (ADR-025 §2); after a role-set table, fill-prior or `role-sets-prior.ts` change; review the diff
node packages/showdown-bridge/scripts/capture-random-corpus.ts   # random-play channel corpus (needs a built engine); also after an engine-facing change
node packages/showdown-bridge/scripts/capture-unavailable-corpus.ts   # channels with `|error|[Unavailable choice]` lines (pending-first play on the fixtures-v1 teams); then rewrite the reduced snapshots (tests/ml/reducer/test_unavailable.py)
node packages/showdown-bridge/scripts/capture-search-fidelity-corpus.ts [--extend] [--dataset DIR]...   # the targeted fidelity corpus `fixtures/showdown/<format>/search-fidelity/` (ADR-026 slice T); after a pin bump, an engine-facing change or an edit of its `targets.json`; DIR is a `generate` output whose dev split supplies teams (see its README); `--extend` keeps the committed battles and adds only what a short target still lacks (#1187)
node packages/showdown-bridge/test/fidelity/report-cli.ts --json fixtures/showdown/<format>/search-fidelity/fidelity-report.json > fixtures/showdown/<format>/search-fidelity/fidelity-report.md   # the full fork-oracle fidelity report (ADR-026 slice F; about 5 minutes, test tooling); after a corpus, pin or record change (a test fails on a stale `inputs`), then edit `admitted` in config/search/world-writers.v1.json to the report's admissions
node packages/showdown-bridge/scripts/capture-illusion-battle.ts   # the Illusion battle (a Zoroark on both sides whose disguises break); then rewrite the reduced snapshots with `ARENEON_UPDATE_GOLDEN=1 uv run pytest tests/ml/reducer/test_illusion_battle.py --no-cov`
node packages/showdown-bridge/scripts/capture-fling-battle.ts   # the Fling of an item already gone (`-enditem` with no name, #1162); then rewrite the reduced snapshots with `ARENEON_UPDATE_GOLDEN=1 uv run pytest tests/ml/reducer/test_fling.py --no-cov`
node packages/showdown-bridge/scripts/capture-baton-pass-battle.ts   # the Baton Pass battles (stages passed on a `[from] Baton Pass` switch, #1099; a passed Substitute, #1102); then rewrite the reduced snapshots with `ARENEON_UPDATE_GOLDEN=1 uv run pytest tests/ml/reducer/test_baton_pass.py tests/ml/reducer/test_baton_pass_volatiles.py --no-cov`
node packages/showdown-bridge/scripts/export-volatile-copy.ts   # which volatiles a Baton Pass / Shed Tail hands over (`noCopy` in the engine's condition data, #1102); after a pin bump
node packages/showdown-bridge/scripts/export-engine-data.ts # engine-data table + hash (ADR-010); after a pin bump
node packages/showdown-bridge/scripts/export-engine-data-2.ts # engine-data version 2 table + summary (ADR-010 erratum A4 item 7); after a pin bump or an export change
PYTHONPATH=.:packages/schema/python python -m scripts.derive_usage_prior --inputs config/opponent-model/<format>/smogon-chaos.<month>-<cutoff>.inputs.json --raw-dir .tmp/chaos --out config/opponent-model/<format>/usage-prior.<month>-<cutoff>.json [--report R.json] [--fallback]   # the Smogon usage prior (ADR-025 §2, #1120; `--fallback` #1209: fill the species the source lacks from the generator-matched prior); the raw files are downloaded by hand into the gitignored .tmp/chaos and checked against the input record's size and SHA-256
node packages/opponent-model/scripts/usage-prior-report.ts PRIOR.json   # the move design's largest deviation from a prior's stated marginals (derivation report)
```

Generate a synthetic team dataset (ADR-013; the pilot of 40,000 draws takes about a minute, then about 1 ms per draw; `pilot` records the pilot alone, `generate --pilot FILE` reuses it; output goes to the `--out` directory and is not committed unless it is a deliberate fixture):

```bash
node packages/team-dataset/scripts/generate.ts generate --config config/teams/gen9championsvgc2026regmc/generator.json --labels config/teams/gen9championsvgc2026regmc/labels.json --format gen9championsvgc2026regmc --out OUT_DIR --dataset-id ID --generation-key HEX64 --assignment-key HEX64 --dev 100 --validation 100 --training 1000 [--reference earlier/records.dev.jsonl ...]
node packages/team-dataset/scripts/sealed.ts <generate|reveal-hash|retire|verify> ...   # owner only, inside the isolated store (docs/runbooks/evaluation-store.md section 9)
node packages/team-dataset/scripts/duckdb-writer.ts write --dataset OUT_DIR --labels config/teams/gen9championsvgc2026regmc/labels.json --out SNAP_DIR --shard-key HEX64 --snapshot-id ID [--splits dev,validation] [--require-pinned]   # Parquet shards + snapshot.json (needs the DuckDB CLI: DUCKDB_BIN or PATH)
node packages/team-dataset/scripts/duckdb-writer.ts verify --snapshot SNAP_DIR/snapshot.json --shards SNAP_DIR --dataset OUT_DIR --labels ...   # shards back to the dataset's records, byte for byte
DUCKDB_BIN=... node packages/team-dataset/scripts/measure-shards.ts OUT_DIR SNAP_DIR [--split dev]   # write/read time and memory
node packages/team-dataset/scripts/check-team-labels.ts   # in make ts and CI: label sets and generator configs under config/teams/ against the pinned engine
```

TypeScript runs directly under Node's type stripping (`.ts` imports, `erasableSyntaxOnly`): no build step, no parameter properties, enums or namespaces.

## Architecture

**Polyglot monorepo** (ADR-002): TypeScript in `packages/*` (pnpm workspace), Python in `scripts/` and `tests/` today plus the generated-contracts package `packages/schema/python` (a uv workspace member next to its schemas; `ml/` for the policy worker in `ml/areneon_ml/worker` (the networks follow), `services/` later), Rust in `crates/` once the first crate lands. `packages/schema` holds the JSON Schema contracts: the rules records (`rules/`, ADR-007) and the cross-language contracts (`contracts/v<N>/`, ADR-008), from which the TS types in `generated/ts/` and the pydantic models in `python/areneon_contracts/models/` (the `areneon-contracts` uv workspace package, `scripts/contract_models.py`) are generated and checked in; serde follows with the first crate. On Python, `areneon_contracts.contracts` validates with python-jsonschema, then parses into the model (`parse`), and hashes with RFC 8785 (`jcs`, `request_hash`). `fixtures/contracts/` is the shared sample corpus (valid and invalid instances of every contract, plus `reader-invalid/` ones that pass the schema and that a TS reader such as `readEpisode` refuses, plus their JCS digests in `hashes.json`): Ajv and python-jsonschema must classify it identically and every language must reproduce the digests. A contract's `$id` (`urn:areneon:schema:<contracts|engine>:v<N>:<name>`) declares its profile, and `src/profile.ts` enforces it as a keyword whitelist: closed objects, bounded integers, local `$ref`s, `kind`-tagged `oneOf`; `engine` schemas, which copy the pinned engine's wire shapes, also allow scalar type unions and get their own Ajv instance (`src/ajv.ts`). `Provenance` gained the optional `collection` as a minor change within v1 (ADR-008 §3): a reader older than it rejects such an Episode, so the generated types and models ship with the schema change. `make contracts` fails on a profile violation or on generated types that differ from a fresh run.

**The reference engine is upstream Showdown, unmodified** (ADR-003). `vendor/pokemon-showdown` is a git submodule pinned to an exact SHA and built with `make showdown`. Areneon never decides legality itself: it asks the engine (`TeamValidator`, `side.choose`) and stops on unknowns. `docs/showdown-champions-gaps.md` lists what the engine does for Champions and what is unverified. It is generated from the gap ledger records in `rules/gaps/` by `scripts/gaps-doc.ts`; edit the records, never the doc (a test compares them). Gap edits move `rules_snapshot_hash` but never `format_rules_hash`.

**`packages/showdown-bridge`** is the only code that talks to the engine:
- `engine.ts`: `loadEngine()` loads `dist/sim` via `createRequire` behind narrow local interfaces (`ShowdownSim`). It refuses a missing build or a checkout whose HEAD is not `PINNED_SHOWDOWN_COMMIT`. A test checks that constant against the submodule gitlink, so a pin bump changes both.
- `rules.ts` / `rules-load.ts` (ADR-007): Areneon's own rules are JSON records in `rules/`, never code constants. That covers which formats it runs and the forbidden engine rules (e.g. `forceopenteamsheets`, because CTS needs Bo1 with sheets declined). They are validated by `packages/schema/rules/v1` (Ajv) and fail closed. `jcs()` (RFC 8785) gives `rules_snapshot_hash` (provenance) and `format_rules_hash` (replay identity). `packages/showdown-bridge/scripts/rules-hash.ts` and everything it pulls in (`rules.ts`, `request-identity.ts`, `canonical-json.ts`, `engine.ts`) import only Node built-ins, because CI reruns old commits' copy of it from bare archives. `canonical-json.ts` (`canonicalJson`, `jcs`) imports nothing at all, so the engine-free observation builder can use it too (ADR-008 §5); a test enforces that.
- `formats.ts`: `resolveFormat` checks a format against the rules snapshot and the pinned engine. It returns the version manifest `{showdown_commit, format_id, regulation, ruleset_hash, format_rules_hash}` plus the embedded format rules record that every fixture (and, later, every episode) carries.
- `scripts/check-rules.ts` (in `make ts` and CI; policy in `rules-channel.ts` / `rules-history.ts`): validates every record and checks `rules/channels/`, which must hold exactly one file per channel in the channel schema's enum. Each channel must name a snapshot built for the current pin, and `seq` rises by 1 per change. A pin bump must also move the channel (CONTRIBUTING).
- `request.ts` / `request-identity.ts`: decode one `|request|` into a phase (`preview`/`move`/`forceSwitch`/`wait`). The body is first validated against the closed engine-mirror schema `packages/schema/contracts/v1/engine-request.schema.json` (the pinned `sim/side.ts` request types, minus fields no supported format has), so only an engine-shaped request is hashed; a pin bump that changes the shape edits that schema. Request identity is `request_seq` + `request_hash`, a SHA-256 of canonical JSON that excludes `rqid`. `request-bytes.ts` is the one definition of the hashed bytes (`requestIdentityBytes`, the `Sha256` port type and `hashRequest`); it imports only `canonical-json.ts`. `parseRequest` is the pure parse step (schema validation, phase, `identity_bytes`; it hashes nothing), `decodeRequest` adds the Node `request_hash`, and `buildObservation` hashes the same bytes through its injected port (a test fixes Node and WebCrypto digests equal on the golden requests and that no other module builds the bytes). `rqid` exists only on the Showdown server path, never on the in-process sim.
- `runner.ts`: `runBattle` runs a seeded in-process BattleStream battle (runtime A1) on copies of the teams (the caller's sets stay as brought) and records `team_hashes` (each seat's set array as brought). Teams are validated first. Each `Actor` sees only its own player's requests. Each request carries its `legal` choice set, judged by a `CloneJudge` on the stream's own battle. The CloneJudge's verdicts on pending candidates go to `hidden_verdicts`, never to an actor. If the actor submits a pending candidate and the engine refuses it as `[Unavailable choice]`, the updated request that follows is a new request (`request_seq` + 1). Any other engine `|error|` aborts; nothing is retried. `|t:|` wall-clock lines are dropped so the same commit, seed and choices reproduce `omniscient_log`. That log holds both sides' hidden information: an evaluation artifact, never actor input. `replayActor` replays recorded choices. The optional `players` option sets each seat's `name` and `avatar` on the `|player|` lines (default `p1`/`p2`, no avatar) so a recorded server battle can be reproduced; `validatePlayers` refuses unsafe names (blank, edge whitespace, no letter or digit, over 18 UTF-16 units, `|` `,` `[` `]`, control characters, double spaces, or two names alike) and avatars that are not 1-32 letters, digits or `-`.
- `teams.ts`: `importPaste` / `exportPaste` wrap the engine's `Teams.import` / `Teams.export`. Showdown silently skips unknown lines (an `SPs:` line zeroes the Stat Points; they belong in `EVs:`), so `importPaste` rejects a paste unless each Pokémon's lines all reappear in that Pokémon's re-export: only the engine's canonical form passes. `validateTeam` returns the pinned `TeamValidator`'s messages verbatim.
- `choices.ts` / `choice-judge.ts` (ADR-006): `enumerateCandidates` turns the player's own request into every engine-distinct joint choice, in a fixed canonical order. It is pure and fails closed on any request field it does not know. Candidates whose legality depends on `maybeTrapped` / `maybeDisabled` / `maybeLocked` are `pending`. `legalChoiceSet` (its `LegalChoiceSet` type is generated from `packages/schema/contracts/v1/legal-choice-set.schema.json`, imported as `@areneon/schema/types/v1/…`) has a `ChoiceJudge` check every non-pending one; it never asks about pending ones, so no hidden-information verdict reaches a policy. `CloneJudge` judges on `Battle.fromJSON(battle.toJSON())`, never on the live battle, and refuses unless the battle is at the request's `request_hash`. `runBattle` builds one per request (A1); the corpus and tests use it too. `Battle.fromJSON` rebuilds `activeRequest` from the Pokémon, so the clone carries the live request over: after an `[Unavailable choice]` the live one was edited in place. `judgeSubmission` (the SubmissionJudge) classifies the engine's `|error|` after a submission. If the engine refuses a non-pending candidate, that is an `EnumeratorDefectError`. Revival Blessing (#910): a request whose `side.pokemon[i].reviving` is set is a forced switch; the one flagged reviving slot offers `switch N` for every fainted Pokémon (active slots included) and `pass` only when no living bench Pokémon exists, and any other shape (a second flagged slot, a reviver outside a forced switch) fails closed. A successful reviving pick clears the reviver's `switchFlag`, which `clearChoice` does not restore, so `CloneJudge` takes a fresh clone after any choice that changed a `switchFlag` (its batch verdicts equal one clone per choice). The corpus has `force-switch-revival` and `-many`, `fixtures/showdown/<format>/revival/` a seeded battle through `runBattle` (`scripts/capture-revival-battle.ts`), and `tests/ml/model/test_revival.py` the reducer and encoder on it. On A2 (`judge: null`), the engine judges only at submission.
- `observation.ts` (ADR-008 §5): `buildObservation` is the only constructor of a `PreviewObservation` / `BattleObservation`. Its battle input is a branded `PlayerChannel` (one player's own stream up to the request being answered), plus that player's own `PrivateTeamSpec`, the version manifest and an optional `DecisionContext`; the omniscient log is a distinct `OmniscientLog` type and cannot be passed. The module imports no engine, runner or Node built-in (a test enforces it), so it runs wherever WebCrypto does; it hashes through an injected `Sha256` port (`nodeSha256` in `player-channel.ts`, `webSha256` on WebCrypto) to exactly `decodeRequest`'s `request_hash`. `player-channel.ts` is the A1 adapter: `playerChannel` validates a `PlayerChannelLog` and every `|request|` in it before branding, and `recordedChannel` cuts a runner record's channel at a `request_seq`. `test/observation.test.ts` holds the ADR-008 privacy tests: golden parsing, the canary test (no opponent secret line or request stats in any observation, with a negative control), schema probes and the type test.
- `episode.ts` / `episode-io.ts` (ADR-008 §3, §5): an `Episode` is one player's recorded battle: their own channel (stored once), their own team, their decisions (a `deadline` record required on A2, absent on A1), provenance, `data_class` and matching `rights`. `episodeFromRecord` writes one from an A1 record, given both teams: it classifies by BOTH (`private` if either is; `rightsFor` follows), checks each team's `teamHash` (JCS of the set array, so it ignores the label) against `record.team_hashes`, and refuses a `synthetic` label whose hash is not in the given `known_synthetic` set; `readEpisode` reads a copy back by its `schema_version` (an unknown version is refused), checks the channel is the player's own and its ending matches `result`, rebuilds the Team Preview observation and every decision's (so the team must be the player's own, in request order), and on A2 requires each decision's deadline record; every refusal is an `EpisodeError`. `playerChannel` refuses `|split|` lines and the opponent's exact HP, the SECRET halves a player never sees. A battle with a reconnect is ONE continuous channel (#813): the server replays the room log to a rejoining connection with no `|request|` or `|error|` line, so the rejoin channel alone has no Team Preview request and `buildObservation` refuses it (the team check anchors on the channel's first request, unchanged; the message names the fix). `continueChannel(earlier, rejoin)` (`player-channel.ts`; reader-side, no schema change) joins the two own channels: the replay lines with the earlier channel's player-alone lines put back where they were, a first request of the rejoin with the earlier last one's `rqid` and body counted once, refusing another player's channel or a replay that does not continue the earlier room log; fold it across several reconnects. The joined channel equals A1's, so its observations equal A1's in full and an Episode stores it as it stores any channel (`readEpisode` is unchanged). `|poke|` lines are no anchor (species and item presence only). `session.ts` and `a2Episode` fold the connections' channels with it (see A2 reconnect below). `episodeFromRecord` also takes an optional `collection` record, which must carry the battle's own `record.seed`. An Episode's optional `provenance.collection` (ADR-009 §4: `league`, `evaluation` or `sparring`, a `kind`-tagged `oneOf`) names the run, the seed (`sodium,<hex>`, null on A2), the evaluation `sealed` flag or the opponent policy; `readEpisode` also refuses a seed on A2 or none on A1, which the schema cannot say. `episodeHash` is SHA-256 of the JCS bytes through the `Sha256` port. `answersRequest` is the stale-invalidation check for a `Recommendation`. A `league` or `evaluation` `provenance.collection` carries a required `opponent_team_data_class`, which `episodeFromRecord` sets from the opponent's `StampedTeam` (never the caller's); `readEpisode` requires the Episode's `data_class` to equal the higher of the own team's class and that field (so a stored Episode relabelled synthetic is refused), and a `sparring` Episode to be `private`.
- `own-team-stats.ts` (#893, ADR-008 §5): the engine-dependent half of the own-team check, outside the engine-free `observation.ts`. `buildObservation` matches the team to the Team Preview request by species, item, ability, moves and nickname only; `assertOwnTeamStats(engine, formatId, team, channel)` builds a battle with the pinned engine from the team the observation will record and requires the engine's own Team Preview request for that player to show the same level, maximum HP and five stats as the channel's first request, Pokémon for Pokémon in team order (no formula is written here; the same team in another order is refused). A difference is an `OwnTeamStatsError` (an `ObservationError`) with a fixed message and a position index only, never a set or a stat; so are a channel that does not start at Team Preview, a team of another size and a team the engine cannot start. It is called by `readEpisode` (so by `episodeFromRecord`, the A2 Episode writer and the training loader; optional `engine`, default the pinned checkout from `pinnedEngine()`, loaded once) and by `policyActor` at the first request of a game (the A1 online observation; optional `engine`, which `policyGame` passes). Limits: the Champions mod ignores IVs (Stat Points replace EVs, IVs fixed at 31) and the request carries no IV, so an IV-only difference cannot be seen (and does not change play); a mirror team that differs in Stat Points, nature or level is refused. Bare Hidden Power: the request names it `hiddenpower<type>`, and the pinned validator allows the move in no supported format ("does not exist in Gen 9"), so no legal team reaches it; the move match keeps refusing it (fail closed) and a test pins the validator's verdict, so a pin bump that allows the move is decided then.
- `training-loader.ts` (ADR-009 §4): `loadTrainingSet` is the one way an Episode becomes training data, and it fails closed. Each Episode goes through `readEpisode` (with `known_synthetic`), then only a `synthetic`, `cc0-synthetic` Episode of collection kind `league` is accepted: a missing collection record, `evaluation`, `sparring` and private data are refused, as is any Episode whose `episodeHash` an `EvaluationRunManifest` lists, and a repeated Episode or manifest. Every refusal is a `TrainingLoaderError`; none is skipped. It writes a stream of `TrainingExample` lines (`episode_hash`, `observation`, `choice`; no provenance, so no seed or opponent id) in Episode-hash order, and a `TrainingSet` (the Episode hashes, the checked manifests' JCS hashes, `data_class`, line count, `stream_sha256`). `readEvaluationRunManifest` / `readTrainingSet` enforce the sorted-and-unique hash lists the profile cannot (corpus samples under `reader-invalid/`). A relabelled copy of an evaluation Episode has another hash, so only the storage partition catches it. `evaluationRunManifest` is the evaluation job's writer (hash-only, sorted and unique); `decisionObservations` (`episode-io.ts`) rebuilds each decision's observation for the examples. On the Python side `areneon_contracts.training.read_stream` reads a stream only with a manifest that matches its digest, line count, Episode hashes and class, validating every line.
- `server-lines.ts` (ADR-005 §5): the A2 server-path filter, an allowlist of the sim's line kinds. `serverOnlyLine` is the stateless check: `>room` headers, plain-text notices and `||text`, `|request|null`, nameless `|player|<slot>|`, and the known server kinds (joins/leaves/renames, chat and PMs, `|raw|`/`|html|`/`|uhtml|`, timer and timestamp lines, room and session state, `|sentchoice|`). `stripServerLines(stream)` returns `{ lines, dropped }`: it keeps sim kinds (`|error|` included), places the engine's `otsrequest` prompt and clear and the sim's `|player|` lines by their position relative to `|gametype|` and `|start`, drops the server's auto-tie `|-hint|` and its inactivity and forfeit `|-message|` lines by exact text, and reports lines of unknown kind in `dropped`. The adapter filters the stream from its first line every time, never a suffix. The channel equals the A1 one up to server-layer `|error|` refusals, which only A2 produces; `|player|` equality is left to the A1/A2 equality test. It imports nothing (a test enforces it), and the golden channels pass through unchanged.
- `choice-corpus.ts`: the golden choice corpus (ADR-006 rule 7). Each case is a seeded battle log that reaches one situation (`CASE_KINDS`: preview, target types, Mega, forced switches, trapping, Imprison, a fainted active, a locked move), plus every candidate with the engine's verdict. Verdicts on pending candidates are hidden state, kept for evaluation. CI replays each log (it never re-runs the search in `scripts/capture-choices.ts`) and compares the result. `missedActions` is the brute-force oracle: it judges every syntactic choice string and fails on any engine-accepted action the enumerator did not offer.
- `team-class.ts` (#817): a class is proven by source, never by a caller's label. `teamHash`, `classOfSets` / `stampTeam` (`synthetic` only for a hash in the known synthetic set, `private` otherwise, `private-data/` teams included), `higherClass`, and `battleClass`, whose branded result is the only value `recordedChannel` takes as a channel's `data_class` (`syntheticFixtureHashes` in `golden.ts` is today's set: the committed fixtures' teams). The set is trusted caller input and the brand is compile-time only; `syntheticTeamHashes(...lists)` builds the set from verified lists of positional `team_hash`es (a non-digest is refused); `datasetSyntheticHashes` / `committedDatasetHashes` in `packages/team-dataset` feed it the teams of the committed, verified datasets, and a caller adds `committedDatasetHashes` (exported from `@areneon/team-dataset`) to the sets it already trusts, e.g. `syntheticTeamHashes(syntheticFixtureHashes(engine), committedDatasetHashes(inputs, format))` (a team is synthetic only if its hash is in the set it passes). `stampTeam` returns a compile-time-branded `StampedTeam`, the only team type `episodeFromRecord` accepts (`runBattle` still takes plain sets: a battle is not an Episode, and `team_hashes` binds the teams to the record). `readEpisode` checks its own team against the required `known_synthetic` and the opponent's class through `collection.opponent_team_data_class`; an Episode without a collection record has no opponent class to check. An A2 stream has no opponent team to hash, so its `data_class` is the caller's label and defaults to `private`.
- `engine-data.ts` (ADR-010 §3): `exportEngineData` reads the species, move, item, ability, type (with the engine's own type chart), status, weather and terrain vocabularies and their numeric attributes from the pinned engine's data for the format's mod, each list sorted by id (the position is the vocabulary index), into the `EngineData` contract (`packages/schema/contracts/v1/engine-data.schema.json`). It refuses any value of a shape it does not know; the vocabularies hold the engine's standard entries plus, so no reference dangles, every base species a standard forme names and every ability a standard species lists even if the engine marks them nonstandard (eight at this pin: cramorant, eiscue, floette, meloetta, minior, ogerpon, terapagos, Aura Guard). Out of v1 scope (a change needs schema_version 2, the contract being closed): natures, volatile conditions, learnsets, evos, requiredItem, move effects, species height and gender. The table is derived from the pinned Showdown data files (MIT, not CC0; `NOTICE-engine-data.txt` beside it keeps the upstream notice). `readEngineData` adds what the profile cannot say (sorted unique ids, references that resolve). `engineDataHash` is SHA-256 of the JCS bytes: the engine-data hash of the encoder version, so a pin bump yields a new one. The golden table and `engine-data.summary.json` (hash, counts) sit in `fixtures/showdown/<format>/`; a test re-exports and compares them. `scripts/export-engine-data.ts` rewrites them.
- `engine-data.ts` version 2 (ADR-010 erratum A4 item 7, #1018): `exportEngineDataV2` / `readEngineDataV2` are the `EngineDataV2` contract (`packages/schema/contracts/v1/engine-data-2.schema.json`, beside v1 as `damage-aware-config-2` is; generated TS and pydantic types, corpus samples `engine-data-2/`): every list of v1 plus `conditions`, the ids of effect type Condition that the mod's condition table and the `condition` of its moves, abilities and items define (`conditionIds`, shared with `volatile-copy.ts`; statuses, weathers and terrains stay in their lists), sorted by id; 148 at this pin. The v1 table, `export-engine-data.ts` and the v1 golden file and hash are untouched; the v2 golden `engine-data-2.json` and `.summary.json` sit beside them (same MIT `NOTICE`; `engine-data.test.ts` re-exports and compares byte for byte, `scripts/export-engine-data-2.ts` rewrites them). Python: `GameData.from_table` and `Vocabulary.from_engine_data` take either version (`GameData.conditions` is an `IdVocab` for v2 and None for v1; nothing in the encoder reads it, so v1 tensors, `encoder_version` and checkpoint ids are unchanged; the encoder option that uses it is #1019). A table is chosen by file: the worker, learner and loop load the `--engine-data` file they are given and the checkpoint check compares its hash, so a v1 checkpoint keeps working with the v1 table and a v2 table needs checkpoints made for it. **Reducer:** with a v2 table `Vocabulary.volatile` checks every `-start`/`-end` volatile id (not `ability:` names, which were always looked up): it resolves as a condition id, a condition id plus the derived suffix (`stockpile1`), the name of a move, ability or item (`Fire Spin`, `Illusion`) or one of `PROTOCOL_VOLATILES` (`typechange`, `typeadd`, `fallen`: Supreme Overlord's `fallen1`..`fallen5`, which the engine prints and no condition defines); a v1 table keeps volatiles opaque as before. `-start|POKEMON|typechange|TYPE[/TYPE]` sets the new `PokemonState.types` (type ids of the vocabulary, plus the engine's typeless marker `???` of Burn Up; an empty list for the copy form `[from] move: Reflect Type`, which names no type; any other type is a `VocabularyError`), cleared on switching out and carried by an Illusion settle; `to_dict` has the `types` key only while it is set, so no committed reduced snapshot changed (none holds a typechange at its end). The shared TS interpreter does not port the field (the parity test strips `types`, as it strips `item_received`); `typeadd` stays a volatile id only. A `-end|…|typechange` (Mimicry), a `|detailschange|` (a permanent forme change, Mega Evolution) and a `|-formechange|` clear `types`, as the engine's `setSpecies` resets them. The `typechange` type is checked against the type vocabulary for v1 tables too (a reducer behaviour change; no encoder change, since the encoder does not read `types`). The worker support of this slice is only that `GameData` / `Vocabulary` accept either table: the worker loads the file it is given and the checkpoint's engine-data hash check is unchanged. A `kind:` prefix (`move: Taunt`) resolves only against that vocabulary or the conditions. `PROTOCOL_VOLATILES` (`typechange`, `typeadd`, `fallen`, `perish`: Perish Song's `perish3`..`perish0`) is held to the pinned source by `tests/ml/reducer/test_protocol_volatiles.py`, which scans `sim/`, `data/` and the mod for literal `-start`/`-end` volatile words: any literal that resolves nowhere, and any stale entry, fails it (skipped without the engine checkout, a failure under `CI`; the python job checks out the submodule source). Known limits: `typeadd` (Trick-or-Treat, Forest's Curse) adds a type and is kept as a volatile id only, no type is tracked; Roost's removal of Flying prints no line and Transform's type copy is not tracked in `types`. Tests: `tests/ml/reducer/test_engine_data_2.py` (every volatile of the random corpus resolves in v2, the refusals, `typechange`), `tests/ml/model/test_gamedata.py`, `engine-data.test.ts`, `samples.test.ts`.
- `team-vocabulary.ts` (ADR-013 §2, #859): `exportTeamVocabulary(engine, format, table?)` is what the team generator may draw from, read from the pinned engine and built on the `exportEngineData` table (it names that table by `engine_data_hash` and its ids resolve in it). It adds the roster the validator accepts, each roster species' abilities and move pool, the natures, the items the format does not ban, and the rule-table values (`adjustLevel`, `evLimit`, Item Clause, team sizes, from the resolved rule table; an unset one is refused, never defaulted). No game rule is written by hand: a species, ability, move, nature or item is in the vocabulary exactly when the pinned `TeamValidator` takes a probe set with it (one Stat Point and a neutral nature, so the "0 Stat Points" check stays quiet) or `checkItem` does not ban it. IVs, the per-stat draw maximum and item weights are `TeamGeneratorConfig` fields, not here. `teamVocabularyHash` (SHA-256 of the JCS bytes) is the `vocabulary_hash` the team manifests carry; `readTeamVocabulary` adds sorted-and-unique ids and, given the table, that every id resolves. The golden `team-vocabulary.json` and `.summary.json` sit beside the engine-data table (MIT-derived, same `NOTICE`); a test re-exports and compares byte for byte (about 10 s), `scripts/export-team-vocabulary.ts` rewrites them.
- `role-sets.ts` (ADR-020 §3, #971): `exportRoleSets(engine, format, vocabulary, table?, dir?)` reads the Champions doubles random-battle sets file of the pinned checkout (`data/random-battles/<mod>/doubles-sets.json`, MIT; the format must be doubles; `dir` defaults to the pinned checkout and exists so a test can doctor a copy) and returns the `RoleSetTable` contract (`packages/schema/contracts/v1/role-set-table.schema.json`, integers only, closed): the commit, `engine_data_hash` and `vocabulary_hash` it was resolved against, `source_path` and `source_sha256`, and per species id a sorted list of `{role, moves[], abilities[]}` (sets sorted by role, then moves, then abilities; moves and abilities sorted ids). Names map to ids by exact name through the engine-data table; the file's `level`, `preferredTypes` and `teraTypes` are dropped (the latter two shape-checked first). It fails closed (`RoleSetError`): a species not in the engine-data table or not in the vocabulary roster, a move or ability name that does not resolve, a move outside the species' roster move pool, an ability the roster does not list for it, a field of the file it does not know, an empty list. A Mega forme's sets name the abilities of the forme it comes from (the form Team Preview shows: venusaurmega lists Chlorophyll, its roster has Thick Fat), so for a roster entry with a required item the exporter accepts the abilities of the roster entries whose forme the stone changes into it (the item's `megaStone` map in the engine's data). `readRoleSetTable(value, vocabulary?)` adds sorted-and-unique ids and sets and, given the vocabulary, its hash, commit, format and engine-data hash and that every id is in the roster (it has no item data, so for a Mega forme it accepts any ability a non-Mega roster entry lists: a hand-edited table that is otherwise consistent passes it, and the exporter plus the byte-for-byte golden test are the exact check; a consumer plays such an entry as the base forme plus its stone, never as a literal Mega forme with those abilities). `roleSetTableHash` is SHA-256 of the JCS bytes. The golden `role-sets.json` and `.summary.json` (342 species, 495 sets at this pin) sit beside the team vocabulary under the engine's MIT notice; `test/role-sets.test.ts` re-exports and compares byte for byte, `scripts/export-role-sets.ts` rewrites them (after `export-team-vocabulary.ts`; it reads the golden vocabulary). No role logic is ported: nothing turns a role into an item or a spread. `check_data_class.py` refuses a role-set table (an object with `source_path`, `source_sha256` and `species`) that holds a `paste`, an `evs` key or a team-shaped object anywhere, `fixtures/` included. The mixture config field and drawing from the table are a later slice.
- `team-records.ts` (ADR-013 §4, #859): the identities and readers of the team records (`packages/schema/contracts/v1/team-{vocabulary,record,label-set,generator-config,dataset-manifest,snapshot-manifest}.schema.json`; all integers-only, no `number`). `canonicalTeamHash(sets)` is SHA-256 of the JCS bytes of the sets sorted by species id (ties by each set's JCS bytes): the same for every permutation, equal to the positional `teamHash` only for the canonical order. `recordHash`, `shardLogicalHash`, `logicalSnapshotHash` (writer-independent) and `snapshotHash` (one physical publication) follow ADR-013 §4. `readTeamRecord(engine, value, labelSet?)` adds the engine round trip (the paste must be the engine's own export of its sets), both team hashes recomputed and the labels' order; `readTeamLabelSet`, `readTeamGeneratorConfig`, `readTeamDatasetManifest` and `readTeamSnapshotManifest` add what the profile cannot say (sorted and unique lists, sums, tiling cell ranges, the recomputed logical hash). A `data_class` of `synthetic` is a constant of every record and manifest. `readTeamDatasetManifest(value, { config, labelSet })` also ties `d_max`, `pilot_draws` and `config_hash` to the config and `label_set_hash` and every cell number to the label set, and checks `merged_cells` (the D20 merge: only merged strata are listed; a stratum is numbered by its lowest member base cell; absence means nothing was merged). `readTeamLabelSet` refuses a default that is also a listed value. The generator is `packages/team-dataset` (below); the Parquet shard reader is a later slice (ADR-013 d1).
- `private-records.ts` (ADR-015 §2, §5, #869): the reader of the `PrivateTeamRecord` contract, crypto-free. `readPrivateTeamRecord(engine, value)` checks `private-team-record.schema.json` (data class `private`, a `paste` of at most 8,192 characters, tags, labels, a boolean `holdout`), the paste's engine round trip, both team hashes, `supersedes_record_hash` null exactly for revision 1, sorted and unique tags and unique label ids; its messages never repeat the paste (tests cover the schema-failure, too-long-paste, engine-refusal and hash-mismatch paths with a canary). `holdout` is the flag ADR-015 derives from a snapshot's keyed HMAC-SHA256 of `team_id` (200000 ppm of teams); this reader has no key and checks only that it is a boolean, so the snapshot reader (#887) recomputes it. `checkPrivateRecordSet` enforces the snapshot rules (unique `team_id`, unique `canonical_team_hash` among active records). `private-samples.ts` builds the corpus samples (the pastes are `fixtures/teams` pastes; one hold-out record). The `PrivateSnapshotManifest` is `private-snapshot.ts` (below).
- `private-snapshot.ts` (ADR-015 §2, #897, slice 1 of #887): the `PrivateSnapshotManifest` contract (`packages/schema/contracts/v1/private-snapshot-manifest.schema.json`, generated TS and pydantic types, integers only) and its pure reader, `readPrivateSnapshotManifest`; no engine, no file system, no key. It mirrors `TeamSnapshotManifest` with `data_class` const `private`, no `dataset_hashes`, one split (`owner`), the `label_set_hash`, `records: {active, retired}` and the `holdout` block `{rule: "hmac-sha256-team-id/1", key: <64 hex>, ppm: 200000}`. Decisions where the ADR left room (also on the ticket): (1) the hold-out block holds the KEY itself, as ADR-015 §2 writes it (the key is not a secret and the snapshot reader recomputes every record's flag from it), not a key commitment; (2) a shard names its `index` (its position, 0 first), never an object path, because the path `snapshots/<snapshot_hash>/shards/<index>.parquet` holds the hash of the manifest that lists the shard; (3) a cell range carries `active_rows` beside `rows`: the ranges tile ALL rows of a shard (retired included) and `cells` count active records only, so the reader checks cells and `records` against the shards from the manifest alone; (4) `first: true` or `parent_snapshot_hash`, exactly one (reader-enforced; the schema leaves both optional). The reader adds shards in strictly ascending `logical_hash` with `index` equal to position, ranges tiling each shard with ascending strata and `active_rows <= rows`, `cells` equal to the shards' active sums and listing exactly the cells with an active record (empty when all are retired), `records.active` equal to the cells' sum, active plus retired equal to the shards' rows, and `logical_snapshot_hash` recomputed (`privateLogicalSnapshotHash`: JCS of `{shards (sorted logical hashes), cells, label_set_hash, manifest, holdout, and parent_snapshot_hash or first: true}`; `privateSnapshotHash` is SHA-256 of the whole manifest and names the snapshot in the bucket). Its messages state a rule and never repeat a value or a key name (a canary test). `holdoutFlag(key, team_id, ppm?)`: HMAC-SHA256 with the 32 bytes the hex key spells, over `team_id` as its 32 lower-case hex ASCII characters, first 8 bytes big endian as n, held out when `n * 10^6 < ppm * 2^64` in integers; it depends on the two ids alone, so a team keeps its flag on every revision. Known answers computed with Python `hmac`/`hashlib` are in the comment of `test/private-snapshot.test.ts`, as are the two manifest digests. The snapshot reader (#899) recomputes each record's flag; the manifest reader needs no key. The corpus samples (`private-samples.ts`, `privateSnapshotSamples`) carry the private label and are listed in `PRIVATE_MANIFEST_MARKERS` (`check_data_class.py`), which accepts such a file only in the strict manifest shape (below).
- `actor-rule.ts`: `actorRule`, the shared A1/A2 actor rule (ADR-005 §1). It plays the first non-pending candidate in canonical order, else the first pending one, and refuses only an empty set (`NoCandidateError`). It is pure and imports only types, so the A2 bot client reuses it.
- `random-legal.ts`: the random-legal baseline (evaluation protocol pool B). `randomLegal(legal, state)` picks uniformly among the non-pending candidates, else among the pending ones (the `actorRule` order of preference), refuses an empty set with `NoCandidateError`, and returns the next state of its explicit Mulberry32 PRNG (a uint32 seed; integer-only, so stable across Node versions; never `Math.random`). `randomLegalActor(seed)` is an A1 `Actor` (it checks the seed at construction); the A2 choice step would thread `randomLegal`'s state through its own battle state, since `session.ts` calls `actorRule` with no per-seat state. Protocol D9 seeding: the caller reduces the HMAC for the seat and policy label to a uint32 (`parseInt(h.slice(0, 8), 16)`; never `Number()` or `>>> 0` on a raw 128-bit value); about 10^5 streams collide in the 32-bit space, every stream is the same 2^32 cycle at another offset, and the label must carry seat and policy id.
- `private-team-source.ts` / `team-inventory.ts` (#775): the owner-team feature inventory, run as `scripts/team-inventory.ts`. Teams come through the `PrivateTeamSource` port (an async iterable of `{name, paste}`; no file system, network or crypto assumed). `PasteDirectorySource` is the one adapter here: it reads `private-data/teams/*.txt` and refuses any path without a `private-data` segment. The inventory runs `importPaste` and `validateTeam`, lists species, items, abilities, moves and Mega use, and flags features that a gap record names (advisory name matches; the detail also lists every gap as `format_wide_gaps`). It writes no file. It refuses unless stdin, stdout and stderr are terminals (and `CLAUDECODE` / `AI_AGENT` are unset; accident prevention) and `/dev/tty` opens, before reading any team; stdout gets counts and per-gap match counts only, and the detail goes to `/dev/tty`. The planned snapshot-backed `PrivateTeamSource` (ADR-015, #888) is not in this package. See `packages/showdown-bridge/README.md`.
- `policy-protocol.ts` / `policy-worker.ts` / `policy-actor.ts` (ADR-010 §2): the policy worker's TS side. `policy-protocol.ts` holds the three worker contracts' validators and reader rules (`readPolicyQuery`: `identity` equals `observation.identity`, no candidate twice; `readHandshake`: contracts sorted by name, checkpoints by role), `contractVersions()` (name, `schema_version` and the SHA-256 of the schema's JCS bytes, for the handshake) and `encodeFrame` (JCS plus `\n`, bounded by the run's maximum line length). `PolicyWorkerClient` spawns the worker with stdio only and an explicit environment, opens the session with the handshake (a different contract set, `encoder_version`, engine-data hash or checkpoint ids is a `PolicyHandshakeError` before any battle), allows one outstanding query per battle and at most `n_parallel` at once, and re-checks every reply (`answersRequest`, the policy id, the `choice` among the query's candidates). Reply lines must be the JCS bytes of their value. Failures are classified for `guardActor`: a dead worker is a `PolicyWorkerError` (its detail names only the class of the last stderr line), an invalid reply a `PolicyReplyError` (the actor turns it into a forfeit `invalid_reply`), `unsupported_candidate` an `UnsupportedCandidateError` and a query the harness cannot send or a worker that refuses it a `PolicyProtocolError` (incidents `unsupported_candidate` and `policy_protocol`). Nothing is retried. A query that overran its budget stays outstanding (`guardActor` cannot cancel it): call `client.beginGame()` before each game, while no game runs on the client, and it restarts the worker if a query was abandoned or the session failed. `policyActor` is the A1 online observation adapter (the player's own stream from the `ActorContext`, the observation through `observe`, the candidates from `enumerateCandidates`, never a `CloneJudge` verdict or the seed) and `sampleU` the hash-based sampler (first 53 bits of SHA-256 over the JCS of `{seed, game, player, policy_id, request_seq}`). `policyGame` takes the battle seed once and uses it for both the engine and the sampler: use it rather than `policyActor` directly. `test/policy-worker.python.test.ts` drives the real Python worker and is skipped where `.venv` does not exist.
- Play temperature and threshold (ADR-023 §1, ADR-010 erratum A6 items 1-2, #1043; a minor change within v1): `PolicyQuery.sampling` of kind `sample` gains the optional `temperature_ppm` (integer, 1 to 1,000,000) and the optional closed `kind`-tagged `truncation` `{kind: "threshold", min_ppm}` (1 to 999,999); argmax takes neither. Writers omit both at the default, so every existing query keeps its JCS bytes, and the readers (`readPolicyQuery`, the worker's `read_query`) refuse an explicit 1,000,000 (reader-invalid sample `temperature-explicit-default`). The handshake's schema hash changes: client and worker ship together. The scorer (`NetworkScorer.probabilities(view, temperature_ppm=...)`) computes `softmax(logits * (10^6 / temperature_ppm))` in float32 over the real candidates (the worker passes the argument only when the query carries one, so the default path is the untouched computation); `sampler.truncate` then gives 0 to every candidate whose probability is below `min_ppm / 10^6` except the FIRST HIGHEST, which is always kept (otherwise a policy over more than 33 candidates could drop all of them at 0.03), renormalises (float32 values over their float64 sum, rounded to float32) and the existing draw follows (float64 sum in canonical order, first candidate whose sum exceeds `u / 2^53`). The policy id keeps `temperature: 1` when `temperature_ppm` is absent; otherwise `temperature_ppm` replaces `temperature`, and a truncation adds `truncation` (`policyId` in `policy-protocol.ts` and `loop/records.py` `policy_id`, vectors computed with Python hashlib and pinned on both sides; sample mode only, so an argmax id never changes). `policyActor` / `PolicySeat` take `play: {temperature_ppm?, truncation?}` and put it in each query; `sampleU` is unchanged (its label holds the policy id, so a tempered policy draws its own `u`). A run's `generation.play` selects the training-time temperature (see Training play temperature); the progress evaluation adds that mode by default.
- Evaluate sampling and `EvaluationReply` (ADR-026 §5, §9 slice Q, #1139; a minor change within v1): `PolicyQuery.sampling` gains the kind `evaluate` (`{kind: "evaluate"}` alone: no `u`, temperature or truncation). The worker answers it with no choice: a new contract `EvaluationReply` (`packages/schema/contracts/v1/evaluation-reply.schema.json`, generated TS and pydantic types, corpus samples `evaluation-reply/` and the `*-evaluate` queries) whose `reply` is `{kind: "evaluation", evaluation: {identity, prior_ppm[], value_ppm}}` or the same `ErrorReply` as a `PolicyReply`. `PolicyReply` is untouched (an evaluate query never gets a recommendation), so every existing query, reply and policy id keeps its bytes; the handshake's PolicyQuery schema hash changes (client and worker ship together) and `EvaluationReply` is not in the handshake's contract list (a worker whose PolicyQuery hash agrees speaks it). ONE rounding rule, stated in the schema: the float32 value widened exactly, then `ppm = floor(x * 10^6 + 0.5)` in binary64 (`sampler.to_ppm`), each entry rounded alone and not renormalised (the sum of `prior_ppm` is 10^6 only up to half a ppm per entry); `value_ppm` is the value head's output unclamped (it may leave 0..10^6; a non-finite or out-of-range value is a `ScoreError`, a worker crash, never a reply). The prior is the probabilities of a sample query at temperature 1 with no truncation, from the same forward pass as the value (`NetworkScorer.evaluate`, batch of one on CPU under `configure_cpu_inference`; the `Scorer` protocol gained `evaluate`). `candidate_features` are read exactly as for any query (a features pair needs the rows, a plain pair refuses them). Reader rules: `identity` equals `observation.identity` and no candidate twice, as for every query (the explicit-default temperature rule does not apply). The TS client has `evaluate(battle, query)` (`EvaluationClient`, beside `ask`; `ask` refuses an evaluate query and `evaluate` any other): same session rules (one query per battle, `n_parallel`), and it re-checks the reply (`readEvaluationReply`, `answersRequest` on the identity, one `prior_ppm` entry per candidate in the query's order; a bad reply is a `PolicyReplyError`, an `unsupported_candidate` an `UnsupportedCandidateError`, a refusal a `PolicyProtocolError`). Tests: `tests/ml/test_worker_evaluate.py` (alone, repeated and after others bit-identical; A1 and A2 forms equal; a features pair; the rounding rule), `policy-worker.test.ts` (the fake worker's `eval-*` modes) and `policy-worker.python.test.ts` (the real worker). Not here: the search actor (slice R) and leaves on hypothetical channels.
- `ActorContext`: `runBattle` calls an `Actor` as `actor(request, { player, channel, team })`, where `channel` is a copy of that player's own stream up to and including the request (no `|t:|` lines, never the omniscient log) and `team` (optional in the type, ADR-018 §2, #944) is that seat's own set array as `runBattle` was given it: a deeply frozen copy taken before the validator fills defaults (the array `team_hashes` binds), never the opponent's. The second argument is optional in the type, so actors that ignore it are unchanged; `guardActor` forwards the context as it is. The context type holds nothing of the opponent's team, the omniscient log, the hidden verdicts or the seed (`test/actor-context.test.ts` has the type test).
- `ml/areneon_ml/worker` (ADR-010 §2, Python): the policy worker, `python -m areneon_ml.worker --run-dir … --preview <id> --battle <id> --engine-data … --max-line-bytes …`. One JCS line per frame, strict (duplicate keys, floats and non-canonical lines refused); first frame the hello, answered with `ready` (and the session ends if the contracts differ); then one `PolicyReply` per `PolicyQuery`, in order, one query per pass. A malformed frame ends the process (exit 2); a query that breaks a contract or reader rule gets an `invalid_query` reply, one with a candidate shape `candidates.py` does not know an `unsupported_candidate` reply. Checkpoints are read only as `<run dir>/checkpoints/<id>.safetensors`, must hash to their id, must hold the network of the role they were named for, and must have been made for the worker's engine-data table and encoder version (`ready` carries the encoder version, the engine-data hash and the checkpoint ids). A reducer or encoder refusal of a query's observation is an `invalid_query` reply (no log line echoed), a candidate the encoder cannot back with the request an `unsupported_candidate` reply. CPU inference is made deterministic (`configure_cpu_inference`) before the first query. `view.py` is what an encoder may read of a query (no `decision_context`, no `rqid` even inside the log's `|request|` lines, no sampling or policy fields), `sampler.py` picks from the probabilities (float32 values summed in float64, first candidate whose sum exceeds `u / 2^53`), and the scorer is `model/scorer.py`'s `NetworkScorer` (below). No module of `ml/areneon_ml` (worker, model, checkpoint, reducer) imports a network module (an AST test scans them; third-party code such as torch is not scanned, and a session test runs with sockets disabled). `PolicyQuery`, `PolicyReply` and `PolicyHandshake` (`packages/schema/contracts/v1`) are the worker's contracts. `PolicyReply`, `PolicyHandshake` and the `query_id` field (correlating replies when a worker serves several battles) go beyond the ADR-010 §5 table and are to be listed in its next erratum.
- `golden.ts`: the golden battle (each side plays `firstLegal`, which applies `actorRule` to the request's legal choice set), team-fixture and verdict builders shared by the tests and the capture script.

**Log-to-state reducer** (`ml/areneon_ml/reducer/`, ADR-010 §3, R10, #838), Python and clean-room (grammar from the repo fixtures, `sim/SIM-PROTOCOL.md` and ADR-006/008; no Showdown client parser): `reduce_channel(player, lines, team, vocab)` turns ONE player's own channel plus that player's own team into a `BattleState` (`own` / `opponent` halves: HP as the player sees it, status, boosts, volatiles, revealed moves/items/abilities, Mega, active slots, weather, terrain, field and side conditions, turn, winner; own exact values come from the player's own requests). Ids are checked against a `Vocabulary` built from the hashed engine-data export, never against lists of its own. It fails closed with typed `ReducerError`s (`UnknownLineError`, `UnknownFieldError`, `MalformedLineError`, `VocabularyError`, `OpponentSecretError`): an unknown line type, tag, positional field or request key raises, a `|split|`, an opponent HP not out of 100 or a request for the other player is an `OpponentSecretError`. A `[from] item:`/`ability:` counts only without `[of]`, and a move is revealed only when chosen (not when called by another effect). Limits, known and deliberate: volatile ids (`-start`/`-end`) are kept as opaque public strings after a shape check, because the engine-data export has no volatile or condition table (a volatile/condition vocabulary, engine-data schema_version 2, is a follow-up the encoder will need); a field or side condition must still be a move or an ability of the vocabulary. Illusion (#851): every switch-in is bound to the Pokemon it is shown as, with a copy of that roster entry as it was before (`_Disguise`), and `|replace|POKEMON|DETAILS` (the engine sends only the new id and details, no HP) says who it was: `_settle` carries everything seen at that position since the switch-in (hit points, status, boosts, volatiles, item and ability changes, moves used) to the real Pokemon's entry, puts the Pokemon it was shown as back as it was before the switch-in (out of play, if the Illusion user took its place), names the real one from the line and records its ability (Illusion). Before that line nothing of the real Pokemon is known: its roster entry stays as the preview gave it (the species is public from `|poke|`; its name, position, hit points, moves and ability are not). The player's own Illusion user is found at its next own request instead (the request lists the real Pokemon in each position, and an own mismatch whose real Pokemon holds Illusion is settled there, so boosts and volatiles are right before the replace; the position keeps the disguise's name for the lines that follow). Limits, deliberate: a disguise that leaves play without a `|replace|` stays attributed to the Pokemon it was shown as (the player saw that Pokemon; a later replace corrects only the stint it ends); a Pokemon that fainted shown as another (no replace) leaves a fainted `phantom` entry (an extra roster entry flagged `phantom` in `to_dict`, not counted against the encoder's `max_team`) when the Pokemon it was shown as is shown again, because a fainted Pokemon cannot come back; the same Pokemon shown in two positions (one is an Illusion user) gets a phantom entry for the second until a replace or an own request says which, and the encoder takes it as the extra row the player sees. Ambiguity the channel cannot settle raises `IllusionError` (a Pokemon shown in three positions, a replace that fits two roster entries or none, a replace with no disguise on record, an own pair the request leaves open). `moves_revealed` is what a Pokemon can have at one time (#938): Transform (`-transform`) replaces the whole list with what the Pokemon uses as the copy (the engine's `moveSlots` is the target's; the target's own list is not copied) and the list it had comes back on switching out or fainting (`moves_base`, not in `to_dict`), and the target's stat stages are copied (the engine's `transformInto`; no line states them), so a Pokemon shows at most four slots plus Struggle (which is no slot); Mimic and Sketch are not moves of the format (not in the engine-data vocabulary: a `|move|` of them is a `VocabularyError`), so their slot replacement is not tracked, and a pin or format that adds them needs it (`-start|P|Mimic|MOVE`, `-activate|P|move: Sketch|MOVE`). `fixtures/showdown/<format>/transform/battle.json` is the captured game that stopped run public-main-002 (a Ditto transformed twice showed nine moves under max_moves 8), `tests/ml/reducer/test_moves.py`. Trace (or Role Play) records the copied ability on both the tracer and the `[of]` target (the tracer's original ability is not tracked). `-activate` of Skill Swap clears both abilities (unknown), as the per-Pokemon result is not stated. Channel lines of the A2 server path (`|t:|`, `|c|`, `|inactive|`) raise here and belong to the A2 adapter slice, which strips them first. The HP colour class the champions mod appends at exactly 50% and 20% (`50/100g`, `20/100y`) is accepted and dropped. Transform (#938): on `-transform` the reducer stashes the user's revealed move list (`moves_base`, not in `to_dict`, so never in an observation) and starts the copy's list empty, filling it only from `|move|` lines; switching out or fainting restores the stash, and the target's stat stages are copied (the engine's `transformInto` copies boosts; `-transform` is SOURCE then TARGET). So a list is at most four slots plus Struggle. The reducer's state logic is not part of the encoder version (`ENCODER_FORMAT` is not bumped), so a reducer change alters the encoded inputs of the games it affects (here those with Transform) without a new encoder version; the bit-identical rerun claim holds per reducer commit, and a run resumed after a reducer change re-verifies only its newest generation. Baton Pass (#1099): the engine's `copyVolatileFrom` gives the incoming Pokemon the outgoing one's stat stages and prints no line for them; the switch line carries `[from] Baton Pass` (Shed Tail passes none), and the reducer and the replay interpreter hand the stages over on such a switch (a reset there made a later `|-boost|`, whose amount is the engine's capped delta, exceed 6: `boost 7 is outside -6..6` stopped run public-main-009). `fixtures/showdown/<format>/baton-pass/battle.json` (`scripts/capture-baton-pass-battle.ts`), `tests/ml/reducer/test_baton_pass.py`. Passed volatiles (#1102): the same `copyVolatileFrom` copies every volatile except those whose condition data says `noCopy` (a Shed Tail switch copies Substitute alone), again with no line. The rule is read from the pinned engine by `packages/showdown-bridge/src/volatile-copy.ts` (`exportVolatileCopy`: the ids of the conditions of the format's mod, from conditions, moves, abilities and items, with `noCopy`, plus the Shed Tail literal, which the export checks the engine source still says) into the reviewed, hashed data file `ml/areneon_ml/reducer/data/volatile-copy.<format>.json` (`volatile_copy_hash` = SHA-256 of the JCS of the other fields; `readVolatileCopy` / `read_volatile_copy` refuse an unknown key, an unsorted list or a wrong hash; `test/volatile-copy.test.ts` re-exports and compares byte for byte, `scripts/export-volatile-copy.ts` rewrites it after a pin bump). The file is package data of `areneon-ml`: `Vocabulary.from_engine_data` loads it by format and keeps it only if its `showdown_commit` is the export's (`not_copied` / `shed_tail_copies`; none for another pin, and then a Baton Pass or Shed Tail switch raises `VocabularyError`, never a guess). The reducer and the interpreter keep on such a switch exactly the outgoing slot's volatiles the rule passes (the stat stages for Baton Pass only, and `ability_suppressed` while `gastroacid` passes: Gastro Acid prints only `-endability`), then the outgoing Pokemon keeps none. The interpreter takes the rule as `dex.volatile_copy` (the replay page embeds it; the `@areneon/public-state` wrapper adds the one committed table to a dex that has none; the raw `.cjs` without it flags the switch line rather than guess). A volatile the engine prints under a derived name (Stockpile `stockpile1`..`3`, Protosynthesis and Quark Drive `protosynthesisatk`) is matched to its condition id by dropping a trailing number or stat id (`atk def spa spd spe`), in both readers. Limits: the engine's `onCopy` hooks are not modelled (Gastro Acid is dropped silently when the incoming Pokemon's ability cannot be suppressed; Power Trick/Shift stat swaps are internal), and a `transform` volatile the reducer records is passed like any other id although the engine's receiver is not transformed. `fixtures/showdown/<format>/baton-pass-volatiles/battle.json` (the same script's second battle: Substitute + Baton Pass users, found when a Substitute is handed on), `tests/ml/reducer/test_baton_pass_volatiles.py`, parity and `packages/public-state/test/baton-pass.test.ts`. Reducer sweep (#919, 350 seeded battles, three mixes, `--only-move 'Baton Pass,Shed Tail'` on the committed fixtures dataset): `unexplained: 0`. Fling of an item that is already gone (#1162): the engine's Fling ends the thrown item in the move's own `onUpdate`, after the hit, and reads the holder's item then; when the hit started a terrain through Seed Sower and a Grassy Seed was eaten at once, `getItem()` names nothing and the line is `|-enditem|POKEMON||[from] move: Fling` (run public-main-010, generation 19, game 390: `VocabularyError: unknown item ''`). The reducer and the interpreter accept an empty item name only with that exact `[from]` tag (the holder ends with no item, lost; the engine's `setItem('')`), and any other empty name is still refused. `fixtures/showdown/<format>/fling/battle.json` (`scripts/capture-fling-battle.ts`), `tests/ml/reducer/test_fling.py`. Known, unchanged: a `-boost ... [from] item: Grassy Seed` that follows the seed's `-enditem` sets the holder's `item` back to the seed (with `item_lost` kept true) until a later line ends it. A repeated `[spread]` tag is accepted (the engine's `attrLastMove` appends one per call that reaches the move line, e.g. Magic Bounce reflecting a spread move; the last value is kept), every other repeated tag with a value still fails closed. Frisk (#1171): `|-item|TARGET|ITEM|[from] ability: Frisk|[of] FRISKER` is the target's item (as before) and now also reveals the Frisk holder's ability (`frisk` on the `[of]` Pokemon; before, no reader credited it to anyone), in the reducer and the shared interpreter alike (`tests/ml/reducer/test_corpus.py`, `packages/public-state/test/lines.test.ts` over every committed Frisk reveal); this changes `readerSha256` (no committed vector pins it: the damage-aware and feature-set identity hashes are recomputed from it, so a run or record made before this change names the old reader hash; `config/search/rerank-1.v1.json` names the belief identity hash, which moved from `1af5ea2a...` to `0588f5ec...` and was edited in place, since no run has used it) and the replay pages' hashed script. `-copyboost|A|B` sets A's boosts from B's (the engine's Psych Up and Costar code; sim/SIM-PROTOCOL.md says the reverse). The #919 sweep (5,500 seeded battles on the generated dataset, five actor mixes) found these two and no other refusal. A player-alone `|error|[Unavailable choice] <text>` (the engine's refusal of a pending candidate, #909; on A2 also `[Invalid choice] <text>` from the server layer) changes no state and the text is dropped; any other `|error|` shape is a `MalformedLineError`. `-zbroken` (a Z-move breaking Protect, champions mod) changes no state. Tests: `tests/ml/reducer/test_unavailable.py` over `fixtures/showdown/<format>/unavailable/` (seeded pending-first battles that reach the refusal, with reduced snapshots `reduced/unavailable-*.json`).

**Recent history** (#1013, ADR-010 erratum A4 item 7; `state.py` `Recent`, `reducer.py`; `STATE_SCHEMA_VERSION` 2). The reduced state now carries what happened lately, from the player's own channel only; the reducer's state is still not part of `encoder_version` and nothing consumes the fields yet (the encoder option is #1014), so the encoder, checkpoints and policy ids are unchanged, but `to_dict` gained keys, so every `reduced/*.json` golden was rewritten (additions only; the replay interpreter does not carry them and the parity test strips `recent`, `condition_turns` and `elapsed`). Per Pokemon `recent` (reset on switching out or fainting, carried by an Illusion `_settle`): `active_turns` (`|turn|` lines since the switch-in, as the world facts' `active_turns`), `last_move` and `last_target` (`own:a` / `opponent:b`: the first target the `|move|` line prints, so the first foe of a spread move; null when the line prints none or `null`) with `last_move_turn` (a `|move|` without `[from]`, `[from]lockedmove` included, as the world facts' `last_move`; Round/Dancer-style action sources are not counted here because the reducer holds no such list), `last_action_turn`/`last_action_order` (the place among the turn's move and `|cant|` lines, from 1), `hp_taken_*` and `hp_dealt_*` for `this_turn` and `last_turn` (the sum of `-damage` decreases, in the units the player sees: exact for an own Pokemon, points of 100 for an opponent; damage dealt is in the TARGET's units, so `hp_dealt_*` is NOT one scale: percent when the Pokemon hit a foe, exact own HP when a foe hit an own Pokemon (an ally hit by a spread move counts too, in the ally's units), and #1014 must not treat it as one scale; an opponent's `hp_dealt_*` is therefore own exact HP, which the canary helper `leaks` does not scan; dealt is the untagged damage after a `|move|`, anything with `[from]` is taken but dealt by nobody; heals and `-sethp` are not counted) and `protect_streak` (consecutive turns of successful stalling uses, as the world facts' `protect_streak`: `-singleturn` of the user after its move succeeds, `-fail` ends it; the engine prints one of the two after every stalling `|move|`, so a stalling move still waiting at the next `|move|`, `|cant|`, switch, `|turn|`, `|win|` or `|tie|` is a `MalformedLineError`, as the world facts' `stall_unresolved`). Which moves stall is engine data: `ml/areneon_ml/reducer/data/stall-moves.<format>.json` (`stall_moves_hash`, `packages/showdown-bridge/src/stall-moves.ts`, `scripts/export-stall-moves.ts` after a pin bump, `test/stall-moves.test.ts` re-exports and compares byte for byte and holds it equal to the world facts' `stall_moves`); `Vocabulary.stall_moves` is None for a pin the file was not exported for, and then EVERY `|move|` (not only a stalling one) raises `VocabularyError`: re-export after a pin bump (`read_stall_moves` refuses an unknown key, a hash mismatch, another format or schema version, as `read_volatile_copy`). Field: `elapsed` (`weather`, `terrain`, `field_conditions` by id) and per side `condition_turns` are the turns since each began (0: this turn; the first layer's turn; a `[upkeep]` weather line starts nothing; `-swapsideconditions` swaps them); the engine's `after_residual` nuance of the world facts is not modelled. Tests: `tests/ml/reducer/test_recent.py` (hand-written logs for every field, the canary and its negative controls).

Tests (`tests/ml/reducer/`): a unit test per line type, the refusals, the canary with negative controls, a drift test of the request key sets against `engine-request.schema.json`, a corpus test over `fixtures/showdown/<format>/random/` (seeded random-legal vs random-legal battles on random synthetic teams, `random-corpus.ts`; CI re-plays each from its committed seed, teams and actor seeds) that requires zero refusals (Illusion's `|replace|` included), with the canary at every request point, `test_illusion.py` (hand-written logs for every Illusion branch, 100% on the reducer) and `test_illusion_battle.py` over `fixtures/showdown/<format>/illusion/battle.json` (a seeded random-legal battle with a Zoroark on both sides whose disguises break, `scripts/capture-illusion-battle.ts`; its canary: before the replace nothing of the real Pokemon is in the state), and golden snapshots `fixtures/showdown/<format>/reduced/p1.json` / `p2.json` for every committed channel (and `illusion-p1.json` / `illusion-p2.json`) (rewrite deliberately with `ARENEON_UPDATE_GOLDEN=1 uv run pytest tests/ml/reducer/test_golden.py --no-cov` and review the diff).

**Encoder, candidate parser, networks, checkpoints and the C1/C6 checker** (`ml/areneon_ml/model/`, `ml/areneon_ml/checkpoint/`, ADR-010 §3, §5, #839). `GameData.from_table(engine_data)` indexes the hashed engine-data export: one `IdVocab` per vocabulary (species, moves, items, abilities, statuses, weathers, terrains, types; index 0 is none, 1 unknown, the engine's ids follow in the export's order) and the numeric attribute tables (types, base stats, weight; move power, accuracy, priority, PP, target and flag one-hots; item flags), each column scaled by the largest value in the data. No list of game content is written in model code, so a vocabulary grows with the table. `Encoder(game, EncoderConfig).encode(query)` reads only `query["observation"]` (`player`, `kind`, `log`, `team.team`) and `query["candidates"]` (`choice`, `pending`): the log goes through `reduce_channel`, the player's last `|request|` gives the Pokémon order (`switch N` / `team NNNN` name the Pokémon at that request position) and the move ids, and it returns tensors without a batch dimension (`EncodedQuery`: one token row per own then opponent Pokémon, the field features, per-candidate slot parts, the picked team tokens and the `pending` flag). It ignores `decision_context`, `identity` (so `rqid`), the `rqid` inside `|request|` lines and every verdict field, so the A1 and A2 forms of a decision give equal tensors (tests over every request of the `fixtures/a2/` recordings and the `policy-query` samples). Volatiles and field/side conditions are engine-owned ids with no vocabulary yet, so they are SHA-256 bucketed into `volatile_buckets` / `condition_buckets`. `encoder_version` is `enc<format>:` + SHA-256 of the engine-data hash, the attribute tables as built, the config (scales included), the feature layout and the index constants (genders, request kinds, events, target codes, slot letters, the bucket hash), so a pin bump or any such change is a new version, never a silent remap. A change in how a value is computed from the state, with the same names and constants, is not visible to the hash: bump `ENCODER_FORMAT`; a golden test pins one version string and the tensor digests of fixed queries and fails on such a change. The A1/A2 equality is also tested on `fixtures/showdown/<format>/a2-pairs.json` (the A1 replay's observations and the A2 frames' observations of the ADR-005 fixtures with candidates from the real `enumerateCandidates`; rewrite with `node packages/bot-client/scripts/capture-encoder-pairs.ts`, a bot-client test compares it with a fresh capture). The encoder raises (fails closed) when an observation's kind does not match its request or candidates, or a Pokémon shows more moves than `max_moves`. `parse_choice` (`model/candidates.py`, re-exported by the worker) splits a canonical candidate into `SlotAction`s (pass, move with optional target and event, switch) or a Team Preview order and refuses any other shape with `UnsupportedCandidate`; `ParsedChoice.format()` is its exact inverse, and `tests/ml/model/test_candidates.py` round-trips every candidate of every `fixtures/showdown/**/choices/*.json`. `CheckpointRecord` (`packages/schema/contracts/v1/checkpoint-record.schema.json`, generated TS and pydantic types, corpus samples in `fixtures/contracts/v1/checkpoint-record/`) is the content-addressed provenance record of one frozen checkpoint; `checkpoint/record.py` reads it (sorted and unique hash arrays, no id referring to the record itself). Part 2 (networks and checkpoints, #839). `PolicyNetwork(role, game, ModelConfig)` (`model/network.py`) is one architecture per role, built and stored separately: a transformer over the entity tokens plus a field token and an aggregation token (attention written out as matmul and softmax, no fused kernel), a candidate head (a `battle` candidate embeds its two slot actions, move, target, event, acting Pokémon and switch-in tokens; a `preview` candidate its ordered team positions; both add the pending flag, cross-attend over the state and score against the aggregation token) and a value head on the aggregation token. Every size is configuration (`ModelConfig`: `d_model`, layers, heads, `d_ff` and the nested `EncoderConfig`; there are no defaults: the `model` section of the run's `TrainingRunConfig` carries the values and code only bounds them), same-seed initialisation is claimed for one machine and software only, vocabulary sizes come from `GameData`, and attribute tables are buffers derived from the engine data, not parameters. `collate` pads queries into a `Batch` (training may batch); evaluation runs one query per forward pass, a batch of one with no padding, on CPU with `configure_cpu_inference()` (deterministic algorithms, one thread), and `tests/ml/model/test_network.py` shows a query's logits are bit-identical alone and after others, and equal for the A1 and A2 forms. A checkpoint (`checkpoint/files.py`) is one safetensors file: the tensors, and `__metadata__` with the format, role, model config, encoder version and engine-data hash. The writer is our own so the bytes are deterministic (the library orders metadata by hash-map iteration); the id is the SHA-256 of the bytes; `load_checkpoint(data, game, expected_id, role)` (the id is required and checked before anything is read; the configuration is bounded, so a crafted file cannot allocate without limit) refuses bytes that do not hash to the id, another role, engine-data table or encoder version, an invalid configuration, and tensors whose names, dtype or shape differ from the network's.
