No pickle and no `torch.load` anywhere (a syntactic AST scan of all of `ml/areneon_ml` (a guard, not a proof: it misses aliased and dynamic imports) bans imports of pickle, dill, cloudpickle, shelve, marshal and numpy and calls of `torch.load` / `torch.jit.load`). numpy is not in the closure: the writer and reader move bytes through `ctypes`/`torch.frombuffer`. `python -m areneon_ml.checkpoint init --role … --seed … --engine-data … --run-dir … --model-config f` (the `model` section of a `TrainingRunConfig`, required) writes a seeded, untrained checkpoint (generation 0, and the input of the worker tests); learner state and training are below (#840). `checkpoint/provenance.py` is the C1/C6 checker (`ProvenanceGraph(records, sources)`; the `sources` registry is a HAND-SUPPLIED, UNHASHED input for now, a documented deviation from ADR-010 §5 until follow-up #853 derives and hash-binds it from the league run record: it must be canonical (RFC 8785) JSON, and `classify` / `independent` print its hash so an evaluation plan can bind it; `python -m areneon_ml.checkpoint classify|independent --records <dir of CheckpointRecord json> --sources <json>`): the ancestry of a checkpoint is itself plus everything reachable through `parent`, `league_opponents`, `exploiter_target` and the generators of its training sets (the `sources` registry maps each training-set hash to the checkpoints that generated it; an empty list is an outside behaviour-cloning or fine-tuning source, and a set missing from the registry is refused); C6 independence is disjoint ancestries (learned checkpoints and data sources; scripted baselines and the team pool are not nodes), C1 calls an opponent `seen` when it is in the candidate's ancestry, a scripted baseline, or a related lineage, and `held_out` only when every opponent checkpoint is an evaluation exploiter of the candidate or an independent lineage (one related member of a Preview + Battle pair makes the pair `seen`). The records must agree with each other: a parent shares its child's encoder version, a parent shares the child's role (a warm start cannot cross architectures), league opponents and the exploiter target may be either half of a frozen Preview + Battle pair, and nothing synthetic descends from a private checkpoint. It fails closed: a referenced checkpoint without a record, a repeated record, a cycle, or an exploiter in its own target's league (C3) is a `ProvenanceError`.

**Mechanics probe** (ADR-016 slice 1, `packages/showdown-bridge/src/mechanics-*.ts`, `scripts/mechanics.ts`). Areneon computes no stat, Speed or damage value: the probe builds a scenario battle from declared sets on the pinned engine and reads the engine's own values. Contracts (`packages/schema/contracts/v1/mechanics-{query,result,config}.schema.json`, generated TS and pydantic types, corpus samples in `fixtures/contracts/v1/mechanics-*/`): a `MechanicsQuery` is `{data_class, format_id, query}` with a `kind`-tagged `query` (`stats`, `speed_order`, `damage`; sets are PokePaste in the engine's canonical form, state uses engine ids, boosts are absolute); a `MechanicsResult` is `computed`, `unsupported` (closed `reason`: `query_kind`, `scenario_rule_table`, `random_effect`; a position or null) or `refused` (closed code `invalid_set` and the set's position), integers only, no free text, with `query_hash` (SHA-256 of the query's JCS bytes), the version manifest, `mechanics_config_hash`, `adapter_format` (`MECHANICS_ADAPTER_FORMAT`, bumped when the probe changes how it reads a value) and `gap_ids`. A document that fails the query schema has no `query_hash`: `readMechanicsQuery` throws `MechanicsQueryError` and the CLI exits 2 with no result. `config/mechanics/<format>.json` (`MechanicsConfig`, read by `mechanics-config.ts`) is reviewed data outside `rules/`: it is not in the rules snapshot, so `RULES_SCHEMA_VERSION` stays 1 and neither `format_rules_hash` nor `rules_snapshot_hash` moves because of it; it lists the rules removed from the target to make the scenario format (`removed_rules`, today the five non-mechanics rules), the exact rule-table keys that removal takes away (`removed_rule_keys`, 12 at this pin), the query kinds in scope with the gap ledger rows each depends on, and `ko_blockers` (items and abilities, slice 5). Scope can only restrict: a kind the config lists that the probe cannot answer throws, never approximates. `mechanicsConfigHash` is SHA-256 of the config's JCS bytes. `mechanics-scenario.ts`: `checkScenario` refuses (`ScenarioError`, and an `unsupported` `scenario_rule_table` result from the probe) unless the scenario format's rule table equals the target's minus exactly the listed keys with no key of its own, the same mod, game type, battle options, shared rule sources and values, and the same level, EV-limit and move-count fields; `startScenario` starts a two-a-side battle at once with no Team Preview. `mechanics-probe.ts`: `probeMechanics(context, query)` takes only a `ParsedMechanicsQuery`, a brand only `readMechanicsQuery` makes, so a Battle, BattleRecord, OmniscientLog, hidden verdicts or PlayerChannel is a compile error (a type test shows it) and a run-time schema refusal. Order: each declared set must be the engine's canonical paste of one Pokemon and pass the target format's `TeamValidator.validateSet` (else `refused`, the validator's own words go to the diagnostics and the CLI's stderr, never into a result), then the scope, then the scenario check, then the probe. `stats` (the six stats of one set, `maxhp` and `storedStats` of a scenario battle) and `damage` (slice 3, below) are answered; **`speed_order`** (ADR-016 slice 2, `mechanics-speed.ts`): Areneon computes no Speed or priority. The probe starts a scenario battle (a side with one declared Pokémon also starts a copy, fainted at once through the engine's `faint`/`faintMessages` so it is no active Pokémon and ties nothing), replaces the engine's PRNG with one whose every method throws, applies the declared state through the engine's own methods and reads the keys with the engine's `queue.resolveAction` (which runs `getActionSpeed`, the priority events and the fractional priority) and `comparePriority`. State is absolute: weather and terrain are set or cleared (a switch-in weather the query does not declare is gone), Trick Room, the four side conditions, status (`setStatus`), HP (`sethp`), the five boost stages and an already evolved Mega (`runMegaEvo`) are written as declared (a Pokemon that is not declared already evolved first tries to take its status BEFORE the field, #1198: it is an observed fact, and Misty Terrain and Electric Terrain refuse a status once up, so a status seen before the terrain started used to refuse the whole query. Only success counts: a refusal there, for instance by a switch-in terrain the query clears, changes nothing and the old place decides, a random draw is raised at the old place, so every query answered before has the same bytes and `adapter_format` stays 1: only refused queries became answers. A Pokemon declared already evolved keeps the old order, Evolution then status, because its new ability may change the field, so Misty Terrain still refuses its status: a known limit), and a state the engine refuses (not a weather, a status the Pokémon cannot take, HP above the maximum) is a `MechanicsProbeError` (no result), as is a move the engine lacks, a Mega Evolution that is not possible (a switch, no stone) and a move that adds actions of its own (Focus Punch's before-turn action). Each action is resolved with a fixed foe target so the move's own target draw cannot end the probe (`getRandomTarget` answers its first candidate: the target never enters the order keys; the contract's action has none). A Mega Evolution is its own action (order 104, speed before the Evolution): Mega groups come first in speed order, `runMegaEvo` runs on the scenario battle in that order, `getActionSpeed` is read again for every action, and the move groups follow, so a Mega's new Speed counts that turn. Groups come in the engine's order values: switches (103), then the Evolutions (104), then the moves (200). Tie groups are the runs of actions `comparePriority` calls equal (indices ascending in a group, an Evolution's index appearing again for its move), never sampled; `order`, `priority` and `speed` are the action's own keys after the Evolutions (a switch is order 103, priority 0; Trick Room makes `speed` negative, the champions mod has no wrap, so Speed 1809 and up acts last: the contract's `speed` minimum is -99999, a bound loosened within v1). Any draw (Quick Claw, a declared sleep) is `unsupported` with reason `random_effect` and the position of the action (or of the Pokémon, while the state is declared); a fractional priority (an item or ability with a fractional bonus; none is legal in the format at this pin) cannot be an integer and is a `MechanicsProbeError`. A random-draw `position` is a Pokémon's index while the state is declared and an action's index afterwards. The side-condition, Trick Room and stage names are the query schema's fixed names mapped to engine ids; a test requires each id in the pinned data. `order_basis` is `turn_start`: a speed change during the turn (Icy Wind, a paralysing hit) is not modelled. Every result lists the config's gap ids (`paralysis`, `trick-room`) and `mega-evolution` when an action or a combatant Mega Evolves (`MEGA_GAP_ID`, a probe constant that `checkMechanicsConfig` requires in the ledger whenever the kind is in scope); the config schema has no list of state inputs, so Tailwind, Trick Room, paralysis and Choice Scarf are carried by the probe code and these gap ids. `readMechanicsQuery` adds the reader rules the profile cannot say for a speed order (each action names a declared Pokémon, once, and no Evolution of one that already evolved). `MechanicsProbeError` lives in `mechanics-error.ts` and is re-exported from the probe. Tests: `mechanics-speed.test.ts` holds the hand-worked fixtures `fixtures/mechanics/<format>/speed/cases.json` (synthetic sets; the expected speeds, priorities and groups were worked on paper from the stat formula, see the README beside it: priority brackets, an exact tie, Tailwind, Trick Room with Speed 1872, paralysis, Choice Scarf, stages, declared and cleared weather, Mega faster and moving first, two Megas, Quick Claw `unsupported`) and the refusals; `mechanics-speed-battle.test.ts` is the required cross-check: for every untied fixture case and for the committed dev dataset's team pairs (with the state the real battle starts in, read from it) the order of the probe equals the order of the `|move|`/`|cant|` lines of a real turn of the engine, skipping a pair whose real turn changes a Speed or field condition between its first and last action. The required `mechanics-stats.test.ts` holds the probe's stats equal to the target format's own Team Preview request stats for every committed fixture team (the `champions-stats.test.ts` seam) and to the five displayed reference cases. CLI: `node packages/showdown-bridge/scripts/mechanics.ts QUERY.json [--config FILE]` prints the result as one JCS line (exit 0 for any result, 2 for no result); `scripts/check-mechanics-config.ts` (in `make ts` and CI) checks the config against the pinned engine and the gap ledger. The `computed-damage-ko-counts` sample is a shape sample, not a probe output; the `speed_order` result sample is a probe output, and the `unsupported` sample comes from a config that lists `stats` alone.  The `damage-formula` gap record names the pin's own Champions `modifyDamage` override as evidence (still `unverified`; the rules snapshot hash moved, the channel did not).

**Damage probe** (slice 3, `mechanics-damage.ts`, `mechanics-random.ts`). The engine's own `getDamage` deals every number. The scenario battle always holds two Pokemon a side: the declared one in slot 0 and a partner of the same species with no ability and no item (so both foes are present and the engine sets `spreadHit` itself, a screen sees more than one active Pokemon, and no ally ability changes the declared Pokemon's damage). Declared state is applied after the start, absolute: Mega (`runMegaEvo`), HP, status, boosts, side conditions, Trick Room, and the field is cleared first so a weather or terrain a switch-in set never survives unless declared. The move runs through the engine's own `useMoveInner` on a clone (target resolution, `ModifyMove`, the immunity and `TryHit` steps) with two instance replacements: the accuracy step passes and the damage loop calls `getDamage` for the defender only and reads the value, so nothing is dealt. 33 clones per query (the reference path, see the one-clone path below): the 16 rolls with `willCrit` set false, the 16 with it true, and one with the move's own crit rule to observe the chance. The recording random source (`recordingRandom`) answers `random(16)` with the roll index and `randomChance` with a fixed outcome, and records every call; `acceptedDraws` accepts a run only when its draws are one `random(16)` (preceded in the observation run by at most one `randomChance`) or none (an immunity, a fixed-damage move), and any other draw (a random base power such as Magnitude, an ability or item with a random effect, any other PRNG member) makes the result `unsupported` (`random_effect`, position null); no ability or item of this pin draws on the damage path, so the refusal is shown with random-base-power moves and the source's own tests. The crit chance is read from the observation run: the engine's one `randomChance(n, d)` pair as passed (`chance`), a crit it makes with no draw (`always`), or none or a cancelled crit (`never`); the probe holds no crit table. A move the engine's immunity or protection steps stop (the run notes it from the engine's own `hitStepInvulnerabilityEvent`, `hitStepTryHitEvent`, `hitStepTypeImmunity` and `hitStepTryImmunity` results) deals nothing: sixteen zeros; a move that never reaches the damage step otherwise (a charge turn, a failed precondition such as Sucker Punch, a self-targeting move) is `unsupported` (`query_kind`); fixed and HP-based damage (`damage`, `damageCallback`, `ohko` in the move data) and a move the engine makes multi-hit after `ModifyMove` (Parental Bond) are `unsupported` (`query_kind`). A spread move always meets two foes (a lone foe cannot be declared). Known gap: Beat Up is not marked multi-hit in the pinned data, so it is answered as one hit. Outside the v1 scope the result is `unsupported` (`query_kind`, position null): a status move and a multi-hit move. A query the engine cannot take (an unknown move, status, weather or terrain id, HP above the maximum, Mega Evolution of a Pokemon that cannot) throws `MechanicsProbeError` (the CLI exits 2, no result). The KO field is slice 5 (`mechanics-ko.ts`, below). Results list the gap ids `damage-formula`, `level`, `mega-evolution` and `stat-points` (the `damage` entry of the config). Required tests: `mechanics-damage.test.ts` holds the probe equal to `fixtures/mechanics/<format>/damage/cases.json` (twelve hand-worked cases, Python integer arithmetic outside the probe from the `modifyDamage` steps: single target, spread, Reflect, Light Screen with spread, rain and sun, a Mega Evolution, burn, an always-crit move, a crit-immune defender, a high-crit-ratio move and absolute boosts with declared HP; base powers and stats are inputs from the pinned data, which patches Slash to 80) and holds the damage equal to the HP lost in seeded real battles on the same declared state (the real turn is played with `choose`; a spy on the PRNG reads the roll the attacker's move drew, the log the crit; 40 non-missing turns per case, at least one crit).

**One-clone damage path** (ADR-022 section 4 mitigation 4, ADR-016, #1179; `mechanics-damage.ts` `probeDamage(..., path)`, `MechanicsContext.damage_path`). The 33 clones above are the reference (`"per-roll"`); the default is `"one-clone"`, which gives the same bytes (so `adapter_format` stays 1 and no feature set id moves). The engine still computes every number. The engine's move step (`useMoveInner`) runs once on the declared scenario battle itself; where the reference stops at the damage loop, the loop calls the engine's own `getDamage` 33 times in turn on that battle: once with the move's own crit rule (the observation, random source answering `randomChance` true), then the 16 rolls with the crit forced off and the 16 with it forced on (the recorder's new `answer(roll, chance)` sets what the next calls answer). Before each call the active move's hit data is reset to what a fresh clone holds; each call's draws are the move step's draws before the loop, the call's own and those after it, which is what its clone drew (equal because `acceptedDraws` is strict: it refuses any draw but one `random(16)` and the observation's one `randomChance`), and `acceptedDraws` judges that list as before. A call is a clone only if it leaves the battle as it found it, so the battle (`toJSON` with the engine's own random source put back, the field effects' lazily set `target` left out) and the active move (minus its hit data) are serialised before the first call and after the last: any difference (an eaten resist berry; a stacking volatile would be another) returns to the reference path on a rebuilt scenario battle (`damagePathStats.fell_back`, 0 in 9,462 bench queries). A move the engine's immunity or protection steps stop before the damage loop deals nothing: one extra reference-style clone shows the draws and the 32 values are 0. Multi-hit after `ModifyMove`, a charge turn and a failed precondition are decided from the observation call alone, as before. `probeMechanics` also keeps what depends on the engine, config, snapshot and format alone (version manifest, config hash, scenario format id) and the validated sets by paste in per-engine tables (bounded, a failure is never kept; a set is copied per query). Equality is tested, not assumed: `test/mechanics-damage-paths.test.ts` runs both paths on every committed damage fixture case (the calc differential's damage set), the KO cases, every `ko_blockers` item and ability as the defender, a Haban Berry defender (the fallback), an immunity, unanswerable moves (Magnitude, Present, a charge turn, status, fixed damage, multi-hit, an unknown move), queries over the committed dataset team pairs (every damaging move, `ARENEON_PATHS_TEAMS` widens the sweep, 240 teams was run once) and the feature builder's requests over three seeded corpus battles, and requires equal result bytes (and equal refusal words). The bench (`feature-bench.ts`, run `public-main-011` g50, 588 + 470 decisions, machine i7-14700KF 28 logical CPUs shared at load 11 to 15, pin 7244fe8e) gave the same `rows_digest` before and after (b5471d97... and 4216b75b...), per-decision mean 196.9 to 36.9 ms (Episodes) and 198.3 to 36.4 ms (progress games), p99 522.6 to 82.8 ms and 638.5 to 109.9 ms; the generation estimate (control 200.2 s, limit 300.4 s) is 1.28x at an ideal 16 threads (met), 1.56x at an ideal 8, and 2.04x at the measured 8-thread pool speed-up of 4.28 (not met, 2.37x at 4 threads). **Kept scenario start** (#1179, second step): `declaredBattle` takes the started scenario (before any declared state) from a bounded per-engine table keyed by (scenario format id, attacker set, defender set) that holds its `toJSON` string, and every caller, the first included, builds its battle with `Battle.fromJSON` of it, so no result depends on the table's contents; the `"per-roll"` reference still starts the scenario itself every time, so the equality test also compares the kept start with a fresh one (`scenarioStartStats`). The bench `rows_digest` is unchanged (b5471d97... and 4216b75b...). Same bench, lower machine load (5.9 to 9 against 11 to 15, so not a clean comparison): per-decision mean 36.9 to 22.8 ms (Episodes) and 36.4 to 23.9 ms (progress games), p99 82.8 to 49.6 ms and 109.9 to 67.2 ms; builder 17.8 ms per seat decision, 552.5 s CPU for the control generation at play and stream writing. The pool no longer scales on tasks this short (measured 1.0 to 2.8x at 4 and 1.3 to 1.8x at 8 threads over three runs, progress games 2.0 to 3.0x), so the pool figures are noisy; the figure that matches how a generation runs (#957, game processes each with their own decisions' rows inline, no pool) is measured with four concurrent single-thread bench processes: per-process builder time 1.50x the solo run's (load 8.8 on 28 threads), so S processes divide the CPU by S over that factor: 2.04x the control's play plus load at S=4 recomputing at play and at stream writing, 1.52x if only play computes the rows (a play-time sidecar), 1.41x at S=5 with a sidecar, and 8.3 (no sidecar) or 4.1 (sidecar) processes for 1.5x at that contention (both trigger items are therefore met only with a play-time sidecar and at least five game processes, or on a quieter machine; verdicts are on the ticket). The remaining cost is the 33 `getDamage` calls and the two serialisations. See the ticket for the trigger verdicts.

**Declared Mega Evolution and the rest of the declared state** (#1243). `runMegaEvo` on a declared already-evolved Pokemon starts the new forme's ability, which can change what the query declared: Intimidate (Mega Manectric) lowers the other combatant's Attack stage (and can consume its White Herb, Adrenaline Orb or Eject Pack), Drought, Sand Stream, Snow Warning or Electric Surge replace the declared weather or terrain. Both probes now make the declared state absolute after the Evolutions. `declaredBattle` (damage) clears and sets the field after both declares and then `restoreStagesAndItem` writes each combatant's declared stages again and puts its set's item back if it changed. `speed_order`'s `declare` keeps its first pass (the #1198 status-first rule, the field, the Evolutions, statuses) and, when any combatant is declared evolved, runs `declareField` again (it removes Trick Room and the four side conditions and clears weather and terrain before setting the declared ones), then restores each item and writes every combatant's stages after all Evolutions. A query with no evolved combatant keeps its bytes. Answers change for damage queries with such a Mega and for speed queries where an evolved Mega's ability replaced the declared field, so `MECHANICS_ADAPTER_FORMAT` is 2: every `damageAwareIdentity` (`damage-aware@v1`: `d3595137...` to `dd8d8687...`), `feature_set_hash`, the mechanics-result corpus samples and `hashes.json` moved. The search identity gained `mechanics_adapter_format` as a thirteenth input (`searchIdentity`, `search_identity_hash`), so a search policy id moves when probe answers change (vector `7f1f9111...` to `015d8f40...`, computed with Python's hashlib and pinned on both sides; no run had used a search policy id). **Consequence:** a pair trained with `candidate_features` under an older `feature_set_hash` (a run in progress on an older commit) cannot be evaluated, warm-started or resumed by code after this merge, because its config names a hash this checkout does not compute (`featureSetup` refuses it); its checkpoints keep their ids. The item and stages are written last, after the declared field (a Seed item under a declared terrain would otherwise fire again), and the item is put back silently (assigned with a fresh effect state, not `setItem`, whose Start event would refire a Seed). Limits: a Protosynthesis or Quark Drive volatile started by a Booster Energy (or the Mega's weather/terrain) is lost when the declared field replaces what a Mega's ability set; Trace (a roster Mega ability copying the other side's ability on start) is not restored, since the declaration has no ability field; an Eject Pack switch cannot be undone; `damage-check.ts` `ownFighter` states an own evolved Mega since #1247 (next paragraph's check, `DAMAGE_CHECK_FORMAT` 2): its shown forme must equal what the request item's `megaStone` data makes of the set's species, it is declared as the set holding the stone, evolved, with the set's own pre-Evolution ability (the Evolution replaces it); any other forme difference stays unstated and counted in `own_unstated` only when the team cannot name it. The sweep asserts hits on an own Mega now constrain and rule no true set out. Tests: `mechanics-damage.test.ts` (Intimidate stage, White Herb), `mechanics-speed.test.ts` (Drought against declared rain with a Swift Swim foe; stages and White Herb read at every action-speed call), `search-config.test.ts`, `tests/ml/loop/test_search_identity.py`.

**KO counts and the parity report** (ADR-016 slice 5, #936; `mechanics-ko.ts`, `mechanics-parity.ts`, `scripts/mechanics-parity.ts`, `docs/mechanics-parity.md`). The `ko` field of a `damage` answer holds integers computed from the engine's two roll vectors as `getDamage` returns them, before the HP clamp and before damage-time effects: `ohko_no_crit` and `ohko_crit` are the rolls (of 16) at or above the defender's current HP, `thko_no_crit` the ordered pairs (of 256) of two independent rolls of the NO-CRIT vector, the same vector for both hits, whose sum reaches it (the crit vector is never used for the two-hit count); never a probability. The defender is read as the scenario battle holds it after the declared state (a Mega Evolution changes its ability); if its item or ability id is on the mechanics config's `ko_blockers` the field is `{kind: "unsupported", reason: "ko_state_change"}` and the vectors, HP and crit chance are still returned. `config/mechanics/<format>.json` `ko_blockers` listed 22 items (Focus Sash, Focus Band, Sitrus and Oran Berry, the 18 type-damage-halving berries) and 5 abilities (Sturdy, Disguise, Multiscale, plus Stamina and Weak Armor, which change the defender's Defense after the first hit); the roster drift check below added 3 items and 8 abilities; every id is checked against the pinned engine by `checkMechanicsConfig` (the list is conservative: an attacker's Mold Breaker is not modelled, so a defender holding a blocker is blocked). The list moved `mechanics_config_hash`, so the config-hash fields of the mechanics corpus samples and `hashes.json` were regenerated and every `damageAwareIdentity` (it names the mechanics config hash) is new; `adapter_format` stays 1 (no value is read differently) and the result contract did not change (`KoCounts` was already in v1). The damage-aware actor still counts its KO share from the rolls itself (`damage-aware-score.ts`) and could read the probe's counts later; it is unchanged. Fixtures: `fixtures/mechanics/<format>/ko/cases.json` (vector cases with the boundary where the lowest roll equals HP, an explicit 256-sum enumeration table, and probe cases that declare the defender's HP on the hand-worked `single-target` damage case, counted with Python outside the probe) and the `ko` entries of `damage/cases.json`; `test/mechanics-ko.test.ts` holds them, the blocker cases (an item, an ability, an empty list, an unknown id) and the immunity case. The parity report is generated like `docs/showdown-champions-gaps.md`: `node packages/showdown-bridge/scripts/mechanics-parity.ts` renders it from the speed, damage and KO fixture files (case ids, descriptions, SHA-256 of each file), the divergence list `fixtures/mechanics/<format>/calc-divergences.json` and the gap rows the config's results depend on (every row with its status, unverified ones counted), and `test/mechanics-parity.test.ts` compares the committed file with a fresh render (edit the fixtures, config or gap records, never the doc). Without the divergence file the report says "differential not built yet (#935)"; the @smogon/calc differential (#935) adds that file (`{data_class: "synthetic", divergences: [{case, reason}]}`, any other shape is refused) and regenerates the report. The report states that the game comparison waits for the owner's in-game captures (#937, Wave 9) and that no gap row is verified by this work.

**KO blocker drift check** (#1166; `mechanics-ko-candidates.ts`, `scripts/check-mechanics-config.ts`, `test/mechanics-ko-candidates.test.ts`). The `ko_blockers` list is tied to the roster through the engine's own data. `koCandidates(engine, formatId, vocabulary)` returns every roster-legal item (the team vocabulary's item list) and ability (every ability some roster species may hold) whose data in the format's mod defines an event handler from `KO_HOOKS`, the only knowledge in code: the handler NAMES `onDamage`, `onDamagingHit`, `onSourceModifyDamage`, `onAnyModifyDamage`, `onAnyDamage`, `onTryHit`, `onEffectiveness`, `onEat`, `onTryEatItem`, `onEmergencyExit` and `onAfterMoveSecondary` (a test requires each to be found on some roster entry, so a mistyped name fails). `checkKoBlockers(engine, config, vocabulary)` (run by `check-mechanics-config.ts`, in `make ts` and CI, against the committed golden vocabulary, which must be for the pin) fails when a candidate is in neither `ko_blockers` nor the new `ko_non_blockers`, when an id is in both, and when a `ko_non_blockers` entry is not a candidate (a stale entry). `ko_non_blockers` is optional in the `MechanicsConfig` contract (`{abilities: [{id, reason}], items: [{id, reason}]}`, sorted by id and unique, a one-sentence reason each, a minor change within v1; a config without it keeps its bytes and then fails the check for every unreviewed candidate); it never changes what the probe answers, only `ko_blockers` does. A handler's presence is a prompt to review, not a verdict: at this pin 92 candidates were found (35 items and 57 abilities, incl. the 27 existing blockers) and classified by one rule, recorded in the reasons. A BLOCKER changes what a repeated hit faces: the defender's HP, stats, item or ability, its presence, the weather or terrain, or the attacker's burn, item or ability (added: Air Balloon, Eject Button, Red Card, Emergency Exit, Flame Body, Mummy, Pickpocket, Sand Spit, Seed Sower, Spicy Spray, Wandering Spirit). A NON-BLOCKER is an immunity or absorb step the probe's own hit steps already apply (sixteen zeros), a constant damage multiplier already in the roll vector (Filter, Solid Rock, Fluffy, Punk Rock, Aura Guard), indirect-damage handling, a boost to a stat that does not enter the damage it takes, an effect on the attacker alone, a status-cure berry, or an effect that fires only on the knockout. Limit: an effect the engine implements in its simulation code instead of a handler has no hook to find (Tera Shell's effectiveness is in `sim/pokemon.ts`); Tera Shell, Ice Face, Shadow Shield and Wonder Guard are not roster-legal at this pin, and the test shows Ice Face and Shadow Shield flagged when a doctored vocabulary adds them. Adding the lists moved `mechanics_config_hash` (`8a16c740...` to `7b2bed9a...`): the config-hash fields of the mechanics corpus samples and `hashes.json`, the pinned identity in `damage-aware-worlds.test.ts` (`a6561ff5...` to `d3595137...`, computed with the test's dummy reader hash) and `docs/mechanics-parity.md` were regenerated, and every `damageAwareIdentity` from now on is new (records written earlier keep the identity they name). The new blockers change behaviour: a defender holding one now gets `unsupported` KO counts (`ko_state_change`), where it got counts before. The corpus gained the samples `mechanics-config/regmc-no-non-blockers` (the field absent), `non-blocker-without-reason` and `unsorted-ko-non-blockers`. The `ko` expectations of `damage/cases.json` are held independent of the module under test: `mechanics-ko.test.ts` recounts them with plain loops of its own (it used `countKos`/`countPairKos`) and `tests/scripts/test_mechanics_ko_fixture.py` recounts them in Python.

**Opponent usage prior and belief configuration contracts** (ADR-025 §2, §5, slice 1 part 1, #1119; `packages/schema/contracts/v1/usage-prior.schema.json` and `belief-config.schema.json`, generated TS and pydantic types, samples in `fixtures/contracts/v1/usage-prior/` and `belief-config/`; readers `packages/showdown-bridge/src/usage-prior.ts` / `belief-config.ts` and `areneon_contracts.usage_prior`; integers only, closed, no derivation or belief code yet). A `UsagePrior` names its format, `engine_data_hash` and `vocabulary_hash`, a `kind`-tagged `source` (`smogon-chaos {month, cutoff, number_of_battles, inputs[{source_id, url, retrieved_at, size, sha256}]}`, `role-sets {role_set_table_hash, fill_prior_hash}`, `generator-matched {generator_config_hash, generator_commit}`), the `derivation` limits (`script_version`, `k_items`, `k_moves`, `k_spreads`) and `species` sorted by preview species id, each with `denominator` and `formes` (each with `weight` and four lists). **Exact integer form (review finding N4, fixed here):** every list states its `denominator`, the integer total its weights are shares of (the source holds rating weights, so it is the summed list weight, never the raw count), and a share is the fraction `weight / denominator`, never ppm. `abilities`, `items` and `spreads` hold `entries` (ids sorted, spreads by nature then the six points), `residual` (mapped mass the truncation dropped, which a consumer spreads equally over the `n` options the vocabulary allows and the list does not name: share `residual / (denominator * n)`) and `unmapped` (names with no engine id, counted and never redistributed); `items` also has `no_item` (the source key `nothing`, a legal option, not unmapped); a `moves` list has `slots`, `entries` as `{id, inclusion}` (numerators over its `denominator`, the number of sets, which is the summed item weight of the forme), `empty_slots` (the source key `''`, mean empty slots per set = `empty_slots / denominator`), `residual` and `unmapped`. The readers add what the profile cannot say, against the `TeamVocabulary` the prior names by hash (a vocabulary hash, format or engine-data hash that differs is refused; the vocabulary is required): sorted and unique ids at every level (inputs by `source_id`), species and formes in the roster, forme weights summing to the species `denominator`, abilities and moves resolving against that forme's roster entry (a forme with a `required_item`, a Mega forme, may use any ability the roster lists, as its list is post-Evolution), items in the vocabulary, natures in the vocabulary and each spread within `ev_limit`, list lengths within the `derivation` limits, every list summing exactly to its denominator (a move list: inclusions plus `empty_slots`, `residual` and `unmapped` equal `slots * denominator`, and no inclusion above the denominator; a move list's denominator equals its forme's items denominator, and `empty_slots` stays below `slots * denominator`). Weights are bounded by 10^12, so TS sums stay exact. `usagePriorHash` / `usage_prior_hash` (= `prior_hash`) and `beliefConfigHash` / `belief_config_hash` are SHA-256 of the JCS bytes. `BeliefConfig` v1 is `identity_hiding_abilities` (sorted, unique, each an ability of the vocabulary), `max_legality_redraws` and `max_disguise_assignments`; no committed config file yet. `check_data_class.py` refuses a usage prior (an object with a `source` that has a `kind`, a `derivation` and a `species` list) that holds a `paste`, an `evs` key or a team-shaped object anywhere, `fixtures/` included, so the corpus holds no such sample: the readers' refusal of them is tested inline. The samples are built from the corpus's slim vocabulary (`usage-prior-samples.ts`; the numbers are invented). The derivation from Smogon data is `scripts/derive_usage_prior.py` (see the Smogon usage prior paragraph); the `role-sets` and `generator-matched` builders and the filter (#1121 and later) are not here.

**Smogon usage prior** (ADR-025 §2, slice 1 part 2, #1120; `scripts/derive_usage_prior.py`, `scripts/usage_common.py`, `config/opponent-model/<format>/usage-prior.<month>-<cutoff>.json` with its `.NOTICE.txt` and `smogon-chaos.<month>-<cutoff>.inputs.json`). A `UsagePrior` of kind `smogon-chaos`, derived offline and deterministically (Python standard library plus the contracts package for RFC 8785 and the reader; no network; the same raw files and script give the same bytes, tested). The raw `chaos` JSON, the `.txt` summary and the `leads` table are downloaded by hand (one sequential client, `robots.txt` read, a `User-Agent` naming Areneon) into the gitignored `.tmp/chaos`, never committed (Smogon states no licence; the owner accepts the aggregate-use risk, ADR-009 erratum 1 and ADR-025 erratum E-9.2; a test greps the tree for the files' hashes and the chaos keys); the input record names each by file, URL, retrieval time, size and SHA-256 and the script refuses a file that differs, and `source.inputs` repeats those facts. **ADR-020's #975 and #976 were not resolved**, so the input record format is this ticket's (the ingestion script's `robots.txt` hash is kept in it as `robots_sha256`) and `usage_common.py` holds the shared pieces (strict decimal reader that refuses duplicate keys and NaN, name table, top k, largest remainder) for #976 to import instead of copying. **Unit rule:** source numbers are read as exact decimals (`Fraction`); a list's integer denominator is its summed weight times 10^6, rounded half to even, and each weight is its exact share of it by largest remainder (ties by id), so a list adds up to its denominator exactly. A move list has `slots` = summed move weight over summed item weight (4; refused if not a whole number within 10^-6), the denominator is the forme's items denominator and the masses are shares of `slots * denominator`, with the empty-slot mass capped at `(slots - 1) * denominator` and an inclusion at the denominator. **Mapping:** a name maps by exact engine id or exact engine name; an unknown name or an id the vocabulary does not allow for that forme (an item outside the item list, an ability or move outside that forme's roster entry, a spread over the Stat Point limit or with an unknown nature) is `unmapped`, counted per kind and never remapped (since #1209 a name also maps by the engine's own id normalisation, `toID`: lower case, every non-alphanumeric dropped, looked up among the engine ids, so `Farfetch'd` finds `farfetchd` and no spelling is listed; the first record, made before, left both unmapped); `nothing` in `Items` is `no_item` and `''` in `Moves` is `empty_slots`, in neither term of the gate. Preview species and formes come from the generator-matched prior of the same vocabulary (`shows_as`, from the engine's Mega stone data): a forme's weight is its entry's summed item weight, so a Mega forme's weight is the stone-holding share, its item its stone and its abilities the post-Evolution ones; the record carries no `holder_abilities` (the consumer reads the pre-Evolution ability from the base forme's list: the approximation of B1/N-I). A source species with no chaos entry has no entry in the record, and `belief()` refuses a roster that holds one (`species_not_in_prior`); at 2026-09 cutoff 1630 that is 69 of the 300 preview species of the vocabulary (231 species, 292 formes in the record from 294 source entries). **Fallback (#1209, second record `usage-prior.2026-09-1630.v2.json`, `prior_hash` `910736ec...`, script `usage-prior-chaos.v2`, own `.NOTICE.txt`; the first record is kept as it was, `1949b487...`, and its re-derivation is v1's, commit d0b8e9c):** `--fallback` fills every preview species with no chaos entry (67 at this month once the two apostrophe names map) from the committed `generator-matched` prior of the same vocabulary (`preview_map`), and the record says so: `source.fallback {kind: "generator-matched", prior_hash, species[]}` (new optional field of the `smogon-chaos` source, a minor change within v1; readers refuse unsorted or duplicate ids and an id that is no species of the record; the reader cannot check the copied content, the derivation script and the record tests do). A filled entry is the map's entry cut to this record's limits: items and moves keep the heaviest (ties by id) and the dropped weight goes to `residual`, which the belief expands equally over the options not named; for moves that is exactly the map's equal inclusions, for items it also gives foreign Mega Stones and other unlisted items their equal share (wider support, no invented set); spreads are the map's one 'other spread' residual, `holder_abilities` are kept. **Why this fallback and not the role-sets prior:** a species the meta never plays has no information, and the map has the widest support (every legal option has mass), while the role-sets prior gives a move outside every role no mass, so an opponent off the curated sets would be refused `evidence_has_no_support`, trading one refusal for another; keeping the refusal would also lose every roster that holds a rare species (see the numbers below). These entries carry no usage information and a consumer must not read them as Smogon data (the NOTICE and the field say so); the derivation report has `fallback_species`. **Effect on the committed corpora** (`opponent-model/test/usage-prior-corpus.test.ts`, 49 battles, 2,152 requests of both seats): under the first record 874 requests (40.6%; 40 of the 98 seat rosters, 23 species) were refused `species_not_in_prior`; under the second 0, and the Team Preview of each of those 40 rosters is answered by `belief()` (a belief over the real prior costs seconds a request, so the test runs `belief` once per roster and checks coverage for the rest). Mapping shares at 2026-09 cutoff 1630: abilities 99.9957%, items 100%, moves 99.9996%, spreads 100% (the first record: 99.88% each), leads names unmapped none, 300 species and 361 formes (292 formes before). Truncation 16 items, 24 moves, 32 spreads (spread rows: nature then the six Stat Points); the limits are in `derivation` and so in `prior_hash`. **Gate (ADR-020 P1):** at least 95% of each list kind's mass maps or no record is written (2026-09: 99.88% for every kind). **Report** (`--report`, integers only): mapping shares and detail per kind, unmapped and dropped species, **N8** from the file's own fields (`usage` sums to 5.998 per team: the counts are over the six registered, not the four brought; the raw counts sum to 6.77 per team and side, so they are not bounded by four either), **O3** (the `leads` names that map to no species, reported only), `prior_hash`; `packages/opponent-model/src/prior-deviation.ts` (`designDeviation`) runs the move model's conditional Poisson design on every forme and reports its largest gap to the stated inclusions in ppm (2026-09: median 43,448, largest 155,224). Tests: `tests/scripts/test_derive_usage_prior.py` (a hand-computed toy file of Rillaboom, Salamence with its Mega forme and a species with no id, with invented numbers: `nothing`, `''`, truncation, largest-remainder ties, a spread over the limit, a move outside the pool, the gate, the refusals), `test_usage_common.py`, `test_usage_prior_record.py` (both committed records pass the reader and the data-class guard, their hashes and inputs, the second's fallback label and that its own species equal the first's, the raw files are in no tracked file, and, where `.tmp/chaos` holds them, the second's byte-for-byte re-derivation), `showdown-bridge/test/usage-prior-committed.test.ts` (the TS reader and the hash of both) and the `smogon-chaos-fallback` corpus samples and `opponent-model/test/prior-deviation.test.ts`. A new month is a new file.

**Opponent belief** (`packages/opponent-model`, ADR-025 §1, §3, §5, §8, slice 2 parts 1, 2 and 4, #1121, #1122 and #1124; engine-free; the bridge does not depend on it yet (the planned direction is bridge → opponent-model → public-state, and `test/packaging.test.ts` checks only that the packages it depends on do not depend on it); it depends on `@areneon/public-state` and the schema types, as TYPES only, and imports no Node built-in, engine or network module: `test/packaging.test.ts`). `belief(input) -> OpponentBelief` is a pure function of the shared reader's opponent half at the request (never a fold of lines) and takes only `BeliefInput` = `{prior: UsagePrior, vocabulary (a TeamVocabulary: rule values, roster lists, items; rules.item_clause may be absent), config: BeliefConfig, opponent: PublicSide, flags: PublicFlag[]}`; the opponent's team, `OmniscientLog`, hidden verdicts, a `BattleRecord`, the own half and the seed have no field (`test/privacy.test.ts`, a type test with `@ts-expect-error` per field and a run-time check that a smuggled field changes nothing). All arithmetic is exact (`BigInt`, reduced `Ratio`s; no float, and `test/packaging.test.ts` scans the sources for `parseFloat`, `toFixed` and the `Math.floor`, `round`, `ceil`, `pow`, `sqrt` and `log` calls; other `Math` uses are not banned). **Model:** per preview slot a forme (the prior's forme weights; a Mega forme's weight is the stone-holding share; the slot's prior species is found by the reader's `base_species`, else the shown `species`), then independently given the forme an ability, an item, a spread (named entries plus one other-spread entry) and a move set, each list with its residual expanded over every option the vocabulary allows (`fields.ts`: weights scaled by the number n of residual options, unmapped mass dropped; items over the format's whole item list plus `no_item` as a named option; the stones of the species' own Mega formes are left out of a base forme's residual, and a forme whose roster entry has a `required_item` holds exactly that item). **Moves** (`moves.ts`): forced moves (inclusion at least the denominator) first, the rest by the conditional Poisson design with odds `a / (b - a)` scaled to integers by their common denominator, the elementary symmetric polynomials by exact integer DP (`elementary`, `elementaryWithout` by the exact recurrence), the count by the two-point rule on `empty_slots / denominator`, capped by the moves that can be drawn; a revealed move is conditioned on (a count below the revealed and forced moves is impossible given them; if every count is, the count is the number of revealed and forced moves, the ADR's never-below rule); the likelihood of the revealed moves multiplies the forme's weight, a move no consistent forme's pool holds (Struggle, a copied move) and every move of a Transformed Pokemon add no constraint. **Item Clause** (`item-clause.ts`): the exact subset DP over the items in order with the slots that already hold an item as the state (2^N, 64 for six; forward and backward tables give every marginal; `no_item` exempt, the slots outside the subset take their no-item weight); `rules.item_clause` absent means independent slots, 1 enforces, any other value is a `BeliefError`; `itemClause.normaliser` is the integer total on the scale in which each slot's `Cell` (forme, item, weight) weights are integers (`top-worlds.ts`, `topWorlds(belief, k)`, is the K most likely (forme, item) combinations by exact best-first search: a DIAGNOSTIC, never a consumer input). **Brought four:** B is uniform over the subsets of `picked_team_size` that hold every required Pokemon (brought, fainted or in play, or the member a shown position is assigned to, below), so P(brought) is `(picked - s) / (N - s)` for the unrequired slots and `brought.subsets` is C(N - s, picked - s) per assignment. **Evidence allowlist** (`evidence.ts`, pinned to the reader by `test/allowlist.test.ts`: every `PublicMon` key is in `EVIDENCE_FIELDS` or `IGNORED_FIELDS`, the sources read no other field, and lines outside the allowlist leave the belief unchanged): the preview species, shown forme, level and gender; `moves_revealed`; `ability` and `item` as the reader shows them; `mega` and `mega_stone`; settled. **Mega (B1):** before the Evolution a stone holder's ability comes from the base species' list (the base forme's ability shares, else uniform over the roster entry's list) and its stone counts as its item; after the reader's `detailschange` + `-mega` the forme, the stone and the Mega forme's own ability list apply; an ability the reader still shows from before the Evolution (one the base species' roster list holds) is ambiguous after it and constrains nothing; a Mega Evolution that no Mega forme of the prior with that stone explains is `forme_not_in_prior`, while a forme change in battle that is not a Mega (a broken Disguise) adds no forme constraint (`forme_unmatched:` note). Evidence the vocabulary cannot place (an item or ability outside every consistent forme's lists) adds no constraint and is listed in the slot's `constraints` (`item_skipped:`, `ability_skipped:`, `move_skipped:`); evidence a list holds with weight 0 (no residual) excludes that forme, and evidence that leaves no world is `evidence_has_no_support`. **Refusals** are `BeliefError` with a code (`BELIEF_ERROR_CODES`) and the roster position: any reader flag or phantom entry (`reader_flagged`), more disguise assignments than `BeliefConfig.max_disguise_assignments` (`disguise_assignments_exceeded`, below), another format or engine data than the vocabulary's, an Item Clause other than absent or 1, a roster outside the format's team sizes, a species with no prior entry, more settled Pokemon than the picked size, and a prior item list with no mass. `BELIEF_FORMAT` is `belief/2`. **Received items and abilities (part 4, #1124):** the shared reader flags an item or ability a Pokemon received or copied: `PublicMon.item_received` (an `-item` line with a `[from]` other than Frisk, which shows the target's own: Trick, Switcheroo, Thief, Pickpocket, Recycle, ...) and `ability_received` (an `-ability` line with `[from]` or `[of]`: Trace, Mummy, Entrainment, Skill Swap, ...); both are sticky for the Pokemon, carried by the Illusion `_settle`, and the Python reducer has no such field (the parity test strips them). `slotEvidence` reads no item (ability) of a Pokemon with the flag, so the belief stays wider and never wrong; a consumed or knocked-off item leaves the reader with no item, so it is no evidence either. `belief` checks `prior.vocabulary_hash` against `BeliefVocabulary.vocabulary_hash` (the `teamVocabularyHash` of the whole `TeamVocabulary`; a new required field) and refuses a mismatch as `format_mismatch`; the prior must have an entry for each of the six preview species. **Illusion disguise mixture (part 2, #1122; ADR-025 §3 N-B, R11; `BELIEF_FORMAT` is `belief/2`, the output shape changed).** A species hides its identity when the vocabulary roster entry of it or one of its prior formes lists an ability of `BeliefConfig.identity_hiding_abilities` (reviewed data; no species name in code); it is unsettled until its own roster entry is in play (the reader settles it at `|replace|`, moving the facts, so the belief is recomputed from the settled state). While such a member is unsettled and a position is shown, the shortcut "the shown Pokemon are in B" is off and the belief is the exact mixture over disguise assignments (`disguise.ts`): injective maps from the shown positions to {the position's own slot} ∪ {an unsettled hiding slot}, each hiding slot used at most once. Shown forme, level and gender stay with the position (they are the details the disguise shows); moves, item, ability, Transform and Mega evidence go to the assigned slot, the assigned slots are required in B, and a position assigned away holds no evidence. Under a possible disguise a revealed move that the vocabulary knows (some roster entry lists it) but no consistent forme of the slot can hold makes that attribution impossible (a move in no pool, Struggle, still adds nothing); abilities and items keep part 1's rules (a foreign ability stays a skipped note, because Trace-like effects make it legitimate). An edge (position, slot) is feasible when the slot's evidence work exists; `hasMatching` (Kuhn's augmenting paths, Hall's condition) must find a complete matching or the belief refuses `evidence_has_no_support`; `enumerate` lists the assignments in option order (the identity first when its evidence is feasible) and returns null above the cap (`disguise_assignments_exceeded`, a fixed message and no position); an assignment whose Item Clause DP is 0 is dropped (the cap counts feasible-edge assignments before that, so it is conservative). The strict-move rule applies only while the mixture is in play (the same move is skipped without an unsettled hiding member). Known limit: an assignment to a hiding slot does not weigh P(the slot holds the hiding ability); this is exact only while the hiding ability is the species' only one (Illusion's Zoroark). An assignment's integer weight is its DP normaliser on the common scale `lcm(scales) / scale` (the joint weight is normaliser / product of the slots' integerising scales; with one assignment the weight is the normaliser, as in part 1); the subset count is the same for every assignment (every assignment requires as many slots as there are shown positions), so it does not weigh them. Decision: the brought-set rule is the ADR's (B holds the assigned slot of each shown position), not the engine's stricter one (the mimicked Pokemon is also in the four), so the belief stays wider, never wrong. Output: `assignments` (each with `moved`, `weight`, `p`, `normaliser`, `required` and its full `SlotBelief`s with cells), `disguise` (`hiding`, `unsettled`, `positions`, `total`), and `slots` as `SlotMarginal`s mixed by `p` (a single assignment gives its own numbers; `constraints` are those common to all assignments); `topWorlds` ranges over all assignments and names the one each world is in. Without an unsettled hiding member (or with nothing shown) there is one assignment, the identity, and the part-1 numbers. The reader's own Illusion-twin limit still refuses (`reader_flagged`). Tests: `test/illusion.test.ts` (hand-written channels: one and two unsettled positions, the cap, Hall's condition, evidence only one member can hold, a replace that settles, a late replace with moves and an item, with worked-out weights), `test/illusion-battle.test.ts` (the committed Illusion battle, both seats, every state before and after each `|replace|`, plus a late-replace variant built from it by moving the replace past a Spite used under the disguise; the real team is read as an oracle only and the true world always has weight above zero). Not here: damage and speed evidence (slice 4). The sampler is below (part 3), the corpus tests are in the part 4 paragraph after it. `test/corpus-smoke.test.ts` runs the belief with a uniform prior at every request of three committed random-play battles (both seats).

**Trapping evidence of the own request (#1127; ADR-025 §3, asked by ADR-026 N-I; `packages/opponent-model/src/trapping.ts`, `packages/showdown-bridge/src/trapping-check.ts`; `BELIEF_FORMAT` is `belief/4`).** What the pinned engine shows (read from `sim/battle.ts` `endTurn` and `sim/pokemon.ts` `getMoveRequestData`; the check below decides it, no rule is written in code): the player's own request carries `trapped` and `maybeTrapped` per own active. For the LAST living own active (with a Pokemon to switch in) `trapped: true` shows only a trap that is not hidden and `maybeTrapped` shows when a foe's species lists a trapping ability; the engine's trap loop compares an ability's display name (`Shadow Tag`) with the holder's ability id (`shadowtag`), so it never skips the held slot and the flag is the SPECIES', whichever ability the foe holds. For an EARLIER active `trapped: true` shows whenever any trap works on it, a foe's hidden ability trap included: that flag and its absence are the ability evidence. `BeliefInput.trapping?: TrappingCheck | null` is the one new input: a port with `observed` (per informative own active the level the request showed: 0 nothing, 1 `maybeTrapped`, 2 `trapped: true`) and `shows(candidate: {species, ability, item})` (the levels that own active would show with that foe in play). It is the player's own information and nothing else of the own side reaches the belief (the type test lists the field, the canary scans stay). `trapExclusions` joins it with the active opponent slots: UPPER bound, an ability whose level exceeds the observed one is excluded (zero weight; the forme's weight scales by the share left; a revealed ability that is excluded is `evidence_has_no_support`); LOWER bound, where the observed level is 1 or 2 and exactly one active foe has an allowed ability that shows it, that foe must (with two such foes the disjunction couples the slots and is left out, with none the flag has another cause). Both are skipped for a foe whose ability is not its species' own (received, suppressed, Transformed), the lower bound whenever any active foe is such, and the whole constraint under a disguise mixture. The bridge's `createTrappingCheck({engine, vocabulary, scenario_id}, {request, team, state})` answers from a scenario battle of the pinned engine (the own side as the request shows it: items, abilities, formes, fainted; both foe actives the candidate; the engine's own `endTurn`; memoised per candidate); it returns null, and a candidate shows what was observed, whenever the scenario could differ from the real battle (not a move request, a field condition, an own team that does not match, a switch-in effect of the start that changed a declared ability or item); an own active is informative only when its request says no `trapped: true` (a Mean Look, a lock or a recharge raise it of their own and the reader does not show every such volatile: the live test found 2,019 informative actives in 1,254 requests whose real foes show exactly the observed level, and with `trapped: true` admitted it found mismatches) and the reader shows it with no volatile. The world sampler takes `trapping: {scenario_id}` (off by default; `scripts/search-worlds.ts` turns it on, `--no-trapping` off) and hands the check to `WorldSamplePort.open`; the counts gain `trapping_checks` and `trapping_seen`. `damage-aware@v2`'s port does not take it (unchanged choices). **Finding.** The format's roster has one species that lists a trapping ability (Mega Gengar, Shadow Tag its only ability). The committed corpora hold few requests that show the flags: the random-play corpus none with `maybeTrapped`, and `unavailable/` and `search-fidelity/` some (a Mega Gengar foe: `trapped: true` on the earlier active, `maybeTrapped` on the last), the rest of the 129 `trapped: true` requests of #1143's `maybe_trapped` class being own causes (a recharge, a lock, a two-turn move, a trapping move or binding volatile). The bridge uses only the actives whose request says no `trapped: true`, so an earlier active's hidden-trap flag is not used (it cannot be told from an own cause without the reader showing every volatile); on the last active `maybeTrapped` is the species', so the species-public information adds nothing to the belief. The constraint therefore changes no belief on the committed corpora and no searched share or V2 rate there (`scripts/search-worlds.ts`, before and after: the random-play corpus, 345 of 1,378 decisions, generator-matched 207 searched, 1,609 valid and 983 refused worlds, 118 V2 refusals both times (the builder's `v2_request` refusals plus the redrawn `V2:-` attempts), the check run for 316 decisions; `--extra`, the `unavailable/` and `search-fidelity/` battles, 1,275 decisions every second one, 673 searched, 5,303 valid and 4,753 refused worlds, 655 V2 refusals both times, the check run for 1,113 decisions of which 2 showed `maybeTrapped`; role-sets 27 and 66 searched both times). Own state the scenario does not model is a declared limit (a type change that is not a reader volatile, a foe's Neutralizing Gas next to a trapper: latent, the roster has neither); an own Illusion holder (the scenario starts the disguise fresh) is the same kind of latent limit. The engine rule is exercised with a species outside the roster added to a copy of the vocabulary (`trapping-check.test.ts`, `opponent-model/test/trapping-engine.test.ts`: Diglett shows `[2, 1]` for Arena Trap and `[0, 1]` for Sand Veil on the case's two actives, so `[0, 1]` observed excludes Arena Trap), and the check matters once a pin or format puts such a species in the roster (the Regulation M-D plan). **Hashes moved.** `BELIEF_FORMAT` `belief/3` to `belief/4`: the belief identity hash of the committed belief `4398a088...` to `1af5ea2a...`, the damage-aware@v2 identity hash `69bb639b...` to `162886d7...` (no baseline config hash moved: the v2 and v1 record bytes are unchanged), the golden belief digests (the format string; the worlds digests are unchanged) with two new cases (`trapping-seen`, `trapping-unseen`), and `config/search/rerank-1.v1.json`'s `belief_identity_hash` and with it the search config hash `3a0e4b7a...` to `754d038f...` and the search identity vector `5fe6ebcd...` to `02768be8...` (both recomputed with Python, `tests/ml/loop/test_search_identity.py`; edited in place: no run has used the record; the search-config contract samples and `hashes.json` were regenerated with `capture-samples.ts`). Tests: `opponent-model/test/trapping.test.ts` (the logic with a stand-in port), `trapping-engine.test.ts` (the real check over the committed battles, soundness and equality of the belief with and without it, the Diglett case), `showdown-bridge/test/trapping-check.test.ts` (the live battle as oracle at every own request of the committed battles, the trapping rule, the declines) and `world-sampler.test.ts`.

**Damage evidence of isolated hits** (ADR-025 §7, §12, R8, N9, slice 4 part 1, #1132; `packages/opponent-model/src/damage.ts`, `packages/showdown-bridge/src/damage-hits.ts`, `damage-check.ts`, `hp-display.ts`; `BELIEF_FORMAT` is `belief/5`). An isolated hit (one attacker, one defender, one hit, nothing the reader cannot account for) and its shown hit points are evidence about the opponent Pokemon that took or dealt it. **Config (a minor change within v1):** `BeliefConfig.damage_evidence` is optional (absent keeps the config's bytes and hash, and the belief reads no damage): `max_checked` (candidates asked per forme and event), `shown_hp_rounding` (`floor|ceil|nearest`: how the engine's display turns hit points into the shown percentage, the FORWARD direction; ADR-018's `assumedHp` rounding is its inverse partner, `inverseRounding` in `hp-display.ts`, and `test/hp-display.test.ts` holds `shownPercent(assumedHp(p, max, inverse(rule)), max, rule) = p` for every percentage and every maximum from 100 to 800; committed `floor`, the Champions display), `non_isolating` (`moves`: a hit of one of these or one used earlier in the same turn, Helping Hand; `lasting_moves`: used earlier anywhere in the channel because they change stats the reader never shows, Guard Split, Power Split, Skill Swap; `abilities`: held or possibly held (an unrevealed Pokemon's roster abilities) by a Pokemon in play besides the two combatants, Friend Guard, Steely Spirit, Plus, Minus, Fairy Aura; `targets`: engine move target types, the spread reduction) and `unmodelled` (candidate abilities and items whose effect depends on state the probe does not declare: Analytic, Stakeout, Supreme Overlord, the HP-threshold abilities of the attacker, Metronome; such a candidate is not asked). The reader (`readBeliefConfig(value, vocabulary)`) adds sorted-and-unique lists and every id in the vocabulary; `createDamageCheck` checks the targets against the engine's moves. The committed values are `config/opponent-model/<format>/belief.damage-v1.json` (a NEW file beside `belief.v1.json`, which is untouched, so no committed record's hash moved because of the config; the Intimidate stage is NOT in the lists: the reader shows stat stages and the probe declares them). **Hits** (`extractHits(frames, ctx)`, bridge, engine-free, over the reader's frames): a `|move|` with no tags and a target, then exactly one `|-damage|` of another side with no tag and no line but `-crit`, `-supereffective`, `-resisted`, `faint`; a reason (`NotIsolated`) otherwise, or when either combatant has a volatile, a temporary forme or a suppressed ability, an opponent item or ability it received or lost, a listed move or lasting move or target or bystander ability, an unrevealed identity-hiding member of the preview (`possible_disguise`) or a field the probe cannot declare (a pseudo-weather but Trick Room, a terrain aside). **Likelihood** (`createDamageCheck({engine, vocabulary, config, mechanics, belief, data_class, cache?}, {player, lines, team, run?})`, a port the model reads structurally as `DamageCheck`): for a candidate set of the slot (a forme, ability, item, named spread) the probe's `damage` query declares the opponent candidate at the shown hit points, the player's own Pokemon from the request in force at the move line (its team set with the request's item, ability and gender) and the reader's stages, status and field; the indicator holds when SOME roll of the no-crit vector (the crit vector after a `-crit`) takes some hit-point value of the range the display rule maps the shown percentage to (`hpRange`, a scan over every value, no inverse formula) to some value of the range of the percentage after (`rollsReach`; a faint needs a roll that reaches the lowest value). An opponent ATTACKER's own shown hit points are a range too and a move or ability may read them (Eruption, Water Spout, Reversal, Blaze): its candidate is declared at both ends of the range, and if the two answers' rolls differ the candidate gets no constraint (no move or ability is named in code; test with a stand-in probe that reads the declared hit points, plus constant-answer negative controls). An own Pokemon the request in force and the team cannot name once (0 or 2+ matches) gives `null` and is counted (`own_unstated`), never a throw. A `DamageCheckCache` belongs to one seat of one game (a port of another seat refuses it). An opponent's Mega forme, an own Pokemon in a forme its set does not have (Palafin Hero), an own evolved Mega whose shown forme is not the one its request item's `megaStone` data makes of its set's species, `unsupported` and a set the validator refuses give `null`: no constraint. **Belief** (`applyDamage`, after every other evidence, off under a disguise mixture): per event and forme the `max_checked` most likely candidates by prior mass (exact integers, ties by ids) are asked, `unmodelled` ones not; the result slot always keeps a forme (`nonEmpty`, a defect otherwise); a failing candidate gets weight 0 and the belief keeps the marginals of the exact posterior over the candidate space with every unchecked candidate passing (mean field: each ability, item and named spread is reweighted by the share of the other fields' mass that passes with it, the forme keeps the passing share of its mass), so the true set keeps a positive weight. **The worlds a consumer samples from this belief are drawn from the PRODUCT of the marginals, so a world can pair an (ability, item, spread) combination that the probe ruled out: a consumer must not assume a sampled world is jointly consistent with the hits (and several events apply one after the other, each from the marginals the previous one left, so independence is re-imposed after every event).** The "other spread" entry names no nature or Stat Points and is never checked. An event whose every checked candidate is ruled out is DROPPED and counted (`damage.contradictions`, note `damage_contradiction:<role>`), never emptying the belief; `OpponentBelief.damage` has `enabled, events, applied, checked, rejected, unsupported, unchecked, contradictions`. A prior whose spreads are only the "other" entry (`generator-matched`) has nothing to check (`unchecked`); the usage prior's named spreads and items are what the evidence narrows. `BeliefInput.damage?` is the port; the model package takes no engine (its packaging test). **Sweep (the oracle, `test/damage-engine.test.ts`):** every isolated event of the 49 committed battles AND the 200 battles of the targeted fidelity corpus judged against the opponent's REAL set: 0 ruled out (1,436 events in the fidelity corpus, 1,010 passed, 426 unsupported); the sweep found three classes the lists now cover (Guard Split on a defender, an own Mega defender whose declared Evolution starts Intimidate, a Palafin Hero own Pokemon) and the negative control (`ceil` or `nearest` as the display rule) rules the real set out. **Not wired:** `damage-aware@v2`, the world sampler and the search do not pass the port (default off, as trapping is for v2; a wiring is a later ticket and would need a record that names `belief.damage-v1.json`). **Hashes moved** (the BELIEF_FORMAT bump, no value of any config): the belief identity hash `0588f5ec...` to `e773c870...` (`config/search/rerank-1.v1.json` edited in place, no run has used it), the search config hash `ba95a2f0...` to `0b124bb3...` and the search identity vector `07f0247b...` to `7f1f9111...` (`tests/ml/loop/test_search_identity.py`, `search-config.test.ts`), `identity.test.ts`'s Python vector, the golden digests (`fixtures/opponent-model/golden.json`, new cases `damage-applied` and `damage-contradiction`; the worlds digests of the old cases are unchanged) and the digests of `usage-prior-cost.test.ts` (reproduced exactly with `format` put back to `belief/4` and the `damage` block removed). **Cost** (`packages/opponent-model/scripts/damage-cost.ts`, not shipped; the committed Smogon usage prior v2, the real probe, one thread, 28 logical CPUs shared at a load of 3.5 to 5.5, not claimed to repeat; the corpus teams are not the prior's teams, so 8.5% of the events rule out every checked candidate and are dropped as `contradictions`): per decision `belief` without the evidence median 168 ms, p95 0.92 s, p99 1.21 s (357 requests, every 6th); with it and a new port per decision median 423 ms, p95 1.56 s, p99 2.69 s, 53,389 probe runs (about 150 a decision); with a per-game `DamageCheckCache` (ports of a game and seat share the probe's answers and the verdicts; every 2nd request, 1,068 requests) median 219 ms against 159, p95 1.16 s against 0.94 s, p99 2.05 s against 1.77 s; the maxima (15.8 s and 21.6 s, one request each) are the same slow request class as without the evidence. The ADR's 500 ms p99 gate is part 2's: it is not met under this prior even by the belief alone. `shown_hp_rounding` is a forward display rule in `BeliefConfig` tied to `assumedHp` by the round-trip test, not a literally shared record: the v1 baseline record's `nearest` does not invert the display, so one shared record was impossible. Tests: `opponent-model/test/damage.test.ts` (hand-worked marginals, the bound and its order, unmodelled, contradiction, unchecked, off), `hidden.test.ts` (invariance with the port on, equal player channel and opponents differing in unrevealed fields: byte-identical beliefs and worlds; negative control: a port that consults the real opponent team differs), `damage-engine.test.ts` (the sweeps, narrowing of the golden battle with the real probe, soundness at sampled requests with a prior that names the real spread among decoys, a Helping Hand put into a real turn), `showdown-bridge/test/damage-hits.test.ts` (every reason and every entry of the committed config) and `hp-display.test.ts`; `showdown-bridge/test/damage-check.test.ts` (the port with a scripted probe).

**Generator-matched prior and SpreadSampler** (ADR-025 §2, §4, §10, N-G, slice 3 part 1, #1125). `showdown-bridge/src/generator-prior.ts`: `deriveGeneratorPrior({vocabulary, config, generator_commit, shows_as})` builds the `UsagePrior` of kind `generator-matched {generator_config_hash, generator_commit}` that today's synthetic opponents are drawn from, purely and in exact integers, by the proposal's rules (ADR-013 §2): species to forme uniform over the roster entries that show as the preview species (weight 1 each; `megaPreviewSpecies(engine, vocabulary, table)` is the `shows_as` map, read from the pinned engine's `megaStone` data: a Mega roster entry shows as its pre-Mega forme, an entry the stone map does not name is its own species), abilities uniform over the entry's list, items the config's `item_weights` over the vocabulary's items (`default_item_weight` otherwise, weight 0 excluded; a forme with a required item holds only it), moves `min(moves_per_set, pool)` without replacement (every pool move has inclusion k/n, the empty slots are counted, which is the conditional Poisson design with equal weights), and one "other spread" entry holding all the spread mass. The item denominator is scaled so every inclusion is an integer (D = weight sum times n / gcd(weight sum, n)); `derivation` is `generator-prior.v1` with `k_spreads` 1. The Item Clause is not in the marginals (the belief's DP applies it): with equal item weights, as committed, its joint equals the proposal's sequential draw; with unequal weights it is an approximation. `generatorConfigHash` is SHA-256 of the config's JCS bytes (the dataset manifests' `config_hash`). The record for `config/teams/<format>/generator.json` is committed under `config/opponent-model/<format>/generator-matched.uniform-v1.json` (JCS bytes, 1.7 MB, `scripts/export-generator-prior.ts`), and `test/generator-prior.test.ts` of the bridge rebuilds it byte for byte. `generator_commit` is `b7cda7af2f1744ad4934d38fb379b1d3cfc3ffc2`, the last commit that changed the proposal's rules; `packages/team-dataset/test/generator-golden.test.ts` pins the digest of 200 seeded proposals computed at that commit, so any change to what the proposal draws fails it and needs a new commit, a new digest and a re-derived prior (this slice only lifted `drawStatPoints` out of the proposal, which the digest shows draws the same). `packages/team-dataset/src/spread-sampler.ts`: `generatorSpreadSampler(vocabulary, config)` is the `SpreadSampler` port (declared structurally; `opponent-model` and `team-dataset` import neither the other): the nature uniform over the vocabulary's natures and the Stat Points by `drawStatPoints` (the proposal's own routine, now an export of `proposal.ts`) with the rule table's total and the config's per-stat maximum, on the world's stream and with no likelihood; the composition roots pass it to `createSampler` as they pass `PoolSource`. `team-dataset/test/generator-prior.test.ts` samples 6,000 worlds of a six-species preview (a Mega-bearing species, one with two Megas, Ditto with one move, Gallade with 106, a three-ability species) and 60,000 proposals (about 360,000 sets) and holds the forme share, ability, item, nature and move inclusion frequencies of both to the prior's exact marginals within 5.5 binomial standard deviations, the Stat Points of the worlds and of the proposal equal (mean, share at 0 and at the per-stat maximum, two-sample 5.5 sigma), and 150 worlds legal and canonical under the pinned validator with no redraw. **Stone holders (#1167; was a known gap).** The pinned validator rewrites a Mega-forme set to its base forme holding the stone with the base forme's first listed ability, so an unevolved Mega holder on a generator team always shows that ability. The prior says it: `UsagePrior`'s `Forme` gained the optional `holder_abilities` (an `AbilityList`, a minor change within v1; a Mega forme only, over the preview species' abilities; the reader refuses it elsewhere), the ability marginal of the holder before the Evolution, while `abilities` stays the Mega forme's own list (the ability after the Evolution). `megaHolderAbilities(engine, vocabulary, shows_as)` derives it with no name written: for each Mega roster entry and each ability the proposal can write, it builds the proposal's own set shape (Mega forme, its stone, a neutral nature, one Stat Point, the first pool move), runs the pinned `validateSet` and counts the abilities left (and refuses a result whose species is not the one it shows as or whose stone changed). The belief reads it: an unevolved Mega forme draws its ability from `holder_abilities` when present, else from the base forme's list as before (so the usage prior is unchanged), and an evolved one from its own list. `GENERATOR_PRIOR_SCRIPT` is `generator-prior.v2`; `deriveGeneratorPrior` takes `holder_abilities`; the committed record and `config/baselines/damage-aware.v2.json` (never used by a run) were rebuilt, so these hashes moved (`damage-aware.v2.json` was edited in place, since a record no run has used is not yet a version in use): the prior's `prior_hash` `e636507c...` to `045f0f60...`, the v2 config hash `cd63fc4c...` to `02e8ee2e...`, the belief identity hash `6e9fa0ce...` to `4398a088...` and the damage-aware@v2 identity hash `6aba4a1b...` to `69bb639b...` (values at this pin, the reader and mechanics configs as of the change; the contract samples of `damage-aware-config-2` and `hashes.json` follow). A corpus team that is not a generator team (the random-play corpus holds a stone with any ability its species lists) can fall outside this support: a holder shown with an ability the validator would not leave it is `evidence_has_no_support` or has no mass as the Mega forme, which `generator-prior-corpus.test.ts` pins (one more refused slot, b14 p2 slot 2). Support (ADR-025 erratum E2, #1169): a residual-0 prior supports only its own list, so `damage-aware@v2` with the generator-matched prior is meant for teams from the same generator, and a usage prior keeps residual mass for real-meta teams. `packages/team-dataset/test/generator-prior.test.ts` holds the validator's result for 8,000 proposed holders equal to the prior's list and the sampled worlds' unevolved Mega formes to it. The `role-sets` prior's fill prior is this record, so its fill hash moves with it (`role-sets.fill-uniform-v1.json` was rebuilt with `export-role-sets-prior.ts`, its `fill_prior_hash` moved `e636507c...` to `045f0f60...` and its `prior_hash` `5b10b065...` to `35611026...`; the role-sets prior itself gets no `holder_abilities`: its pre-Evolution abilities come from the role-set table, not from the validator's rewrite). **Quotas and rejections** (journal of #1125): 20,000 raw proposals, 18,590 accepted by the validator (92.9%; the pilot of the committed config rejects 2,998 of 40,000, 7.5%, against the 99.4% ADR-025 quotes; `packages/team-dataset/scripts/measure-quotas.ts` reproduces the measurement (`--draws 20000 --pilot --dataset DIR --moves fakeout`; the 1,747-team figure needs a generated dataset, which is not committed; the script's own run with its fixed seeds gave 18,541 of 20,000 accepted, 92.7%, 2,964 of 40,000 pilot draws rejected, 7.4%, and on the 240-team committed fixture dataset Fake Out in 28 sets against an expected 11.3); ADR-025 erratum E2), and the accepted sets keep the proposal's marginals (Mega-forme share 21.7% against 21.5%, move rms z 1.00); a generated quota-balanced dataset (1,747 teams, 10,482 sets) keeps them too (items max |z| 2.35, natures 2.46, moves rms z 1.03) except Fake Out, which the label quotas lift from an expected 76.6 to 96 sets, so the quota effect is small and moves the label moves most. Not in this slice: a mixture config (3b).
**Role-sets prior** (ADR-025 §2, §4, R12, #1126). `showdown-bridge/src/role-sets-prior.ts`: `deriveRoleSetsPrior({vocabulary, table, fill, shows_as})` reads the committed `RoleSetTable` (ADR-020 §3) as a `UsagePrior` of kind `role-sets {role_set_table_hash, fill_prior_hash}` (the contract's field is `fill_prior_hash`, the hash of the `generator-matched` record that supplies items, natures and Stat Points; the ticket text's `assumption_record_hash` is not in the contract). One implementation: the belief and `sample` read it like any prior, and it is ADR-026's role-set arm and the fallback for a format without usage data.
