Exact integers, no float: a table entry (a roster entry) weighs its number of roles, grouped under the species it shows as (`megaPreviewSpecies`), so a preview species is uniform over ALL its roles, the roles of its Mega formes included; a role is a move POOL, so a set holds `min(slots, pool)` distinct moves of its role drawn uniformly (the conditional Poisson design with equal weights, as the generator prior) and the forme's inclusion of a move is the exact mean of k/n over the roles that hold it, with the empty slots counted; a move outside every role of the forme has no mass and no residual, so the belief gives it probability zero (`evidence_has_no_support` when it is revealed): the known mismatch with the uniform-team generator, stated in `test/role-sets-prior.test.ts` of `team-dataset`. Abilities are uniform over a role's abilities, roles equal. The table names a Mega forme's PRE-Evolution abilities and the belief reads a stone holder's pre-Evolution ability from the BASE forme's list (B1), so the base forme's list is the mixture over every role of the preview species (the stone holders' roles included; Aggron's Sturdy is in it though its base role says Rock Head), and a Mega forme's own list is the roster's Mega ability (post-Evolution, uniform; the table says nothing about it). A species whose table entries are all stone holders would have no base forme in the record and the belief would read its pre-Evolution ability uniformly from the vocabulary (none at this pin; a unit test covers it). Items, the Mega stone and the "other spread" entry are the fill's forme by forme, its item list scaled to the move denominators; the nature and Stat Points come from `generatorSpreadSampler` on the sampler's stream, so worlds for one belief and different streams differ in item, nature and Stat Points (so Speed and bulk). Nothing turns a role into an item or a spread. #1167 (the validator shows an unevolved Mega holder the base forme's first listed ability in GENERATED teams) does not touch this record: its abilities come from the table, not the generator; a #1167 change to the generator-matched record is a new fill and so a new `fill_prior_hash` and a rebuilt role-sets record (a new file, never an edit). `config/opponent-model/<format>/role-sets.fill-uniform-v1.json` is rebuilt byte for byte by `packages/showdown-bridge/test/role-sets-prior.test.ts` (exact moves inclusions by cross multiplication, ability mixtures, items equal to the fill's shares, hash sensitivity to table, fill and vocabulary, refusals); `packages/team-dataset/test/role-sets-prior.test.ts` samples 6,000 worlds (forme shares, moves inside a role, single-role k/n, ability and stone, item/nature/Stat Point frequencies, streams differ, the zero-probability move, 150 worlds legal under the pinned validator with no redraw).

**Opponent sampler** (`packages/opponent-model/src/sampler.ts`, `draw.ts`, `identity.ts`, `canonical.ts`, `sha256.ts`, `showdown-bridge/src/set-legality.ts`, ADR-025 §4, §5, slice 2 part 3, #1123). `createSampler({legality, spreads?})` returns `{sample(belief, stream), sampleWorlds(belief, stream, n)}`; `sample` takes a belief and 32 bytes and nothing else (a type test; the seed, the opponent's team, the omniscient log have no parameter), the ports are bound once when the sampler is made, and the result is `{world, redraws}`. An `OpponentWorld` has the six preview slots; each has `brought` (exactly `picked_team_size`) and, for a brought slot, a `WorldSet` (`species` the preview id, `forme` the Mega forme id for a Mega slot with its stone as `item` and the pre-Evolution ability until it has evolved, `item` or null, `ability`, `nature`, `stat_points`, sorted `moves`, `level`, `gender`, `shown` = the nickname, species, level and gender as the channel shows them, so the preview's for a Pokemon not yet seen; a level the channel does not show is `belief.sampling.level`, the rule table's adjustLevel). The belief gained `sampling` (`level`, `max_legality_redraws`) and `BeliefVocabulary.rules.adjust_level`, so the sampler needs nothing but the belief; `BELIEF_FORMAT` is `belief/3`. Draw order: the disguise assignment (by weight), the brought set (the assignment's required slots, then a uniform draw of the rest by successive uniform picks), the Item Clause backward pass over the items in id order (the state is the set of slots still open: per item the weights are "unused" and, for each open slot that can hold it, its weight times `backward[k+1]` of the remaining open slots; `clauseTables` was split out of `solveItemClause` for it; without an Item Clause every slot draws its cell alone), then per brought slot in preview order forme with its item (a cell of the item), ability, spread, moves. Moves draw the count from the model's posterior, then the open candidates one by one with the conditional Poisson design (suffix elementary symmetric polynomials, exact; forced and revealed moves are always in). All integers come from `BitStream.below(T)`: L = bitlen(T) + 64 bits read big-endian from the stream of 256-bit blocks `SHA-256(JCS({counter, purpose: "opponent-world/1", stream: <hex>}))` (counter from 0 across the whole world, leftover bits carried to the next draw), accept `n < floor(2^L / T) * T`, return `n mod T`, else read the next L bits; no float. Decisions where the ADR leaves room: a draw with one possible outcome (T = 1, or one positive weight in `choose`) reads nothing; a legality redraw drops the leftover bits and continues at the next counter (`restart`), and the whole world is drawn again; at most `max_legality_redraws` redraws (so `max + 1` worlds), then `BeliefError` `legality_redraws_exhausted`; the tables (item DP per assignment, move suffix tables) are built once per belief (`prepared`, a `WeakMap`). `sampleWorlds` gives world i the stream `SHA-256(JCS({index: i, purpose: "opponent-world-index/1", stream}))` and weight 1/n; the redraw sum is returned. `SetLegality` (`legal(set)`) is declared in opponent-model and implemented by `createSetLegality({engine, vocabulary})` in the bridge: the pinned `validateSet` on a copy, then the canonical paste round trip; a Mega slot is checked as the preview species holding the stone (an evolved Mega ability, which the base form cannot hold, is judged with each ability the species lists and the set is legal if the validator takes any of them), an id the vocabulary does not list is illegal. The bridge does not import opponent-model (adding it would change the workspace dependencies the importer image copies; the composition root joins the two); the bridge type is structurally the port, and `test/legality-engine.test.ts` assigns one to the other. `SpreadSampler.other(species, forme, below)` is declared for the prior's "other spread" entry (a spread with no nature or Stat Points of its own; the `generator-matched` prior's rule enters here, slice 3); without it a sampled "other" spread is `BeliefError` `spread_sampler_required` (new code, with `legality_redraws_exhausted`). Identity (§5): `beliefIdentityHash({prior_hash, belief_config_hash, vocabulary_hash, reader_sha256, version_manifest})` is SHA-256 of the JCS of those plus `belief_format` (a vector made with Python is in `test/identity.test.ts`); `beliefDigest` and `worldsDigest` are the SHA-256 of the canonical JSON of a belief and of `sampleWorlds` (`canonical.ts`: RFC 8785 for strings, safe integers, arrays and objects; every bigint, `Ratio` parts included, is written as its decimal string; a float, `undefined` or a lone surrogate is refused). The package carries its own SHA-256 and JCS (`sha256.ts`, `canonical.ts`; engine-free, no Node built-in; tests hold them equal to the platform's SHA-256 and to Python vectors). Golden digest test (`test/golden.test.ts`, fixture `fixtures/opponent-model/golden.json`): four synthetic cases (preview only, revealed facts, an evolved Mega, a disguise mixture) pin the digests of the belief and of eight worlds and `belief_format`; a change of how a value is computed fails it, and the fix is a `BELIEF_FORMAT` bump with the fixture rewritten (`ARENEON_UPDATE_GOLDEN=1 pnpm exec vitest run packages/opponent-model/test/golden.test.ts`, reviewed). Tests: `test/sampler.test.ts` (sampling frequencies against the exact marginals of brought, items, abilities, spreads, formes and moves, and a joint of two slots, on a case whose Item Clause normaliser exceeds 2^64, asserted in the test; five standard errors on fixed streams; the revealed facts, Mega, the assignment drawn first, no Item Clause, the redraws, the spread port, the type test), `test/draw.test.ts` (the draw against a Python vector with T above 2^64, the rejection branch through an injected block source), `test/legality-engine.test.ts` (the real pinned validator on the committed random-play channels), `showdown-bridge/test/set-legality.test.ts`. Part 4 moved `prepared` out of the package index (internal; tests import it from `src/sampler.ts`), made the move count's integer weights part of the per-belief move tables (no `integerise` per draw), and gave the bridge `STAT_IDS`, `IV_DEFAULT` and `defaultIvs()` in `engine.ts` (the set legality and the assumed set shared two copies of the IV constant; the package's own `STAT_IDS` is engine-free and a test holds it equal to the bridge's, as `canonical.ts`'s JCS is held equal to the bridge's by `test/carry.test.ts`). Not here: the `generator-matched` and `role-sets` priors and `SpreadSampler` implementation (slice 3); any consumer.

**damage-aware@v2** (ADR-025 §4 to §6, §13, ADR-018 errata E-18.2 and E-18.3, slice 3 part 2, #1128; `showdown-bridge/src/damage-aware-actor.ts`, `-worlds.ts`, `-assumed-set.ts`, `team-dataset/src/damage-aware-worlds.ts`). The first consumer of the belief: the v1 scorer over sampled worlds, a new policy id `damage-aware@v2`; **v1 is unchanged** (its record, `baseline_config_hash` 1df63656…, identity bytes, choices and report have no new key; `damage-aware-worlds.test.ts` pins the hashes and `damage-aware-invariance.test.ts` still passes, its helpers moved to `damage-aware-invariance-support.ts`). **Contract:** a new schema version beside v1, not a minor change (ADR-008 §3: the v1 type would have had to become a union and every v1 reader would have been touched): `damage-aware-config-2.schema.json` (`DamageAwareConfigV2`, `schema_version` 2; generated TS and pydantic types, samples under `fixtures/contracts/v1/damage-aware-config-2/`). `config/baselines/damage-aware.v2.json`: `assumed_set {source: "belief", prior_hash, belief_config_hash, worlds: 8}` in place of v1's field rules, and v1's `hp_rounding`, `scoring` and `epsilon_ppm` with the same values; the prior is the committed `generator-matched` prior of `uniform-v1` and the belief config is `config/opponent-model/<format>/belief.v1.json` (`illusion`, 32 redraws, 64 assignments), both named by the SHA-256 of their JCS bytes (the composition root hashes the files it reads; `check-damage-aware-config.ts` in `make ts` requires the committed files to be the named ones). **Port:** the bridge does not import the opponent model (importer image, see Opponent sampler); `damage-aware-worlds.ts` declares `BeliefWorldsPort` structurally (`identity_hash` = `belief_identity_hash`, `prior_hash`, `belief_config_hash`, `prior_format_id`, `sample({opponent, flags, stream, n})`) and the composition root `team-dataset/src/damage-aware-worlds.ts` (`createBeliefWorlds`, `beliefWorldsFactory`; `team-dataset` now depends on `@areneon/opponent-model`, the one package that joins the model, the bridge's `createSetLegality` and the generator's `generatorSpreadSampler`) builds it: it loads the prior and belief config from `config/opponent-model/<format>/` and refuses (`BeliefWorldsError`) a file whose hash is not the record's, a prior of another format (N10), and a prior not derived from the committed `config/teams/<format>/generator.json`. The actor re-checks the port against the record at construction (prior hash, belief config hash and prior format: `DamageAwareError`, never the M-C prior after a pin bump); at run time the model's `format_mismatch`, `item_clause_unsupported` and `spread_sampler_required` (`BELIEF_DEFECTS`) are defects of the run and propagate, every other code is a fallback. **Play:** at a scored request (a move request with two or more candidates, as v1) the actor asks the port for `worlds` worlds of the opponent from the reader's opponent half and ALL flags the reader has raised in this game, on the stream SHA-256(JCS({request_hash, request_seq, player, purpose: "damage-aware-worlds/1"})) (`worldsStream`; every input is the player's own request, so v2 stays a pure function of the player's view; `epsilon_ppm` keeps its own Mulberry32 stream and is drawn before the worlds, so the world stream never depends on it), builds each opposing active's assumed set from the world's set for that roster position (`buildWorldAssumedSet`: the same `AssumedSet`, public facts win over the world as in v1: shown or lost item, revealed ability, level, Mega and stone, shown hit points; `revealed_moves` are the channel's revealed moves and the only threats, a sampled move never is) and scores every candidate exactly as v1 does (`scoreChoices` is v1's loop, unchanged); the per-game probe memo by query hash is shared across the worlds. The candidate with the best SUM over the worlds wins, the first of equal sums in canonical order (the sum orders candidates exactly as the mean does, the divisor being the same; no integer division loses information). A world that contradicts a public fact (a position the world does not bring, a set the validator refuses once the public facts are applied, another species, a revealed move missing, a Mega Evolution its forme does not explain, an ability that blocks the status the Pokemon is shown with, the probe's own verdict) is dropped and counted (`contradicted_worlds`), not repaired; with no world left the request plays the `actor-rule` candidate (`no_consistent_world`). Known limit: with an unsettled identity-hiding member and a shown position the world does not say which slot a shown Pokemon is (the disguise assignment is not in the world), so v2 reads the world's slot at the roster position and a world that does not hold the revealed moves there is dropped. **Identity (E-18.3):** the identity preimage gains `belief_identity_hash` only when the config is a v2 record (`damageAwareIdentity` refuses a belief hash without one and a v2 record without one); a policy id that names a record version (`v1`, `v2-x`) must be the version of the record the actor plays, so a v1 record cannot play as `damage-aware@v2`. **Report:** `report().belief` (v2 only) has `worlds`, `decisions`, `worlds_sampled`, `redraws`, `contradicted_worlds`, `no_consistent_world` and `refusals` by the model's code (`reader_flagged`, `disguise_assignments_exceeded`, `legality_redraws_exhausted`, ...); a refusal plays the `actor-rule` candidate, is counted there and not in `scored_decisions`, and the v1 counts (`reader_fallbacks`, `assumed_set_fallbacks`, `probe_refusals`, `unsupported_queries`) keep their meaning. Reader flags are sticky for the belief (it refuses with `reader_flagged` for the rest of a game; the request that carried the flagged line is counted in `reader_fallbacks`, the later ones in `belief.refusals`), unlike v1's per-request rule. The records that store counts (`LeagueRunRecord.baselines`, the progress records) have no field for the belief counts yet: v2's `counts` keep the nine v1 keys and the belief counts stay in the report and are not persisted in the run files (a follow-up if a run needs them stored). **KO counts (PR #203):** v2 keeps counting the KO share from the 16 rolls like v1 (decision): the probe's `ko` counts for a single hit equal that count, a defender on `ko_blockers` gets `unsupported` where the rolls still give a share, and "the mean of the v1 score" is v1's scorer unchanged, so one scorer serves both versions; moving both to the probe's counts is a new baseline version for both. **Format:** the N10 check compares the prior's format with the record's; a request carries no format, so the wiring must give the actor a record for the battle's format, as for v1. The cost figures are provisional until the section 13 head-to-head run (random-legal games are short). **Wiring:** `damageAwareSetupV2(engine, formatId, beliefWorlds)` builds the v2 setup from the committed record and the factory; `GenerationOptions.belief_worlds` / `damage_aware_v2` and `runGenerationCli(..., shardScript, beliefWorlds)` let a training config name `{actor: "damage-aware", policy_id: "damage-aware@v2"}` as a baseline opponent (no contract change; `team-dataset/scripts/play-generation.ts` passes `beliefWorldsFactory`; a plan that names it without a factory is refused, so the bridge's own `scripts/play-generation.ts` passes none, so a run config that names v2 must also name a `team_dataset`, the only case in which the Python driver starts that script). Not wired, by the ticket's scope: v2 in a training mix (an ADR-010 A4 experiment, ADR-025 §10), v2 as a progress or private-test opponent (the progress records, readers and `damage-aware-*` match ids are v1's; decided by the §13 cost gate and a separate ticket) and the teacher run. **Tests:** `showdown-bridge/test/damage-aware-v2.test.ts` (a stand-in port: construction refusals incl. N10, the world stream, the mean over worlds with a test that has teeth (the mean and the first world disagree), v2 on one world equals v1 on the same sets, every refusal and fallback counted, the status drop, flags reaching the belief, determinism, epsilon separate), `damage-aware-assumed-set.test.ts` (`buildWorldAssumedSet`), `damage-aware-worlds.test.ts`, `league-generation.test.ts` (v2 as a training opponent), and `team-dataset/test/damage-aware-worlds.test.ts` with the real model: the port and its refusals, the invariance test (equal choices AND equal probe questions at every request with an equal player channel when the opponent's unrevealed nature, Stat Points, items and moves 2 to 4 differ, a changed battle seed, a changed actor seed), the negative control (a port whose worlds are the opponent's real sets makes the probe be asked different questions at such a request; choices rarely differ because a sampled move is never a threat, so the control compares what v2 can leak through, the questions), determinism, and a training generation against `damage-aware@v2`. **Cost gate (N-H, `team-dataset/scripts/damage-aware-v2-budget.ts`, random-play corpus teams against `random-legal`, seat alternating, real probe and belief, one thread, a shared machine):** 40 games (462 decisions, 385 scored; 502 including Team Preview) of `damage-aware@v2` per decision: median 1,206 ms, mean 1,158 ms, p95 2,436 ms, **p99 2,984 ms** (scored decisions alone p99 3,149 ms), worst 4,516 ms, so the gate (p99 at most 5 s) holds with N = 8 and N is not lowered; the same 40 games of `damage-aware@v1`: mean 131 ms, p99 357 ms (v2 about 9 times v1: about 8 times the probe queries per decision, 46,208 against 5,330 for the games, and no memo saving: the worlds differ, so almost no query repeats); a game of v2 takes a mean of 13.5 s (v1 1.5 s). Belief outcomes over the 40 games: 3,080 worlds sampled, 0 legality redraws, 8 worlds dropped as contradicting a public fact, and 26 refused requests (14 `reader_flagged`: the reader's flags persist for the rest of a game, 12 `evidence_has_no_support`: the belief found no world for the evidence of that request, a finding about the belief for slice 2's owner, not about this baseline), each played by the `actor-rule` candidate and counted. At a 200-game match against a learned policy with about 25 baseline decisions a game the mean decision is about 97 minutes, over the 30-minute match budget of ADR-018 section 6 and ADR-025 section 13: v2 does not fit the progress-evaluation budget on this machine, so it is not a progress opponent (it remains an experiment and head-to-head baseline, as section 13 provides). A shared machine (load average 6 to 7 from other runs), one thread, figures that are not claimed to repeat.

**Prediction-quality harness** (ADR-025 §12, §13, slice 3 part 3, #1129; `team-dataset/src/prediction-quality.ts`, `scripts/prediction-quality.ts`; evaluation-only). Over seeded A1 games (random-legal against random-legal, run key `prediction-quality-1`, teams of the committed dev split `fixtures-v1`, both teams and both channels recorded: `oracleGames`) it scores, at every request of both seats, the belief of the committed v2 record (`qualityContext`: the same prior, belief config and v2 port the baseline uses) against the opponent's REAL sets for the Pokemon the real player brought: item, ability and move top-1 (the expected hit under a uniform tie-break among the options tied at the top, integer ppm) and log-loss (mean -ln p of the true value in micro-nats; a true value with no mass is counted in `zero_mass`, never averaged), for the belief, for the prior alone (the belief at the seat's Team Preview request, which holds no evidence) and for `damage-aware@v1`'s fixed rule (`buildAssumedSet`; a point prediction, so no log-loss and no move row: v1 predicts no unrevealed move), per phase (`preview` / `battle`), a ten-bin reliability table (calibration) of every option of the belief and the prior, and the joint-consistency rate of the N sampled worlds (the share of (world, brought slot) pairs whose item AND exact four moves equal the real set, against the product of the single-field rates, plus the mean move overlap and the legality redraw rate). Moves are judged on the not-yet-revealed true moves against the option list without the revealed ones, by the inclusion probability (so a belief conditioned on revealed moves is not directly comparable to the prior's inclusions: descriptive only). Belief refusals are counted by code (`refusals`) and left out of the rows, as are requests with no one-to-one truth (`no_truth`, an Illusion twin in the roster), v1 failures and world-sampling refusals; nothing is fixed here (the belief soundness work is #1169). The real team is read by evaluation code only (this module, its script and its tests), as `CloneJudge` verdicts are; the reader's flags are those raised up to each request, as the actor sees them, and the real Pokemon are matched to the roster through the pinned engine's species data; the output is counts and integers (a test walks every leaf and greps for every species, item, ability and move of the games), no actor, bridge or package source imports the module and the package index does not export it (a source scan). Accuracy is descriptive and never deciding (§13). The cost gate is measured by the existing `scripts/damage-aware-v2-budget.ts` (30 seeded games against `random-legal`, one thread, real probe; one shared machine, load 5 to 8, so pessimistic): `damage-aware@v2` per decision median 1252 ms, p95 2668, p99 3210, worst 4123 ms (v1: 132, 263, 337, 446), so the N-H per-decision gate (p99 at most 5 s) passes, but v2 asks about 8.7 times v1's probe queries (34,730 against 3,969) and costs about 9.4 times v1's mean decision, so the ADR-018 extrapolation for a 200-game match at 25 decisions a game is about 105 minutes against the 30-minute budget: v2 does not fit it as a progress opponent at N = 8 (it stays an experiment and head-to-head baseline; lowering N is a new v2 identity). The figures are of one machine at one time and are not claimed to repeat. Tests: `test/prediction-quality.test.ts` (the primitives and one slot worked by hand, three seeded games through the real belief, port and v1 rule, the refusals, the outputs, the scan).

**Corpus soundness, invariance, canary and cost of the belief and sampler** (ADR-025 §8, slice 2 part 4, #1124; finding N6). `test/corpus.ts` loads every committed battle (the golden battle, the random-play corpus, `unavailable/`, the Illusion battle and the fling, baton-pass, transform and revival battles) as `Point`s: one per request of each seat's own channel, the reader's state at it and the flags of the channel. The opponent's real team and real brought set (read from the opponent's own channel: its own requests list what it brought) are a separate `Truth`, read by the oracle `sound`/`ranks` only and never passed to `belief`; a request with an Illusion twin (a phantom roster entry) has no truth and `belief` refuses it. `test/soundness.test.ts`: every request has a belief but the counted reader flags (`reader_flagged`: one flagged channel, the twins), and the real sets (forme, ability, item, moves) and real brought set are possible under at least one disguise assignment at every request of both seats, with a skewed prior (named entries plus a residual; the first ten random battles and every other corpus) and with the uniform prior (all 49 battles, 2152 requests). The ability of an evolved Mega is not judged (the team file holds the pre-Evolution ability; the Mega test of part 1 covers it). The uniform-prior run gives the N6 rank table (`rankTable`): per phase and field, the rank of the true value (the number of options at least as likely, ties counted against the belief, so a flat belief ranks last), top-1 and top-3 shares and the mean rank over the options in ppm; the figures are on the ticket. `test/n6.test.ts`: Trick and Switcheroo, Knock Off, berry consumption and Mega Evolution on hand-written channels (the received item adds no evidence; the true item stays possible). `test/hidden.test.ts` (real engine, three seeded random battles): INVARIANCE, pairs of battles with an equal player channel up to a request, whose opponent differs in items (rotated), nature, Stat Points, moves 2 to 4 and which two Pokemon stayed behind (`team 1256` against the first legal preview), give byte-identical `beliefDigest` and `worldsDigest` at every such request (the real validator behind the legality port); the negative control hands the filter the real team's items and moves through the opponent half of the state and the two beliefs differ. The belief takes the reader's state and nothing else, so the equality holds by construction; the test pins that nothing outside it (module caches, the sampler's memo, the port) carries the difference. CANARY: the real team of a battle holds marker items, moves and natures in unrevealed fields while the prior names none of them and keeps no residual, and no marker appears as a JSON token in a belief or in worlds unless a revealed fact explains it; the negative control (a prior that names the markers, as one built from the real team would) is flagged by the same scan. There is no incremental path (a belief is a pure function of the reader's state at the request), so there is no incremental-equals-recomputed test. `test/measure.ts` and `test/cost.test.ts`, `scripts/measure.ts` (not shipped): wall-clock per decision (one belief) and per world (`sample`, the first world of a belief builds its tables) and the legality redraw rate; the test runs a small corpus and gates the p99 at 2000 ms (a loose, change-of-kind gate for a shared machine). Measured (49 battles, 2152 requests, one thread, the real validator, this machine, shared; not claimed to repeat): per decision median 8.1 ms, p95 13.6 ms, p99 15.7 ms, max 35.9 ms under the uniform prior and median 13.9 ms, p95 20.6 ms, p99 26.9 ms, max 49.2 ms under the skewed one; per world the first world of a belief (tables built) median 3.0 and 4.0 ms, p99 7.1 and 7.3 ms, a later world median 1.6 and 1.3 ms, p99 4.2 and 3.1 ms; 0 legality redraws in 2670 worlds under each prior (the rate is 0 ppm).

**Belief cost under the Smogon usage prior (#1241).** Under `usage-prior.2026-09-1630.v2.json` (long item and move lists, residuals over the whole vocabulary) the numbers the belief multiplies are thousands of bits long, and the profile (`node --cpu-prof`, `packages/opponent-model/scripts/usage-cost.ts`) put 98 percent of `belief()` in the `gcd` of `ratio()`, from the per-cell `ratio(joint * weight, Z * item weight)` and the `add`s of huge-denominator fractions. Changed, with no byte of any belief or world moved (`BELIEF_FORMAT` stays `belief/4`; `test/golden.test.ts`, the whole package suite and a before/after comparison of belief and world digests on every 8th request of the committed corpora, 269 requests, are unchanged): `gcd` is Lehmer's algorithm (leading 40 bits drive a run of Euclid steps, cofactors applied to the full numbers; `Number` holds only exact integers below 2^53 and the quotient is `(x - x % y) / y`, no `Math`), `add` and `mul` do Knuth's gcd-light forms (4.5.1: the sum needs a gcd only of the denominators' common part, a product only the two cross gcds, and the identity operands return the other operand), a forme's probability is `P(item)` times the cell's share of the item's weight (small gcds) with equal cells counted once, and `ratio(joint, Z)` is reduced once per distinct joint (the residual items share columns). `test/ratio.test.ts` holds the new `gcd`, `add`, `mul` and `div` equal to Euclid and to the naive reduced fraction on shared factors, Fibonacci, zeros, negatives and unequal sizes; `test/usage-prior-cost.test.ts` pins the digests of the four slowest committed requests as computed BEFORE the change and a loose 8 s gate. `scripts/usage-cost.ts` (not shipped) prints per-request time and digest (`--every K --offset O --worlds N --only GAME:SEAT:REQUEST --out FILE`) for such a before/after run. Figures (269 requests of the corpora, 2 worlds each, one thread, load average about 1.3 from other work): per belief before median 612 ms, p95 25.2 s, p99 37.4 s, max 82.5 s; after median 148 ms, p95 928 ms, p99 1.26 s, max 2.06 s. Not done: the remaining time is the sums over formes in `mix` and the reductions of `joint / Z` per distinct item, and the sampler's per-belief tables were not profiled separately (the before/after comparison also drew two worlds per belief and found them byte-identical).

**Belief refusals in played games (#1169; found by the damage-aware@v2 cost run of #1128).** The run refused 26 requests of 40 games on the random-play corpus teams: 12 `evidence_has_no_support` and 14 `reader_flagged`. Reproduced without the actor (the committed `generator-matched` prior, `belief()` at every request of both seats, the real team read by the harness oracle only): exactly those 12 and 14 on the baseline seats. **Cause of the 12: not a mishandled evidence class, a team outside the prior's support.** Five slots (random b08, b14, b15, b20 and b26) hold the Mega Stone of another species (Salamencite on Rotom-Fan, Glalitite on Annihilape, Froslassite on Bellibolt, Aggronite on Houndstone, Tyranitarite on Liepard): a legal set that the corpus draws and the generator never draws (`config/teams/<format>/generator.json` weighs every stone 0 so a stone appears only with its Mega species), so the prior gives it no mass and, by the belief's contract (`belief.test.ts`: without a residual a revealed fact outside the list has no world), the request is refused from the moment the stone is shown. The ticket's premise (the 40 games used uniform-v1 teams) was wrong: the budget script plays the random-play corpus teams, which the prior is not derived for. On the generator's own teams the belief never refused: 440 seeded games (random-legal and `uniform-all`, both seats, every request, the fixtures-v1 pool and a freshly generated 1,500-team dev pool, Illusion excluded as the run configs do) gave (a one-off measurement kept on the ticket, not a committed script) 0 `evidence_has_no_support`, 0 `reader_flagged` and the real team and brought set possible at every request. Candidates ruled out by that run: moves called by other moves, forme changes, received or restored items and abilities, Item Clause across the six, the move-count rule, and #1167's Mega-to-base rewrite (the pool normalises it; the belief took every such team). Decision: the belief and the prior stay as they are (making the belief accept a zero-mass fact would contradict its tested contract, and smoothing the prior would stop it being the generator's distribution and move `prior_hash`, the v2 config and the identity). No hash moved. A consumer that plays teams the generator cannot draw (a Mega Stone on a non-holder) gets `evidence_has_no_support` there and plays the `actor-rule` candidate, counted; the cost and progress runs of v2 must use the dataset pool, not the random-play corpus. Tests: `opponent-model/test/generator-prior-corpus.test.ts` pins the five slots and the cause (each refused slot shows another species' stone with no mass in any forme of its species' entry, checked per slot; at every other request the real forme, ability, moves and, but for such stones, item are possible), and `team-dataset/test/generator-belief.test.ts` plays 12 seeded games (8 random-legal, 4 uniform-all) on the fixtures-v1 pool and requires a belief and a sound oracle result at every request. The corpus oracle (`test/corpus.ts`) now takes both Mega formes of a gendered species (meowsticmmega and meowsticfmega share a stone) as the true forme. **The 14 `reader_flagged`** are one game: random b39 seat p2, where the reader flags `a Pokemon shown in two positions (an Illusion twin) is not reconciled` and keeps a `phantom` roster entry for the rest of the game, so every later request is refused (the belief refuses a phantom entry by itself, so the flag adds nothing there). **Flag scope, decided: stays game-wide.** The reader flags a line for fourteen reasons; thirteen are lines it could not take (a malformed field, an unknown side or stat, a request it cannot read, an `|error|` that is not a choice refusal, a `|split|`), whose effect on any Pokemon is unknown, so no Pokemon can be named; the Illusion twin is the only one tied to a Pokemon, and its entry already carries the refusal. Scoping it would need a twin-aware belief (the phantom as a second shown position that may belong to the species' own slot or to an unsettled hiding member, a change to the disguise mixture); not done, since the flag was seen in 1 of 40 baseline-seat games of the cost run and in no generator-team game (Illusion is excluded from the pools). The tested behaviour is unchanged: a flag or a phantom is `reader_flagged`. ADR-025 §3 (N2) says the support is everything the vocabulary allows; a prior with residual 0 (the generator-matched one) has the support of its own list, which is the deviation; it is an erratum candidate for the owner, not edited here.

**Calc differential** (ADR-016 slice 4, `packages/showdown-bridge/test/mechanics-differential.test.ts`, `fixtures/mechanics/<format>/calc-divergences.json`, #935). `@smogon/calc` (MIT) is an exact-pinned `devDependency` of `@areneon/showdown-bridge` (0.12.0, no range) and is on `.github/dependabot.yml`'s `ignore` list, so a bump is a deliberate PR that re-triages the list. It is an oracle only: never a runtime path, a fallback or a tie-breaker, imported by that test alone, and no set data from the calc's front end is used. Calc generation 0 is Champions (the calc's data tables list `CHAMPIONS` first and `Stats.calcStat` has a generation 0 branch: base + Stat Points + 20, HP base + Stat Points + 75). The test runs every damage fixture case (no-crit and crit rolls, both in a Doubles field so the spread and screen modifiers apply; the declared weather, terrain, screens, boosts, status and HP; a Mega attacker by the calc's own Mega forme) and the six stats of every set of the damage cases, the five reference cases, the golden and legal teams and the committed dataset teams through the probe and the calc. A species the calc names differently is mapped by the engine's data (the engine's base forme spelled out, a cosmetic forme to its base species), and a species it lacks stays a divergence. `calc-divergences.json` (`data_class` synthetic, also the input of the mechanics parity report's calc section, `docs/mechanics-parity.md`, regenerated with `node packages/showdown-bridge/scripts/mechanics-parity.ts` after a list change; a test compares the committed report with a fresh render; the report treats a case id as a free string) lists every difference as `{case, reason, probe, calc}` with both exact values; an unlisted difference fails, an entry that no longer differs or differs otherwise fails, and the entries are sorted and unique. Triage follows ADR-016 section 4: the hand-worked fixture or the in-game value decides, never the calc. At this pin it holds one entry, `damage:always-crit:no_crit` (the fixture's no-crit vector for Frost Breath is the probe's forced non-crit run; the calc ignores `isCrit: false` for an always-crit move); every other roll and stat agrees. `ARENEON_PRINT_DIVERGENCES=1` prints the found differences as JSON when the list is being rewritten.

**Fixtures** live only under `fixtures/` (CC0, synthetic or sanitized, with provenance). `fixtures/showdown/<format>/` holds the golden request fixtures plus `battle.summary.json` (the log hash and choices), `channels/p1.json` and `p2.json` (each player's own stream of the golden battle as a `PlayerChannelLog`, the only battle input an observation may be built from), and `choices/` the choice corpus. `random/` is the random-play channel corpus (`b00.json`..., each a seed, the two synthetic teams, the actor seeds and both players' `PlayerChannelLog`, plus `manifest.json`). `fixtures/teams/<format>/` holds synthetic PokePaste teams, a `manifest.json` (provenance, expected verdict and reason) and `verdicts.json` (the validator's log). CI re-captures and compares the request fixtures, summary and verdicts, so engine behaviour drift fails the build. Pastes keep Showdown's trailing spaces (`.editorconfig`, `.gitattributes`).

**TrainingRunConfig, PPO learner and learner state** (`packages/schema/contracts/v1/training-run-config.schema.json`, `packages/showdown-bridge/src/training-run-config.ts`, `ml/areneon_ml/learner/`, ADR-010 §1, §5, #840). `TrainingRunConfig` is the one record of a run's values: lineage and format, every seed (`run_key`, the two initialisation seeds, the learner's generator), the `model` section (encoder and network sizes), the `generation` plan (self-play games, games per scripted baseline, `max_turns`, `n_parallel`, latency budgets, handshake timeout, `max_line_bytes`), the `ppo` settings and the learner's `device` (`cpu` or `cuda`) and thread count. Contracts carry no floating-point numbers, so real values are integers in millionths (`learning_rate_micro`, `clip_micro`, ...) or billionths (`adam_eps_nano`); `areneon_ml.learner.config.read_run_config` is the one place that divides them. `ppo.target_kl_micro` (optional, ADR-010 erratum A1, a minor change within v1): after each complete epoch, if the mean of that epoch's per-minibatch `approx_kl` (minibatch order) is greater than it, the role's remaining epochs are skipped; the train report then carries per role `epochs_run` and `kl_stopped`, and both keys are omitted without the field so existing reports keep their bytes (the dashboard reader takes them as optional keys). `ppo.learning_rate_schedule` (optional, ADR-010 erratum A1, a `kind`-tagged `oneOf`: `constant` or `linear {final_micro, generations >= 1}`): update `t` (the learner generation counter, from 0) uses `L0 + trunc((Lf - L0) * min(t, generations) / generations)` millionths in exact integers (`LinearSchedule.rate_micro`), set on both optimisers before the epochs; the learner-state sidecar records `learning_rate_micro` (the newest update's rate) only for `linear`, so other runs keep their `learner_state_hash`, and a restore refuses a sidecar whose rate is not the schedule's. The reader rules the profile cannot say (heads divide `d_model`, `max_picks` ≤ `max_team`, a baseline listed once) are checked by `read_run_config` and by the TS `readTrainingRunConfig`, which also gives `runConfigHash` (SHA-256 of the JCS bytes, `CheckpointRecord.run_config_hash`). League mixture and freeze-interval values are not in v1: they come with the league scheduler (Wave 5). The learner is PPO with GAE over the TrainingExample stream: `learner/data.py` reads a stream only through `read_stream` with its manifest, refuses a set that is not `learner_ready` or was not played by the `behaviour_policy_id` it trains, and splits each Episode into one Preview step and one Battle trajectory (encoded from the example's observation and candidates alone). The reward is the game score at the last step of a trajectory (win 1, tie ½, loss 0, `ppo.GAME_SCORE`); every earlier step, a refused pending choice among them, has zero immediate reward. `learner/trainer.py` recomputes the behaviour policy's log-probability and the value of every decision from the frozen weights with no gradient (no probability is stored in an Episode), computes advantages with GAE, then runs the configured epochs over minibatches shuffled by the learner's own generator; the loss is the clipped surrogate over a softmax across the real candidates (`ppo.py`, checked against hand-computed values) plus the value error and the entropy bonus. Determinism claim: on CPU, with the config's thread count and deterministic algorithms, the same inputs on the same machine and software give bit-identical weights; nothing is claimed across machines or torch builds, and a CUDA learner is not bit-reproducible (its run config, with `device: cuda`, hashes differently). The learner state (`learner/state.py`) is `learner-<g>.safetensors` (both networks' weights, the Adam moment tensors, the generator's state tensor) plus `learner-<g>.json` (JCS: generation, run config hash, seeds, PPO settings, optimiser step counts); `learner_state_hash` is SHA-256 of `JCS({tensors_sha256, sidecar})`. `restore_state` loads only through safetensors and a JSON parser, recomputes the hash, refuses a state of another run config, a sidecar that contradicts the networks and tensors of another name, dtype or shape, and changes the learner only after every check. `checkpoint/files.py` gained `pack_safetensors` / `unpack_safetensors` (float32 and uint8) beside the checkpoint writer.

**Supervised cloning learner** (`packages/schema/contracts/v1/clone-run-config.schema.json`, `packages/showdown-bridge/src/clone-run-config.ts`, `ml/areneon_ml/learner/clone*.py`, ADR-019 track 1, ADR-010 erratum A2, #967). `python -m areneon_ml.learner clone --config C --stream-dir D --engine-data E --version-manifest V --out O` trains a Preview and a Battle network, separately, to imitate a scripted teacher (`damage-aware@v1`) from its learner-ready `TrainingSet` (`D/stream.jsonl` and `D/training-set.json`, read only through `trajectories` / `read_stream`), and writes the new directory `O` (never overwritten; built beside it and renamed): `checkpoints/<id>.safetensors`, `records/<id>.json` (a `CheckpointRecord` each, named by id so `python -m areneon_ml.checkpoint classify|independent --records O/records --sources O/sources.json` reads it as it is), `sources.json` (the teacher set hash mapped to `[]`, an outside behaviour-cloning source), `clone-state.safetensors` / `clone-state.json` (weights, Adam moments, generator state, a JCS sidecar of `kind: "clone"`; its `state_hash` is the records' `learner_state_hash`), `clone-report.json` and `clone-run-config.json`. `CloneRunConfig` v1 (integers only, closed; `cloneRunConfigHash` / `CloneConfig.hash` is SHA-256 of its JCS bytes and the records' `run_config_hash`) holds the lineage, format, seeds (`run_key`, the two initialisation seeds, the learner generator), the `model` section (the shape of `TrainingRunConfig.model`; heads divide `d_model`, `max_picks` ≤ `max_team` are reader rules on both sides), the `teacher` (`behaviour_policy_id` `<name>@<commit>`, never a learned `policy:<hash>` pair, and `training_set_hash`, the SHA-256 of the manifest's JCS bytes), the `clone` settings (`epochs`, `minibatch_size`, `learning_rate_micro`, Adam betas and epsilon, `max_grad_norm_micro`, `holdout {key, ppm}`) and the learner `device` / `threads`. `clone.check_teacher` fails closed: the manifest must be synthetic, `learner_ready`, played by the named teacher and hash to `teacher.training_set_hash`. The loss is `ppo.policy_terms`' softmax over the real candidates (padding masked), `-mean(log p[teacher choice])`, per minibatch of a seeded shuffle (`torch.randperm` on the learner generator, Preview first then Battle); the value head has no label and is untrained here (the PPO phase learns it). The held-back part is chosen per Episode by `held_back(key, episode_hash, ppm)`: HMAC-SHA256 over the 64 hex characters, first 8 bytes big endian as n, held back when `n * 10^6 < ppm * 2^64` (the rule shape of ADR-015; ppm at most 500,000; a role with nothing left to train on is refused). The report holds, per role, the loss of each epoch and the agreement on the training and the held-back parts (steps, mean cross-entropy, top-1 hits against the teacher's choice, and `chance`, the mean of 1/candidates), deterministic values only. The records carry `parent` absent, `training_set_hashes` the teacher set, no league opponents, `data_class` synthetic, a `team_set_manifest_hash` over the own teams the examples' observations carry, and the version manifest of the teacher games (the `--version-manifest` file: the teacher run must provide it). Determinism claim as for the PPO learner (CPU, one machine and software; `configure_learner` sets it up). `Trajectory.episode` (new, optional) lets the split find an Episode; `state.gather_tensors` is the shared tensor collection of `pack_state`. Not here: `TrainingRunConfig.warm_start`, the loop and the KL anchor (next slices), the teacher run (#965).

**Warm start** (`TrainingRunConfig.warm_start`, `ml/areneon_ml/loop/warm_start.py`, ADR-010 erratum A2, ADR-019 §4, §6, #966). An optional `warm_start: {preview, battle}` (two 64-hex checkpoint ids; a minor change within v1: absent keeps today's bytes and `run_config_hash`, an older reader refuses it; both readers also refuse one id for both roles) makes generation 0 a declared cloned pair instead of the seeded random pair. Nothing is fetched: `python -m areneon_ml.loop import-clone --clone-dir O --run-dir R` copies a `learner clone` output into the run directory (the checkpoints into `checkpoints/`, the records into `warm-start/records/<role>.json`; it checks each record is a clone record, each file hashes to its record's id and `sources.json` maps the teacher sets to `[]`; rerunning is a no-op and it never replaces a record with other bytes). At the start of generation 0 (and when a run is resumed at generation 0) `load_warm_start` loads both through `load_checkpoint` and refuses, as a `LoopError` (exit 3), a file missing from the store, one that does not hash to its id, another role, another engine-data table, encoder version or `model` section than the run's (the whole `ModelConfig` must be equal) or bytes that do not re-serialise as themselves, and a record that is not a clone record (empty `training_set_hashes`, a `parent`, an exploiter target or league opponents, not synthetic), made for another encoder version or format, or naming another checkpoint. Generation 0 freezes the pair with no learner step: its checkpoint ids are the cloned ids and its `records/<role>.json` are the clone records byte for byte (a checkpoint has one record and the provenance graph refuses a second), so `learner_state_hash` and `run_config_hash` inside them are the cloning run's; the generation's manifest and learner state (`learner-0`) are this run's. The learner's Adam moments and generator start fresh from the cloned weights (the supervised phase's optimiser state is not carried over); generation 1 is the first policy-gradient step and its records name the cloned ids as `parent`. `sources_registry` / `export_provenance` map every training set a generation-0 record names to `[]` (an outside behaviour-cloning source), so `classify` / `independent` see the teacher set in the ancestry: two warm runs on one teacher set are not independent (C6, ADR-019 §6), runs on different teacher sets or none are. Resume and the SIGKILL claims hold (tests kill at every stage of generations 0 to 2 of a tiny warm run and compare manifests and learner state bytes). **Battle-only (#1165):** `warm_start` may be `{battle}` alone (`preview` optional, a minor change within v1; `{preview, battle}` and an absent key keep every hash and byte; both readers refuse `preview` as the only key through the schema's `required: [battle]`, and one id for both roles). Only the named roles are imported (`import-clone --roles battle` copies the Battle checkpoint and record and neither reads nor copies the output's Preview; `import_clone(..., roles=("battle",))`), loaded and checked (`load_warm_start` iterates the named roles), and carry clone records; the other role starts from the seeded init of its `seeds.init_*` exactly as a scratch run's generation 0 (same checkpoint id) with an ordinary built record (no training sets, no parent, this run's state hash), and `Sealing.records` holds the clone records of the warm roles only. `ppo.value_warmup` and `ppo.anchor` act only on the warm-started roles (`Learner.warm_roles`): an unwarmed role trains as plain PPO from update 0, the anchor networks are given exactly for the warm roles (`LearnerError` otherwise), and `train-report.json` carries `value_warmup`, `anchor_kl` and `anchor_beta_micro` for the warm roles alone (the learner-state sidecar's `anchor_beta_micro` is the schedule's value for the warm role). Provenance: the Preview lineage starts at the seeded init (generation 0's Preview has no teacher set), the Battle lineage names the clone; from generation 1 both descend from the cloned Battle network through the games it played (the sources registry maps the set to the pair that played it). Why: damage-aware@v1 plays one fixed actor-rule Preview choice, so a cloned Preview would start near-deterministic and the anchor would hold it there. Tests: `tests/ml/loop/test_warm_start_battle_only.py`. Not here: the KL anchor, human-data warm starts, the experiment runs.

**Value warm-up** (`ppo.value_warmup`, `ml/areneon_ml/learner/trainer.py`, ADR-019 §4, #1111; `docs/training-run.md` section 5). Optional `{generations: N}` in the `ppo` block (integer at least 1; a minor change within v1: absent keeps today's run config hash, learner-state hash and every generation file); a reader rule on both sides (`readTrainingRunConfig`, `read_run_config`) requires `warm_start`. The learner updates with index below N (`value_warmup_active`; generation 1 is update 0) train the value head only: every parameter outside `value.*`, the shared trunk included, has `requires_grad` off for the update (restored after), so the cloned policy's outputs stay byte-identical, the backward pass is the value error alone (`value_coef` is not applied and `target_kl` is skipped), and Adam, which skips a parameter without a gradient, holds moments for the value head only; the same optimiser serves PPO afterwards (the value head's moments carry on, the rest start at update N + 1). The learning-rate schedule's index counts the warm-up updates. `RoleReport.value_warmup` (`true`, in `train-report.json` only on a warm-up update; the dashboard reader's optional key set gained it) and the sidecar's `ppo` block record it only when set. Resume needs nothing new: the position is the restored generation counter. Tests: `tests/ml/learner/test_value_warmup.py` (frozen policy, value-only Adam state, byte-identical logits, resume at and inside the warm-up) and `tests/ml/loop/test_value_warmup.py` (a warm loop: only `value.*` tensors move through the warm-up, killed anywhere resumes to the same files).

**Entropy schedule** (`ppo.entropy_schedule`, `ml/areneon_ml/learner/{config,trainer,state}.py`, ADR-023 §3 step 3, ADR-010 erratum A6 item 4, #1048). Optional in the `ppo` block, a `kind`-tagged `oneOf` (a minor change within v1; absent or `constant` keeps every `learner_state_hash`, train report and generation file, and the run config hash of a record that carries `constant` still differs from one without the key): `{kind: "constant"}` or `{kind: "linear", final_micro, generations}` (schema defs `EntropySchedule` / `EntropyLinearSchedule`: a final of 0 is allowed, which the learning-rate `LinearSchedule` forbids). Update `t` uses `E0 + trunc((Ef - E0) * min(t, generations) / generations)` millionths, `E0` = `entropy_coef_micro`, in exact integers (`LinearSchedule.rate_micro`, the one helper the learning-rate schedule and the anchor share; `Learner.entropy_coef_micro(update)`), set on the loss weights of both roles before the epochs; `read_run_config` divides by 10^6 in its one place. **Value warm-up interaction (decided):** `t` counts POLICY updates, as the anchor's does: with `ppo.value_warmup` of N updates the index is `max(0, u - N)`, so the decay is not spent while the policy is frozen; the warm-up updates report `E0` (the entropy term is not in the value-only loss). The index is one value per update for both roles, so a role that a battle-only warm start leaves unwarmed trains plain PPO through the warm-up with `E0`. The sidecar records `entropy_coef_micro` (the newest update's weight) and each role of `train-report.json` the same key, ONLY under a `linear` schedule; `restore_state` refuses a sidecar whose weight is not the schedule's (present or absent: "entropy weight"), and the dashboard reader's optional key set gained `entropy_coef_micro` (an integer). Tests: `tests/ml/learner/test_entropy_schedule.py` (hand values falling to 0, rising and past `generations`, the warm-up offset, flat schedule equals no schedule, resume, tampered sidecar), `tests/ml/loop/test_entropy_schedule.py` (a tiny loop: keys per generation, byte-identical rerun, kills at four stages resume to the same hashes), `packages/showdown-bridge/test/training-run-config.test.ts` and the corpus samples `training-run-config/*entropy-*`. Not here: a target-entropy controller, the schedule values of EXP-T2.

**Training play temperature** (`generation.play`, `ml/areneon_ml/learner/{config,ppo,trainer}.py`, `loop/driver.py`, `packages/showdown-bridge/src/league-generation.ts`, `progress/run.py`, ADR-023 §1 and §6 T1, ADR-010 erratum A6 item 3, #1046). Optional `generation.play: {temperature_ppm}` in `TrainingRunConfig` (a minor change within v1; the schema allows 1 to 1,000,000, both readers refuse an explicit 1,000,000 as the policy query does; absent keeps every byte, id, hash and report, present enters `run_config_hash`, so it is a new run from generation 0). **Play:** `playOne` gives the pair's seat `play: {temperature_ppm}` (the existing sampling field and policy-id rule), so each query carries it; `Frozen.policy_id` (driver) is `policy_id(preview, battle, temperature_ppm=tau, features=...)`, which is the `--policy-id` the Node side labels the Episodes with, the pair id of `league-run.json`, and the `behaviour_policy_id` the loader and `learner/data._ready` check, so a tempered run refuses a TrainingSet played at temperature 1 (`LoopError` on the league record, `LearnerDataError` on the manifest) and the reverse. **Learner (on-policy for the tempered policy):** `ppo.temper(logits, tau)` multiplies the float32 logits by `1e6 / tau` computed once in float64 (the scorer's rule, so the learner's softmax is the very policy that was sampled; `None` returns the logits untouched), and `policy_terms` / `anchor_kl` take `temperature_ppm`: the old log-probability (`_score`, frozen weights), the new log-probability, the entropy, the approximate KL and with it the `target_kl` early stop, and the anchor's KL (both `pi_theta` and the anchor's cached log-probabilities, `_anchor_scores`) are all on `softmax(z / tau)` over the real candidates, masked after the scale. The value head is untouched. `train-report.json` carries `temperature_ppm` per role (value warm-up updates too) only when the field is set (the dashboard reader's optional key set gained it, an integer of at least 1); the learner-state sidecar gains nothing (the run config hash covers it). **Evaluation:** `progress_generation` adds `ExtraMode(temperature_ppm=tau)` to the modes of a run that declares it, with no `--modes` flag (`_with_run_mode`; not when the evaluation is played alone, the Preview ablation and the search candidate, and not twice when `--modes` already names it), so its matches are `<opponent>-sample-t<tau>` beside argmax and sample. Not here: a temperature schedule across generations, training-time thresholding, a default play mode for any run, the EXP-T1 run. Tests: `tests/ml/learner/test_play_temperature.py` (R4: a hand-built three-candidate example, old/new log-probabilities, entropy and approximate KL at tau 0.5 equal hand values and differ from temperature 1; the anchor's KL at tau; the padded candidate; the trainer's `_score` and `_anchor_scores`; the report key), `tests/ml/loop/test_play_temperature.py` (a tiny loop: the request carries the tempered id, a rerun is byte-identical, kills at four stages resume to the same hashes, the other id is refused both ways), `tests/ml/progress/test_run_play_temperature.py`, `league-generation.test.ts` (queries carry the temperature), `training-run-config.test.ts` and the corpus samples `training-run-config/*play-*`.

**Value-head diagnostics** (`ml/areneon_ml/learner/diagnostics.py`, ADR-010 erratum A4 item 1, #1001; diagnostic only, no stop, schedule or gate reads it). Every role of `train-report.json` now carries `explained_variance` (`1 - Var(return - V) / Var(return)`, population variances in float64 over the returns the learner trains on and the frozen-weight values it recomputes before the update; a return with zero variance gives the fixed value 0.0, as a constant predictor does) and `value_calibration` (10 bins of predicted value, `floor(V * 10)` clamped to 0 and 9, each `{count, mean_value, mean_score}` against the FINAL game score of the decision's trajectory; an empty bin is count 0 with means 0.0). Floats are stored as floats like the other report values (same CPU computation, so reruns are byte-identical). They are written for every new report (new runs' report and manifest bytes change; committed generations are never rewritten, and a report without the keys still reads): the dashboard reader takes both keys as optional and together, refuses a calibration that is not exactly 10 closed bins, and the dashboard charts explained variance per generation and shows the last generation's calibration table per role, labelled as a diagnostic. Tests: `tests/ml/learner/test_diagnostics.py` (hand-computed table, constant predictor, zero variance), `tests/ml/report/test_report.py`.

**Preview bootstrap** (`ppo.preview_bootstrap_micro`, `ml/areneon_ml/learner/{config,trainer}.py`, ADR-010 erratum A4 item 3, #1005). Optional integer 0 to 1,000,000 in the `ppo` block (a minor change within v1: absent keeps every hash, report and file; an older reader refuses it; no cross-field reader rule). With w = micro / 10^6 above 0 the Preview step's terminal reward is `(1 - w) * score + w * V_battle(first Battle decision of the same Episode)` (the pairing is `Trajectory.episode`), and the Preview advantage is GAE on that reward minus the Preview value (for its single step: return minus value). `V_battle` is the Battle network's FROZEN value: `Learner.train` reads the Battle role's log-probabilities and values once (`_frozen_battle`) BEFORE either role is updated (Battle trains first in `ROLES`) and the Battle table reuses that same pass, so the Battle update cannot change the Preview targets and with the field absent or 0 nothing is read early and the bytes are today's. A Preview trajectory with no Battle trajectory of its Episode is a `LearnerDataError`. Under bootstrap the returns the learner trains on, and the explained variance of #1001 is taken against, are the bootstrapped Preview returns (the value-calibration bins still compare the frozen value with the final score). A value warm-up of a warm Preview trains its value head toward the same bootstrapped return, a battle-only warm start leaves the unwarmed Preview bootstrapping, and the anchor and the entropy schedule are untouched. The Preview role of `train-report.json` gains `preview_bootstrap_micro` only when the field is present (the learner-state sidecar already holds the `ppo` block). Tests: `tests/ml/learner/test_preview_bootstrap.py` (hand-computed returns at w = 1 and a partial w, a reordered `ROLES`, absent equals 0, the refusal, warm-up and battle-only start), corpus samples `training-run-config/*preview-bootstrap*`.

**Model options** (`ml/areneon_ml/model/options.py`, ADR-010 erratum A4 item 2, #1002; the base of the architecture arms of Epic #1000). `model.options` (network) and `model.encoder.options` (encoder) are optional closed objects of the `TrainingRunConfig` (a minor change within v1; `EncoderConfig.options` and `ModelConfig.options` hold them as sorted tuples). **Absent keeps today's bytes**: `as_dict` omits the key when no option is set (an empty object counts as none for the bytes and `encoder_version`; its run config hash still differs from an absent key), so `encoder_version`, checkpoint bytes and ids, learner state hashes and the layout of every existing record are unchanged (tests pin a checkpoint id and the encoder version computed before the change). Present, an option is part of the run config hash, `encoder_version` (encoder options, through the config) and the checkpoint's `model_config` metadata. `REGISTRY` (per scope, `OptionSpec(name, check, layout_changing)`) holds the options below, starting with the encoder option `candidate_matchup`; any other name is refused, and each further arm's ticket adds its spec and its schema property (tests register test-only specs with `monkeypatch`). **`candidate_matchup`** (ADR-022 tier B, ADR-010 erratum A5 item 1, #1023; `"model": {"encoder": {..., "options": {"candidate_matchup": true}}}`, only `true` is a value, layout changing): a battle candidate's move slot actions gain seven categorical columns after the five base ones (`BASE_CAND_COLUMNS`): for each opposing position and the ally, the engine-data type-chart cell (`GameData.type_chart`, the export's `types[].damage_taken` code) of the move's type against each of the position's first two shown types, and a same-type flag (the move's type is one of the acting Pokemon's shown types). A cell is `RESERVED` + the export's code (so the network embeds the engine's own codes; no multiplier, product of types or same-type bonus value is written anywhere, and a source scan in `tests/ml/model/test_matchup.py` holds that), `NONE` for an empty or fainted position, a missing second type, a status move, a switch or a pass. Only the species a position shows enters (`temp_species` or `species`, else the roster's base species): never an unrevealed item, ability or move, and a type change by a volatile is not tracked. The format has no Terastallisation (the reducer refuses a `-terastallize` line). The Battle network embeds the columns (`matchup`, `same_type` embeddings, wider `slot_parts`); the Preview network ignores them. With the option absent the layout, `encoder_version`, tensors and checkpoint ids are today's (pinned tests); with it the config and layout move `encoder_version` and `GameData` gained the chart tables (not part of `attribute_digest`). The checkpoint `format` is `"2"` (`FORMAT_OPTIONS`) only for a config with a `layout_changing` option and `"1"` otherwise; `load_checkpoint` refuses a format that does not fit the file's options and, given `configured` (the caller's `ModelConfig`), a file whose network or encoder options differ (`CheckpointOptionsError`, a `CheckpointFileError`); the worker passes the model section of `<run-dir>/run-config.json` as `configured` when that file exists (a hand-made run directory without it is checked by the checkpoint alone; exit 4 on a mismatch or an unusable section). The learner-state sidecar carries `model_options` (`network` / `encoder`) only when an option is set, and `restore_state` refuses any difference, in either direction, with `LearnerOptionsError` (a `LearnerStateError`) before the run config hash check. The `clone-run-config` contract takes the same optional `model.options` and `model.encoder.options` objects (#1168; same rules, absent keeps the record's bytes and `clone_run_config_hash`; present flows through `ModelConfig.from_dict` into the clone's networks, `encoder_version`, checkpoint metadata and format), so a clone made with `candidate_matchup` warm-starts only a run that sets it and a run without it (or the reverse) refuses the clone at generation 0 (`tests/ml/loop/test_warm_start_clone_options.py`).

**`candidate_target`** (encoder option, ADR-010 erratum A4 item 7, #1011; `model.encoder.options: {"candidate_target": true}`, only `true` is a value, layout changing, checkpoint format `"2"`, both `TrainingRunConfig` and `CloneRunConfig` accept it). Each battle move part gains a third token column (`Layout.cand_tok` is `("actor", "switch_in", "target")`, `cand_tok` [N, SLOTS, 3]): the token of the Pokemon the move's target location names, read in the engine's convention (`getAtLoc`: L > 0 is the foe in opposing active slot L - 1, token `n_own` + its roster index; L < 0 is the own active slot -L - 1, the own token at that request position), -1 for an untargeted or spread move, the user itself, a switch, a pass, an empty or fainted slot. The Battle network gathers that token's embedding (`_gather(mons, tok[..., 2])`) as one more d-wide slot-part input; the Preview network ignores it. Absent keeps `Layout.as_dict`, `encoder_version`, tensors and checkpoint ids (`cand_tok` stays width 2; the golden version is pinned); it composes with `candidate_matchup` and `candidate_features` (independent columns). `collate` refuses queries of two token layouts and the Battle network a batch of another. `src/choice-channels.ts` replays the committed choice corpus on the pinned engine into the fixture `choice-channels/channels.json` (`scripts/capture-choice-channels.ts`; `test/choice-channels.test.ts` compares it with a fresh replay; the Python `tests/ml/model/test_target.py` reads only the fixture and checks every corpus candidate against an independent species mapping read from the log and request).

**`factored_preview`** (network option, ADR-010 erratum A4 item 4, #1007; `model.options: {"factored_preview": true}`, only `true` is a value, layout changing, checkpoint format `"2"`; both `TrainingRunConfig` and `CloneRunConfig` accept it, the first NETWORK option in `REGISTRY["network"]`). The Preview network scores an order as `sum over places p of u(place p, token p) + v(token at place 1, token at place 2)` (`place_score` and `lead_score`, each Linear(2d, d), GELU, Linear(d, 1); the place embedding `place` is kept; order kept, `max_picks` at least 2 or `ConfigError`). The candidate projection, `pending`, the two candidate norms, the cross-attention and the interaction `score` are not built for Preview under the option (so the pending flag is not read there; the value head is unchanged); candidate set, order and the masked softmax over real candidates are unchanged. The Battle network ignores the option (same tensors and logits). Absent keeps every Preview tensor name and logit (`test_factored_preview.py` pins the names and the existing golden digests pin the logits; `test_options.py` only updates the registry assertion). `tests/ml/model/test_factored_preview.py`: the logits equal a loop-computed sum on the trunk's tokens, a zeroed-term check, bit-identical alone and after other queries, A1/A2 forms equal, padding, and the checkpoint loads only under the option.

**Training-data inspector** (`ml/areneon_ml/inspector/`, ADR-017 §4a, §4c, #922; `python -m areneon_ml.loop inspect --run-dir R [--out D]`, default `<run-dir>.inspect/`, never inside the run directory; the pages are written to a temporary sibling, flushed and renamed over the output, which may be new or an earlier inspector output and is otherwise refused; read-only on the run directory and the dataset, standard library only for the pages, Node for the pool). It writes static, linked, offline pages for a synthetic public run: `index.html`, `distributions.html` (pool against all drawn teams: generator labels, species, items, moves, abilities and Mega Stone holders, top 15 as SVG bars plus a table of every value), `drawn-g<N>.html` (the same for one generation and how often each team was drawn), `games-g<N>.html` (every planned game: seed, both teams linked, opponent kind and baseline seat, winner, turns, outcome, forfeit loser and cause, `not recorded` for a generation older than the per-game cause) and `teams-<k>.html` (the pool's teams in hash order, 500 per page, with readable sets, labels, the games that drew each and its training-game counts by opponent kind; a win share only from 10 games, no interval). The pool is rebuilt by the loop's own function: `packages/team-dataset/scripts/print-pool.ts` (`listPool` in `pool-listing.ts` over `datasetPoolDetail` in `loop-pool.ts`, which `datasetTeamPool` now wraps, or `interimPoolSource` for a run without a dataset); a Mega Stone is whatever the engine's data marks as one. `readers.py` reads `plan.json`, `generation.json` and `team-set.json` with closed key sets (an unknown or missing key is an `InspectError`; the pin is two-step: `tests/ml/inspector/test_readers.py` ties the readers' key sets to the committed fixture run, and `training-loop.python.test.ts` (which plays a fresh tiny loop with the real worker, so it runs where the Python environment exists, as in the typescript CI job) requires the fresh run's `plan.json`, `generation.json` (top level, game rows, manifest) and `team-set.json` key sets to equal the fixture's, so a loop writer change fails one of the two), `league-run.json` and `run-config.json` through the loop's own readers, and every generation's manifest is verified before it is read. `model.py` refuses on any mismatch: a plan index outside the pool, a `team-set.json` or `generation.json` team that is not what the plan draws, an Episode that is not a synthetic `cc0-synthetic` league Episode of the run, whose own team does not hash to its plan seat or whose result differs from `generation.json` (games are joined to Episodes by seed and seat; only ids and counts are read from an Episode), and a lineage named private (a name match only: the real defence is the data, since every Episode must be labelled synthetic and `cc0-synthetic` and the pool comes from the loop's own function over a verified synthetic dataset or the committed fixture teams). `page.py` holds the §4c rules: a `<meta>` CSP first in `<head>` (`default-src 'none'`, one `sha256` script, one `sha256` style, `img-src 'none'`, `base-uri 'none'`, `form-action 'none'`), HTML escaping of every data string, data in `<script type="application/json">` blocks with `<`, `>`, `&`, U+2028 and U+2029 escaped, one script of ours with no data in it that filters rows through `textContent` and properties only, links built from fixed names and hex ids. The committed fixture is `fixtures/loop/inspector-run/` (a pruned two-generation tiny loop: the binary learner states and checkpoints are not kept and its manifests are resealed; `pool.json` is the Node side's output, compared byte for byte by `packages/team-dataset/test/pool-listing.test.ts` and by `tests/ml/inspector/test_command.py`). Tests: hostile-string canary with a negative control, no remote reference and CSP on every page, a build with sockets blocked, one canonical result for the same files. Manual check of a rendered page with no network and the CSP in force: `google-chrome --headless=new --host-resolver-rules="MAP * ~NOTFOUND" --virtual-time-budget=3000 --dump-dom file://…/games-g000001.html` shows the filter box un-hidden (the hashed script ran). Replay pages, the dashboard and the progress evaluation are later slices of ADR-017.

**Replay viewer** (`packages/replay-view/web/`, the shared interpreter in `packages/public-state/src/`, `ml/areneon_ml/inspector/replay*.py`, ADR-017 §4, §4a, §4b, §4c, #915; `python -m areneon_ml.loop replay --run-dir R --generation G [--game 3,5-7] [--out D] [--engine-data F]`, default out `<run-dir>.inspect/`, no `--game` means every played game of the generation). The training-game replay is the two players' own channels side by side, read from their Episodes (joined by battle seed and seat by the inspector's own checks: the manifest, each Episode's file hash, labels, `validate("episode")`, no `|split|`, results equal to `generation.json`); a forfeited game has no Episodes and so no replay, the hidden half is never filled in, and a non-public lineage or non-synthetic Episode is refused. It writes `<out>/replays/g<NNNNNN>/game-<NNNNNN>.html` (each file atomically) and `replays/index.html`; `inspect` leaves `replays/` as it is and its games table links to the pages that exist (a relative link, "not built" otherwise). **Clean room**: `interpreter.cjs` (it lives in `packages/public-state/src/`, below) is written from this repository's fixtures, `sim/SIM-PROTOCOL.md` and the reducer's grammar; no line of any Showdown client was used. Both viewer files are `.cjs` classic scripts: Python inlines `interpreter.cjs` then `view.cjs` byte for byte as the page's ONE hashed script (`replay_page.assets()` reads the interpreter from `replay_page.INTERPRETER`), the board CSS after the inspector's as its ONE hashed style block, and each file ends with a `typeof module` guard so the tests can `require` it (the page has no `module`). Coverage counts them: `vitest.config.ts` includes `packages/replay-view/web/**/*.cjs` and the root's `packages/*/src/**` covers the interpreter, so the per-file 90% rule (lines, branches, functions, statements) applies, and one test runs the joined script in a fresh `vm` context as the page does. No bundler, no npm runtime dependency; `tsc` checkJs runs through the package's `typecheck` (`globals.d.ts` declares `ArReplay` for the view, because the typedefs make the interpreter a module to the checker). Guards are `need(ok, reason)` calls, not `if`/`return`, so a branch is counted once. The interpreter keeps the reducer's state shape (`own` / `opponent`, snake_case fields, plus `labels` for display names) and `packages/public-state/test/parity.test.ts` compares it with the Python reducer's states at every request point of the golden, random-corpus, `unavailable/` and Illusion channels (a Python child on the root `.venv`, skipped without it) and with the committed `reduced/` goldens; a line it cannot take is kept verbatim and flagged, never dropped (`flags`), and a sweep of every committed channel requires none but the one named limit: an Illusion twin shown in two positions is not reconciled (the reducer's pair tracking is not ported; `|replace|` itself is, so the real Pokemon takes the slot and the disguise is put back). The view draws an SVG board (type-coloured tokens from the species table embedded for the species the game uses, HP bars eased between states only, never changing a state; the toggle and `prefers-reduced-motion` turn the easing off), caption bar, play/pause/step/jump/speed and a view selector, a decision panel (the request's own options with the recorded choice and `source_policy_id` marked: it shows what the log and the Episode say, with no rule of its own; the candidate list is not recomputed), the own team, and the log and raw log; `#f=N` opens a frame. The two channels are kept at the same moment by matching the lines both players see. **Page security** (§4c): `page.document(..., shell=Shell(script, style, data_id))` is the one shell (CSP meta first in `<head>`, hashes of exactly those two blocks, `base-uri` and `form-action` `'none'`), all data is in one `application/json` block, the script writes text through `textContent` and builds nodes with `createElement` / `createElementNS` only (`make()` refuses `style`, `on*` and URL attributes (`href`, `src`, `xlink:href`, ...) unless a slice opts in for a tag and name with `allowUrlAttribute`, and class names made of data pass through `token()`; the SVG namespace name is built from pieces so a scan for a URL scheme in a page stays exact). Tests: `tests/ml/inspector/test_replay*.py` (refusals, the page's own structure, CSP hashes recomputed, no remote reference, hostile canary with negative controls, sockets blocked, deterministic bytes, `inspect` keeping and linking `replays/`) and `packages/replay-view/test/` (parity, every handler, hostile strings through a DOM stand-in, source scans for sinks). The headless render (a headless Chrome or Chromium with every host name unresolvable) is a manual check: `ARENEON_RENDER_TEST=1 uv run pytest tests/ml/inspector/test_replay_pages.py` (`ARENEON_RENDER_BIN` names the binary). The sprite pack, progress-game replays, re-simulation and the Showdown export are later slices.

**Shared player-view state reader** (`packages/public-state`, ADR-018 §2, ADR-017 §4b, #943; `item_received` and `ability_received` on every `PublicMon`, #1124, see the opponent belief paragraph). The replay interpreter moved here from `packages/replay-view/web/`, unchanged in form: `src/interpreter.cjs` is still a classic script with the `typeof module` guard, Python inlines its exact bytes (`replay_page.INTERPRETER`), and every replay page was byte-identical when it moved (#1124 added two fields to the interpreter, so the inlined script, its CSP hash and `readerSha256`, hence the damage-aware baseline identity, changed). `src/index.ts` is the typed ESM wrapper (`createRequire`): `interpretChannel(player, lines, dex?)` (player view only; the omniscient mode is not exposed), `createInterpreter`, `parseLine`, `toId`, the state types (`PublicState`, `PublicMon`, ...), `INTERPRETER_PATH` and `readerSha256()` (the reader's file hash, a part of the baseline identity of ADR-018 §5). The package is engine-free and takes no team, omniscient log or seed. The interpreter-versus-reducer parity test (`test/parity.test.ts`, with `reduce_points.py`) and the interpreter's line tests (`test/lines*.test.ts`) are owned there; `replay-view` keeps its view, security and omniscient tests and the joined-script test, and its `test/support.ts` re-exports the shared fixtures.

**World facts** (`packages/public-state/src/world-facts.ts`, ADR-026 §1 and §9 slice L, #1136). The facts the reviewed world writers need and the shared reader does not track, derived from ONE player's own channel by a module of its own: `WorldFactsReader` (`feed` a line, `facts()` at any point), `worldFacts` and `worldFactsAtRequests` (the facts before each `|request|` line, a refusal counted where it stands). It imports nothing (a test enforces it) and has its own `WORLD_FACTS_FORMAT` (`world-facts/3`, bumped when a fact is computed differently; part of the search identity, ADR-026 §5) and file hash (`worldFactsSha256()`, in `index.ts`); `interpreter.cjs`, `readerSha256()` and every replay page are unchanged.
