The input is a branded `OwnChannel` that only `ownChannel(player, lines)` makes (a bad player, a line that is no protocol line and a `|split|` marker are refused), so an omniscient log, a team or a seed is a compile error (type test); that the lines are one player's own stream is the caller's duty (the bridge hands over a `PlayerChannel`'s lines, which `playerChannel` has checked), beside the caller-supplied `WorldFactsData` (`stall_moves`, the ids of the moves that add the engine's `stall` volatile; `terrains`; `swap_side_conditions`, the list Court Change swaps; `toxic_stage_max`, the Toxic stage ceiling; `action_sources`, the `[from]` texts of the move lines the engine runs as move actions of their own (Round, Instruct, Dancer); reviewed engine data, no game rule or list in the module, and the parity test reads them from the pinned `data/moves.ts`, `data/conditions.ts` and `sim/battle-actions.ts` (`test/world-facts-data.ts`, shared with the world builder tests) and fails if the pin differs; no committed record of this data exists yet, the composition root of search supplies it; only protocol wire tokens such as `tox`, `psn` and Poison Heal's `[from]` text are in the code). Per side (`own` / `opponent` as the player sees them) and position letter: `active_turns` (the engine's `activeTurns`: `|turn|` lines since the `|switch|` or `|drag|`; a `|swap|` carries it), `last_move` (the last `|move|` without a `[from] move/ability/item` tag; `[from]lockedmove` is the Pokemon's own), `protect_streak` (consecutive turns of successful stall uses; the `stall` volatile survives one turn, a `-fail`, a switch and a faint end it), `toxic_ticks` (the `[from] psn` damage lines, or Poison Heal heals, since the later of the `-status|…|tox` and the switch-in, which resets the stage as the engine does); per side `total_fainted`, `fainted_this_turn`, `fainted_last_turn` (the engine's `faintedLastTurn`) and the side conditions with `started_turn` and `layers` (`-sidestart` lines, `-swapsideconditions` swaps them); on the field the weather, terrain and pseudo-weathers with their `started_turn` (0 before `|turn|1`; a `-weather|…|[upkeep]` line starts nothing). Remaining durations are the writers' subtraction from `started_turn`, not a fact here. **`world-facts/2` (slice W part 2, #1140)** adds what the writers need and the log shows: `move_actions` per active (the engine's `activeMoveActions`, which Fake Out and First Impression read in this pin: own `|move|` lines, `[from]lockedmove`, tagged lines listed in `action_sources`, `|cant|` lines and the confusion self-hit `-damage|…|[from] confusion`; the `-activate|…|confusion` before it is printed at every attempt and counts nothing), `volatiles` per active (`started_turn` of each `-start`, forgotten at `-end`, a switch and a faint; `moved_before`: the Pokemon had run a move action that turn, which Taunt's own start handler reads through the queue), `source` on weather, terrain, field and side conditions (the `[of]` Pokemon of the start line, else the actor of the latest `|move|` line, any tag) and `after_residual` on every dated condition and volatile (it began after the `|upkeep` of its turn, or before turn 1, so the engine has not counted it down yet: a weather set by a replacement after a faint is still full when the next turn begins), and `move_uses` per side and nickname (own move choices by move id). A volatile a Baton Pass handed over has no `-start` since the switch-in, so it has no fact (the world builder then refuses it). Typed refusals (`WorldFactsError`, a code and a line index, a fixed message that quotes no line; the first one is kept and thrown by `facts()` from that line on, earlier requests keep their facts): `bad_player`, `secret_marker`, `malformed_line`, `unattributable_line` (a move, faint, swap or switch from a position nobody holds), `illusion_replace` (any `|replace|`; the ADR makes Illusion R), `stall_unresolved` (a stall move with no `-singleturn` or `-fail` of its user before the next action) and `toxic_tick_unobserved` (a badly poisoned Pokemon in play at `|upkeep` with no tick line since the residual began: Magic Guard, or Poison Heal at full health). Tests: `packages/public-state/test/world-facts.test.ts` (hand-written lines for every fact and refusal, 100% lines) and `packages/showdown-bridge/test/world-facts-parity.test.ts` (in the bridge because it needs the engine), which replays every committed battle (random-play, `unavailable/`, Illusion, Baton Pass, Fling, golden) through the pinned engine and requires, for both seats at every request, the facts equal an oracle recomputed from the OMNISCIENT log by Pokemon name (a backward scan from the cut that matches the channel prefix, sharing no state machine with the module), the turn, weather, terrain, field and side-condition layers equal the shared reader's state, the stall, Court Change and Toxic cap data read from the pinned `data/moves.ts` and `data/conditions.ts`, and the only refusal the Illusion replace (88 of 1,960 requests at this pin); a negative control (an empty stall list) makes the sweep fail. Not here: the fidelity corpus (slice T). Limits: the corpus reaches no hazard with two layers (the hand-written tests do), and `activeTurns` is the log's count, confirmed against the engine's source (`nextTurn` increments it for every Pokemon in play) rather than against a live battle.

**Damage-aware baseline inputs** (ADR-018 §3 to §5, slice 1, #943; the scorer, `ActorContext.team` and the A1 actor are slice 2). `DamageAwareConfig` (`packages/schema/contracts/v1/damage-aware-config.schema.json`, generated TS and pydantic types, samples in `fixtures/contracts/v1/damage-aware-config/`, integers only) is the reviewed assumption record, committed as `config/baselines/damage-aware.v1.json` (a new version is a new file, never an edit): the rule for every unrevealed opponent field (`item`: `none` or `fixed`; `ability` and `filler_move`: first or last in the team vocabulary's order; `nature`; a six-value `stat_points` spread), `hp_rounding` (`ceil`, `floor`, `nearest`), the `scoring` weights in ppm (`ko_weight_ppm`, `turn_order_discount_ppm`, `crit_weight_ppm`) and `epsilon_ppm` (0 for evaluation). `damage-aware-config.ts` reads it (`readDamageAwareConfig(value, vocabulary)`: the schema, then the format, the nature, a fixed item and the spread's sum against the pinned engine's `TeamVocabulary`; `damageAwareConfigHash` is the `baseline_config_hash` of the identity); `scripts/check-damage-aware-config.ts` (in `make ts` and CI) checks the committed file against the golden vocabulary, which `team-vocabulary.test.ts` holds equal to a fresh export. `damage-aware-assumed-set.ts` builds an `AssumedSet` from ONE opponent `PublicMon` of the shared reader, the vocabulary, the record and the engine: a revealed species (a forme the roster does not list falls back to its base species; a Mega Evolved Pokemon is its base forme holding the revealed stone, `mega: true`), level, gender, item, ability (only one the vocabulary lists for the species), and moves are used as revealed, the rest follows the record, and an unrevealed move is never invented: a `filler_move` is added only to a set with no placeable revealed move, listed in `filler_moves` and never a threat; a revealed move no legal set can hold (Struggle, Transform copies) is listed in `unplaceable_moves`. The set must pass the target format's `validateSet` and be the engine's canonical paste (else `AssumedSetError`). `assumedHp(percent, maxHp, rounding)` turns the shown percentage into hit points in integers (a shown percentage above 0 never becomes 0); the caller reads the maximum from the probe's `stats`. `test/damage-aware-assumed-set.test.ts` builds a set for every opponent Pokemon at every request of the committed random-corpus channels (both seats), checks each against the validator and runs the canary (nothing but revealed facts and the record's values; a negative control shows the canary flags a nature, spread, item or move taken from the real team).

**Progress-game replays** (`ml/areneon_ml/inspector/progress_replay*.py`, `packages/replay-view/web/`, ADR-017 §3, §4, §4b, §4c, #925). `python -m areneon_ml.loop replay --progress-dir P --generation G [--match NAME] [--game K] [--run-dir R] [--out D]` writes `<D>/progress-replays/g<NNNNNN>/<match>-<k>.html` and `progress-replays/index.html` (default `D` is `<run>.inspect` for `<run>.progress`, never inside the progress directory, nor inside the run (given by `--run-dir` or the `<run>` beside a `<run>.progress` root); `--run-dir` is optional and adds the lineage check and the run-config tie of the progress record; without `--progress-dir` the command is the training-game replay and needs `--run-dir`). It reads a committed progress generation (its `manifest.json` must match its files), `progress.json` through `read_progress_record` and each stored `ProgressGameRecord` through `read_game_record` (schema, a private data class is invalid, choices among candidates, team hashes, `omniscient_log_sha256`, the seed), then ties the two: the game is named for itself, `record_hash` is in the match's `game_record_hashes`, its outcome (by seat) equals the game row's, and a played game's log ends in the recorded winner and turn count. The page is the same graphical board and controls with one OMNISCIENT view: the engine's log has both sides' exact values and has no `|request|` lines, and the page says so at the top (both sides' hidden information; never policy input; local use only; do not share or commit it). The interpreter's `omniscient` option (`interpretChannel(player, lines, dex, true)`) makes p1 `own` and p2 `opponent`, builds both rosters from `|poke|` lines, accepts exact HP on both sides (`state.omniscient` is true, winner is `p1`/`p2`/`tie`), refuses a `|request|` and a `|split|` marker (the engine's omniscient stream already carries the secret line and no marker, so one is a defect: fail closed). The mode is the page generator's, not the data's: `replay_page.assets(mode)` writes `var AR_MODE = "player"` or `"omniscient"` as the first line of the hashed script, `boot` passes it to `init` as `options.omniscient`, and a data block that claims `omniscient` on a player-view page changes nothing (a test). Decisions are placed by the log's structure (`decision_points`: Team Preview after `|teampreview`, the k-th move decision at `|turn|k`, a forced switch at that seat's replacement `|switch|` after one of its faints, else at the end of its turn; a refused choice and its retry share a place); request numbers are shown, never used to place, because they count wait requests. The decision panel shows each seat's candidate list and chosen choice, policy id and any engine refusal; the page labels the candidate seat, mode and opponent (baseline or the previous pair). `inspect` keeps `progress-replays/` as it keeps `replays/`. Version 2 games (#951): `read_progress_games` brings the other game of a both-seats pair with a selected one (`--game 2` builds games 2 and 3 when both are stored), each `ProgressGame` knows its `pair` and `twin`, and the page links to the other game of its pair (a link made from the match id and game number only), says "Pair k, game 1 of 2", names the anchor generation with the `seen, same-lineage comparison` label and the ablation's Preview variant in the seat labels; the index lists a version 2 generation by pair (it reads only the generation's `schema_version` to decide, display only). A version 1 game's page and index are byte-identical to before. Known limits: an Illusion twin is flagged as in own channels; a forfeit's log is not checked against its outcome (the harness ends it, and the committed forfeit sample's log shows turn 4 against an outcome of 3 turns, so a bound on the log's last turn does not hold); the dashboard link is not built (there is no dashboard yet), only the inspector's index when `D/index.html` exists. Tests: `tests/ml/inspector/test_progress_replay.py` (a progress root made by the real progress code with the stand-in simulator side) and `packages/replay-view/test/omniscient.test.ts` (the interpreter on the engine's own omniscient logs of the committed random battles, cross-checked with the record's winner and turns and with each player's own channel).

**Damage-aware actor** (ADR-018 §1, §2, §4, §5, §6, slice 2, #944; `packages/showdown-bridge/src/damage-aware-actor.ts`, `-view.ts`, `-score.ts`, `-choice.ts`, `-identity.ts`). `damageAwareActor({engine, vocabulary, config, mechanics, snapshot?, data_class, commit?, seed?})` is an A1 `Actor` for ONE seat of ONE game (a second use of it, another seat or a shorter channel is a `DamageAwareError`); it needs `ActorContext.team` (a missing context or team is a `DamageAwareError`) and plays under `guardActor` with role `scripted`. At Team Preview it plays the `actor-rule` candidate. At a move request with at least two non-pending candidates it feeds the new channel lines to the shared reader (`createInterpreter`, incremental), joins each own active Pokemon of the request to its set of `ActorContext.team` (by the request's name; exact HP, status and held item from the request, boosts and Mega from the reader) and builds each opposing active's assumed set (`buildAssumedSet`, never the real team). Every candidate is the sum of its slot parts; a part is a damaging move's value per target (status moves, switches, `pass`, ally targets and a locked move without a target score 0; the request's target type picks single, every opposing active, or a random one averaged; any other type fails closed), each from ONE `damage` query (one attacker, one defender, one move, one declared field: weather, terrain, Trick Room and the four side conditions read from the reader) whose result is `damageValue` in integer ppm: the mean over the 16 no-crit rolls of `min(roll, hp) / hp` plus `ko_weight_ppm` times the share of rolls that reach the target's HP, mixed with the crit vector by the probe's exact chance times `crit_weight_ppm` (the probe gives no KO counts yet, so the share is counted from the rolls here). A spread move is the sum of its single-target values (no spread modifier and no ally damage in v1). The turn-order discount: when a foe's revealed damaging move can knock the actor out (some no-crit roll reaches its HP) one 1v1 `speed_order` query (the own Pokemon's action against that foe's first KO-capable revealed move; other actives do not enter the order) puts both actions in the order the engine gives, and a foe in a strictly earlier tie group discounts that (action, target) term, not the whole action, by `turn_order_discount_ppm`. The highest non-pending score wins, the first of equal scores in canonical order (so all-zero scores play the `actor-rule` candidate); forced switches, a lone candidate and pending-only sets are answered without scoring. A `unsupported` probe result scores 0 and is counted; a speed order the probe cannot queue (an action that queues more than a move and a Mega Evolution, as Focus Punch does) is counted as `unsupported` too and applies no discount (every other probe failure propagates); `refused` throws. A reader-flagged line among the lines fed since the previous request (per request, not sticky: a later request scores on a state that holds the flagged line verbatim, which is the reader's one known limit, an Illusion twin), an own Pokemon the reader does not show, or an `AssumedSetError` plays the `actor-rule` candidate and is counted (`reader_fallbacks`, `assumed_set_fallbacks`). `report()` gives `{policy_id: damage-aware@<commit> (v1 by default), identity, identity_hash, counts}`; the counts (`decisions`, `preview_decisions`, `scored_decisions`, `queries`, `probe_runs`, `unsupported_queries`, `reader_fallbacks`, `assumed_set_fallbacks`, `epsilon_plays`) are integers per game, and the ProgressGameRecord fields that store them are slice 4. A per-game memo of probe results by query hash saves repeated queries (a pure function of the query, so no answer changes; `probe_runs` counts the probes actually run). `damageAwareIdentity` / `damageAwareIdentityHash` is the baseline identity of ADR-018 §5: SHA-256 of the JCS of `{baseline_config_hash, mechanics_config_hash, adapter_format, reader_sha256, version_manifest}`. `epsilon_ppm` above 0 needs the seat's baseline seed and draws from the `random-legal` Mulberry32 stream (`randomBelow(1,000,000)`, a small export added to `random-legal.ts`, once per move or forced-switch request, then `randomLegal` when the draw falls under it); with 0 nothing is ever drawn (a spy test). The actor's choice is a pure function of the pin, both configs, the reader, the own channel, the own team and the candidates: `test/damage-aware-invariance.test.ts` plays seeded battle pairs against a first-legal opponent whose team differs in unrevealed fields (nature, Stat Points, rotated items, moves 2 to 4) and requires equal choices at every request with an equal player channel, shows the test has teeth with a baseline given the real team (`assume` option, different choices), repeats it for a changed battle seed and a changed actor seed, and checks two runs equal in choices, counts and identity. The test seams are `probe`, `random` and `assume` options (the defaults are the real probe, the real stream and `buildAssumedSet`). **Measured cost** (`scripts/damage-aware-budget.ts`, the random-play corpus teams against `random-legal`, baseline seat alternating, real probe; one shared machine, Intel i7-14700KF, load average 9 to 18 from another run, so the figures are pessimistic and are not claimed to repeat): two runs of 30 games each (run 2 also gives the extrapolation below; per game it measured a mean match of 6.2 minutes per 200 games and 10.5 at the 95th percentile game, run 1 5.9 and 9.6) gave per decision a median of 169 and 174 ms, a mean of 168 and 167 ms, a 95th percentile of 359 and 349 ms and a worst of 518 and 505 ms (302 and 318 decisions, all well under the run's 30 s battle budget), and per game a mean of 1.78 and 1.87 s (11 to 12 decisions a game: random-legal opponents lose early), about 13 probe queries a decision, about 10% of them `unsupported`. ADR-018 assumes about 25 baseline decisions per game against a learned policy: at the measured mean decision that is 14 minutes for a 200-game match, and 29 minutes if every decision cost the 95th percentile, against the proposed 30 minutes (so the budget holds with little margin in the pessimistic reading; the owner confirms or changes it).

**Targeted fidelity corpus** (ADR-026 Evidence 1, finding N-A, slice T, #1137; `packages/showdown-bridge/src/search-fidelity-corpus.ts`, `scripts/capture-search-fidelity-corpus.ts`, `fixtures/showdown/<format>/search-fidelity/`). Seeded A1 battles (`runBattle`, both seats, `random-legal` or `pending-first` per seat, the random corpus's 30-turn cut) that reach the rare states a search world must reproduce, so each world writer and admitted id can meet its exercised-turn gate. The targets are the reviewed input `targets.json` (no id is a constant in code): per target the moves and abilities a team must hold to be drawn for it (as the reducer sweep's `--only-move`), the regular expressions over the PUBLIC channel lines that count as one occurrence (a turn counts once; lines before `|turn|1` are turn 0; `consecutive` counts a turn only when the same actor, the pattern's first group, matched on the turn before, for Protect chains) and `min_turns`; `unreachable` lists what no legal team can hold and `checkUnreachable` fails when the roster can (a pin bump that adds Download must make it a target). Targets (each `min_turns` 24, since slice F admits a writer or id after 20 exercising turns): Fake Out, Protect chains, Encore, Disable, Last Respects, Counter, Mirror Coat, weather and its expiry, screens and their expiry, Trick Room and Tailwind with their expiry, Toxic (status and ticks), Intimidate; Download is unreachable at this pin. The search (`captureFidelityCorpus`, pure in the pool, targets and `seed_base`) has two phases: it plays `attempts_per_target` battles per target (the target's team on a seat that alternates, any team opposite, the four actor mixes in turn, seeds from `seed_base`) and pools those in which the target occurs, then keeps greedily the battle with the highest gain per turn played (turns added to every target still short, capped at the shortfall) until each target has its `min_turns` or `max_battles` is reached; a target left short is logged, never lowered. Teams: the committed fixtures-v1 dev split plus a generated dev split (`--dataset DIR`; the committed one holds no Last Respects or Mirror Coat team); only the teams of kept battles are committed, each file naming its team `origin` (dataset id, canonical team hash), and the README records the generate command and the public keys. A battle file is a random-corpus file plus `actors` and `origin`; `loadRandomCorpus(FIDELITY_DIR)` reads it. `summary.json` is the coverage summary, a pure function of the committed channels and targets (`fidelitySummary`: per item turns, battles and the turns per battle, plus the total battle turns, the denominators slice F counts against); the replay test (`test/search-fidelity-corpus.test.ts`, explicit 120 s budget) re-plays every battle from its seed, teams and actor seeds, recomputes the summary and requires every `min_turns`. Occurrence is counted on player 1's channel only (public lines). Not here: the fidelity comparison itself (slice F). **Extension for the oracle's yield (#1187).** Slice F's report counts EXERCISING AND EQUAL turns (F1 and F2), which a target's occurrence count only approximates (toxic: 40 occurrence turns gave 26 exercising and 17/16 equal), so the corpus was extended and each added target names the report row it feeds. `targets.json` gained, per target, the optional `items` (a held item draws a team: White Herb), `against` (the opposite team must hold a move, ability or item: White Herb needs an opposing Intimidate user for the scenario start to consume it), `attempts` (phase-1 battles for a rare state) and `report {item, min_equal}` (the report row and the number of EQUAL exercising turns it needs in F1 and in F2); `min_turns` of those targets is an occurrence floor calibrated against the report (toxic 60, Throat Chop 80, Yawn 100, Torment 55, Flash Fire 12, Focus Energy 40, White Herb 55), and `fidelity.test.ts` holds the committed report to every `report.min_equal` (24) with none blamed or missed. `captureFidelityCorpus(..., {existing})` / `--extend` keeps the committed battles (ids and bytes unchanged), counts them towards every target, plays phase 1 only for targets still short and numbers the new battles on; its seeds start at `0x7000 + 0x1000 * committed battles`, so an extension never repeats an earlier seed, and a rerun of `--extend` on the extended corpus adds nothing. The corpus is now 200 battles (77 original plus 123 added), 2,770 battle turns, 5,528 own move decisions, about 21 MB (it was 9 MB); the greedy stopped at `max_battles` 200 and overshoots the original targets (weather 625 turns, Intimidate 150), so it is not minimal: it is the first extension that met the yield, with the teams of the same generated dev split (regenerated at a later commit, its 145 original origin teams are all present). **Admissions (report of the extended corpus, F1 equal / F2 equal):** `toxic_stage` 31/28 (class W again), start trigger `whiteherb` 49/32, volatiles `encore` 41/37, `taunt` 37/42, `throatchop` 38/28, `yawn` 30/24, `torment` 45/47 (E), `flashfire` 28/24 (E), `focusenergy` 38/47 (E); `disable` still fails (blamed 4/11).

**Choice location of a foe, and `damage-aware@v3`** (#1180). The pinned engine's `Pokemon.getAtLoc(L)` returns `foeSide.active[L - 1]`: in a choice `move N L`, location 1 is the foe in active slot 0 (`p2a`, the reader's slot `a`) and 2 the foe in slot 1. `damage-aware-view.ts` `foesOf` numbered them the other way (`loc: letters.length - position`), so `damage-aware@v1` and `@v2` score a single-target move part against the foe the engine will NOT hit (spread and random-target moves use every foe and are unaffected). **Known limitation of v1 and v2, kept bug-for-bug** (their records, identities, choices and every past result, `teacher-h1-001` and #1131's head to head included, stand as they were; the view's `engine_targets` option is absent for them). The convention is held to the engine by `test/damage-aware-target-convention.test.ts`: a scenario battle on the pinned engine (`getAtLoc` and a played `move 1 2` that damages the slot-b foe), `foesOf` under both numberings, a feature row keyed `target: L` against the foe in slot L - 1, and v3 against v1 on seeded requests. **Fixed:** the `probe-damage/1` feature builder always uses the engine's numbering (`engine_targets: true`; nothing persisted used the rows, so the feature set id and preimage stay). **New baseline version:** `config/baselines/damage-aware.v3.json` is v1's values plus `target_convention: "engine"`, a new optional field of the `DamageAwareConfig` v1 schema (a minor change within v1: absent keeps v1's bytes and `baseline_config_hash`; an older reader refuses such a record; a new schema version was not needed because every other shape is v1's). A record that says `engine` plays as `damage-aware@v3` (the actor ties the policy id to the record: a v3 record cannot play as `v1`, nor a v1 record as `v3`), has its own `baseline_config_hash` and identity, and `check-damage-aware-config.ts` requires it to equal v1 in every value but the field and v1 to lack the field. `damageAwareSetup(engine, format, "v3")` and the scripted head-to-head (`--arm-a damage-aware@v3`, `SCRIPTED_POLICY_IDS`, `ARMS`) take it; `damage-aware@v2` still has the legacy numbering (a v2 with the engine's numbering would be a new belief record version: not built). **Bench `rows_digest`** (`feature-bench.ts`, run `public-main-011` g50 with `--progress-dir public-main-011.progress-da --progress-games 20 --pools ""`, 394 Episode and 406 progress decisions): `35a2b2b5...` / `be281958...` before the fix and `d1bdd98e...` / `ffb3a087...` after (the earlier digests in the one-clone paragraph were of another sample and are history, as is any earlier statement that rows are keyed by the reversed numbering). **Scripted head to head, v3 against v1** (#1130 harness, `exp-1180-v3-v1-set1`, 100 pairs (200 games) both seats on the evaluation-only dataset `eval-1131-set1`, 4 game processes, descriptive and not the Training gate): v3 won 154 games and v1 46 (no ties, no forfeits), pair score 770,000 ppm for v3 with a 95% pair-level bootstrap interval of 715,000 to 825,000 ppm (pair scores: 3 pairs lost both games, 40 split, 57 won both); v3 identity `fcbce7d7...`, v1 `602ddf74...`. The baseline's choices under the engine's numbering are better than under the reversed one on these teams; this is a baseline comparison on one dataset, nothing more.

**Candidate feature builder** (ADR-022 tier A, slice D1 builder part, #1026; `packages/showdown-bridge/src/candidate-features.ts`, `damage-aware-view.ts`). `candidateFeatures(engine, configs, channel, team, candidates, memo?)` is a pure function of the player's own `PlayerChannel` (the channel must end in the request being answered, read from its last `|request|` line), the player's own team as brought, the candidate strings and the configs `feature_set_hash` names. It feeds the channel to the shared reader, builds each opposing active's assumed set exactly as the v1 baseline does (the shared `damage-aware-view.ts`: `buildAssumedSet`, the public-status ability rule, `assumedHp`; the belief-based sets are ADR-025 slice 5, #1135, and would be a new feature set id) and asks the probe ONE `damage` query per distinct row `(slot, move position, target position, Mega flag)` that some candidate (pending ones included) uses with a damaging move into an opposing active; a spread or random-target move gets one row per foe it can hit, each a single-defender query; a status move, a switch, a pass and an ally or self target have no row. A request with no move to score (Team Preview, forced switch, wait) has no rows. Rows are returned in canonical order (slot, move, target, Mega false first), so bytes depend neither on candidate order nor on the memo. A row is `computed` with `mean_ppm` (the mean over the 16 no-crit rolls of `min(roll, hp) / hp`, floored, the baseline's `rollsValue` with no KO weight), `max_ppm`, `min_ppm` (the largest and smallest roll, clamped at `hp`, as a share of `hp`) and `ohko_rolls` (the probe's own `ko.ohko_no_crit` count of the 16 rolls, or null when the probe's `ko` is `unsupported`; ADR-016 slice 5 exists, so the row has it from the first feature set), or `unsupported` with the probe's closed reason, never a guess. `hp` is the shown percentage applied to the assumed maximum HP with the record's `hp_rounding`. `featureSetIdentity` / `featureSetHash`: SHA-256 of the JCS of `{feature_set: "probe-damage/1", mechanics_config_hash, assumed_set_config_hash, adapter_format, reader_sha256, version_manifest}`; a change of any part (or of what a row holds) is a new feature set. The per-game memo is `createFeatureMemo()` (`results` keyed by the probe query's hash, the defender's HP stays in the query so it stays in the key; `counts` has `queries`, `probe_runs` and `unsupported_queries`, where `queries` includes the maximum-HP and status-ability queries the assumed sets need). A request that cannot be featured throws `FeatureBuildError` with a code (`no_request`, `reader_flagged` (a line the reader could not take since the previous request), `reader_gap`, `assumed_set`, `probe_refused`) and a fixed message; the caller (D2's `policyActor`) decides what then, and a defect (a shape the baseline does not know) propagates as `DamageAwareError`. The type rule: the channel parameter is the branded `PlayerChannel`, so an `OmniscientLog`, `HiddenVerdicts`, `BattleRecord`, a seed or an unchecked log is a compile error (a type test), and a source scan requires no runner, omniscient or seed import. The shared module `damage-aware-view.ts` holds what the baseline and the builder both read a request with (own combatants, foes, declared field, the memoised probe call, `DamageAwareError`); `damage-aware-actor.ts` uses it and is unchanged in behaviour (its tests pass unmodified; `foesHit` takes the foes as a thunk so a move that hits no foe builds none and asks the probe nothing, as before). Measured on four recorded random-play seats (two seeded battles, both seats, real probe, one shared machine): 15 to 19 decisions each, 106 to 148 rows, memo hit rate on the probe queries 19 to 21 percent (random teams repeat few queries; a longer game with stable matchups would hit more), 65 to 105 ms per decision. Invariance (#1029, `test/candidate-features-invariance.test.ts`, real probe): seeded battle pairs whose p2 teams differ in nature, Stat Points, items, moves 2 to 4 and abilities give byte-identical `CandidateFeatures` (rows and `feature_set_hash`) at every p1 request with an equal channel (at least 20 points over at least 2 pairs); a revealed ability is used as revealed; a builder given the real opponent team through `FeatureConfigs.assume` (a test seam) gives different rows (negative control); a canary scan of never-public names in the JCS bytes; a type test. Not here: latency and the worker-thread pool, the `PolicyQuery` / `TrainingExample` field, the encoder (`probe-damage/2`, incoming threats and turn order, is the next paragraph).

**Feature builder `probe-damage/2`** (ADR-022 erratum E-22.3, #1172; `packages/showdown-bridge/src/candidate-features-2.ts`; the contract fields are #1174 (next paragraph), the invariance extension #1173, the encoder #1175, none of them here). `candidateFeatures2(engine, configs, channel, team, candidates, memo?, trace?)` builds `probe-damage/1`'s `rows` with the very code `candidateFeatures` runs (`damageRows`, exported from `candidate-features.ts`, which `/1` calls unchanged: the two sets give equal `rows` bytes, a test) and four more arrays, all on ADR-018's v1 assumed opponent sets and the shared reader: relations and values under the assumed foe (revealed item, ability, stages, status and Mega forme as revealed; everything else from `damage-aware.v1.json`), never facts about the real opponent, and the docs and labels say so. `feature_set_hash` is `/1`'s preimage with the id `probe-damage/2` (`featureSetIdentity(engine, configs, FEATURE_SET_2)`, which refuses a mechanics config that does not list `speed_order`). **Own Pokemon** (position = index into the request's `side.pokemon`): the living actives of a move request (a fainted one has no row), plus every bench Pokemon some `switch N` candidate names (a forced switch has only these); an active slot where a candidate Mega Evolves also has the own-Mega variant (the Pokemon declared Mega Evolved). A bench Pokemon is read from the request and own team alone (`damageView.ownBench`: stages 0, Mega as the reader's roster shows it, so a Pokemon never in play is no `reader_gap`). **`threats`** (own, mega, foe, move): one single-defender `damage` query per foe revealed move (`AssumedSet.revealed_moves`: damaging, target type reaching an opposing Pokemon by `foeTargetKind`, so single, spread and random targets; a foe with no revealed damaging move has none) into each own Pokemon at its exact HP from its own request; the row values are `/1`'s (`damageValues`: `mean_ppm`, `max_ppm`, `min_ppm`, `ohko_rolls`, or the probe's reason). **`speeds`** (own, mega, foe): exactly one row per own position, own Mega flag and living foe; `relation` `own_first` / `tie` / `foe_first` from the tie groups of one `speed_order` query per group of at most two own positions (a Mega variant alone, declared Mega Evolved), every combatant declared with a switch action so the engine orders them by action speed alone (Trick Room, Tailwind, paralysis, stages, a held Scarf, a Mega's new Speed); a query the probe does not answer is asked again for each own Pokemon alone. **`own_priorities`** (slot, move position, Mega flag) and **`foe_priorities`** (foe, move id): the engine's `priority` key of each own move part of the candidates (status moves, pending candidates and the Mega variant included) and each revealed foe move, from batched `speed_order` queries (round k asks the k-th move of every combatant; the Mega variant asks its Pokemon alone with the action Mega Evolving); a batch that is not `computed` is re-asked one action at a time, so only the offending row is `unsupported` and no row depends on how the moves were batched (a test). The probe resolves each action against a fixed foe target, so a target-dependent priority event is not modelled (as ADR-018). `unsupported` reasons are the probe's closed ones plus `unqueueable` and `fractional_priority`, which `damageView.queryLimited` now names (the memo stores `{limit}` where it stored `null`; `ask` still returns `null` for the baseline, whose behaviour and counts are unchanged). **Decision where E-22.3 is silent:** a pseudo-move the request names that the engine's data lacks (`recharge`, the forced turn after a recharge move) cannot be queued: its `own_priorities` row is `unsupported` with `query_kind` and nobody is asked about it (without this, 15 of 794 sampled decisions were refused as `probe_refused`); the reader rule "every own move part is covered" still holds. Sorted keys: threats by (own, mega false first, foe, move id), speeds by (own, mega, foe), own priorities by (slot, move, mega), foe priorities by (foe, move id); ids compare as code units. Requests: Team Preview and wait give empty arrays; a forced switch gives `threats` and `speeds` for the Pokemon its candidates name and nothing else. The five `FeatureBuildError` codes are unchanged (`refusing` maps the baseline's signals; a speed-order query the engine refuses for a state the builder declared is `probe_refused`). **Known limit, found by the bench:** `mechanics-speed.ts` `declare` sets the terrain before the combatants' statuses, so a burn or other status seen before a Misty Terrain began is refused by the engine ("the engine refused the declared status") in a speed-order query while the damage queries (which declare in another order) accept it: 4 of 794 sampled decisions (0.5%) are `probe_refused` for that reason on `/2`, none on `/1`. Fixing the order is a probe change (a new `MECHANICS_ADAPTER_FORMAT`, hence a new `feature_set_hash`) and so a decision for the contract slice, not made here. The cost split is a measurement only: `candidateFeatures2`'s optional `trace` (`CostTrace`: queries, probe runs and ms for `setup` (the foes' assumed sets), `rows`, `threats`, `speeds`, `priorities`) never changes a byte; `FeatureServiceSpec.feature_set` (default `probe-damage/1`) selects the builder in the service, the pool and `scripts/feature-bench.ts --feature-set probe-damage/2`, and `FeatureResult.parts` / `RunStats.arrays` / `RunStats.parts` carry it (integers and milliseconds, no names). Tests: `test/candidate-features-2.test.ts` (rows equal `/1`'s; one speed row per key; relations from a controlled order and, on the real probe, every speed row equal to a move-against-move `speed_order` query of equal priority under Trick Room, Tailwind, paralysis, a Mega and an exact tie; priority rows equal the probe's keys for Protect, a terrain-dependent move, a quick move and a Prankster status move; only the offending row `unsupported` when a batch fails, with each of the three reasons; threat rows equal direct `probeMechanics` damage queries at the own Pokemon's exact HP and status, with single, spread and random targets, a status-only foe, a Mega variant, a forced switch and a bench switch-in; determinism with and without the memo, in shuffled candidate order and for any batching; the hash changes with each of its six inputs). **Measured cost against the `/2` gate (E-22.3.4)**, `scripts/feature-bench.ts` on generation 50 of the control run `public-main-011` (80 evenly spaced Episodes, 794 move decisions, and 12 stored progress games, 222 decisions), one thread, pin `7244fe8e`, Intel i7-14700KF (28 logical CPUs) shared with a running training (load average 5 to 9 at the start, so pessimistic), the damage path one-clone (14,376 of 14,403 damage runs; 27 fell back): per decision `/2` median 43.2 ms, mean 45.9, p95 82.8, p99 101.6, max 292.9 (progress games: median 56.6, p95 103.7, p99 157.8) against `/1` on the same decisions median 22.2, mean 23.0, p95 42.9, p99 59.8; 24.6 probe queries per decision against 11.5, split `setup` 2.0, `rows` 9.6, `threats` 6.2, `speeds` 1.8, `priorities` 5.1 (time: rows 17.5 s, threats 11.1 s, priorities 3.7 s, speeds 1.6 s, setup 1.2 s over the sample); memo hit rate 8.2% (`/1` 10.2%: the new queries repeat little across turns on random-play-like games, the expectation of E-22.3.4 that threats repeat more is not borne out); rows per decision 9.6 plus, per array, threats 6.2, speeds 5.8, own priorities 7.6 and foe priorities 2.7, with `unsupported` shares of 10.8% (all `query_kind`: fixed-damage moves), 2.9% (`random_effect`), 5.6% (mostly `random_effect`) and 3.9%. **Verdict: the p99 gate (at most 500 ms per decision on one thread) is MET; the generation gate (at most 1.5 times the control's play plus load, builder counted twice, #1185's per-process recomputation merged) is NOT MET on the estimate** below 16 ideal threads: control play 184.2 s plus load 16.0 s, 15,484 decisions, builder CPU 36 ms per seat decision times 2 times 15,484 = 1,116 s, giving 657% at 1 thread, 239% at 4 (the four game processes of today's runs), 170% at 8 and 135% at 16 (`/1` on the same sample: 379%, 170%, 135%, 117%); the pool measured 2.09 times at 2 workers and 2.48 times at 4 on the loaded machine, which on the same arithmetic gives 367% and 325% (`/1`: 278% and 250%). The one-clone path is already in these numbers, so the next mitigation is the play-time sidecar (ADR-022 section 4: read by the loader instead of recomputing), which by the same arithmetic halves the builder's CPU (558 s) and gives 170% at 4 threads and 135% at 8; below that a new decision is needed (E-22.3.4), for example a feature set without bench targets. The estimate assumes otherwise idle threads and no overlap of the builder with play; it is not a run. **Invariance, negative control and canary (#1173, ADR-022 erratum E-22.3.5; `test/candidate-features-2-invariance.test.ts`, test only, no production change):** over 19 seeded battle pairs of the random-play corpus (both battles of a pair built with the real probe, p2 playing `firstLegal`), 145 request points in 16 of them where the two player channels are equal (measured at this pin: 144 featured and 1 refused with the same `reader_flagged` in both battles, which the test requires; move requests and 15 forced switches; 128 after a move reveal, 49 after an item or ability reveal, 8 with Trick Room or Tailwind up because the leads play them in both battles), `rows`, `threats`, `speeds`, `own_priorities`, `foe_priorities` and `feature_set_hash` are byte-identical although the opponent holds an unrevealed Choice Scarf and Quick Claw, a Speed nature and Stat Points moved to or off Speed, the roster's other ability (a priority-changing one, picked by the engine data's `onModifyPriority`, first where the roster has one: Prankster on Sableye, Klefki and Whimsicott), and unrevealed priority, priority status and damaging moves 2 to 4. Explicit assertions: a threat or foe-priority row exists only for a move the reader shows as used, a foe with no revealed damaging move has no threat row and a foe with no revealed move has no priority row, and the probe is declared the revealed ability, item and move. Negative control: a builder handed the real opponent team through the `assume` seam differs on `threats`, `speeds` and `foe_priorities` (each asserted separately). Canary: no unrevealed move, item or nature of the real team appears as a string token or substring in the JCS bytes of any array or as a foe move id (negative controls: the scan flags a planted marker, and the real-team builder does put unrevealed moves into the foe rows). A guard test requires that each kind of difference (Scarf, Quick Claw, nature, Speed Stat Points, a priority-changing ability, priority, priority-status and damaging moves) is really applied in some pair, so a legality fallback cannot drop one silently. The `/1` type test is repeated for `candidateFeatures2` (`@ts-expect-error` per forbidden argument).

**`probe-damage/2` on the wire** (ADR-022 erratum E-22.3.2, #1174; stacked on the builder above and the `/1` transport). The closed `candidate_features` object of `PolicyQuery` and `TrainingExample` gains the optional arrays `threats`, `speeds`, `own_priorities` and `foe_priorities` (a minor change within v1; integers only; each row `kind`-tagged `computed` or `unsupported`, `relation` a closed enum, `priority` -9999..9999 as the probe's `ActionOrder.priority`, move ids `^[a-z0-9]+$`; the `unsupported` reasons are the probe's three for `threats` and those plus `unqueueable` and `fractional_priority` for the order arrays). The same schema defs sit in both contracts; `feature_set` stays a free string, so the **presence rule is a reader rule** (as #1027 left it): the four arrays are present exactly when `feature_set` is `probe-damage/2` and absent for every other set. Every `/1` value, sample and digest is unchanged (only the handshake samples moved, as their `PolicyQuery` schema hash did). Reader rules in both languages (`feature-rows.ts` `featureRowsProblem` / `featureCoverageProblem`, `worker/features.py` `rows_problem` / `coverage_problem`, `areneon_contracts.training._check_features`): presence, and each array strictly ascending by the builder's key; in a query (`coverage_problem`, so `invalid_query` at the worker): `threats` name only implied (position, Mega flag) pairs, `speeds` hold EXACTLY one row per (own position, own Mega flag, foe location) for each implied own Pokemon (a living active; a living bench Pokemon some `switch N` candidate names; the Mega variant of an active slot where a candidate Mega Evolves) against every foe location any array shows, `own_priorities` hold exactly one row per own move part of the candidates (the pseudo-move `recharge` is an `unsupported` `query_kind` row) and none in a forced switch or Team Preview, `foe_priorities` none without a move request, and a switch to a Pokemon the request does not list is refused. Completeness of `threats` against the revealed moves needs the state and is held by the equality test only. **Dispatch:** `feature-build.ts` (`buildFeatures`, `featureSetOf`, `configuredSetHash`, `isFeatureSetId`) is the one place a set id picks the builder; `FeatureConfigs.feature_set` (optional, default `probe-damage/1`) names a run's set for `loadTrainingSet` / `writeFeatureRows` / `readFeatureRows` / `featureSetup`, and `policyActor` builds the set its worker STATED (the hash check against the built object is unchanged). **Encoder untouched (#1175):** `FEATURE_SETS` in `model/options.py` is still `/1` only and the run-config / clone-run-config enums still refuse `/2`, so no real checkpoint states `/2` yet and the Python encoder refuses `/2` rows; the worker reader accepts them for a worker given a `/2` statement (tests give it directly). Tests: `test/candidate-features-2-transport.test.ts` (reader rules, handshake refusals, and the equality test: a seeded A1 game through `policyGame` against stand-in workers that state `/2` with the real probe, the whole object in each `PolicyQuery` equal byte for byte to what `loadTrainingSet` recomputes, valid examples that cover their candidates, and `writeFeatureRows` / `readFeatureRows` round trip), `tests/ml/test_worker_features.py`, and the samples `battle-features-2`, `preview-features-2`, `ready-features-2` (valid), `features-2-*` (invalid: unknown relation or reason, a limit reason on `threats`, float, out-of-range priority, extra field, bad move id; reader-invalid: array under `/1`, missing array, unsorted or duplicate rows, a missing speed or own-priority row, a foreign own position). Not here: the cost measurement against the 1.5x generation gate (a real short generation with `/2`, after #1175).

**Feature builder pool, bench and step-2 trigger measurement** (ADR-022 section 4 mitigation 3 and section 5, #1030; `packages/showdown-bridge/src/feature-service.ts`, `feature-pool.ts`, `feature-bench.ts`, `scripts/feature-bench.ts`). `createFeatureService(spec)` is `candidateFeatures` behind a task interface (`FeatureTask`: game key, player, the own channel lines up to the request, own team, candidates, optional `final`): it loads the pinned engine and the reviewed records once, keeps one probe memo per game (dropped after a `final` task) and returns the outcome (`features`, or the `FeatureBuildError` code), the build time in ms and the probe counts the task added. `FeaturePool(size, spec)` runs one service per Node worker thread (a `data:` worker module that imports `feature-service.ts` and only wires messages to `answerMessage`, as the private shard decode does, so no worker script sits outside the coverage gate); `run(tasks)` returns the results in task order whatever order the threads finish in, so the rows are `candidateFeatures`' bytes and never depend on scheduling or size (size 0 runs on the calling thread; a test requires sizes 0, 1 and 3 and a second batch to give identical rows). A game goes to the thread that saw it first (least loaded), so its memo serves its later decisions; a defect in a thread is a `FeaturePoolError`, never a retry. No play or stream-writing process calls the pool yet: it has no consumer until the contract field lands. The bench (`node packages/showdown-bridge/scripts/feature-bench.ts --run-dir R --generation G [--episodes N] [--progress-dir P --progress-games N] [--pools 4,8] [--out FILE]`) is read-only on run directories and prints counts and milliseconds only (no species, move, team or line), with the machine model, logical CPUs, load average and the engine pin: per-decision single-thread latency (median, mean, p95, p99, max) over the move requests of an evenly spaced sample of the generation's Episodes (candidates by `enumerateCandidates` on the request, as `policyActor` makes them; Team Preview and forced switches have no rows and are counted apart), rows per decision, memo hit rate on the probe queries, the `unsupported` rows by reason and errors by code, then the same decisions through each pool size (wall time, decisions per second, rows equal to the single-thread run's). With `--progress-dir` the stored progress games are replayed through the runner from seed, teams and recorded choices (`progressDecisions`; the omniscient log is never read, each seat's channel is the replay's). The generation estimate (`estimateWallTime`) adds the builder's cost twice (at play and again at stream writing; sample mean per seat decision times the control's `counts.decisions`) divided over T threads to the control generation's play plus load from its run-metrics record and compares it with 1.5 times that; it assumes otherwise idle threads and no overlap with play, so it is an estimate, not a run. The probe-dominated rate (EXP-DF2's secondary measure, "computed on assumed sets", `addDominated`): per slot of a recorded choice that plays a move with a computed row, the chosen move's `mean_ppm` (the chosen target's row; any foe's for a spread move; the chosen Mega flag) against the largest computed `mean_ppm` of any row of the same slot; dominated when twice the chosen is below the best; switches, passes, status moves and unsupported rows are counted apart, never in the rate. The bench reads synthetic Episodes and progress games only (it refuses another data class); `max_turns` for the replay comes from the run config; the pool warms every thread before timing and reports the measured speed-up beside the ideal estimate. A caller that omits `final` keeps a game's memo for the pool's lifetime. Tests: `test/feature-pool.test.ts`, `test/feature-bench.test.ts`.

**Scenario world builder** (ADR-026 Evidence 1, W3, slice W parts 1 and 2, `packages/showdown-bridge/src/world-builder.ts` with `world-state.ts`, `world-checks.ts`, `world-redraw.ts`, `world-refusal.ts`, `world-engine.ts`, #1138 and #1140). `createWorldBuilder({engine, format_id, scenario_id, hp_rounding, writers}).build(input)` starts a scenario battle (ADR-016: `checkScenario`'s format, started at once, no Team Preview) with the four brought Pokemon a side, built from the player's own view and a FULL opponent team: `WorldInput` is `{request` (the own move request), `team` (`ActorContext.team`), `state` (the shared reader's `PublicState`), `flags` (the reader's flagged lines; any one refuses, `reader_flagged`, unless every one is a line kind of the record's `unavailable_mechanics` row), `facts` (the world facts of the player's own channel at this request, `world-facts/2`; null when that module refused: `facts_unavailable`; another turn than the state's: `facts_mismatch`), `opponent` (`{members: [{roster_index, set}]}`, its own input type: each member names the reader's roster entry it is, so slice B maps ADR-025's `OpponentWorld` onto it; the members are exactly `teamsize` of them, cover every roster entry seen in play, and may include unseen ones)}`. It takes no live battle, `BattleRecord`, omniscient log, hidden verdict, channel or seed (a type test). The own side is placed in the request's order on the player's seat (the request's `condition`, `details` gender and `stats` give HP, status, gender and a stats cross-check against the engine), the opponent side as the actives in their slots, then the bench in the reader's order; the reader empties a slot when its Pokemon faints, so a fainted member fills an empty slot. A set with no gender takes the shown one, a shown nickname replaces the set's. **E items** (part 1), through the engine's own methods and absolute (a switch-in's weather, terrain and stages are replaced): the permanent forme (`formeChange(..., true)`) and the temporary one and Mega Evolution (`runMegaEvo`), then `faint` and `faintMessages` (the engine puts a fainted Pokemon back to its set's species, so an own fainted Pokemon gets its shown forme again: its request shows it), then status (`setStatus(..., ignoreImmunities)`), HP (`sethp`; opponent HP is `assumedHp` with the world's maximum), the seven stages, own PP (`deductPP` to the request, a fainted Pokemon's too), weather, terrain, field conditions (`addPseudoWeather`) and side conditions with their layers (`addSideCondition` once per layer), each condition's source being the Pokemon the facts name (`FactsSource`, by shown nickname; one the log does not name is the side's first Pokemon and `defaulted_sources` counts it). Whether an id is a weather, terrain, status or condition is the engine's `conditions.get(id).effectType`. **Classes (part 2).** Every state item is classified by the reviewed record `config/search/world-writers.v1.json` (the `WorldWriters` contract, below): `classify` (`world-state.ts`) reads the request, the reader's state, the flags and the facts, never a hidden value, and each item present is written (W), counted (X) or refused (R). **W writers**, each fed by the facts and each equal to the live engine's value in the live test: `toxic_stage` (R in the committed record after slice F found too few equal oracle turns, W again since #1187; the writer is live-tested: `statusState.stage` = the facts' ticks; the engine resets it at a switch-in, so a benched one is never read), `bench_status` (the engine's `setStatus` refuses a Pokemon out of play: the writer sets `status` and `statusState` and runs the engine's own `Start` handler), `active_turns` (`activeTurns` and `activeMoveActions`), `protect_counter` (the engine's stall condition added once per consecutive use, its own restart logic sets the counter, one turn of it left), `last_move` (`lastMove`, `lastMoveUsed`), `choice_lock` (the choice condition added with `activeMove` set to the last move when the world's item `isChoice`; the engine's disable event then locks the other moves, V2 compares the own request), `total_fainted` and `fainted_last_turn` (`faintedThisTurn` set so the turn rollover leaves the log's value), `condition_duration` (the engine's own length, which the source's item sets through its callback, less the turns since the log started it, one fewer when it began `after_residual`; never a fresh full duration), `volatile_duration` (the same for an admitted volatile, which is added through the engine's `addVolatile` with a pending move in the queue exactly when the facts say the target had not moved, because Taunt's start handler reads the queue), `item_removed` (a consumed or knocked-off item), `start_trigger_restore` (an item or ability that a scenario-start trigger changed goes back to the set's, only for a trigger id the record admits) and `turn_number` (`battle.turn` is set one below and the engine's own `endTurn` runs: it is the T class, resetting `moveThisTurn`, `hurtThisTurn`, `usedItemThisTurn` and the like, and runs the lock, disable and trap events that make the request). **X, counted in `resampled`:** `sleep_counter` and `confusion_duration` (the engine's own draw at the world's fixed scenario seed), `opponent_pp` (full PP, counted when the facts show a move used more than 4 times) and `bench_order` (two or more unseen living members on the opponent's bench: counted per world with a hidden bench, not only on turns with a random-switch effect, because no effect list is in code; the per-turn count is the search's, with a reviewed list of those effects); an admitted volatile of class X is added through the engine and not counted separately. **R, typed and counted** as `state_unwritten` with the item and class: volatiles named by `slot_conditions`, `locked_moves` (also a last move in that row and an own request that lists fewer moves than the set holds), `truant_slow_start` (an ability in play), `substitute`, `illusion_transform` (a phantom entry, a hiding ability on an own Pokemon in play or on any living opponent member), `unavailable_mechanics`, an item or ability that was replaced or changed (`item_replaced`, `ability_changed`: Trick, Skill Swap, Trace) and every other id (`unadmitted_id`; the committed record admits `encore` and `taunt`, the ids the fidelity oracle of slice F supports). **Checks.** V1: each set passes the target format's `validateSet`, is the engine's canonical paste, and each opponent set keeps every revealed fact and Item Clause; V2 (`world-checks.ts`): after the turn rollover the world's own request (`activeRequest`, no `rqid`, no side name) equals the real one field for field, and `enumerateCandidates` gives the same list with the same pending flags; V3: the opponent as the engine renders it for the player equals the reader's state; V4: weather, terrain, field and side conditions with their layers equal the reader's and every remaining duration the writers computed is at least 1 (a condition the log still shows has not run out); V5: the request's seat is the state's and nicknames are the shown ones. **Refusals** (`WorldRefusal`) are typed and counted, carry fixed words, the `check` (V1 to V5 or null), the `state_class`, the `item` and `redraw` (whether drawing the opponent's unrevealed sets again can fix it: a revealed fact, the Item Clause, a trapping flag or candidate list nothing in the world explains, a condition that ran out under the sampled item, a hiding or turn-parity ability of a sampled set); `counts()` has every code, `checks`, `classes`, `check_class` (`V2:-`), `unwritten` by item, `resampled` and `written` by item (built worlds) and `defaulted_sources`. **Bounded redraw** (`createRedrawer(builder, max_redraws).build(draw)`): asks `draw(attempt)` for an input, builds it and draws again after a refusal with `redraw` set, at most R more times, returning the world or the last refusal with the draws used; counts `worlds`, `attempts`, `redraws`, `exhausted`, `final_refusals` and `redrawn` by check and class. It draws nothing itself (slice B supplies the seeded stream). The number of active slots is the request's `active` length everywhere (no literal): the scenario battle must have the same on both sides or the world is `start_failed`. Known limits: an effect that starts inside the residual block before `|upkeep` is dated as not yet counted down (`after_residual` false) although part of the residual may have run (rare, V4 cannot see it); `WorldFactsData` has no committed source yet (the search's composition root supplies it; tests read the pinned engine); the own request's `trapped: true` from a trapping move (Mean Look, Block) is a volatile the reader never shows, so those worlds fail V2 on the flag (typed `V2:-`, `redraw`, 20 requests of the corpus); Disable is not reproducible (its start handler also reads the active move for Cursed Body), so it is not in the live test's admitted set; Taunt, Encore, Throat Chop and Yawn equal the live engine (the record admits none, a test admits them); Dancer and Instruct lines under the facts' `action_sources` are counted from the engine's source, not a Pokemon's name; the Copycat and Mirror Move battle-level last move is not written; a Pokemon whose Illusion has not broken is refused rather than mixed (ADR-025's disguise assignment is slice B's); `hp_rounding` must invert the Champions display (`floor(100 * hp / max)`): `ceil` does, the committed `nearest` and `floor` do not for every maximum, and V3 then refuses those worlds. Tests: `test/world-builder.test.ts` builds a world at every own move request of the committed random-corpus channels for both seats with the opponent's TRUE brought team as input (1,378 requests: 847 built and passing all five checks; 473 `state_unwritten` as R, 29 `facts_unavailable` (the Illusion replace), 20 V2 on a trapping flag, 9 `reader_flagged`; each R refusal checked against an independent reading of the input), one test per write, check and refusal; `test/world-writers-live.test.ts` plays the committed seeded battles through `runBattle` with a test-only spy on the engine's `BattleStream` and compares every W item of the world with the LIVE battle at the decision (845 worlds; Toxic stage 24, benched status 62, Protect counter 35 with one counter above 3, weather duration 120, terrain 49, field 31, side conditions 144, faint counters, a gone item 373, last move; with a test record admitting Taunt, Encore, Throat Chop and Yawn: 906 worlds; with a Choice Scarf given to one Pokemon a side: 253 locks, V2 passing); `test/world-state.test.ts`, `test/world-checks.test.ts`. Measured (this machine, shared): about 2.5 ms per built world (start, eight `validateSet` calls, the writes, the turn rollover, V2 to V5), the scenario start itself about 15 ms the first time.

**World writers record** (ADR-026 Evidence 1, B2 and N5, #1140; `packages/schema/contracts/v1/world-writers.schema.json`, generated TS and pydantic types, samples in `fixtures/contracts/v1/world-writers/`, reader `world-writers.ts`, committed as `config/search/world-writers.v1.json`; integers only, closed). `items` classifies each of the 27 state items of the closed list (`STATE_ITEM_IDS`, the schema's enum, held equal by a test) exactly once, ascending by id, with a class (`E`, `W`, `X`, `T` or `R`) the implementation offers for it (`STATE_ITEMS`: a W item can also be `R`, which refuses a world that holds it; X likewise; `per_turn_fields` is T; the refusal rows are R), a `condition` where the writer adds an engine condition (`protect_counter` `stall`, `choice_lock` `choicelock`, `confusion_duration` `confusion`) and `ids` on the refusal rows (the engine's condition, move or ability ids whose presence is the item; `unavailable_mechanics` names protocol line kinds). `admitted` lists the ids of the generic classes, ascending by kind then id: `volatile` (class `E` add only, `W` add and count the remaining turns down, `X` engine draw) and `start_trigger` (class `W`, an item or ability id whose scenario-start change is restored). Every id not listed is R, and `toxic_stage` is W since #1187 (the oracle found 31 (F1) and 28 (F2) equal Toxic turns); the fidelity oracle (slice F, below) admits ids by evidence, and the committed record lists the admitted ids of the report (`start_trigger whiteherb`, `volatile encore`, `flashfire`, `focusenergy`, `taunt`, `throatchop`, `torment`, `yawn`; #1187), edited in place in `v1`: the record is reviewed data that no run, search identity or pinned hash outside its own test has used yet, the `admitted` list is the field slice F fills, and no schema or reader rule makes a committed record immutable (a record a run HAS used would be a new file, as `damage-aware.v1.json` is). `readWorldWriters(value, engine)` adds what the profile cannot say (every item once and in order, an offered class, `condition` and `ids` exactly where the item takes them, sorted and unique ids, no id under two rows, an admitted id resolving in the pinned engine's data, the confusion condition not admitted twice); `worldWritersHash` is SHA-256 of the JCS bytes (a vector from Python is pinned), the `world_writers_hash` of the search identity (ADR-026 §5). No game id is written in code: the committed record names them and `test/world-writers.test.ts` checks every one against the pinned engine. The record's `format_id` is the format it was reviewed for (`loadWorldWriters` refuses another).

**Locked, charging and trapping states refused (#1186; found by slice F, `world-facts/3`, `WORLD_BUILDER_FORMAT` 2).** The fork oracle's 138 F1 differences from `mustrecharge` 64, `twoturnmove` 47, `partiallytrapped` 27 and `dig` 14 (and Fly, Bounce, Dive, Solar Beam, Phantom Force, Sky Attack, Meteor Beam, `trapped`, `trapper`) came from engine volatiles the shared reader never shows and the builder neither wrote nor refused. `ActiveFacts` gained `locks` (the engine condition ids of the states a Pokemon in play is in now, sorted), derived from the player's own channel by the facts module: `mustrecharge` from `-mustrecharge` until the `|cant|…|recharge`; `twoturnmove` from `-prepare` (a `[premajor]` message such as Chilly Reception's is none; this covers every charge turn and the semi-invulnerable states of Fly, Dig, Dive, Bounce and Phantom Force) until the Pokemon's next own `|move|` (a `[from]lockedmove` one included) or `|cant|`; `partiallytrapped` on the target of an `-activate|…|move: M|[of] …` where M is in the new `WorldFactsData.partial_trap_moves` (the moves whose engine definition gives `volatileStatus: 'partiallytrapped'`, read from the pinned `data/moves.ts` in `test/world-facts-data.ts` like the stall moves) until `-end … [partiallytrapped]`; `trapped` (`-activate|…|trapped`) on the target and `trapper` on the Pokemon whose move did it. A trap holds while one of its partners is in play (it ends silently when a Pokemon leaves, which the fact follows; a Pokemon may hold several), a switch or a faint drops a Pokemon's own locks, and a trap whose trapper the channel does not show is `unattributable_line`. The ids are the engine's condition ids and the module holds no move list. `classify` (`world-state.ts`) refuses a world in which any Pokemon of either side holds a lock, as R, through the record's row for the id: `locked_moves` already named `mustrecharge` and `twoturnmove`; the record gained the R item `trapping` (ids `partiallytrapped`, `trapped`, `trapper`, checked against the engine by `readWorldWriters`; the schema enum, generated TS and pydantic types and the corpus samples follow), and an id with no row is `unadmitted_id`, never ignored. `config/search/world-writers.v1.json` hash moved `2a4777d5…` to `12b5760a…` (a new item; re-pinned in `world-writers.test.ts`; no committed run names the record yet, `search-config.test.ts` uses placeholders). The own request's `trapped: true` is not a refusal of its own: an opponent ability (Shadow Tag, Arena Trap) also sets it and stays the V2 evidence refusal. Parity: `world-facts-parity.test.ts` derives `locks` from the omniscient log with its own scan (stints in play per Pokemon) and requires equality at every request of both seats. The slice-F report was regenerated: worlds built 2913 to 2670, refused 2383 to 2626 (`locked_moves` 336 to 514, `trapping` 132 new, `v2_request` 69 to 11); the unmodelled-state table lost `mustrecharge`, `twoturnmove`, `partiallytrapped`, `dig`, Fly, Solar Beam, Phantom Force, Solar Blade, Bounce, Sky Attack, Meteor Beam, `trapped`, `trapper` and Dive (F1 turns 138 to 5, F2 151 to 10); the remaining ones are Ally Switch and Unburden (the volatile an item loss gives an Unburden holder), out of this ticket; the choice difference kind fell 76 to 0 and F1 fidelity of the non-X turns rose from 76.67% to 84.41% (F2 59.92% to 66.45%) because the refused turns are no longer compared; the admissions did not change. `test/world-writers-live.test.ts` no longer requires the "stall counter > 3" tick: its one corpus turn (b18) also holds a recharging Pokemon and is refused now.

**Fork oracle, fidelity report and admissions** (ADR-026 Evidence 1, B2 and N-A, slice F, #1142; `packages/showdown-bridge/test/fidelity/` (`oracle.ts`, `evaluate.ts`, `run.ts`, `report.ts`, `report-cli.ts`), `test/fidelity.test.ts`, `test/fidelity-isolation.test.ts`, `fixtures/showdown/<format>/search-fidelity/{oracle-sanity.json,fidelity-report.json,fidelity-report.md}`). **Test tooling, not shipped.** `captureDecisions` replays a committed seeded A1 battle with `runBattle` (actors by kind; a spy on the engine's `BattleStream` reads the live battle at each own move request, as `world-writers-live.test.ts` does) and returns each own move decision with `fork` = `toJSON()` of the live battle at the request (the test-only fork, rebuilt with `Battle.fromJSON` as `CloneJudge` does), the `WorldInput` (the player's channel, own team and the opponent's TRUE brought sets) and the recorded joint choices (the last unrefused choice of each seat in the turn; one decision per seat and turn, the retry after a refusal). The oracle world is the production builder's with `copyRandomState` (the world's `prng` becomes a clone of the fork's) just before the choices. `compare` plays one joint choice on a fresh revival of each (both sides' choices cleared first; the opponent's `switch N` translated to the world's bench order by nickname BEFORE the fork plays, because a switch reorders its side) and compares the player-view lines of the turn (`playerView`: the secret half of a `|split|` for the seat), the random state after the turn (a draw-order divergence has equal lines and another state) and the next own request (`plainRequest`, `firstDifference`); the kinds are `outcome`, `draw_order`, `request` and `choice` (a choice one battle takes and the other refuses). **F1** plays the recorded choices; **F2** plays a fixed stride sample of K own candidates (non-pending, canonical order; there is no policy here to rank them) against M opponent replies (the fork's own legal set), `k` 3 and `m` 2 in the report. Every differing comparison is classified by `stateDelta` (fork against world before the turn, as tags of fixed words: `own|opponent:hp|pp|status|status_state|item|ability|boosts|volatile:<id>|active_turns|last_move|active_slots|...`, `field:*`; a fainted pair is not compared further, a benched Pokemon's status counter is not read) and `blameOf` maps a tag to a cause: `writer` (an item of the record or an admitted id), `information` (the opponent's exact hit points and PP and bench PP: what a player cannot know), `unmodelled` (a volatile no writer or admitted id covers, named by its engine id) or `engine` (another field); a difference with no tag is `unclassified`. The arms start from the committed record with `admitted` emptied (so the evidence never depends on the admissions it supports): `base`, `volatile:<id>` (that one volatile admitted, class W when the condition has a duration or duration callback in the engine's data and E otherwise; the candidates are the volatiles the corpora show on an active Pokemon, minus the refusal rows and the confusion condition) and `trigger:<id>` (a `trigger-probe` arm admits every ability and item id of the corpora's teams; where it restores something, each in-play id is admitted alone and counts only when the single id covered the restore). **Exercised turns:** an item counts a non-X turn when it is present in the built world and some Pokemon acted (`|move|` or `|cant|`), an id when its carrier acted; a turn with a resampled X item (`world.resampled`) is X-class: reported on its own and in no other count. **Admission rule** (`passes`): at least `N_EX` = 20 exercising turns in F1 AND in F2 that are EQUAL (an exercising turn that differs for another classified cause is counted and reported but is no evidence for the item), none blamed (a difference classified against the item, or that no state difference names, blames every item present) and none missed (a difference classified against a writer in a turn where it was absent: it wrote nothing where its field differs); "all equal" is read after classification, so a difference that is information-limited or an unmodelled state does not count against an item, and its raw equal count is the evidence; the attribution is by the tags of the pre-turn state difference, not by a counterfactual run, and the fields `stateDelta` does not compare cannot be named (the report lists these limits, and that the always-written writers are exercised every turn). The corpora are `random/`, `random-choice-lock` (the random corpus on teams with one Choice Scarf a side, the rule of the live test: the natural corpora give the choice-lock writer too few turns), `unavailable/` and `search-fidelity/` (5,296 own move decisions in 159 battles; 2,913 worlds built, the rest counted refusals by code). `report-cli.ts [--subset N] [--no-candidates] [--json FILE]` writes the full report (about 5 minutes, one thread); `fidelity.test.ts` runs a 4-battle subset of each corpus in CI (explicit 300 s budget, about 1 minute) and prints its report, holds the primitives, the classification and the admission arithmetic with edge cases (19 against 20 turns, F2 failing alone, X excluded, a carrier that did not act, a missed write), the **sanity gate** (`oracle-sanity.json`: 15 hand-picked turns of three corpora, no X item and only bookkeeping items written, on which F1 and the F2 sample equal the real turn line for line, with negative controls: without the random-state copy or with doctored stages the comparison differs), and the committed report: its `inputs` (corpora bytes, pin, the record without `admitted`, `FIDELITY_FORMAT`) must equal a fresh computation (stale otherwise: rerun the script), its admissions must equal the record's `admitted`, and an item the record lists as W with a report row must pass. The committed JSON holds no wall-clock value (the script prints it on stderr). The full report is produced offline and CI checks its freshness and arithmetic; CI recomputes only the subset. `fidelity-isolation.test.ts` is the **isolation scan**: no shipped source (packages, `ml`, repository `scripts`, `services`, `crates`; test directories skipped) names an oracle identifier (`copyRandomState`, `captureDecisions`, `translateChoice`, `stateDelta`, `OracleSpec`, `prng.clone(`), imports `test/fidelity/`, calls `fromJSON` outside the three reviewed files (`choice-judge.ts`, `mechanics-damage.ts`, `reply-model.ts`: each forks a battle it built) or assigns a battle's `prng` outside the two probes that install their own recording source (`mechanics-damage.ts`, `mechanics-speed.ts`); the package exports only its entry point; the controls show each leak is flagged. **Findings** (`fidelity-report.md`, at this pin): F1 equal in 76.7% of 1,415 non-X turns (1,085) and F2 in 59.9% (848; 77.5% of the 8,442 non-X pairs), X-class turns apart (1,498; F1 76.5%, F2 66.6%). The causes: the opponent's exact hit points and PP (information-limited, the largest); engine state the world lacks (`mustrecharge` 64 F1 turns, `twoturnmove` 47, `partiallytrapped` 27, `dig` 14, `fly`, the charge moves, `unburden`, `trapped`: volatiles the reader never shows and the builder does not refuse, because nothing in the log names them before they act; a follow-up for the builder is to derive a recharge or charge turn from the last move and refuse it as R); a fainted opponent active's slot (`active_slots`, 46); a freeze counter (3). No difference is classified against a writer. 10 of the 11 W writers of the evidence record pass; `toxic_stage` has 26 exercising turns, none blamed or missed, but only 17 (F1) and 16 (F2) equal ones, so by the rule it was NOT admitted then (it is since #1187, see the targeted fidelity corpus paragraph; the figures of this paragraph are of the original 159-battle corpora): the committed record gives it class R (a world with a badly poisoned Pokemon is refused as `state_unwritten` `toxic_stage`, counted) until a follow-up adds Toxic battles to the targeted corpus (`world-writers-live.test.ts` still checks the writer itself against the live battle, with a test record that makes it W). The arms start from `evidenceRecord` (no id admitted, every offered writer W), so the evidence does not depend on the classes the committed record currently gives. Of the 15 candidate ids only `encore` (45 F1 / 53 F2 exercising turns, 35 / 32 equal) and `taunt` (24 / 35, 20 / 25 equal) pass; `disable` fails (1 turn blamed in F1, 8 in F2: its start handler also reads the active move, as the live test found), and `throatchop` (12), `yawn` (4), `torment` (8), the E-class ids and the `whiteherb` trigger (6) lack exercising turns (a corpus or target change can raise them). Not here: S0's go decision (the experiment ticket reads the report), leaf quality and cost.

**Search worlds, the opponent sampler of the search** (ADR-026 sections 2 to 4, slice B, #1143; `packages/showdown-bridge/src/world-sampler.ts`, `packages/team-dataset/src/search-worlds.ts`, `scripts/search-worlds.ts`). There is no sampler of its own and no interim one: the search's `OpponentTeamSampler` is ADR-025's `sample(belief, worldStream(stream, index))`, the stream `sampleWorlds` gives world `index`, asked by index. Attempt r of world i (0 <= r < R) is sampler index `i * R + r`, no index twice (checked); the stream is the harness's per-decision search stream (`searchStream`: SHA-256 of the JCS of `{seed, game, player, policy_id, request_seq, purpose: "search/1"}`, a Python vector in the test), 32 bytes, and the sampler path has no seed, battle, `BattleRecord`, `OmniscientLog`, hidden verdict or opponent team input (`WorldRequest` is the world input without its opponent; `WorldSamplePort.open` takes the reader's opponent half and flags; type tests and an import scan). The bridge does not import the opponent model: `WorldSamplePort` / `WorldSource` are structural (`open` computes the belief once per decision, `draw(stream, index)` samples), and the composition root `createSearchWorlds({engine, vocabulary, reader_sha256, manifest, arm}, files?)` in `packages/team-dataset` joins `belief`, `createSampler`, `createSetLegality` and `generatorSpreadSampler`. A belief refusal is a coded `refused` result (counted by the model's code; `BELIEF_DEFECTS` throw); a draw the model refuses (`legality_redraws_exhausted`) is a failed attempt and the next index follows. `createWorldSampler({engine, vocabulary, format_id, port, builder, settings: {worlds N, attempts R, min_valid W_min}})` has no defaults (the search config of slice R will carry 8, 4 and 4; the report script's flags default to them): per world i it draws index `i*R+r`, maps the `OpponentWorld` onto `WorldInput.opponent` (`mapWorld`: each brought slot is a member naming its roster entry and a whole set in the engine's names; a Mega slot is the preview species holding its stone, an ability the preview species cannot hold (an evolved Mega's) is its first listed one) and builds; the first attempt that passes V1 to V5 is the world, a refusal the builder marks `redraw` goes to the next index, any other is final, and a world with no valid attempt is refused and counted. A decision is `searched` only with at least `min_valid` valid worlds, else the pair's own choice is the caller's (`below_min_valid`). Counts (`WorldSamplerCounts`, per arm): decisions, searched, below W_min, belief refusals by code, worlds asked/valid/refused, `attempts`, `redraws` (this module's R bound: indices after the first of a world) and `legality_redraws` (ADR-025's, inside the draws) apart, draw refusals by code, redrawn and final refusals by code and `check:class`, a valid-count histogram and coverage by evidence class (`evidenceClass`, first match: `maybe_trapped` (the own request's `trapped` or `maybeTrapped`), `volatile`, `timed_condition`, `unseen_brought`, `rest`).
