Two prior arms through the same code: `generator-matched` (primary, label "belief matched to the synthetic generator: an upper bound for this evaluation") and `role-sets` (secondary, `ROLE_SETS_LABEL`; its record must be filled from the committed generator-matched prior and derived from the committed role-set table, both checked by hash, and a doctored record is a `SearchWorldsError`); every port carries `prior_kind`, `prior_label`, `prior_hash`, `belief_config_hash` and the ADR-025 `identity_hash`. **HP rounding (decision):** a shown opponent percentage is turned into hit points by `ceil` (`SEARCH_HP_ROUNDING`), because the Champions display is `floor(100 * hp / max)` and `ceil` inverts it for every maximum of 100 or more (a test runs maxima 100 to 800); damage-aware v1's `nearest` does not for every maximum and its record is unchanged (V3 would refuse those worlds), so the search says `ceil` itself and slice R carries it in its config. Measured on the 1,378 own move requests of the committed random-play channels (true opponent team never read; the corpus teams are not the generator's), N=8, R=4, W_min=4: `generator-matched` searched 803 decisions (6,266 valid worlds of 10,400 asked, 458 redraws, 0 legality redraws, belief refusals 69 `evidence_has_no_support` and 9 `reader_flagged`, 497 below W_min); the 129 `maybe_trapped` decisions have 0 searched (the own request is trapped by something the sampled sets never explain, the interim of ticket #1127 does not fix it), `volatile` 25 of 352, `timed_condition` 258 of 287, `unseen_brought` 200 of 241, `rest` 320 of 369; the dominant refusal is the builder's R class (`state_unwritten`, 3,729 worlds); `role-sets` is refused by its belief at 1,264 decisions (`evidence_has_no_support`: the random-play teams hold moves outside the role sets, the mismatch the arm is labelled for) and searched 66. Known limit: with an unsettled identity-hiding member the world does not say which slot a shown Pokemon is, so the builder refuses such worlds (`opponent_team_mismatch`, 10 worlds) and the disguise assignment is not carried. Tests: `showdown-bridge/test/world-sampler.test.ts` (stand-in port and scripted builder: the attempt rule, W_min exactly, every count and refusal, the mapping against the true teams on every committed request, HP inversion, evidence classes, privacy), `team-dataset/test/search-worlds.test.ts` (the real model: both arms, doctored priors, `draw` equal to `sampleWorlds`, counts consistency, same stream same worlds). `node packages/team-dataset/scripts/search-worlds.ts [--worlds N --attempts R --min-valid W --every K --out FILE]` prints the counts of both arms. Not here: the search actor (slice R), trapping-flag conditioning inside the belief (#1127).

**SearchConfig, search identity and the policy id with search** (ADR-026 section 5, slice R part 1, #1144; `packages/schema/contracts/v1/search-config.schema.json`, `packages/showdown-bridge/src/search-config.ts`, `config/search/rerank-1.v1.json`; generated TS and pydantic types, samples in `fixtures/contracts/v1/search-config/`; integers only, closed). `SearchConfig` is reviewed data: `algorithm {kind: "rerank-1"}`, `top_k` and `top_k_rule` (`prior-top-k` or `distinct-slot`), `worlds {kind: "sampled", n, redraws, hp_rounding, prior {kind, label}}`, `belief_identity_hash` (ADR-025's), `replies {max_damage_ppm, uniform_draws}`, `leaf {kind: "value-head" | "hp-ko-differential"}`, `budget_ms`, `a2_clock_floor_ms`, `min_valid_worlds` and `seed_derivation` (const `search/1`). The committed record holds ADR-026's defaults (k = 4, N = 8, R = 4, M = 3 as one max-damage reply at 500,000 ppm plus two uniform draws, W_min = 4), frozen only by S0's freeze commit. Decisions where the ADR left room: (1) `hp_rounding` is a config field (`ceil`, from #1143: it inverts the Champions display for every maximum of 100 or more, which damage-aware v1's `nearest` does not), not only the world sampler's code constant, because it changes which worlds pass V3 and so what is played, and `search_config_hash` then covers it (a test holds the config equal to `SEARCH_HP_ROUNDING`; `seed_derivation` equals `SEARCH_PURPOSE`); (2) `budget_ms` is the existing 30 s per-decision guard a latency forfeit is judged against, not a cut-off in the search (its work is fixed by k, N, M and R), and `a2_clock_floor_ms` (30,000, one guard period of bank) is a provisional value S0 may change in a new file; (3) `readSearchConfig` adds `min_valid_worlds <= n` and exact reply weights (the uniform replies share `1,000,000 - max_damage_ppm` in whole ppm; with no uniform draw the max-damage reply carries all of it). `belief_identity_hash` is ADR-025's hash of the committed v2 belief at this pin, reader and version manifest: `packages/team-dataset/test/search-config.test.ts` computes it with the composition root and requires the committed value, so a change of the reader or the pin makes a new config file. **Search identity:** `searchIdentityHash(config, inputs)` is SHA-256 of the JCS of `{search_config_hash, belief_identity_hash, mechanics_config_hash, damage_aware_config_hash, damage_value_format, world_writers_hash, world_builder_format, world_facts_format, reply_model_format, leaf_format, reader_sha256, version_manifest}`; it refuses a belief identity the config does not name and a malformed hash or format. Two constants are new: `WORLD_BUILDER_FORMAT` (`world-builder.ts`, 1; bump when the builder places or checks something differently for the same input) and `LEAF_FORMAT` (`search-config.ts`, 1; the leaves do not exist yet); `DAMAGE_VALUE_FORMAT`, `REPLY_MODEL_FORMAT` and `WORLD_FACTS_FORMAT` (`world-facts/2`) are the existing ones, and the caller (part 2) supplies the hashes of the mechanics, damage-aware and world-writers records. **Policy id:** `policyId(pair, mode, play, features?, search?)` and `areneon_ml.loop.records.policy_id(..., search=None)` add `search: <search identity hash>` to the preimage only when present, together with whatever `mode`, `temperature_ppm`, `truncation` and `features` the pair's underlying play mode has (a search over another mode is another id); label `SEARCH_POLICY_LABEL`, "learned pair with decision-time search (one-ply re-rank)". `fixtures/policy-ids/existing.json` lists nine ids computed before this change (every mode, temperature, truncation and features combination) and both sides require them byte for byte; the search identity and two search ids have vectors computed in Python and pinned in `packages/showdown-bridge/test/search-config.test.ts` and `tests/ml/loop/test_search_identity.py` (Python `search_identity_hash` takes the thirteen inputs since #1243 added `mechanics_adapter_format`; the vector there is `015d8f40...`). Not here: the search actor, the invariance test and the A2 skip record (part 2, #1145), the worker's `evaluate` kind (Q).

**Probe max-damage reply** (ADR-026 section 2, finding B1, rules N-L, slice D, `packages/showdown-bridge/src/reply-model.ts`, #1141). `createReplyModel({engine, vocabulary, config, mechanics, snapshot?, data_class, probe?}).reply(world, memo?)` is the opponent's reply in a scenario world (a `ScenarioWorld` of the world builder; it takes nothing of the real game, a type test and a source scan): a new code path beside the damage-aware actor (not the actor, not its `assume` seam, no `PublicState`, reader or channel; `damage-aware@v1`'s bytes, choices and identity are unchanged, the invariance tests pass as they were), built on the shared `damageView` (the memoised probe call and its refusal classes) and the pure `damageValue` under the weights of `config/baselines/damage-aware.v1.json` (`config` is that v1 record; its hash is the search identity's `damage_aware_config_hash`). The world is cloned (`Battle.fromJSON(toJSON())`, which rebuilds every side's request from the state as written; the world's own requests are those of the start), so the world passed in is never changed; the opponent's request of the clone goes through `decodeRequest`, `enumerateCandidates` and a `CloneJudge` on the clone (`legalChoiceSet`; the world is ours, so its verdicts reveal nothing), and only non-pending candidates are valued. Attackers and defenders are the engine's own active Pokemon: the declared set (`mon.set`, exported as the canonical paste), current HP (null for full), status, the five stages and `species.isMega`; the field (weather, terrain, Trick Room, the four side conditions, the attacker's side first) is read from the world's battle. **Rules (N-L):** one `damage` query per (move, defender) and `damageValue` of each answer; a spread move (`allAdjacentFoes`, `allAdjacent`) is the sum over the player's living actives (foes only, as ADR-018 v1; the ally is not counted), a random-target move (`randomNormal`) their floored mean, a single-target move the foe at the choice's location (the engine's `getAtLoc`: 1 is the foe in active slot 0; a reviewer found that `damage-aware-view.ts` `Foe.loc` (`letters.length - position`) is the reverse, so v1 and v2 value a single-target move against the other foe; left unchanged here because it would change `damage-aware@v1`'s choices and identity, follow-up); an `unsupported` answer scores 0 and so does a query the engine refuses to state (a `ProbeRefusal`, counted in `probe_refusals`; any other failure, and a wrong mechanics config, propagates); a slot part is eligible only if it is `pass` or a damaging move aimed at foes, so the reply never switches and never uses a status move or a move at an ally; a slot with no eligible part in any legal candidate (a Pokemon with only status moves, a locked move with no target type) is held to its first legal part in canonical order (`forced_slots`); the highest summed value wins, the first of equal values in canonical order; when it is 0 the reply is the `actorRule` choice of the world's legal set (`kind: "all_zero"`, `all_zero` counted). `reply` returns `{choice, kind, value}` (`value` in ppm); `counts()` has `replies`, `max_damage`, `all_zero`, `forced_slots`, `candidates`, `queries`, `probe_runs`, `unsupported_queries`, `probe_refusals`. **Memo:** `newWorldMemo()` is the per-world memo of probe answers by query hash (a pure function of the query, so sharing one across replies on the same world changes no answer); `reply` makes a fresh one when none is given. **Formats for the search identity:** `REPLY_MODEL_FORMAT` (1; bump when the rules or what a world's state is read as change) and `DAMAGE_VALUE_FORMAT` (1, in `damage-aware-score.ts`; bump whenever `rollsValue`, `critChancePpm` or `damageValue` return another number for the same input; `reply-model.test.ts` pins hand-worked vectors). Limits: a temporary forme a world shows and the attacker's volatiles are not stated to the probe (the world builder refuses volatiles); one reply per call (the two uniform draws and the 1/2, 1/4, 1/4 weights are slice R part 2); KO counts are not used (the share is counted from the rolls, as v1). Tests: `test/reply-model.test.ts` (hand-worked worlds with a stub probe: spread against single, the target of a single-target move, ties in canonical order, all-zero, unsupported, a refused query, a status-only slot, a random-target move, the field read from the world, no change to the world, the memo, refusals; and 40 worlds built from the committed channels with the real probe: legal, never a switch). Measured (this machine, shared, load 16, the probe not yet sped up by #1179): 40 worlds built from the committed random-corpus channels, 6.8 `damage` queries per world (270 in all, none answered from the memo: the queries of a world are distinct), mean 135-146 ms and p95 230 ms per world, of which about 6 ms is the clone, enumeration and judge and the rest the probe (about 19 ms a query); ADR-026 section 3 estimated about 16 queries per world at 13 ms.

**Search actor, `rerank-1`** (ADR-026 sections 1, 2, 4, 6 and 7, slice R part 2, #1145; `packages/showdown-bridge/src/search-actor.ts`, `search-turn.ts`, `search-leaf.ts`, `world-facts-data.ts`, `packages/team-dataset/test/search-actor*.ts`, `scripts/search-budget.ts`). `searchActor({setup, client, battle, player, team, manifest, channel_class, seed, game, policy_id, identity_hash, sampling, play?, bank_ms?, reply_model?, on_decision?})` is an A1 `Actor` for ONE seat of ONE game (it wraps a `policyActor` for every decision it does not search). `searchSetup({engine, format_id, vocabulary, port, facts_data, data_class, ...})` joins the reviewed records (the committed `rerank-1.v1`, damage-aware v1 weights, the mechanics config, the world writers) with the opponent model's `WorldSamplePort` and checks they agree (format, the config's belief identity and prior arm, the prior's format; `SearchError` `setup_mismatch`); `searchPolicy(setup, manifest, pair, mode, play)` gives the search identity (`damage_value_format`, `world_builder_format`, `world_facts_format`, `reply_model_format`, `leaf_format` and the hashes of the other inputs, all read from the setup) and the policy id (ADR-010 preimage + `search`). **Algorithm.** A move request with at least two non-pending candidates is searched; Team Preview, a forced switch, fewer than two non-pending candidates, a belief refusal, fewer than W_min valid worlds and the A2 clock skip play the pair's own choice in its underlying mode (`sampling`/`play`, the control's mode; the policy id and `u` stream are the search policy's) and are counted by reason (`FALLBACK_REASONS`). The worker's `evaluate` on the real observation gives the prior; the top `top_k` non-pending candidates by prior (a stable sort on the canonical order: ties by canonical rank) are searched. The worlds come from `createWorldSampler` (slice B) on the harness stream `searchStream({seed, game, player, policy_id, request_seq})`. In each valid world the M replies are the probe max-damage reply (weight `max_damage_ppm`, skipped when 0) and `uniform_draws` uniform draws over the world's non-pending legal replies (`legalReplies`, added to `reply-model.ts` beside `foeRequest`; draw j of world i is `floor(u * n / 2^64)` of the first 8 bytes of SHA-256 over the JCS of `{draw, purpose: "search-reply/1", search_stream, world}`, an addition to the ADR's `{search_stream, world, purpose}` preimage so every draw has its own hash; weights `(10^6 - max_damage_ppm) / uniform_draws`, exact by the config reader). The engine seed of a world is `engineSeed(stream, world)` (`sodium,` + the first 32 hex of SHA-256 over the JCS of `{purpose: "search-engine/1", search_stream, world}`) and is the same for every candidate and reply. One turn is played on `Battle.fromJSON` of the world's serialisation (made once per world; the world is never changed) with `PRNG` replaced by that seed: `playChoices` (`search-turn.ts`) restarts the clone with a capturing `send`, chooses for the seat then the opponent, calls `sendUpdates` as the engine's stream does, and returns this seat's view of the output (`playerView`: the engine's own `|split|` rule, the secret half of its own lines and the shared half of the opponent's, so the opponent is seen only as a share of 100) and its next `|request|`. A `wait` request (the opponent must switch) is played on with the opponent's first non-pending legal choice in canonical order (`foeSwitchChoice`, the `actor-rule` choice: a forced switch offers switches only, which the max-damage reply never plays), step by step until the seat has a request or the game ends. The leaf is the game score (`outcomeScore`: win 1,000,000, tie 500,000, loss 0 ppm) when the game ends, else the config's leaf: `value-head` appends the player-view lines to the REAL channel (`context.channel`), builds the observation (`observe`, the hypothetical channel ends at the world's next own request) and asks `client.evaluate` (batch 1, one query at a time; `value_ppm` unclamped), memoised by the appended lines within the decision (`leaf_memo_hits`); `hp-ko-differential` (`hpKoDifferential`) is `500000 + floor((hp share difference + standing share difference) / 4)` in ppm over the world's brought Pokemon (integers). Score of a candidate = the sum over the scored worlds and replies of weight times leaf (the same divisor for every candidate, so the sums order exactly as the means do); the best sum wins, ties by prior rank. A world whose reply model fails (`ReplyModelError`) or whose turn the engine refuses is dropped and counted (`worlds_dropped`: `reply_model`, `choice_refused`; any other error is a defect and propagates); fewer than `min_valid_worlds` scored worlds play the pair (`below_min_valid`). **Not supported, refused:** a pair that reads candidate features (`client.featureSet` not null) is `SearchError` `features_pair` at construction: its queries carry probe rows per candidate, and a hypothetical channel would need them built for every leaf (a later slice's decision). **A2.** `bank_ms()` below `config.a2_clock_floor_ms` skips the search (`clock_floor`; `searchSkipReason(bank_ms, floor_ms)` is the one rule); `A2BotOptions.search = {a2_clock_floor_ms}` makes the session record `search_skipped: "clock_floor"` on a battle `DecisionRecord` whose timer snapshot shows less bank than the floor (absent otherwise and for Team Preview; an optional field, a minor change; the session still answers with the actor rule, since no policy is wired into A2, and A2 results are never evaluation). **Counts per game** (`report().counts`, integers; wall time is printed, never stored): decisions, searched decisions, fallbacks by reason, worlds asked/built/refused, redraws and ADR-025's `legality_redraws` apart, refusals by code and by `check:class`, belief refusals by the model's code, worlds dropped by reason, resampled X counters, worlds scored, turns, wait continuations, leaf queries / memo hits / game-end / hp-ko leaves, and the replies drawn; `report().reply_model` has the probe counts. **Privacy.** The decision's only inputs are `SearchInput` = `{world: WorldRequest, channel (the player's own lines), top, stream}`: the live battle, `BattleRecord`, `OmniscientLog`, hidden verdicts, the seed and the opponent's team have no field (`test/search-actor.test.ts`, a type test pinning the key sets); the seed reaches only `searchStream` (so another battle seed draws other worlds, as it draws another `u`). Nothing searched is stored: the actor returns a choice string; `test/search-actor.test.ts` of `team-dataset` shows the Episode and the training examples of a search game equal those of a replay of its choices and carry no search key. **Tests** (real engine, real opponent model, a stand-in worker whose value is the hit-point difference of the observation's own log): determinism (same choices, log and counts), invariance (opponents with the same species and other hidden sets give equal choices AND equal scores at every request with an equal player channel) with the negative control (a port whose worlds are the opponent's real sets gives different scores at every searched request and different choices at some), the fallbacks, the drops, the leaves, the second seat, the reply weights, a finished game (game-end leaves), and `search-actor.python.test.ts` (the real worker, tiny seeded networks, skipped without the Python environment). The `reply_model`, `on_decision` and `bank_ms` options are test and tooling seams. **Cost** (`scripts/search-budget.ts`, real worker at the size of `config/training/public-gpu16.json`, `fixtures-v1` teams against `random-legal`): 3 games (24 searched decisions of 49 move decisions; k = 4, N = 8, M = 3) gave per searched decision a median of 2,778 ms, a mean of 3,176 ms, a 95th percentile of 4,432 ms and a 99th of 5,410 ms (worst 5,410 ms; a sample this small makes the 99th the worst), against the slice's 10 s bound for the whole decision, worker round trips included; per game a median of 21 s; 2,268 simulated turns, 1,898 value queries and 232 memo hits in the 3 games. The pair was seeded and untrained at the real size (d_model 256, 6 layers, CPU, batch 1); one shared machine at a load average of 10 to 12 from another run, one thread, so the figures are pessimistic and not claimed to repeat. In the same sample 19 of the 43 non-Preview, non-forced decisions had fewer than W_min valid worlds (all 155 refused worlds are the builder's `state_unwritten`), so the pair played them. The gate is S0's. `world-facts-data.ts` is the one reader of the world facts' engine data (the stall moves, the Court Change list, the Toxic ceiling, the action sources; the tests' `readWorldFactsData` delegates to it). Not here: progress and head-to-head integration (slice P), S0 and E-S1, search in training, Team Preview or forced switches, a pair with candidate features, the Illusion disguise assignment in a world (slice B's known limit), the trapping-flag conditioning (#1127) and bug #1186 (unmodelled locked or charging volatiles).

**Candidate features on the wire** (ADR-022 slice D2, ADR-008 erratum E2, ADR-010 erratum A5 items 2 and 4, #1027; builds on the candidate feature builder above; owner decision 2026-10-28: may merge ahead of the #1030 verdict; a real short generation with features, after #1028, is the final trigger evidence). `PolicyQuery` and `TrainingExample` gain the optional closed `candidate_features {feature_set, feature_set_hash, rows[]}` (a minor change within v1; integers only; `rows` are `kind`-tagged `computed` {slot, move, target, mega, mean_ppm, max_ppm, min_ppm, ohko_rolls (null when the probe gives none)} or `unsupported` {slot, move, target, mega, reason: the probe's closed reason}, at most 128). **Absent keeps every byte:** writers omit the field (never `null` or empty), so no Episode, stream, `TrainingSet`, manifest or policy id moves, and `test/candidate-features-transport.test.ts` pins the digests of the samples that predate it; the handshake samples' digests did change, because `hello` and `ready` carry the `PolicyQuery` schema's hash (as for #1043: client and worker ship together). The handshake `ready` gains the optional `feature_set` `{feature_set, feature_set_hash}` (none when the checkpoints need none; the real worker states one from its checkpoints' metadata, #1028; `Worker(..., features=FeatureSupport(feature_set, damaging))` is the seam, `features.damaging_moves(game)` the engine-data predicate). `PolicyWorkerOptions.features` is the set this side can supply: a worker that states another set, or any set when none is supplied, is a `PolicyHandshakeError` before any battle; `client.featureSet` is what the worker stated, and `ask` refuses (`PolicyProtocolError`) rows sent to a worker that stated none, no rows to one that did, or rows of another set. `policyActor` / `PolicySeat` take `features: {configs: FeatureConfigs, memo?}` and attach `candidateFeatures`' rows (an empty list at Team Preview and forced switches) only for a client that states a set, after checking the built set equals the stated one (a mismatch is a `policy_protocol` incident). **A request the builder cannot feature (`FeatureBuildError`: `reader_flagged`, `reader_gap`, `assumed_set`, `probe_refused`, `no_request`) forfeits the seat as `crash`, detail `no candidate features: <code>`**, counted with the other policy forfeits: no rule fallback (the played policy would be partly scripted and its decision would not be the policy's own, which PPO recomputes), no incident (a reader limit would stop a generation); the closed forfeit causes are unchanged (a new cause would be a major change), and any other builder error reaches `guardActor`, which makes it a `crash` forfeit too. Reader rules, in both languages (`feature-rows.ts`, `worker/features.py`; the TS client's `readPolicyQuery`, `readTrainingExample`, the worker's `read_query` and `StreamChecker`): rows strictly ascending by (slot, move, target, mega false first); in a query, every damaging move part of every candidate has a row, checked by the worker (`invalid_query`) and by `featureCoverageProblem` (the caller gives it the dex's damaging test). **Decision where the ticket left room:** the coverage check asks for at least one row of (slot, move, mega) for a damaging part that aims at a foe location or, with no location, belongs to a spread or random-target move (`FOE_SPREAD_TARGET_TYPES` in `feature-rows.ts` and in `worker/features.py`, held equal by a test), and not for the row of the exact location, because the engine lists candidates that aim at a fainted foe's location (`move 1 2` with the second foe down) which the builder rightly gives no row and the worker holds no battle state to tell absence from a missing row. The query's `feature_set_hash` must equal the session's. In a stream, one `StreamChecker` also requires the examples to carry one feature set or none (recorded in `classes` so the parallel pieces join it). **Training data:** `TrainingInput.features` `{engine, configs}` (also on `StoredTrainingInput`, so `writeTrainingStream`, `writeStoredTrainingStream` and `loadTrainingSet`) recomputes each example's rows with the same builder from the Episode's own channel prefix, its own team and the example's candidates, one memo per Episode; a decision it cannot feature is a `TrainingLoaderError` (the game that played it forfeited, so it should have no Episode). Without the option nothing changes. Naming the set from a run config (the encoder option `candidate_features`, the policy-id preimage) is #1028 (next paragraph). **Equality test** (`test/candidate-features-transport.test.ts`): a seeded A1 game through `policyGame` against stand-in workers that state the set, with the real probe; the rows in each `PolicyQuery` equal, byte for byte (JCS), the rows `loadTrainingSet` recomputes from the written Episodes for every decision of both seats, each example is a valid `TrainingExample`, and its rows cover its candidates. Corpus samples: `policy-query` `battle-features`, `preview-features`, `training-example` `battle-features`, `policy-handshake` `ready-features` (shape samples: the rows are the golden turn request's damaging move parts with invented numbers), invalid (extra row field, float, unknown kind or reason, `null`, no rows, short hash) and reader-invalid (unsorted, duplicate, missing row). Not here (see the next paragraph for the encoder and worker): a sidecar of play-time features, any field inside an observation.

**Candidate features in the encoder and the loop** (ADR-022 tier A, slice D3, ADR-010 erratum A5 items 2 to 4, #1028; stacked on the wire slice above). The encoder option `candidate_features` (`model.encoder.options`, `ml/areneon_ml/model/options.py`) is an OBJECT value `{feature_set: "probe-damage/1", feature_set_hash}` (the registry's first option with an object value: `OptionSpec.parse` reads it into the frozen `FeatureSetOption`; `FEATURE_SETS` lists the sets the encoder can read, so `probe-damage/2`, erratum E-22.3, is a registry entry and a row-group change later, not built here). It is layout changing (checkpoint format `"2"`, `load_checkpoint(configured=...)` and the learner-state sidecar compare the object like any option), and both `TrainingRunConfig` and `CloneRunConfig` accept it (schemas, generated TS and pydantic types, corpus samples `candidate-features`, invalid `candidate-features-true`, `-short-hash`, `-unknown-set`). The hash enters `EncoderConfig.as_dict`, so `encoder_version` and the checkpoint metadata; a pin bump, a probe or reader change or a new assumption record is a new hash, a new encoder version and a new run. **Absent keeps every byte:** `Layout.as_dict` adds `cand_num` and `encoder_version` adds the `features` constants only with the option, `EncodedQuery.cand_num` / `Batch.cand_num` are None and left out of `tensors()` without it, and the golden encoder version, tensor digests, checkpoint ids and policy ids are pinned unchanged. **Encoding (`model/features.py`, `Encoder._feature_part`):** a battle move part has, per foe location 1 to `SLOTS`, one learned category (`feature_kind_<loc>`: no row, computed, or one per closed reason of an unsupported row; embedded by the Battle network) and five numbers (`mean`, `max`, `min` as `ppm / 10^6`, a known flag and `ohko_rolls / 16`; zeros for an unsupported row: nothing is guessed), projected by one Linear into one more d-wide part of the slot. A single-target move fills its target's group, a spread or random-target move one group per foe it has a row for; a status move, a switch, a pass and an ally target carry the none category and zeros. **Fail closed (the encoder's own rule, stricter than the worker's coverage check):** a damaging part aimed at a foe location whose foe is alive in the reduced state and has no row is an `EncodingError`; one aimed at a fainted or absent foe (the engine lists such candidates; the builder gives no row) has none to find; a spread part with no row at all is refused (`_foe_alive` reads the location in the engine's numbering, which the rows use since #1180: location L is the foe in active slot L - 1). The query must carry exactly the set the config names (rows under a config without the option, no rows under one with it, another set or hash, a row twice or past `SLOTS` are refused). `FOE_SPREAD_TARGET_TYPES` now lives in `model/features.py` (the worker re-exports it; the TS list is still held equal by a test). The Preview network keeps the layout but ignores the numbers; `collate` refuses a batch mixing layouts and the Battle network refuses a batch of another candidate-column layout. **Worker:** `encoder_input` / `IncrementalView` carry `candidate_features` into the view only when the query has them (so a view without them is what it was, and `view_digest` includes them); `areneon_ml.worker.__main__` builds `FeatureSupport` from its checkpoints' model config (the stated `ready.feature_set`, none for a pair without the option; the scorer has already refused a pair with different encoders). **Learner:** `_Collector.add` passes the example's rows to the encoder, `to_device` and the parallel piece transport move `cand_num` (None travels as null); a stream of another set, or with rows to an encoder without the option, is refused by the encoder. **Policy id:** `policy_id(..., features=hash)` (Python, `loop/records.py`) and `policyId(pair, mode, play, features?)` (TS) add `features: <feature_set_hash>` to the preimage only for a pair with the option (vectors computed with hashlib and pinned on both sides; every other id is unchanged); `Frozen.features` carries it through the loop (`features_hash(config)`). **Run config to play and load:** `featureSetup(engine, config)` (`league-generation.ts`) builds the `FeatureConfigs` from the committed `config/baselines/damage-aware.v1.json`, the mechanics config and the rules snapshot and refuses (`GenerationError`) a config whose named hash this checkout does not compute; `generation-cli.ts` passes the statement to every `PolicyWorkerClient` (`features`) and the configs to `GenerationOptions.features`, which gives the pair's seats `features: {configs}` and the training loader `features: {engine, configs}`; without the option nothing is built, sent or recomputed. **Family label:** `FEATURES_FAMILY` ("learned policy that consults the engine's damage calculation on assumed opponent sets at decision time") is on the dashboard (`ReportModel.family`, a note with the latency and same-probe remarks, absent for a pair without a set). **Decisions:** (1) the progress evaluation and the private test first refused a run whose pair reads features (`ProgressError`); #1181 lifted the refusal (next paragraph). (2) The row numbers are scaled by constants hashed into `encoder_version` (`PPM`, `OHKO_ROLLS`), not parameters. **Tests:** `tests/ml/model/test_features.py` (hand-built queries to rows, categories per closed reason, every refusal, batch-1 bit-identical alone and after others, A1 and A2 forms with the same rows equal, padded batch, the checkpoint format), `tests/ml/learner/test_features.py` (stream to steps, training gradient reaches the feature projection, the parallel piece transport, learner-state restore, family label), `tests/ml/test_worker_features.py` (the real worker states the set from its checkpoints), `packages/showdown-bridge/test/feature-setup.test.ts` (the hash check and a generation that plays with the real probe: every example carries the very rows that were sent), the policy-id vectors, and a tiny-loop run (below). **Tiny-loop determinism (`tiny.json` plus the option, the real probe and the real worker, two generations of six games; run by hand with the driver and a worker wrapper that drops `-I`, because the in-test `-I` subprocesses import the installed contracts):** an independent rerun and a run stopped after generation 1 and resumed to generation 2 reach the same learner-state hash (`c1366992...`), the same checkpoints and the same training-set hash, and `diff -r` of the three runs' generation directories is empty; generation 2's stream held 73 examples carrying 792 rows of one feature set, with no forfeit.

**The encoder reads `probe-damage/2`** (ADR-022 erratum E-22.3.3, #1175; `model/features.py`, `encoder.py`, `network.py`, `batch.py`). `FEATURE_SETS` gains `probe-damage/2` and `candidate_features` takes it in `TrainingRunConfig` and `CloneRunConfig` (schema enum, a minor change within v1; the formerly invalid `-unknown-set` samples now say `probe-damage/3`, new valid `candidate-features-2` samples). The set is `/1`'s `rows` read exactly as before plus the four arrays, placed where they belong: **own tokens** get the pooled threat rows into them (own Mega flag false) and one `speed_relation` category per foe location (`speed_<loc>` columns at the end of `mon_cat`, none on a foe token or without a row); **foe tokens** get the pooled `foe_priorities` rows; **move parts** get `own_priority_kind` and the numeric `own_priority` (the last `cand_num` column); a **Mega part** also gets the Mega variant's `speed_mega_<loc>` categories and its pooled threat summary (the Battle network's extra slot-part input, zero for any other part); a **switch part** needs nothing new (its switch-in token holds the advisory next-turn threats and speeds). A threat row is the foe's token before any summary, its move embedding, a `probe_kind` category (computed or the closed reason; the reasons are the contract's five), `/1`'s five numbers and its foe move's priority (`priority / PRIORITY_SCALE`, 8) with the kind of that foe priority row; rows are pooled by masked sum and max per token (`_pool`) through learned row and pool projections. The variable-length rows are `thr_idx/cat/num` and `foe_idx/cat/num` on `EncodedQuery` and `Batch` (flat fields, so the parallel loader transport carries them; padded index -1), present only for `/2` (`tensors()` omits them otherwise, `collate` refuses a mix of layouts). Layout, the kinds, `PRIORITY_SCALE` and the array constants enter `encoder_version` only for `/2`; `/1` and absent keep their versions, tensors and checkpoint ids (pinned in `tests/ml/model/test_features_2.py`). **Fail closed:** a missing array, a duplicate row, an own position or foe location the request or state lacks (a fainted foe has no token, the builder gives it no rows), a threat whose foe move has no `foe_priorities` row in a move request (a forced switch asks no move, so the builder emits none there: its threats take the NONE priority kind and a zero number; the readers in `feature-rows.ts` and `worker/features.py` require the row exactly in a move request, which `candidateFeatures2` always satisfies since it prices every revealed foe move it threatens with, an unsupported priority still being a row), a move part with no own-priority row and a candidate (move, Mega or switch-in variant) missing a speed row for any foe location the arrays name are `EncodingError`s; arrays under `/1` are refused. `Arrays` in `features.py` is the indexed form. Family label for `/2`: `FEATURES_2_FAMILY` ("... damage calculation and turn order on assumed opponent sets at decision time"). The worker states the set from its checkpoints as for `/1` (`worker/features.py` already held the `/2` reader rules). Tests: `tests/ml/model/test_features_2.py`, `tests/ml/learner/test_features_2.py`, `tests/ml/test_worker_features.py`, and `training-loop-features.python.test.ts` (now both sets). Generation cost with `/2` is measured with `node packages/showdown-bridge/scripts/feature-bench.ts --run-dir R --generation G --feature-set probe-damage/2` on a committed generation and a real generation of a `/2` config (EXP-DF3, #1176).

**Evaluating a pair that reads candidate features** (ADR-022 §6, §7, #1181; the progress evaluation, its head to head and the private test). No contract changed and a pair without the option keeps every byte, id and record. The seats of a run whose config sets `candidate_features` build the rows with the builder `policyActor` uses in training: `pairFeatureSetup(engine, config)` (`progress-cli.ts`, `featureSetup` with a usage error) names the feature set from the run's config, fails closed when this checkout computes another hash, and gives the pair's `PolicyWorkerClient` its statement (`FrozenPairClientOptions.features`; the handshake checks the worker's `ready.feature_set` against it) and `ProgressOptions.features` / `PrivateTestOptions.features` the `FeatureConfigs`, which every seat of that run (the candidate, previous generation, anchors, the ablation's Previews) carries as `PolicySeat.features`. A build failure forfeits the game as `crash` like any policy failure (a latency overrun is a scored loss), nothing is invented. **Each run has its own statement:** for a head to head (`--versus-dir`) or the private test's `--opponent-run-dir` the Node side reads that directory's `run-config.json` itself (the progress command checks it against the `--versus` run config hash) and sets that pair up from ITS config, so a features pair against a no-features pair gives each seat its own worker, statement and rows (the DF2 control); the client option falls back to the evaluated run's setup only when no pair is passed, never for another run's pair (`null` means none). **Policy ids:** Python derives every id with `policy_id(..., features=features_hash(config))` from the config of the run the pair belongs to (candidate, previous, anchor, ablation, `_versus_pair`, the private test's pair and opponent), so they equal the training ids; `read_progress_record` (`_check_run`) recomputes the candidate's and the same run's previous and anchor ids, and leaves a `run` opponent's id to `_check_versus` at evaluation, against THAT run's config (the record does not carry it; a read-back root does not recompute it, which also lets a no-features candidate meet a features run and two different feature sets meet). Latency and the family label are unchanged (the dashboard names the pair). Tests: `packages/team-dataset/test/progress-features.python.test.ts` (real workers and probe: a features run and a plain run, baselines, anchor, previous, head to head, ids equal to `policyId`, no forfeit, byte-identical rerun), `tests/ml/progress/test_features.py`, `packages/showdown-bridge/test/private-test.test.ts`.

**Engine re-simulation of a training game** (`ml/areneon_ml/inspector/resimulate*.py`, `packages/showdown-bridge/src/resimulate.ts` and `scripts/resimulate.ts`, ADR-017 §4a, #926). `python -m areneon_ml.loop inspect --run-dir R --resimulate G:K [--out D] [--node node] [--engine-data F]` re-runs game K of generation G on the pinned engine and writes ONE page, `<D>/resimulations/g<NNNNNN>/game-<NNNNNN>.html` (default `D` is `<run>.inspect/`, never inside the run; it does not rebuild the inspector pages, and a later `inspect` keeps `resimulations/` as it keeps `replays/`). The two Episodes are read by the replay reader (`read_games`: manifest, file hashes, labels, `validate("episode")`, results equal to `generation.json`); the Node script runs `runBattle` with `replayActor` from the game's seed, both Episodes' own teams (as brought), the config's `max_turns` and both seats' recorded choices, refusals (`[Unavailable choice]`) included, and prints both players' channels, the omniscient log, the winner, the turns and each decision with the request's legal set (choices and pending flags; no `CloneJudge` verdict, `hidden_verdicts` is never emitted). `accept` takes the result only if both channels equal the stored Episode channels line for line, the winner and turns equal the `generation.json` row, the log ends in that result, and the choices the engine took (refusal text included) are the recorded decisions; any difference is an `InspectError` that names a seat and a line number and quotes no line, and nothing is written. A forfeited game has no Episodes and is refused before the engine runs; the input is read by a closed-key reader on both sides (`readResimulationInput`). The page is the omniscient view of the progress-game replays (`replay_page.assets("omniscient")`, the same board, one hashed script and style, one data block, `textContent` only) labelled "Engine re-simulation, checked against both recorded channels", with the warning that it holds both sides' hidden information (local use only; do not share or commit it); the run directory is only read (a test compares every file before and after) and the loop still never stores an omniscient log. Teams come from the Episodes, which the loop checked against `team_hashes` when it wrote them, and the byte-equal channels pin them. Tests: `tests/ml/inspector/test_resimulate.py` (a fake runner for acceptance, every tamper and the page; the real engine on all five fixture games where it is built), `packages/showdown-bridge/test/resimulate.test.ts` (an unavailable-corpus battle with refusals).

**League seeds and the LeagueRunRecord** (`packages/showdown-bridge/src/league-seed.ts`, `league-run-record.ts`, `packages/schema/contracts/v1/league-run-record.schema.json`, `ml/areneon_ml/loop/league.py`, ADR-009 §3, ADR-010 §4, #843). `league-seed/1` is the pure derivation of everything a league game is built from: `leagueGameStreams(run_key, generation, game)` is SHA-256 over the JCS of `{run_key, generation, game, purpose: "league-game"}`; the first 16 bytes are the battle seed in the engine's `sodium,<32 hex>` form, bytes 16 to 23 are the two sides' team draws (the caller takes them modulo its pool) and bytes 24 to 27 seed a scripted baseline's stream. `planGeneration` calls it, and it was moved out of the planner byte for byte: **no seed, plan or committed hash changed**, so the tiny-loop determinism tests pass unchanged. `policyStreamLabel(seed, game, player, policy_id)` is the per-seat policy stream label (protocol D9 with the game index); `sampleU` adds the request number to it and keeps its draw. A test pins the vector `league-seed/1` gives for four (run key, generation, game) triples and one `sampleU` draw, all computed outside the TS code with Python's hashlib, and the Python `league_game_seed` reproduces the same four. Seeds and stream labels are the harness's: the worker is given only the draw `u`, never a seed, and no observation or `TrainingExample` carries one. The `LeagueRunRecord` (contract v1, integers only, hashed by `leagueRunRecordHash`) is written as `league-run.json` beside `plan.json` for each generation (`playGeneration` builds it, `writeGeneration` writes it before `generation.json`). It holds `run_id` (the run key), `run_config_hash`, `generation`, `seed_derivation` (the schema fixes `league-seed/1`), `plan_hash` (SHA-256 of `plan.json`'s JCS bytes), `pair_policy_id`, the counts (`games`, `played`, `forfeits`, `episodes`) and `opponents` (games per `opponent_policy_id`). Reader rules the profile cannot say, in `readLeagueRunRecord` and `read_league_run_record`: `played + forfeits = games`, two Episodes per played game, opponents sorted and unique and summing to `games`. `checkLeagueRunRecord` and the Python reader tie a record to its `TrainingRunConfig` (run key and hash). The Python loop driver reads `league-run.json` and `plan.json` after each generation and refuses one whose record belongs to another run, config, generation or pair, whose plan does not hash to `plan_hash`, whose forfeits differ from `generation.json`, or whose plan carries a battle seed the run key does not give. The league scheduler, the `L2` lineage and the exploiters (#844, #845) build on this and are not here. Damage-aware training opponent (ADR-018 slice 3, #945): `Baseline.actor` gained `damage-aware` (a minor change within v1; `baseline_games` holds up to three entries; an older reader refuses such a config), and `LeagueRunRecord` gained the optional `baselines` (policy id, baseline identity hash, games and the nine summed per-game counts of `DamageAwareActor.report()`; absent for every other run, so those keep their bytes; reader rules: sorted and unique, games equal the opponent entry's, probe runs and unsupported queries at most the queries, preview plus scored decisions at most the decisions; one identity hash per policy id per generation or the generation fails). `league-generation.ts` builds a fresh `damageAwareActor` per baseline game from `config/baselines/damage-aware.v1.json` and the mechanics config (golden vocabulary: the committed format only), seeded with bytes 24-27 of `league-seed/1` (drawn only when `epsilon_ppm` > 0), and requires the config's `policy_id` to be the one the actor plays as; the seat is never queried against the worker and the loader still takes only the pair's seats. Wall-clock cost is not recorded in the run files (see `docs/training-run.md` for the measured figures). `checkLeagueRunRecord` and the Python reader also require `baselines` to list exactly the config's damage-aware entries (policy id and games); the one-identity-hash rule is enforced when the record is built.

**Progress evaluation** (ADR-017 slices 4 and 5, `packages/showdown-bridge/src/progress-*.ts`, `packages/team-dataset/src/progress-pool.ts`, `packages/team-dataset/scripts/progress.ts`, `ml/areneon_ml/progress/`, `python -m areneon_ml.loop progress`; the docs section is `docs/training-run.md` section 4). A progress view of a committed generation, never the Training gate: the frozen pair plays `random-legal@v1` and `actor-rule@v1`, each in sample and argmax mode, on the VALIDATION split of the run's `team_dataset` (a run without one is refused), read through `readDataset` (`datasetValidationPool`; the bridge cannot import the team-dataset package, so `runProgressCli` takes the pool as a port, as `runGenerationCli` does). Design B-i (version 1 records; a version 2 record plays pair `k` twice, see below): game `k` of every match is the disjoint ordered validation pair `k` (`progress-pairing/1`: SHA-256 order of `{run_key, canonical_team_hash, purpose: "progress-pairing"}`, cut into consecutive pairs; more games than pairs is refused, never wrapped) with the seed of `progress-seed/1` (`progressGameStreams`: SHA-256 of the JCS of `{run_key, game, purpose: "progress-game"}`, bytes 0 to 15 the battle seed, bytes 16 to 19 the baseline stream; the ADR vector is pinned in TS and Python), the candidate on p1 for an even game and p2 for an odd one, no seat swap (v1). The same pairs and seeds serve every opponent, mode and generation. Refused before any game: a validation team whose played-form hash is in the newest generation's `team-set.json`, a progress seed equal to a seed of any committed `plan.json` (checked in TS and again in Python, at evaluation time against the plans committed then: a later generation's plan is not covered by an earlier record and generation 0 sees no plans yet; the practical risk is about 2^-128 because the purpose strings differ), more games than the pairs allow (v1: `--games` above half the dataset manifest's validation teams; v2: above the number of validation teams, two games per pair; Python checks before the worker starts, Node refuses exactly), a non-synthetic checkpoint record. A1 `policyGame` with `guardActor`, one worker query at a time (`n_parallel` 1), worker on CPU; a policy forfeit is a scored loss for the failing side with its cause, a harness incident fails the evaluation and commits nothing. The contracts are `ProgressEvaluationRecord` and `ProgressGameRecord` (`packages/schema/contracts/v1`, integers only, `data_class` the constant `synthetic`; the `previous` opponent variant carries the previous pair's checkpoint ids). The readers (`progress-records.ts`, `areneon_ml.progress.records`) add the rules the profile cannot say: counts equal the game rows, matches sorted and unique, rows complete with alternating seats, forfeits and turn-cap ties recounted, a stored game's choices among its candidates, team hashes, `omniscient_log_sha256` (SHA-256 of the JCS of the log's lines) and the seed recomputed; the Python reader also ties a record to its run config and checks each policy id from its checkpoints and mode (`policy_id(preview, battle, mode)`; sample and argmax are different policies). The Wilson interval (`progress/stats.py`, integer arithmetic only, parts per million) is derived on display, never stored, and always labelled "conditional on the fixed validation plan" (a deviation from protocol 6.2: an erratum candidate). For a schema-version-2 record (every pair from both seats) the games are not independent, so `summary_of`, the dashboard and the ablation use the pair-level percentile bootstrap instead (#948, ADR-017 E2 items 2 and 6; `stats.pair_interval`: 4,000 resamples of pairs, ranks 100 and 3,899 of the sorted integer sums, a SHA-256 counter stream seeded from the run key, generation and statistic label, parts per million rounded outward; labelled descriptive and conditional, no multiplicity control; `docs/training-run.md`); version 1 keeps Wilson unchanged. **Both seats, anchors, schema version 2 (ADR-017 erratum E2, #939 and #947).** A new evaluation writes a `ProgressEvaluationRecordV2` (`progress-evaluation-record-2.schema.json`, `schema_version` 2, beside the unchanged version 1; `readProgressEvaluationRecordV2` and `read_progress_record`, which dispatches on `schema_version`; a v1 root is verified and kept, never rewritten; what this paragraph's predecessors say about alternating seats, one game per pair or game index `k` describes v1). Every validation pair is played TWICE: game g is pair g>>1, the candidate on p1 for an even g and p2 for an odd g, the teams stay on their seats and both games share the pair's `progress-seed/1` seed (`ProgressGameRecord` gained the optional `pair`, its seed then being the pair's; the sampling `u` still differs because the seat is in its label), so `--games` is even and the pair is the unit of analysis. Anchors: `--anchors 0,10,20` (those earlier than the evaluated generation), `--anchor-games` (even, default 200), matches `anchor-g<A>-<mode>`, opponent `{kind: "anchor", generation, policy_id, checkpoints}`, one worker per anchor (`--anchor G,PREVIEW,BATTLE,POLICY_SAMPLE,POLICY_ARGMAX` and `--anchor-games` on the Node side; the fields are comma separated because a policy id holds a colon); `plan.anchor_games_per_match` is present exactly when anchor matches are, `teams.pairs_used` is half the largest games. The Python side verifies each anchor's checkpoint and policy ids from generation A's own records (`_check_anchors`, at evaluation and on verification). Not here, a separate ticket: the replay pages for v2 (#951); `summary_of` and the dashboard give the pair-level interval (#948, below). **Team Preview ablation (#940).** `python -m areneon_ml.loop progress --generation G --preview-ablation` (G >= 1, alone: no `--anchors`, no `--previous-matches`, not `--all`) plays G's Battle network, argmax, against `random-legal@v1` and `actor-rule@v1` with (i) its own Preview, (ii) a uniformly random Preview and (iii) generation 0's Preview, as six `preview-<learned|random|g0>-<baseline>-argmax` matches of a `ProgressEvaluationRecordV2` (optional match field `candidate_preview` `{kind: learned|random|generation(+generation, checkpoint)}`; same pairs, seeds and both seats). The random Preview is `policyActor`'s `preview_actor` (`PolicySeat.random_preview`): `randomLegalActor` over the engine-judged `request.legal` of the candidate's OWN Preview request, seeded by `randomPreviewSeed` (the first 8 hex digits of SHA-256 over the JCS of the policy stream label of seed, pair, seat and `random-preview@v1`); the worker is not asked, `assertOwnTeamStats` still runs. Generation 0's Preview runs in a third worker (`--ablation-preview GEN,PREVIEW,POLICY_ID_ARGMAX` and `--ablation-random-policy-id` on the Node side; the worker takes any Preview and Battle pair). A candidate's policy id is `policy_id(preview or 'random-preview@v1' or the earlier checkpoint, battle, mode)` (`candidate_preview_id`); the Python side checks the earlier Preview against that generation's own records (`_check_ablation`). `progress/ablation.py` derives, per baseline and variant, the mean pair score and, against the learned Preview, the mean paired difference and the better/equal/worse pair counts, in integers, each with its pair-level interval (#948). The Python command reads the run read-only (no run lock; the generation manifest is verified), builds `<run-dir>.progress/.g<N>.partial` (never inside the run), has Node write `progress.json` and `games/<match>-<k>.json` (the first `--record-games`, default 8), re-reads and cross-checks every file, then writes `manifest.json` last and commits with one `os.replace` under an `flock` on `.lock`; a committed directory is verified and skipped, never rewritten. `--all` evaluates the committed generations whose number is a multiple of 5, generation 0 included (`scheduled_generations`, `SCHEDULE_EVERY`; ADR-017 E2 item 3, #950); `--generation G` evaluates any one. **Previous-generation matches (slice 5, #924; opt-in since E2, #950).** With `--previous-matches` (`progress_generation(previous_matches=True)`; off by default, a no-op at generation 0) the pair from generation 1 also plays the previous generation's frozen pair `P_(G-1)`: `previous-sample` (sample against sample) and `previous-argmax` (argmax against argmax), 6 matches instead of 4. The plan, pairs, seats and seeds are the baselines' exactly (`progress-seed/1` has no match identity: the ADR reuses game index `k` across matches on purpose, and `previous-*` games hold a different pair of policy ids, so the sampling draws `u` (hashed over seat and policy id) differ); the match row names the opponent as `{kind: "previous", policy_id, checkpoints}` with the checkpoint ids of generation G-1, and the printed summary labels it "seen, same-lineage comparison: it can cycle". One worker session holds one frozen pair, so `runProgressCli` starts a second `PolicyWorkerClient` for `P_(G-1)` (flags `--previous-preview|battle|policy-id-sample|policy-id-argmax`, all or none; both clients `beginGame` before each previous game) and each seat queries its own client; a Python request carries `previous: PreviousPair` (None at generation 0 and whenever the flag is off; the status says `none: generation 0 has no previous generation` or `none: previous-generation matches are opt-in ...`). `_check_previous` refuses a record with `previous-*` matches that were not asked for, one asked for without both, and any whose opponent checkpoints or policy ids are not the verified generation G-1's. The runner records a choice when its actor answers, which with two workers can come in either seat order, so `buildProgressGameRecord` stores the decisions ordered by `request_seq` then seat (without it two reruns of a previous match differed in bytes). A root committed by slice 4 has no previous matches at generation 1 and up: it is verified and skipped, never rewritten (a committed generation without them is skipped whatever the flag says), and the output says `previous: none: ... evaluate into a new --progress-dir to add them` (a progress root is atomic per generation, so adding matches would mean a second record version; a new progress dir gives the same baseline bytes again). Measured (this machine, shared, CPU, batch 1): about 0.6 s per game and about 25 s of start-up per command, so 600 games with `--previous-matches` at generation 1 and up is about 6.5 minutes (#940). The game records hold the omniscient log of both sides: an evaluation artifact for the owner, refused by `readEpisode` and the training loader (a test passes one to the loader). The corpus samples avoid the `private` label (the guard refuses it anywhere), so the class tests flip it in code.

**Damage-aware opponent in the progress evaluation** (ADR-018 slice 4, #952; opt-in: `python -m areneon_ml.loop progress --damage-aware [--damage-aware-budget-minutes 30]`, Node `--damage-aware-budget-ms`). `playProgress` takes `damage_aware: {setup, match_budget_ms, clock?}` (`setup` is `damageAwareSetup(engine, format)`, the reviewed records of slice 3) and adds `damage-aware-argmax` and `damage-aware-sample` beside the other baselines: the same pairs, seeds (`progress-seed/1` unchanged; `epsilon_ppm` must be 0, so the baseline draws nothing, and the actor still gets bytes 16-19 as its seed), seats and `PolicyGame` path as `actor-rule`, a fresh `damageAwareActor` per game behind `guardActor` (role `scripted`), never queried against a worker, and it sees only its own channel and `ActorContext.team`, so the omniscient game record keeps its rules. Its opponent entry is `{kind: "baseline", policy_id: "damage-aware@v1"}`. Added within v1 (existing records keep their bytes and hashes, a root committed earlier is verified and skipped): `GameRow.baseline_counts` (the baseline seat's nine integer counts for that game) and `Match.baseline` `{identity_hash, counts}` (the sum over the rows) in `ProgressEvaluationRecordV2`, and `ProgressGameRecord.baseline` `{seat, identity_hash, counts}` for a stored game. Reader rules (TS `progress-records.ts`, Python `progress/records.py`): only a damage-aware match carries them, every row of it does, the match's counts are the rows' sum, probe runs and unsupported queries do not exceed queries, preview plus scored decisions do not exceed decisions, one identity hash per record, a stored game's `seat` is the one whose policy is damage-aware and its decisions count equals that seat's recorded decisions (one more is allowed after a forfeit), and the Python run ties each stored game to its match and row (`check_game_baseline`). The identity hash is recomputed by `playProgress` from the records and the pinned engine (`damageAwareIdentity`) and every game's report must equal it. A match whose elapsed wall-clock passes the budget (checked after every game) throws `ProgressError` and commits nothing; the printed line and the Python result carry `game_ms` (mean and slowest game per match) and the damage-aware status, the records no time. Rows are labelled `seen scripted baseline: never held-out evidence` in `summary_of`, the dashboard (a counts table) and the replay pages. Tests: `packages/team-dataset/test/progress.test.ts` and `progress.python.test.ts` (real worker), `tests/ml/progress/test_damage_aware.py`, `test_report_v2.py`, `test_progress_replay_v2.py`, and the corpus samples `progress-evaluation-record-2/*/baseline-*` and `damage-aware-match`, `progress-game-record/*/baseline-*` and `damage-aware`.

**damage-aware@v3 as an evaluation and training opponent** (#1183; builds on #1180 and the `--teacher` choice of #1182). `python -m areneon_ml.loop progress --damage-aware [--damage-aware-version v1|v3]` (Node `--damage-aware-version`, which needs `--damage-aware-budget-ms`; the Python command refuses a version without `--damage-aware` and an unknown one before any work) plays `damage-aware@v3`, the v1 scorer with the engine's choice-location convention, from `config/baselines/damage-aware.v3.json` (`damageAwareSetup(engine, format, "v3")`; `playProgress` takes `damage_aware.version`, absent is `v1`). **v1 is the default and unchanged**: its match ids (`damage-aware-argmax`/`-sample`), opponent entry, argv to the simulator side, records and bytes are exactly as before (the Python request names the version to Node only for v3). The v3 matches are `damage-aware-v3-argmax` and `damage-aware-v3-sample` (`damageAwareMatchPrefix`; they cannot collide with v1's and sort after them), the opponent is `{kind: "baseline", policy_id: "damage-aware@v3"}`, the pairs, seeds and seats are the baselines' (`progress-seed/1` unchanged), and the identity hash, per-game counts and match sums are as for v1 (the identity differs because the record does; one version per evaluation, so the one-identity rule holds). The actor refuses a record that is not the policy id's version (a v1 record never plays as v3). `damageAwareBaseline` takes the policy id (default v1). The readers need no change: `isDamageAware` / `is_damage_aware` match on `damage-aware@`; Python `_check_damage_aware` takes the asked version (None for a committed root, where it reads the version from the matches and requires one whole set of one known version), and a committed v3 root is verified and skipped like any other. The dashboard's counts table and the "seen scripted baseline" label follow the `baseline` block, so v3 rows are labelled by their match id; a v3 replay page names its policy id after "the damage-aware scripted baseline" (v1 pages keep their wording); the move-use diagnostic marks any `damage-aware@` opponent as the reference, v3 included (pinned by a test over both ids). **Training baseline:** a `TrainingRunConfig` baseline `{actor: "damage-aware", policy_id: "damage-aware@v3"}` plays the v3 record (`GenerationOptions.damage_aware_v3`, read from `config/` when a plan game needs it; `DAMAGE_AWARE_V3_POLICY_ID`); any other damage-aware policy id besides `@v2` still reads the v1 record (so `damage-aware-x@v1` keeps failing as it did), `LeagueRunRecord.baselines` lists `damage-aware@v3` with its own identity hash and counts as it does v1, and the checker ties it to the config. No contract changed (the schemas take any `damage-aware@` policy id); new corpus samples `progress-evaluation-record-2/damage-aware-v3-match` and `progress-game-record/damage-aware-v3`. Tests: `team-dataset/test/progress.test.ts` (v3 ids, policy, identity differs from v1, other matches equal v1's, rerun byte-identical, a v1 record cannot play as v3), `progress.python.test.ts` (the real worker through the Python command, twice, byte-identical; a lone version is a usage error), `showdown-bridge/test/league-generation.test.ts` and `progress-records.test.ts`, `tests/ml/progress/test_damage_aware.py` and `test_main.py`, `tests/ml/inspector/test_progress_replay_v2.py`, `tests/ml/report/test_move_use.py`. Not here: v3 in the private test (`private-test` still plays v1), and v3 as the Training-gate opponent (a separate decision).

**Search candidate and the `self-no-search` control in the progress evaluation and the head to head** (ADR-026 sections 5, 8 and 9 slice P, #1146; `packages/showdown-bridge/src/search-progress.ts`, `progress-evaluation.ts` `ProgressOptions.search`, `progress-cli.ts` `--search-mode`, `packages/team-dataset/src/progress-search-setup.ts`, `ml/areneon_ml/progress/{run,records}.py`; a pair without the option keeps every record, id and byte). `python -m areneon_ml.loop progress --search-mode argmax|sample` evaluates the generation's frozen pair PLAYED WITH decision-time search (the committed `rerank-1.v1` config, `progressSearchSetup`: the generator-matched belief, the golden vocabulary, `searchSetup`) instead of the pair itself, in that ONE play mode (the pair's strongest no-search mode, declared by the caller; the search falls back to it, so it is also the control's mode). It is evaluated ALONE (a "search record": every match is a search match; no `--anchors`, `--previous-matches` or `--preview-ablation`; a pair that reads candidate features is refused, `SearchError` `features_pair`; use a progress directory of its own, a committed directory of the other kind is refused). Matches are `search-<opponent>-<mode>` against `actor-rule`, `random-legal`, the opt-in damage-aware baseline, `--versus-run-dir` (the head to head against another run's pair: `search-run-<mode>`, the usual refusal of the same run key stays) and the control `search-self-no-search-<mode>`. The candidate's `policy_id` is `policyId(pair, mode, play, undefined, search_identity_hash)` (slice R part 1), so the Python record reader derives it (`policy_id(..., search=match.search.identity_hash)`). **Control opponent kind (N-C, minor change within v2):** `{kind: "self-no-search", policy_id, checkpoints, mode}` is the SAME pair played by `policyActor` without search in the match's mode (its ordinary id); both readers tie it to the run (checkpoints equal the record's pair, id equals the pair's id in that mode) and the mode to the match's; the head-to-head tool's refusal of a run against itself is unchanged for every other kind. **Counts (optional, absent keeps every byte):** `Match.search {identity_hash, counts}` (the sum of the rows'), `Match.opponent_search` (the control's, same shape, its `identity_hash` is the search policy's it belongs to), `GameRow.search` / `GameRow.opponent_search` (per game, counts only) and `ProgressGameRecord.search` / `opponent_search` `{seat, identity_hash, counts}`. `SearchCounts` is the actor's per-game counts as integers (decisions, searched decisions, fallbacks by reason, worlds asked/built/refused, redraws, legality redraws, refusals by code and by `check:class`, belief refusals, worlds dropped by reason, resampled counters, worlds scored, turns, wait continuations, leaf queries / memo hits / game ends / hp-ko leaves, replies drawn); the open-keyed tables are lists of `{key, count}` sorted by key, because contracts are closed (`searchCountsRecord`). The control carries the same block with searched decisions 0, every search count 0 and only the structural fallbacks it shares with the search (Team Preview, forced switch, fewer than two non-pending candidates; `controlSeat`), so decisions and fallbacks are counted for both sides; wall time is printed, never stored. Reader rules (`checkSearchOfMatch` / `_check_search_of_match`, `checkSearchOfGame`): a match is named `search-...` exactly when it carries `search`; every row has it and the match counts are the rows' sum; searched plus fallback decisions do not exceed decisions, built plus refused worlds do not exceed asked, scored do not exceed built; a self-no-search match needs the control's counts on every row, equal to the sum, and a quiet control; one search identity per record, search matches only in a search record (`a search record holds search matches only`); a stored game's search seat decisions equal that seat's recorded decisions (one more after a forfeit), the control's seat is the other one with the same identity hash, and `check_game_search` ties each stored game to its match and row. `PolicySeat.make` lets a seat build its actor from the options `policyGame` gives `policyActor` (the search and the control use it; both are guarded as role `policy`); a search game's actor `report()` must carry the evaluation's policy id and identity. **Labels** (everywhere a search result is shown: `summary_of` (`search.label/prior_label/note`, the control's `comparison`), the dashboard's "Decision-time search counts" table and the match rows, the replay pages and index): "learned pair with decision-time search (one-ply re-rank)", the prior label "belief matched to the synthetic generator: an upper bound for this evaluation" (the committed config's, pinned by a test), "search results are never held-out evidence", and the control "control: the same frozen pair played without search". **Evaluation-dataset route:** a run whose validation split is smaller than 2n teams is evaluated on a larger evaluation-only dataset through the existing `--eval-dataset-dir/--eval-dataset-hash` (#974, `max_pairs`). This ticket adds the disjointness #974 did not cover: with the option, `runProgressCli` takes the canonical and played-form hashes of every dev and TRAINING record of the RUN's own dataset (`ProgressPorts.run_teams`, `datasetTrainingHashes`: the whole splits the loop draws from, not only the teams drawn into `team-set.json`) into `training_teams`, and `progressPlan` also refuses a pool team whose canonical hash is in it (`a validation team is a dev or training team of the run's own dataset`). The Node command enforces it (the dataset is read there); it is not a rule of the record or of the Python reader. The control plays the search's own fallback mode (`--search-mode`), so the declared mode is the "strongest no-search mode" of ADR-026 only if the caller declares it so. Tests: `team-dataset/test/progress-search.test.ts` (real engine and opponent model, stand-in worker: match ids, both sides' counts, the control, stored games, rerun byte-identical, refusals, the own-dataset hashes), `progress.python.test.ts` (the real worker through the Python command, twice, byte-identical), `showdown-bridge/test/progress-records.test.ts`, `tests/ml/progress/test_search.py`, `tests/ml/report/test_report_v2.py`, `tests/ml/inspector/test_progress_replay_v2.py` and the `progress-evaluation-record-2/*search*`, `*control*`, `*self-no-search*` and `progress-game-record/*search*` corpus samples. Not here: S0 and E-S1 (experiment tickets), search in training, the scripted head-to-head with search arms, the `private-test` with search, previous/anchor opponents of a search record.
**Extra play modes and the play-mode gap** (ADR-023 section 1, section 2, #1044 and #1042; `packages/showdown-bridge/src/progress-modes.ts`, `ml/areneon_ml/progress/modes.py`, `gap.py`; the docs paragraph is `docs/training-run.md` section 4). `progress --modes sample:t<ppm>,sample:th<ppm>,sample:t<ppm>:th<ppm>` (a closed grammar, ppm 1 to 999999, an explicit 1,000,000 refused; `argmax` and `sample` are always played and may be listed) adds extra play modes, each a sample at a declared temperature and/or probability threshold, to the baseline matches (`random-legal@v1`, `actor-rule@v1`, `damage-aware@v1`/`v3`) and, with `--versus-run-dir`, to the head-to-head with BOTH sides in the mode (the other run's pair gets its own policy id in the mode, computed from its checkpoints and its own feature hash); anchors and previous-generation matches keep their two modes; not with `--preview-ablation` or `--search-mode`. The matches are named `<opponent>-sample[-t<ppm>][-th<ppm>]` (one fixed rule) and the version 2 `Match` gained the optional `temperature_ppm` and `truncation` (`{kind: "threshold", min_ppm}`; a minor change within version 2: a reader older than the fields rejects such a record; absent keeps today's bytes and hash). Reader rules, both sides: the fields only on a `sample` match against a baseline or another run's pair, never a search or ablation match, the match id ends in `-sample` plus the settings' suffix (and a plain match is not named for settings), and the Python reader recomputes the candidate's `policy_id` from the pair, the mode and the settings (`policy_id(..., temperature_ppm, truncation)`); the TS reader has no policy-id constants, as before. `ProgressOptions.extra_modes` (TS) takes each mode with the policy ids the Python side computed (`--extra-mode <mode>,<candidate-policy-id>[,<versus-policy-id>]` on the Node command); the candidate seat gets `play` in its `PolicySeat`, so the query carries `temperature_ppm`/`truncation`, and the other run's seat gets the same. `_check_extra_modes` (Python) requires the extra matches to be exactly the modes asked for, each against every opponent that plays plain sample, with the sibling's opponent (the other run's pair: its id in the mode). The **play-mode gap** is derived on display and never stored (`progress/gap.py`, `play_mode_gap(record)`): for each baseline and the cross-run head-to-head of a version 2 record, per pair `d_k` = argmax pair score minus the mode's pair score (`paired_differences`), the mean and the pair-level bootstrap interval (`pair_interval`, stream label `gap:<opponent>:argmax:<mode>`, `sample` or `sample:t500000`...), the scores and the pairs argmax did better, equally and worse; labelled "diagnostic: play-mode gap, conditional on the fixed validation plan"; a version 1 record says "not available: version 1 record"; anchor and previous-generation rows carry "both sides play the same mode; the play-mode gap is not read from them"; a Preview ablation or search record has no gap. It is in the printed result (`play_mode_gap`; `summary_of` also gained `mode`, the label, for every match), the dashboard (a "Play-mode gap" section, a Mode column in the matches table, the mode in the generation table; the generation charts keep the plain modes only) and the replay pages (the mode label in the seat and context words). Over the local `public-main-003` progress roots the rows reproduce ADR-023 M1: g50 against `damage-aware@v1` +19.0 points [+10.5, +27.0] (the ticket's upper bound +27.5 came from the throwaway script's own resamples; this stream label gives 27.0). Tests: `tests/ml/progress/test_play_modes.py`, `packages/showdown-bridge/test/progress-modes.test.ts`, `packages/team-dataset/test/progress.test.ts` ("extra play modes"), `progress-modes.test.ts` (the command's flag), `progress.python.test.ts` (real workers, byte-identical rerun, today's bytes without the flag), the corpus samples `progress-evaluation-record-2/*/extra-mode-*` and the report cases in `tests/ml/report/test_report_v2.py`. Not here: extra modes for anchors, any gate use.

**Head-to-head between runs** (ADR-010 erratum A4 item 6, #1003; builds the `run` opponent kind that #969 describes: one implementation). `python -m areneon_ml.loop progress --run-dir A --generation G --versus-run-dir B --versus-generation H [--run-games 200] --progress-dir NEW` adds `run-sample` and `run-argmax` to the evaluation of run A's generation G: B's frozen pair, each pair from both seats on A's plan and `progress-seed/1` seeds, pair-level interval from `stats.py`, summary `diff_*` (pair score minus 1/2) and the label "cross-run comparison". Run B is read only (no lock, never written): `_versus_pair` (`progress/run.py`) refuses the same directory or run key, another format or `team_dataset.dataset_hash`, a run without a team dataset, an uncommitted generation, a non-synthetic record, and `versus_relation` compared pairs that share a checkpoint or one in the other's ancestry (runs with equal init seeds share the seeded generation-0 pair: labelled, not refused; it builds the C1/C6 graph from both runs' records, one node per shared checkpoint id, every generation verified, for the printed `head_to_head.lineage`: "independent lineage" or "related lineage: shared start"). TS: `ProgressOptions.versus` / `run_games` (a worker per run: `--versus`, `--versus-dir`, `--versus-encoder-version`, `--versus-team-set`, `--run-games` of `progress-cli.ts`; the other run's team set joins the training teams and its plans' seeds the league seeds). Record: opponent `{kind: "run", run_id, run_config_hash, generation, policy_id, checkpoints}` (minor change within v2), `plan.run_games_per_match` exactly when run matches are, matches named `run-<mode>`, never the record's own `run_id`. Dashboard section "Head to head against another run", replay wording, samples `run-*`. With `--eval-dataset-dir/--eval-dataset-hash` the two runs may name different team datasets (the evaluation dataset's validation split is the common one; #1244, EXP-M1), and that dataset must not be the other run's own training dataset nor hold any dev or training team of EITHER run (the Python side refuses the dataset; the Node command, which reads the datasets, also refuses it with a `UsageError` and takes `ports.run_teams` of the other run's config, from `--versus-dir`'s `run-config.json`, into `training_teams` beside the evaluated run's, so `progressPlan` refuses a validation team in either run's whole dev or training split); without it the same-dataset rule stays. Opt-in, not with the Preview ablation, not with `--all`; a committed directory is verified and kept (lenient without the flag). Tests: `tests/ml/progress/test_versus.py`, the `progress.python.test.ts` case with two real tiny runs.
**Move-use diagnostic** (#1164; `ml/areneon_ml/report/move_use.py`, `packages/showdown-bridge/src/move-classes.ts`, `packages/schema/contracts/v1/move-class-table.schema.json`; part of the proof plan #1163, reported beside a proof and never an adoption measure). A read-only computation over the stored `ProgressGameRecord`s of a committed progress generation (head-to-head matches included, the `run-*` ones): per match and seat (the candidate and the opponent; a damage-aware opponent is marked as the reference), the slot actions in each move class and how often a move of a class was among a slot's candidates and not chosen. It is labelled "descriptive move-use diagnostic: not a strength measure" and ranks nothing. It is in the printed `progress` result (`move_use` per generation, integers) and in a dashboard section "Move-use diagnostic" (two tables: chosen count and share per class; not chosen of offered), with the class table's JCS hash and the engine-data hash on the page and both fixture files among the page's inputs. Only the stored games count (the first `--record-games` of each match), so a count is over those games, not the 200. The classes are a `MoveClassTable` (contract v1, sorted lists of move ids, closed; golden `fixtures/showdown/<format>/move-classes.json` and `.summary.json`, MIT-derived like the engine-data table and under its `NOTICE`; `node packages/showdown-bridge/scripts/export-move-classes.ts` rewrites them after a pin bump or a rule change, and `test/move-classes.test.ts` re-exports and compares byte for byte). Decision where the ticket left room: the fields the classes need (boosts, secondaries, side condition, weather, terrain, pseudo-weather, stalling move) are NOT added to the engine-data export, because that is a schema_version 2 and moves the engine-data hash that every checkpoint, encoder version and worker handshake is bound to; the table is a sibling export that names the engine-data table by hash (`engine_data_hash`), its ids resolve in it and its `status` list must equal the table's Status moves exactly (`readMoveClassTable`, `read_move_classes`, which also refuses a table of another engine-data table, format or mod). The rules are the exporter's (reviewed code over the engine's own fields, no move name): `status` is the Status category; `lowers_target_stat` a boosts table with a negative stage on a foe-target move or a secondary with chance 100 that lowers one; `raises_own_stat` a positive boosts table on a self or ally target, a `self`/`selfBoost` boost, or a chance-100 secondary self boost; `side_condition` a `sideCondition`; `field_condition` a weather, terrain or pseudo-weather; `protection` the engine's `stallingMove` (Protect, Detect, King's Shield, Spiky Shield, Baneful Bunker, Endure; Wide and Quick Guard are side conditions). A move may be in several classes. Not read: effects an engine callback produces (Parting Shot, Belly Drum, Haze at this pin) and secondaries with a chance below 100; every `move_targets` kind must be in the exporter's friendly, foe or no-Pokemon group, so a pin that adds one refuses the export. `switch` is the one class the Python side makes: a `switch N` slot action of a move request (a forced replacement after a faint is a forced switch request and is not counted); there is no separate speed-control class (Tailwind and Trick Room are side and field conditions, Icy Wind a stat-lowering move).
