How a candidate becomes a move: the record keeps no request, so `move N` is the N-th move of the Pokemon in that slot, rebuilt from the omniscient log's `|switch|`, `|drag|`, `|replace|`, `|faint|` and `|swap|` lines and the seat's own team (display name, the engine lists a set's moves in the set's order), at the frame `decision_points` places the decision. A slot action is `unresolved`, counted apart and never guessed, when its Pokemon cannot be named, the slot offers a move number past the set's moves or only `move 1` for a set of several (a lock, a charge turn, a recharge, Struggle), or the seat used in that game a move its set does not hold outside a `[from]` call (Transform's copied moves). Known limits: an Illusion user's slot may name the disguise; a refused choice and its retry count once (the retry); about 5 percent of slot actions are unresolved on the local progress records. Reading the games is the cost: `progress_move_use` runs every stored game through `read_game_record` (about 0.1 s each), so a dashboard over 200 stored games per match takes minutes. Memory (#1245): `stored_games` is a generator and `progress_diagnostics` (`dominated.py`) folds each game into both tallies (`MoveUseTally`, `DominatedTally`) in one pass, to the same bytes (a test compares it with the held-list result): one parsed game alive at a time (about 1.5 MB of heap per stored game, measured with tracemalloc on `public-main-011` g50); memory does not grow with the number of stored games. `verify_progress_dir` ties a match's stored games by the exact name `<match_id>-<k>.json` and the record's own `match_id`, not by prefix, and refuses a stored game that belongs to no match. The ticket allowed adding the statically dominated rate of #1025 (ADR-022 M2) to the same pass; it is not here, because it needs its own reviewed config record with a versioned hash, the type chart and the targeted species of each action, and the M2 definition. Tests: `tests/ml/report/test_move_use.py` (hand-built games with known counts, the pinned classes, the table refusals, the page with CSP, hostile strings and a negative control), `tests/ml/progress/test_main.py`, `packages/showdown-bridge/test/move-classes.test.ts` and the `move-class-table` corpus samples.

**Dominated-move rate** (#1025; `ml/areneon_ml/report/dominated.py`, `config/diagnostics/<format>/dominated-move.v1.json`; ADR-022 measurement M2 and slice M; a secondary diagnostic, never a gate, stop, reward, mask or adoption measure). Beside the move-use diagnostic, over the same stored `ProgressGameRecord`s (`move_use.stored_games` reads a generation once; `progress_move_use` and `progress_dominated` take its games, in `progress` and in the dashboard model; the head-to-head `run-*` matches are matches like any other). Labelled everywhere "approximate static proxy: not a mechanics claim, never a gate or adoption measure". For each slot action that uses a damaging move against the opposing actives it computes a static score in exact rationals: base power times the type-chart cell values of the target's shown types, times a same-type factor when the move's type is one of the user's shown types; a spread move is the sum over the alive foes times a spread factor, a random-target move the mean over them. The action is *dominated* when its score is below the threshold share of the best score among the same Pokemon's enabled damaging moves (the slot's `move N` candidates, each on its best target) and *immune* when its score is 0 while that best is positive (an immune action is also dominated). Counted apart and left out of the rates: status moves, moves aimed at the ally, damaging-category moves with no base power, actions the table cannot score (a species or move the engine-data lacks, a fainted target, no foe on the field), and unresolved slots (the move-use rules). The slot's Pokemon is resolved as for the move-use diagnostic; the shown species per slot follows `|switch|`, `|drag|`, `|replace|`, `|swap|`, `|faint|` and `|detailschange|` (a temporary type change is not tracked). A foe's location in `move N L` is the engine's (L is the foe in active slot L - 1, #1180). No rule in code (owner rule R7): move type, category, base power and target, species types and the chart cells come from the hashed engine-data export; the value of each `damage_taken` code (`effectiveness_ppm`: 0 normal, 1 super effective, 2 resisted, 3 immune), the same-type, spread and threshold factors (ppm) and which move targets are single, spread or random live in the reviewed, versioned record `config/diagnostics/<format>/dominated-move.v1.json` (closed keys; `read_dominated_table` refuses an unknown or missing key, a non-integer, a code the chart uses but the list lacks, a target kind the engine-data lacks or in two groups, and another engine-data table by its `engine_data_hash`; a new version is a new file). Its JCS hash (`config_hash`) and the engine-data hash are on the page and the file is among the page's inputs. Output per match and seat (candidate and opponent, a damage-aware opponent marked as the reference): scored, dominated, immune and the apart counts, in the printed `progress` result (`dominated` per generation, integers; the Wilson interval is derived, `progress/stats.py`) and a dashboard table "Statically dominated damaging moves" whose caption says the interval is binomial and not clustered by game. Only the stored games count (the first `--record-games` of each match). Tests: `tests/ml/report/test_dominated.py` (hand-built games with worked answers: a best move, a score exactly at the threshold, a dominated spread move, an immune target, a spread move summed over alive foes, a fainted target, status, ally target, a switch and an empty candidate list; the config refusals; the page, no table without games or config, a hostile canary) and `tests/ml/progress/test_main.py`. Measured on `public-main-002` g30 (48 stored games, read-only): see the ticket; the ADR's M2 table came from a throwaway script over more games and its figures are not reproduced to the digit.

**Scripted head-to-head** (ADR-025 §13, #1130; `packages/showdown-bridge/src/scripted-h2h*.ts`, `packages/team-dataset/scripts/scripted-h2h.ts`, `ml/areneon_ml/progress/scripted.py`; the docs section is `docs/training-run.md` section 7). `python -m areneon_ml.loop scripted-h2h --config C --arm-a damage-aware@v1 --arm-b damage-aware@v2 --pairs N --out D [--eval-dataset-dir DIR --eval-dataset-hash H] [--game-processes S] [--wall-time-cap-minutes M] [--looks FILE]` plays two scripted baselines against each other on the ADR-017 E2 plan, with no learned policy and no worker: the validation split's disjoint pairs (`progress-pairing/1`, `progressPlan`), every pair twice (game g is pair g >> 1, arm a on p1 for an even g and p2 for an odd one, teams on their seats), `progress-seed/1` seeds, the pair as the unit. The arms are `actor-rule@v1`, `random-legal@v1`, `damage-aware@v1` and `damage-aware@v2`; a damage-aware arm is built from its reviewed config record (v2 also the composition root's belief port, `damage-aware-worlds.ts`), named in the output by its baseline identity hash (a v2 arm also by its belief identity, prior and belief-config hashes and N), and plays under `guardActor` with role `scripted`: a harness incident fails the run and nothing is written; a forfeit is a scored loss for that arm, counted per arm and cause. The team pool is `datasetValidationPool` of the config's `team_dataset`, or of `--eval-dataset-dir`/`--eval-dataset-hash` (an evaluation-only dataset; the config still supplies the run key, format, turn cap and latency budgets); there is no training team set or league plan to keep the pairs clear of, because nothing trains. A game is a function of its index alone (the pair's battle seed, the arms' own streams), so `--game-processes S` (the generation loop's rule: process k plays the games `g mod S = k`, a child of the same script with the internal `--shard k/S` writes `shard.jsonl`, the parent merges in game order) gives the same record for every S, and a rerun gives the same bytes (the record holds no wall-clock value). `--wall-time-cap-minutes` is the declared cap: checked after every game by every process (a game in flight is bounded by the battle latency budget), a run that passes it fails as a gate failure and commits nothing; the record carries `plan.wall_time_cap_ms` when a cap was declared. The contract is a new, separate `ScriptedHeadToHeadRecord` (`packages/schema/contracts/v1/scripted-head-to-head-record.schema.json`, generated TS and pydantic types, integers only, `data_class` the constant `synthetic`, samples in `fixtures/contracts/v1/scripted-head-to-head-record/`), not a `scripted` candidate kind in the v2 progress record: that record is bound to a run's generation, manifest and frozen checkpoints (all required) and a scripted pair has none, so a new contract changes no existing record or sample. Rows carry arm a's seat, the outcome from the arms' side (`a`, `b`, `tie`; a forfeit names its loser and cause), and for a damage-aware arm its nine integer counts plus `probe_refusals` above 0, for `damage-aware@v2` also the belief counts per game (decisions, worlds sampled, legality redraws, contradicted worlds, requests with no consistent world, refusals by the model's code), so a v2 arm's refusals and its fall-backs to the `actor-rule` candidate (`reader_fallbacks`, `assumed_set_fallbacks`, `probe_refusals`, `no_consistent_world`, the refusals) are all counted; each arm's `counts` and `belief.totals` are the sums of its rows, the result recounts from the rows, and `readScriptedH2HRecord` / `read_scripted_record` check the same rules (rows 0..games-1 with the pair and seat implied by the index, sorted forfeits and refusals, counts and belief present exactly where the policy has them). The Python command (`ScriptedRequest`) runs the Node script, reads the record back with its own reader, ties it to the request (run key, arms, pairs, turn cap and latency budgets from the config, cap, dataset hash), derives the interval (`summarise`: `stats.pair_interval` of arm a's pair scores in half-games, 4,000 resamples, seeded from the run key and the two policy ids, parts per million rounded outward; arm b's from the mirrored scores on the same draws; never stored, labelled `scripted head-to-head on validation teams; not the Training gate; no learned policy`), appends one `scripted-h2h` line to the validation look log (the default `<out>.looks.jsonl`, or `--looks` so a replication shares the chain; the look names generation 0 and the match `damage-aware-v1-vs-damage-aware-v2`), and only then renames the directory into place (`D` is new, never overwritten; built in `.D.partial`); a refused run logs nothing. v2 plays dataset (generator) teams: a team outside the usage prior's support makes the belief refuse and v2 fall back to `actor-rule` (counted; expected 0 on dataset teams, #1131). Tests: `packages/showdown-bridge/test/scripted-h2h-record.test.ts` (samples), `packages/team-dataset/test/scripted-h2h.test.ts` (rerun and split equality, v1 against v2 with the real belief, the cap with an injected clock, an incident, refusals, the command with two real game processes), `scripted-h2h.python.test.ts` (the Python command over Node, skipped without the Python environment) and `tests/ml/progress/test_scripted.py`.

**Evaluation-key option** (#1177; `ml/areneon_ml/progress/evaluation_key.py`, `progress-evaluation.ts` `ProgressOptions.evaluation_key`, `progress-cli.ts` `--evaluation-key`; the flag and the record field names are fixed, the EXP-H2 queue checks for them). `python -m areneon_ml.loop progress --evaluation-key KEY` makes KEY replace `run_key` in `progress-pairing/1` and `progress-seed/1` for every match (baselines, damage-aware, anchors, previous, `run-*`, ablation: pairs, battle seeds, baseline streams), so the plan is a function of (KEY, validation teams) alone and two runs evaluated with one KEY play the same pair, candidate seat, seed and team hashes in every game (a paired comparison between runs; EXP-H2 computes the paired summary itself with `stats.paired_differences` and `stats.pair_interval`; no dashboard summary here). Sampling labels (seed, seat, policy id), `run_id` and the bootstrap stream keep their meaning. KEY is an id token and a key equal to the evaluated run's `run_key` is refused (`ProgressError` on the Python side before any worker, `ProgressError` in `playProgress`): the default already is that key, so the flag has one spelling per plan. Record: optional `plan.evaluation_key` of `ProgressEvaluationRecordV2` and optional `evaluation_key` of `ProgressGameRecord` (minor changes within their versions; absent keeps every byte and hash; a v1 evaluation record is closed and refuses the field; the readers on both sides refuse a key equal to `run_id`, a game with a key and no `pair`, and recompute each seed from `evaluation_key ?? run_id`; the Python `verify_progress_dir` also requires each stored game's key to be the record's). `_check_evaluation_key` is strict at write time and for a committed directory (`_skipped`: kept only for the same key, both ways), lenient for `report` and `replay` unless a key is asked for, as `_check_eval_dataset`. The committed-plan seed checks (`check_not_training_seeds` for the run and the `--versus` run, `league_seeds` on the Node side) run on the key's seeds. The look log names the key per match (optional `evaluation_key` of a match entry; `Look.evaluation_key`; `progress`, `report` and `replay` fill it), and the dashboard notes it (`ProgressFacts.evaluation_key`). Not covered: `scripted-h2h` (it keeps the run key). Tests: `tests/ml/progress/test_evaluation_key.py`, `packages/team-dataset/test/progress-evaluation-key.test.ts` (two run keys, one KEY: equal plans, seeds, seats, team hashes and the random-legal baseline log in every stored game; negative control without the key; refusals; byte-identical rerun), the `plan-label*` corpus samples of the two contracts and the dashboard case in `tests/ml/report/test_report_v2.py`.

**Eval-dataset option** (ADR-020 E-17.1, #974; `packages/showdown-bridge/src/eval-dataset.ts`, `ml/areneon_ml/progress/eval_dataset.py`: one implementation for `progress` and `scripted-h2h`). `--eval-dataset-dir DIR --eval-dataset-hash HEX64` (both or neither) measures a generation on the VALIDATION split of another verified dataset than the run's own. TS: `EVAL_DATASET_FLAGS`, `readEvalDataset`, `withEvalDataset(config, dataset)` replace the config's `team_dataset` before `poolSource` builds the pool (`datasetValidationPool` reads it through `readDataset` and refuses a hash that is not the one given); `playProgress` still gets the run's own config. Python: `EvalDataset`, `from_values`, `validation_teams` (the pair limit is read from the evaluation dataset's manifest, `max_pairs(config, eval_dataset)`), `ProgressRequest.eval_dataset`, `progress_generation(..., eval_dataset=)`. The same refusals as the run's own split (a team in the newest `team-set.json`, a progress seed equal to a committed plan's, more games than pairs) plus the hash mismatch and the run's own training dataset passed through the option (`UsageError` on the Node side, `ProgressError` on the Python side, before any worker). Record: optional `teams.evaluation_dataset_hash` of the v2 record (a minor change within v1 of the contract; absent keeps every existing byte; `teams.dataset_hash` stays the run's own and the two never agree, a reader rule on both sides). `_check_eval_dataset` ties the record to the option at write time and on a committed directory, which is kept only for the same dataset (`verify_progress_dir` is lenient without the option so `report` and `replay` read any root; `_skipped` is strict both ways). The dashboard (`ProgressFacts.evaluation_dataset`) adds the note "Evaluation teams: the validation split of another verified dataset ...". Every look is logged by the unchanged `progress` look. Tests: `tests/ml/progress/test_eval_dataset.py`, `test_main.py`, `tests/ml/report/test_report_v2.py`, `packages/team-dataset/test/progress-eval-dataset.test.ts` (a second tiny dataset regenerated from the committed pilot under other keys) and the `progress.python.test.ts` case (real worker, byte-identical rerun). The scripted head-to-head's flags were deliberately renamed from `--dataset-dir/--dataset-hash` to match; its record puts the given hash in `teams.dataset_hash` (it has no run), unlike the progress record, and it does not refuse the run config's own dataset. Only the validation split is used; the evaluation-only split of the held-out slice (ADR-020 slice 3) is not built and will come through this option.

**Validation look log** (`ml/areneon_ml/progress/looks.py`, ADR-017 erratum E2 item 5, #949). Every command that shows a validation result (`progress`, `report` with a progress root that holds a generation, `replay --progress-dir`, `scripted-h2h`; `inspect` shows none) appends one line to the sibling `<progress root>.looks.jsonl` (`<run-dir>.progress.looks.jsonl` by default; never inside the progress root or the run) under an exclusive `flock`. A line is the JCS bytes of `{schema_version, seq, prev, at, command, run_id, generations, matches[{generation, match_id, games}], games}` and `prev` is the SHA-256 of the line before (`GENESIS` for the first); `verify_looks` refuses a dropped, changed, reordered, non-canonical, torn or time-travelling line, and `append_look` verifies the log first and checks the new line by the same rules. A command logs after its inputs verified and before it shows a result, so a refused command logs nothing; the dashboard shows the count and the log's SHA-256 among its inputs, and a re-run of `report` is a look of its own, so its page differs by the count. Validation is never quoted as held-out evidence.

**Run dashboard** (`ml/areneon_ml/report/`, ADR-017 §4, §4c, slice 6, #914; `python -m areneon_ml.loop report --run-dir R [--progress-dir P] [--inspect-dir D] [--out report.html|DIR]`, default `<run-dir>.report.html` beside the run, never inside the run or progress directory; standard library only; the page is written to a temporary sibling, flushed and renamed). One offline page: the progress evaluation per opponent and mode per generation (Wilson intervals from `progress/stats.py` through `summary_of`, labelled conditional on the fixed validation plan; the previous-generation matches labelled `seen, same-lineage comparison: it can cycle`; forfeits by side and cause), the self-play outcomes and game length, training forfeits by cause (`not recorded` for a forfeit written without one), games, examples and steps per generation, the training diagnostics (losses, entropy, approximate KL, clip fraction, under a heading that says loss is not skill), a score-by-seat table (the ADR's diagnostic, counts only), a generation table and the SHA-256 of every input file. Version 2 records (ADR-017 erratum E2, #951; each generation is read by its own `schema_version`, so a root may mix both and a version 1 page is byte-identical to before): the matches carry the pair-level bootstrap interval and the number of pairs, the anchor matches get an "Anchor comparisons (paired differences)" table (pair score minus one half with its descriptive interval, labelled `seen, same-lineage comparison: it can cycle`; the anchor games are named in the games-per-match note), and a Preview ablation generation gets a "Team Preview ablation" table (`ablation_summary`: per baseline and Preview variant the mean pair score and its interval, the difference to the learned Preview with its interval and the pairs better, equal and worse) and its matches are left out of the score charts; the score-by-seat caption says every pair is played from both seats. Every page says "Progress view on validation teams. Not the Training gate. Loss is not skill." Without `--progress-dir` (or for a generation not evaluated) the page says `not evaluated yet`; nothing is estimated and no zero is invented (wall-clock time is not recorded in the run files, so none is shown). `--inspect-dir` adds relative links to `games-gNNNNNN.html` and `replays/gNNNNNN/<id>.html` only for files that exist there. `model.py` reads the loop files with the inspector's closed-key readers and the contract readers, verifies every generation's manifest and every progress directory's manifest, ties each progress record to the run config and to the SHA-256 of the generation manifest it names, never reads Episode content (only counts), and refuses a lineage named private or a non-synthetic checkpoint record (`ReportError` is an `InspectError`, exit 3). `page.py` reuses the inspector's escaping and CSP helpers: a `<meta>` CSP first in `<head>` with one hashed style and one hashed script, tip texts in a JSON data block, written with `textContent` only. Tests (`tests/ml/report/`): numbers recomputed from the files, canary with negative controls, CSP hash recomputation, no remote reference, sockets blocked, determinism, refusals; the realistic fake generations come from `RichPlayer` in the test support.

**Incremental load** (#958): `learner/data.py` `trajectories` reduces each Episode's channel once instead of re-reducing every example's log from its start. An `Encoder.session()` (`EncoderSession`) feeds a `ChannelReducer` (the reducer driven line by line; `reduce_channel` is a loop over it) only the lines it has not seen and encodes at once, while the live state equals `reduce_channel`'s for that prefix, and `worker/view.py` `IncrementalView` strips `rqid` from each line once. A log that does not extend the lines already fed restarts the reduction, so the tensors always equal `Encoder.encode(encoder_input(query))` (`tests/ml/learner/test_incremental.py` over every committed battle channel; the encoder version and `ENCODER_FORMAT` are unchanged). The remaining load cost is `read_stream`'s per-example contract validation of the whole stream.

**Generation loop** (`ml/areneon_ml/loop/`, `packages/showdown-bridge/src/league-generation.ts`, `generation-cli.ts`, ADR-010 §1, #840). `python -m areneon_ml.loop run --config <TrainingRunConfig.json> --run-dir R --engine-data <engine-data.json> --generations N [--node node] [--worker-python <python>]` continues the run in `R` until N generations are committed (resuming from the last one, a no-op if N are there); `provenance --run-dir R --out D` writes `D/records/` and `D/sources.json` (derived from the run's own manifests) for `python -m areneon_ml.checkpoint classify|independent`. Generation 0 is the seeded random pair (ADR-010 §4) unless the config declares `warm_start` (see Warm start); generation g plays the pair frozen at g-1 on A1 (`play-generation.ts`: the Python worker on CPU torch behind the TS client, `policyGame` for self-play and for each configured scripted baseline), writes an Episode for every played seat (`episodeFromRecord`, collection kind `league`, `run_id` = the config's `run_key`, the seed the battle ran with), loads only the pair's own seats through `loadTrainingSet` (`behaviour_policy_id` = `policy:` + SHA-256 of `JCS({preview, battle, mode, temperature, precision})`; a baseline's Episodes are kept but the loader would refuse them), trains (`learner/`), freezes the new pair and commits. A forfeited game's row in `generation.json` carries `forfeit: {loser, cause, request_seq, detail}` and the file a `forfeit_causes` count by cause (both absent without a forfeit, so such a run writes the same bytes); the policy worker client's handshake timer ends with the handshake, not the session (#912: left running it killed the worker 60 s into a session). The frozen pair always plays with temperature-1 sampling, never argmax (`playOne`; the policy id says `mode: sample`), and a baseline seat is never queried or loaded (tests fail on either). The match plan (battle seeds, team indices, baseline seats) is a pure function of the config, the generation and the team pool (`planGeneration`), so a killed generation is replayed from the same plan. Run directory: `run-config.json`, `checkpoints/<id>.safetensors` (the store the worker reads), `generations/g<NNNNNN>/` (Episodes, `stream.jsonl`, `training-set.json`, `plan.json`, `generation.json`, `train-report.json`, `learner-<g>.safetensors|json`, `records/{battle,preview}.json`, the cumulative `team-set.json`, `manifest.json` = SHA-256 of every other file plus the checkpoint ids and hashes). `run()` holds an exclusive `flock` on `<run dir>/.lock` for its whole run (a second driver on the directory fails fast; the operating system releases the lock when the holder dies; caveats: `fcntl` is POSIX only, NFS needs a working lock service, and a WSL2 `/mnt/c` directory (DrvFs/9p) may not honour `flock`, so keep run directories on a local Linux filesystem; `generations/`, `checkpoints/`, the run directory and its parent are fsynced when first created). A generation is built in `.g<NNNNNN>.partial`; every file and directory in it is fsynced (the Node side's own files too: `writeGeneration` fsyncs each file, then both directories, `generation.json` last), the manifest is hashed from the flushed bytes, and one `os.replace` of the directory commits it, followed by an fsync of `generations/` (`write_checkpoint` and `run-config.json` are written the same way, temp file, fsync, rename, fsync of the directory; stale `checkpoints/.*.partial` files of a killed run are deleted at start); the next start deletes partials, finds the newest committed generation, verifies its manifest (only the newest: the learner state, records and team set a resume needs live there, `latest_complete` checks that the numbers run 0..n without a gap, and `provenance` verifies every generation), restores the learner (`restore_state`, hash checked), and refuses unless the restored weights are the stored checkpoints (written again if the store lost them) and the records agree with the state and the manifest (checkpoint id, learner state hash, run config hash, training-set hashes). Each generation's two `CheckpointRecord`s carry the lineage, the parent (the previous generation's same-role checkpoint), the TrainingSet hash, the run config hash, the learner state hash, the encoder version, the cumulative team-set manifest hash and the format's version manifest (from `format-manifest.ts`). The team pool is the interim one (`trainingTeamPool`): the golden team, the legal paste fixtures and the random-play corpus teams, all committed CC0 synthetic and proven synthetic by that source; the ADR-013 team generator does not exist yet. `generation.excluded_abilities` of the config keeps out teams that hold an ability on its list; the tiny and the current configs list `Illusion`, a stop-gap for the reducer's former `|replace|` gap, which #851 closed. To drop it: remove `"Illusion"` from `generation.excluded_abilities` of a NEW run's config (a new `run_key`; the list is part of the run config hash, and the pool, hence every team index of `planGeneration`, changes with it, so never edit a run in progress), then sweep first: `node packages/team-dataset/scripts/reducer-sweep.ts --config <config> --mix uniform-all --battles 300 --only-ability Illusion --out OUT.jsonl` and `uv run python scripts/reducer_sweep.py OUT.jsonl` must report `unexplained: 0`. A new run restarts from generation 0 (ADR-010 §4); the committed `config/training/public-gpu16.json` still lists Illusion until a follow-up decides. Claims: with the CPU learner, one thread count and the same machine and software, an independent rerun gives byte-identical generations and a SIGKILLed run resumes to the same learner-state hashes (`training-loop.python.test.ts` SIGKILLs the driver's whole process group at three points of generation 2 and resumes each to the same hashes: `play`, while Node and the worker are alive (the partial may still be empty); `write`, while Node writes its files (`generation.json` is written last, so Node and the worker are still alive); `seal`, after `train-report.json` exists and before the manifest, so the driver's train/seal phase is killed by a real signal (the window is short; a missed one is retried in a fresh directory). The driver's read and commit phases are otherwise exercised by in-process stage killers (exceptions), `tests/ml/loop/` kills at each stage with a fake simulator side); a CUDA learner is not bit-reproducible and nothing is claimed across machines or torch builds. The training self-play and the learner may batch; evaluation never does (§2). Memory (#1069): a game process writes each Episode to `<out>/episodes/<hash>.json` (durably) as its game ends and keeps none (`GenerationOptions.episodes_dir`, `PlayedEntry.stored`; further game processes write into the starter's directory via the internal `--episodes-dir`), and `writeStoredTrainingStream` (`training-loader.ts`) reads them back one at a time in hash order, so the stream, the TrainingSet and every file are the bytes the in-memory path writes (a test and a full-size `diff -r` check it); `--node-heap-mb` (`NodePlayer.node_heap_mb`) caps each Node game process's V8 heap, `--load-processes N` / `--load-memory-mb` let the load phase check and encode the stream file in forked workers (`learner/parallel.py`: pieces cut between Episodes by `stream_split_points`, each worker runs `StreamChecker` and the encoder on its piece, results joined in file order as one byte blob each (JSON header plus raw tensor bytes; no pickle) because torch's multiprocessing reductions send each tensor through its own file descriptor; contract and encoder refusals keep their serial type, message and precedence; a stream that cannot be cut, or an Episode in two pieces, is read by one process), and `ExampleReader` (`areneon_contracts.training`) skips re-validating the strings of a log prefix the previous valid example already had (sound only while the schema says nothing more about `log` than an array of at least one string; `_log_shortcut_sound` checks that, a differential test compares verdicts and messages). All of it is operational: none is in the run config and no hash moves. `docs/training-run.md` has the sizing guide. A forfeited game's `PlayedEntry` carries `stored: []` on the spilled path (#1184; without it `playGeneration` refused any generation with a forfeit as "not all kept the way this generation keeps its Episodes"), so a forfeit is kept like an in-memory one and a generation without forfeits writes the same bytes. **Feature rows by the game processes** (#1185, ADR-022 section 4): with a candidate feature set and `--game-processes` above 1, the stream's rows were recomputed serially by the parent after the games (a tail of about 330 s that did not shrink with the processes, #1179). Now every game process, the parent included, recomputes the rows of the Episodes of the frozen pair that it wrote as soon as its games are done (`feature-recompute.ts` `writeFeatureRows`, from the written Episode files, one `<hash>.rows.json` each in a scratch directory the parent makes and passes as the internal `--features-dir`; `GenerationOptions.features_dir`), and the stream writer takes a file through `TrainingInput.features.precomputed` only when it names that Episode and the feature set and holds one entry per decision, else recomputes (a missing, stale or damaged file costs time, never a byte; an Episode that cannot be read or featured gets no file, so the loader's own refusal is raised by the stream writer as before). The files are never in a generation, an Episode or a contract; the stream, TrainingSet and every hash are byte-identical to the serial path for 1, 2 and 3 processes (`test/feature-recompute.test.ts`). A run without `--game-processes` or a feature set is unchanged. **No large string** (#998): Node's string limit (about 512 MB) was hit by `loadTrainingSet`'s joined stream at about 480 games. `writeTrainingStream` (`training-loader.ts`) writes `stream.jsonl` in 1 MiB chunks through `ChunkedFileWriter` (`chunked-file.ts`) as the examples are made, keeps none, and hashes incrementally, to the same bytes and `stream_sha256` as `loadTrainingSet` (which stays, for streams that fit a string, and refuses past `max_string_chars`); `playGeneration` takes `stream_path` (the CLI passes `<out>/stream.jsonl`) and then `GenerationResult.stream` is null; the game-process handoff is `shard.jsonl` (one JCS entry per line, read by `readFileLines`); Python reads the stream by path (`check_stream_file`, `stream_lines`, `trajectories(Path, ...)`) and hashes generation files in chunks (`layout.sha256_file`). Tests inject small `chunk_chars` / `max_string_chars`. Measured: 800 games, 1.25 GB stream, written in 20 s at 0.88 GB peak RSS (the 1,600 Episodes are held in memory); Python check 126 s at 50 MB. League mixtures, a freeze interval and exploiters are Wave 5. **Throughput (#957).** `python -m areneon_ml.loop run --game-processes S --policy-workers K` (operational flags of `NodePlayer`, never config fields, so no run config hash moves; both default to 1 and are then not passed) shares a generation's games over `S` Node processes, each with `K` worker processes. `playEntries(options, {index, of})` plays the games `g mod of = index` (a game stays on one worker, chosen by its place in the wave; waves hold `n_parallel` games per worker) and returns `PlayedEntry`s (row and Episodes); `playGeneration` puts its own slice and `options.others` (the other slices) in plan order, refuses a game missing or played twice, and loads the training set once. `runGenerationCli` is the parent (shard 0): it starts `S-1` children of the same script (the `shardScript` argument, `process.argv[1]` of both scripts; `--shard INDEX/COUNT` is internal) before it loads its own pool, each child writes `shard.jsonl` in a scratch directory that the parent reads and removes with the directory (also on failure); a child ends if the parent dies (its stdin closes). Nothing about the generation depends on the split: a game's result is a function of its plan entry alone (the worker is deterministic and `u` is the harness's), and `training-loop.python.test.ts` and `league-generation.test.ts` require the same files and learner-state hashes for any `S` and `K`. Measured on this machine (shared, CPU, full size 384 games, generation 1): the script took 332 s and 330 s with one worker and 101 s, 102 s and 102 s with `S=4 K=3` (`S=3 K=3` 110 s, `S=6 K=2` 105 s; the generation directories are byte-identical to the one-worker run's); about 34 s of both is the pool load (`readDataset` validates every record, then `datasetPoolDetail` normalises each team again), which every game process repeats in parallel, and about 12 s the parent's serial merge (training loader, hashing, files). Progress evaluation stays unbatched and unchanged.

**Failing game processes** (#1100). A game process that exits non-zero fails the generation at once: `startShard` reports it on `exit` (not `close`, which waits for every holder of its stderr pipe) with its stderr tail, `playGeneration` aborts this process's own games (`GenerationOptions.signal`, checked before each wave and after each worker restart, so no wave and no worker restart follows) and rethrows, the CLI's `finally` kills the other children, closes the workers and removes the scratch directory, and the play scripts `process.exit` on a non-zero code so a game still running cannot keep the process alive (the cause of a 7.6-hour hang: `others` rejected, the parent returned 1, but its own games went on and restarted workers). An idle watchdog (`live_children`, `idle_grace_ms` default 60 s) fails a generation that still awaits the other processes' games with none alive and none of its own in flight. The driver's `--generation-timeout-minutes` (default 180, `NodePlayer.generation_timeout_s`) keeps the Node process in the driver's own process group (so a kill of the driver's group still ends it, as `training-loop.python.test.ts` requires) and past the limit kills it and every descendant found under `/proc` (`PlayError`, nothing committed). All operational: no output byte changes.

**Run metrics** (ADR-010 erratum A1 item 3, `ml/areneon_ml/loop/metrics.py`, `packages/schema/contracts/v1/run-metrics.schema.json`, `RunMetrics`, integers only). After `commit_generation` (and after the `committed` stage hook) the driver writes `<run-dir>/metrics/g<NNNNNN>.json` atomically: milliseconds per phase (`play` the simulator side, `load` reading the generation's files and encoding, `train`, `write` the driver's own files and the checkpoint store, `seal` fsync, manifest and rename; generation 0 has only write and seal), counts (games, decisions, optimiser steps), per-hour rates (games over play, the others over train; truncated; 0 for a phase with no time), `peak_accelerator_memory_bytes` (`torch.cuda.max_memory_allocated` around the train phase; null on a CPU learner) and the config's device and thread count. Optional keys (#1069, a minor change within v1, written only when measured): `peak_accelerator_reserved_bytes` (`torch.cuda.max_memory_reserved`, CUDA learner only; the driver calls `torch.cuda.empty_cache()` after each train phase so the allocator's cache does not outlive it) and `peak_rss_bytes` `{driver, game_process, policy_worker}` (Linux resident-set peaks: the driver's `ru_maxrss` is its whole-run high-water mark; the play script prints a second stdout line `{"resource_usage": ...}` after its summary line with the largest game process and policy worker, read from `/proc/<pid>/status` `VmHWM`, the process's own address space; `process.resourceUsage().maxRSS` is only the fallback without `/proc`, because the kernel's `ru_maxrss` survives fork and exec and a game process started by the Python driver reported the driver's peak, #1110). It is outside the manifest and every hash, never read by the loop, the loader or the learner, so generations stay byte-identical; a crash between the commit and the write loses that generation's timings and a resume does not write them (no tamper evidence either). `Loop.clock` is the time source (a test replaces it). `read_run_metrics` ties a record to the config, the generation and its own rates. The dashboard (`report/model.py` `read_metrics`) reads each present file, refuses one that is misnamed, of a generation the run lacks or off-contract, shows the figures in a table and says `not recorded` otherwise, and lists each file's SHA-256 among its inputs. The `train-report.json` keeps only deterministic values.

**Dataset pool for the loop** (`packages/team-dataset/src/loop-pool.ts`, `scripts/play-generation.ts`, `docs/training-run.md`). `TrainingRunConfig` gained the optional `team_dataset` `{path, dataset_hash}` (a minor change within v1). Absent: the interim pool and the bridge's script, byte-identical to before. Present: `datasetTeamPool` reads the directory with `readDataset` (a directory as `generate` wrote it, `records.<split>.jsonl` for dev, validation and training, is read like the committed layout and also yields `training`), requires `dataset_hash` (`datasetHash`: SHA-256 of JCS of `{manifest_hash, splits: {dev, validation, training}}` with each split the sorted `record_hash`es of the records `readDataset` verified, so any swapped, added or removed record changes it) to match, takes `trainingPool`'s dev and training records (never validation; a validation duplicate, forged hash or hand-built dataset is refused), normalises each team through the pinned validator on a copy (a Mega-forme set starts as its base forme with the base ability, the form the player's Team Preview request shows and an observation is checked against), applies `generation.excluded_abilities`, sorts by team hash and builds `known_synthetic` with `syntheticTeamHashes` from the pool's own teams only. The bridge cannot import this package, so `runGenerationCli` takes a `PoolSource` port (`interimPoolSource` refuses a config that names a dataset); the Python `NodePlayer` starts the team-dataset script when the config names one. `dataset-hash.ts` verifies a directory and prints the dataset hash for the config; `loop run` and `play-generation` refuse a `run_key` starting `CHANGE-ME`. `config/training/public-gpu16.json` is the committed GPU run config (placeholder `run_key` and `dataset_hash`, changed per run).

**Teacher data** (`packages/showdown-bridge/src/teacher-generation.ts`, `teacher-cli.ts`, `packages/team-dataset/scripts/teacher-run.ts`, ADR-019 track 1, #965; the docs section is `docs/training-run.md` section 5). `damage-aware@v1` plays seeded A1 games on the training pool and the result is an ordinary league collection: Episodes (`collection.kind` `league`, `run_id` the teacher run's key, `opponent_policy_id` the other seat's policy, the seed the battle ran with), a `loadTrainingSet` stream and a learner-ready `TrainingSet` whose `behaviour_policy_id` is `damage-aware@v1` (`damage-aware@v1-eps<ppm>` with `--epsilon-ppm`, ADR-018 Q5: another identity). No contract changed. Default games are the teacher against itself (both seats train); `--random-games` / `--actor-rule-games` add baseline opponents (the teacher alternates seats; the baseline seat's Episode is kept in `episodes/` but not loaded). Plan: `planGames` (shared with `planGeneration`, byte-identical) under generation 1 of `league-seed/1`; a game depends on its plan entry alone, so `--game-processes N` (the loop's `startShard` / shard-file machinery) gives the same bytes. The teacher is a `guardActor` `scripted` role: an overrun or any failure is a `baseline_failure` incident that fails the run. `teacher.json` holds the baseline identity hash, the budget and the per-game and summed integer counts (fallbacks, unsupported queries, epsilon plays). The damage-aware actor counts a fractional-priority speed order (Quick Claw and the like) as `unsupported` like the Focus-Punch limit (it was an uncaught `MechanicsProbeError`). Any other `MechanicsProbeError` from a query it built is a `ProbeRefusal`: that request is answered by the `actor-rule` candidate and counted in `probe_refusals` (new `DamageAwareCounts` field; optional `BaselineCounts.probe_refusals` in the LeagueRunRecord, a minor change within v1, written only above 0 so existing records keep their bytes, never above `queries`). Root cause of public-main-004's crash (game 604, `baseline_failure: the engine does not take that status`): the assumed opponent ability (first in vocabulary order, Limber) blocked a paralysis the player had SEEN, so the declared state was refused; `assumedFor` now rules out an unrevealed ability the engine refuses with the public status and assumes the next. The probes (`mechanics-damage.ts`, `mechanics-speed.ts`) declare a status as an observed fact (`setStatus(..., ignoreImmunities)`), so a type immunity (Corrosion on a Steel type) no longer refuses a query; an ability or engine hook still does. The pool request (`PoolRequest`) is the part of a run config that names format, dataset and excluded abilities, so `datasetTeamPool` serves teacher runs without a `TrainingRunConfig`. `--teacher damage-aware@v1|damage-aware@v3` (#1182; default `v1`, so absent keeps every byte of today's runs; anything else is a usage error, exit 2) selects the committed record the teacher plays (`TeacherSettings.teacher_version`, `teacherContext` loads `loadDamageAwareConfig(version)`, the actor's `commit` is `v3` or `v3-eps<ppm>`); the Episodes', the TrainingSet's, `teacher.json`'s and the printed line's policy id then say `damage-aware@v3` and `teacher_identity_hash` is v3's baseline identity (the one the scripted head to head reports), while the opponent games stay `random-legal@v1` / `actor-rule@v1`; `loadTrainingSet` under `damage-aware@v1` refuses a v3 run's Episodes, and `learner clone` takes a config naming `damage-aware@v3` with no contract change. v2 is not a teacher (it needs the belief port).

**KL anchor** (`ppo.anchor`, `ml/areneon_ml/learner/{config,ppo,trainer,state}.py`, `loop/driver.py`, ADR-019 §4, ADR-010 erratum A2 item 3, #968; `docs/training-run.md` section 5). Optional `{kl_micro, schedule}` in the `ppo` block (`kl_micro` 1 to 10^8; `schedule` is `constant` or `linear {final_micro 0 to 10^8, generations >= 1}`, schema defs `AnchorSchedule` / `AnchorLinearSchedule`, since the learning-rate `LinearSchedule` forbids a final of 0; a minor change within v1: absent keeps every hash, report and file); both readers require `warm_start`. The loss adds `beta * KL(pi_theta || pi_anchor)` per decision (`ppo.anchor_kl`: softmax over the real candidates, padding masked), `pi_anchor` the warm-start pair loaded by the driver (`_new_learner` calls `load_warm_start` again, a separate frozen copy: `Learner(..., anchors)`, required exactly when the config has an anchor; evaluation mode, `requires_grad` off) and scored once per generation per role with no gradient (`_anchor_scores`, one log-probability vector per step over its real candidates). `AnchorSettings.beta_micro(t)` is the integer rule of `learning_rate_schedule` started from `kl_micro`; `Learner.anchor_beta_micro(update)` decides what `t` counts: with `value_warmup` N the updates below N have beta 0 (policy frozen; no anchor pass, KL reported as 0) and update `u` >= N uses `t = u - N` (the decay starts with the first policy update; the learning-rate schedule keeps counting the warm-up), without it `t` is the update index. `RoleReport.anchor_kl` (mean over the role's minibatches, measured before each step) and `anchor_beta_micro` are in `train-report.json` only when the anchor is set (the dashboard reader's optional keys, both or neither, KL not negative; it charts "Anchor KL" beside approximate KL); the learner-state sidecar records `anchor_beta_micro` (the newest update's beta) only when set and `restore_state` refuses a sidecar whose beta is not the schedule's. The ADR-023 amendment is applied (#1046): when the run declares `generation.play`, the KL is between the tempered policies (both the trained and the anchor policy at tau; see Training play temperature). Tests: `tests/ml/learner/test_anchor.py` (reader and schema refusals, beta at 0, mid and past `generations` including the warm-up offset, the KL against a hand computation on three candidates, once-per-generation scoring, report and sidecar keys, resume), `tests/ml/loop/test_anchor.py` (a warm-started tiny loop with and without a warm-up: report and sidecar beta per generation, rerun and kills at four stages reach identical bytes) and the dashboard cases in `tests/ml/report/test_report.py`.

**GPU learner** (`ml/gpu-env/`, ADR-010 §4, §5.2, #840). The learner runs on the local GPU from a separate uv project, `ml/gpu-env/` (its own `pyproject.toml` and `uv.lock`, `areneon-ml` and `areneon-contracts` by path, `torch` 2.14.1 from the PyTorch cu130 index through `override-dependencies`; the root `pyproject.toml` lists it under the workspace `exclude`, it is never a member or in `SHIPPED`, its venv is gitignored). `cd ml/gpu-env && uv sync`, then run `ml/gpu-env/.venv/bin/python -m areneon_ml.loop run ... --worker-python <root .venv python>`: the learner is CUDA torch, the A1 worker stays CPU torch from the shipped closure (C-4). A config with `learner.device: "cuda"` is refused where there is no CUDA device (`configure_learner`), never replaced by the CPU, and `configure_learner` sets `CUBLAS_WORKSPACE_CONFIG=:4096:8` (if unset) with deterministic algorithms on (warn-only on CUDA). `python -m areneon_ml.learner bench --config … --engine-data … --stream-dir <generation dir> --repeats N` times the learner on a recorded stream (one untimed warm-up pass, decisions and optimiser steps per second, peak CUDA memory); the figures are measurements and are not claimed to repeat. The network builds every tensor on its batch's device (a `meta`-device test catches a CPU-only constant). `tests/scripts/test_gpu_env.py` keeps the two torch versions in step and the CUDA packages out of the root lock; `ml/gpu-env/README.md` holds the operator's install steps, the reproducibility statement and the declared licences of the CUDA packages. sm_120 result (RTX 5070 Ti, driver 610.88): `torch.cuda.get_arch_list()` of 2.14.1+cu130 is `sm_75 sm_80 sm_86 sm_90 sm_100 sm_120`, so the stock cu130 wheel has native sm_120 kernels; the numbers measured are on the ticket.

**Team generator** (`packages/team-dataset`, ADR-013 §2, §3, #816; imports the engine only through the bridge). `config/teams/<format>/labels.json` (a `TeamLabelSet`) and `generator.json` (a `TeamGeneratorConfig`) are data; `check-team-labels.ts` (in `make ts` and CI) reads them against the pinned engine (every id exists, sorted and unique, no default equal to a listed value, every leaf can hold in the vocabulary, at most 65,536 cells). `labeller.ts` is a pure interpreter of the label-set record (`all_of` of leaves, first match else default) and holds no id (a test greps its source for the committed ids); a base cell is the label values read as a mixed-radix number, first label most significant (64 cells for the five committed labels). `stream.ts`: draw k of a plan has seed `"sodium," + hex32(HMAC-SHA256(generation_key, JCS({config_hash, k, plan_id, purpose: "draw"})))`; the pilot's preimage is `{config_hash, k, purpose: "pilot"}` (no plan id: one recorded pilot serves every plan of a config). `proposal.ts` draws a team uniformly from the bridge's vocabulary (roster, move pools, abilities, natures; level, team size, Stat Point total and Item Clause from the rule table; IVs, the per-stat maximum, gender, nicknames and integer item weights from the config); `draw.ts` puts it in canonical order, round-trips it through `importPaste`/`exportPaste`, asks `validateTeam` (on a copy: the validator normalises sets in place) and labels it. `plan.ts` makes the quota plan from the pilot counts: proportional quotas with a floor of `n_stratum_min` per split, and the D20 merge before any generation (a group is affordable when it has `pilot_min_hits` hits and `quota * pilot_draws <= d_max * hits`; an unaffordable group is replaced by the group one level up, dropping the last label first, which absorbs every cell under it; a plan that is infeasible even with all cells merged is refused). `binner.ts` bins accepted draws in counter order (full bin: overflow; known hash: duplicate; else binned), so a bin is an exact sample of the proposal conditioned on its stratum; `dedupe.ts` is the two-stage known-hash index (a sorted 64-bit prefix array, 8 bytes per hash, then an exact comparison in memory or in a sorted file); `rejection-log.ts` writes and hashes the log as the run goes. `generate.ts` runs it: pilot (recorded, or run), plan, one stream, then each stratum's full bin is dealt to dev, validation and training by HMAC-SHA256(assignment_key, canonical hash) order (the assignment rule of §2 so that every record has its split; `assignSplits` of #860 owns this later), every record is re-read with `readTeamRecord` and the labeller, and a bin still short at `d_max` re-plans with the short strata coarsened one more level under `<plan_id>-r<n>`. `provision.ts` sizes the ranges from a negative-binomial fill-time quantile; `range.ts` summarises a range of draws as one SHA-256 over fixed-width per-draw records, the unit the two-platform check compares. `command.ts` is the CLI (`generate`, `pilot`); output is JCS-canonical, so the same commit, config, label set, keys and references give the same bytes (`records.<split>.jsonl`, `manifest.json`, `coverage.json`, `pilot.json`, `rejections.jsonl`). The generation key of a public dataset is a public key given on the command line; sealed generation is #861. Package tests run the real validator on a few thousand draws; keep any new generation test small. Quotas: a split's share of a stratum is its share of the ACCEPTED pilot draws (rejected draws belong to no cell), while the cost rule uses the rate per draw of the stream; the `n_stratum_min` floor per stratum and split can lift a split above its request (20/20/100 over 23 strata delivers about 55/55/116), so `coverage.json` lists requested and delivered per split and the CLI prints any difference. `provisioning.exceeds_d_max` (and a CLI warning) says the 0.99 fill quantile is beyond `d_max`, because the merge decides on mean fill times and the stream stops at `d_max`. `coverage.json` also records `replan` (0 or the re-plan count) and each base cell's `held` teams per split inside merged strata. The seed preimage is the JSON object `{config_hash, k, plan_id, purpose}` (the pilot's has no `plan_id`), not the tuple the ADR writes: an ADR-013 erratum candidate. `moves_per_set` is a named config field (optional in the schema, required by the proposal). `generator.json` gives every Mega Stone (the items the roster's Mega formes require) weight 0 so a stone appears only with its Mega species; otherwise about half the free item slots hold a useless stone and the Mega label is nearly constant (a test holds the label's stones and the zero weights equal to the roster's required items, so a pin bump that adds a stone fails). Scale limits of this slice (#883): the dataset files stream. `writeDataset` writes each `records.<split>.jsonl` in 1 MiB chunks (`writeLines`), and `recordHashes` and `readDataset` read line by line (`eachLine`, `endsWithNewline` in `dataset-io.ts`), so no file is held as one string and the 512 MB string limit no longer applies to them; the bytes written are unchanged. What still sits in memory: the records themselves (`GenerateResult.records`, and `readDataset`'s `VerifiedDataset`, as objects, roughly the file size or more), the known hashes as JS strings while `sortedUnique` builds the sorted list (about 100 bytes per hash; 1.2M hashes peak near 400 MB of RSS in `scripts/measure-streaming.ts`, which writes, reads back and indexes 1.2M padded lines of a 930 MB file; the previous joined-string code fails on it with `RangeError: Invalid string length`), the binner's in-run `Set`, which holds at most 2^24 (about 16.7 million) binned teams (a V8 limit, unchanged), and the JCS string of the sorted hash list that `datasetHash` and `recordHash` build (fine to some millions of records). The CLI reports an oversize failure as exit 1; 10^8 teams needs the shard slice's binary builder. `assign-splits.ts` (#860): `assignSplits(pool, plan, assignment_key, n_stratum_min?)` is the one split rule (ADR-013 §2): within each stratum it orders the teams by HMAC-SHA256(assignment_key, canonical hash) and deals dev, validation and training by the plan's quotas; it is pure, order-insensitive, and refuses a stratum that is not exactly its bin, a team twice, a quota below `n_stratum_min` and a key that is not 64 lowercase hex digits; `generate.ts` calls it. **Committed fixtures** (`fixtures/teams/<format>/datasets/fixtures-v1/`, CC0, see its README): `teams-dev.jsonl`, `teams-validation.jsonl` (JCS lines), `manifest.json`, `hashes.json` (sorted `record_hash` / `canonical_team_hash` lists per split and the manifest hash: a Parquet shard of the split must list the same `record_hash`es), `pilot.json`, `coverage.json`, `run.json` (the public keys, sizes and generator commit); the training split is generated in the same run but never committed. `dataset-reader.ts`: `readDataset(dir, ctx)` trusts nothing (manifest against config and label set and the engine's version manifest; the reference pool: `generate --reference` also writes `reference-hashes.txt` (the sorted, unique canonical hashes it deduplicated against, one per line; absent without `--reference`), and the reader recomputes `reference_pool_hash` from `ctx.referenceHashes` if given, else from that file, refusing a missing, doctored, unsorted or malformed list and any record that repeats a listed team, so the evaluation paths (`--eval-dataset-dir`, the scripted head-to-head, `datasetValidationPool`) read such a dataset with no extra input, #1170; each record through `readTeamRecord`, the labeller, `importPaste` round trip and `validateTeam`; every stratum holds its quota; no team in two splits; the hash lists recomputed), `trainingPool(datasets, engine)` takes only `readDataset` results, recomputes every canonical hash from the paste, and returns a branded pool of dev records that `drawTrainingTeam` alone accepts: a validation record, a forged hash, a hand-built dataset or pool, or a team repeating a validation team is refused (`readDataset` also checks `vocabulary_hash` and `reference_pool_hash` against its context; `generator_commit` is checked only by the regeneration test). `fixtures.ts` + `scripts/fixtures.ts` (`write` / `check`, `--full` also reruns the 40,000-draw pilot, about 45 s) regenerate the files from the committed config; a test (about 5 s with the recorded pilot) requires them byte for byte.

**Mixture proposal (ADR-020 §1-§3, #972).** `TeamGeneratorConfig` gains the optional `proposal {kind: "mixture", components}` (a minor change within v1; absent keeps every draw, fixture, dataset hash and the generator golden digest). Components are `uniform` and `role_sets {role_set_table_hash}`, each with an integer `share_permille`; `readTeamGeneratorConfig` requires them sorted by kind, unique and summing to 1000 (a `usage` component fails the schema; it comes with the sampler slice), and `config_hash` covers the field. With a mixture, draw k's FIRST draw from its own seeded stream is `rng.below(1000)` against the cumulative shares in component order (`Proposal.component(rng)`, recomputable from the seed), then the chosen path runs; `uniform` is the old path. Component R lives in `role-set-proposal.ts`: `team_size` distinct table entries uniformly, a role uniformly among the entry's sets, up to `moves_per_set` moves without replacement from the role's pool, an ability from the role's list; item (a Mega entry keeps its required item), nature and Stat Points use the uniform draws (`Proposal.#set`). A Mega-forme entry is proposed as the forme it shows as (`megaPreviewSpecies`) holding its stone, with the role's pre-Evolution ability. `Proposal(vocabulary, table, config, roleSets?)` refuses a role_sets component with no `RoleSetsSource` (`roleSetsSource(engine, vocabulary, engineData, table)` reads it against the vocabulary) or whose hash is not the one named; `generate` takes `roleSets`, the CLI `--role-sets FILE` (default the golden `fixtures/showdown/<format>/role-sets.json`, read only when the config has a role_sets component); the sealed generator takes no table, so a config with a role_sets component is refused there (a uniform-only mixture is accepted; `sealed.test.ts`). Two table entries may show as one species (a base forme and its Mega forme): the validator rejects such a team, as in the uniform path, which costs about 2% extra validator rejections of R teams (a reviewer's simulation: 200,000 draws, about 2.0% of R teams). `config/teams/<format>/generator.mixture-r.json` (role_sets 700, uniform 300) is checked by `check-team-labels.ts`, which now reads every `generator*.json` and requires a role_sets hash to be the committed table's. Tests: `packages/team-dataset/test/mixture.test.ts`.

**Pilot gates, contamination counts and evaluation-only datasets (ADR-020 §6-§8, E-13.5, E-13.6, #973).** All new report fields are omitted when no `proposal` is set (a uniform dataset keeps every byte; `fixtures.ts check` stays green). `proposal.gates {p2_min_accept_permille, p3_min_bits, p4_min_strata_permille}` is optional on the config (minor change within v1; the ADR's proposed 500, 40 and 750 are in `generator.mixture-r.json`, which therefore has a new `config_hash`; no dataset used it). A mixture's pilot records `components` (`draws`, `rejected`, `cell_hits` per component in config order, the component of pilot draw k recomputed from its seed by `Drawer.pilotComponent`; optional on the `Pilot`, absent for uniform; `generate` refuses a mixture with a recorded pilot lacking it). `pilot-gates.ts` (`proposalCoverage`) writes `coverage.proposal`: per component the pilot counts, P1 (role_sets: share of the table's species, move and ability ids that resolve in the vocabulary, required to be 1000: the ADR fixes R at 100%, so there is no config field; one comes with U; uniform has none; the usage threshold arrives with U), P2 (accepted pilot draws, permille), P3 (`bitsOf`: the largest b with the bound <= 2^-b, an exact BigInt rational: size!/(N)_size x the product of the six largest per-entry bounds, where an entry's bound is 1/(abilities x C(pool, moves)) (role_sets: the mean over the entry's roles) x the nature, gender and Item Clause item factors (the clause removes the `floor((size-1)/limit)` largest weights from the total; a required item is 1); Stat Points are ignored, which keeps it an upper bound; a team is the set of its members, moves unordered), the pilot and delivered per-cell counts and the delivered split counts (the component of each delivered record is recomputed from its seed); `mixture_p3_bits` (share-weighted sum); P4 (`strata` of the final plan against `reference_strata`, the plan of `--uniform-pilot`, a pilot of the same config without `proposal` made by `pilot --without-proposal`, at the same d_max, sizes and parameters; thousandths); `failed` (`P1:<kind>`, `P2:`, `P3:`, `P4`, `gates:not_configured`; a gate with no threshold or no reference is not met) and `publishable`. A PUBLISHING run is one given `--publish`: the files are still written, and the exit code is 1 when any gate failed or could not be evaluated (stderr lists them); without it the notice is printed and the exit stays 0. `coverage.population` (E-13.6) states the proposal and shares and "conditioned on the merged cells, weighted by the published quotas" (mixture or evaluation-only runs only). `--d-team N` adds `coverage.near_duplicates {d_team, pairs[{of, against, teams, near}]}` (validation to dev, validation to training, dev to training; counts only) using `NearIndex` (moved to `near-index.ts` with `teamDistance` and `SealedError`, re-exported by `sealed.ts`, behaviour unchanged). `--evaluation-only --d-team N --reference FILE...` (needs a pure component config, one component) builds a `NearIndex` of the references' pastes and `Binner` drops, after the full-bin and duplicate checks, every candidate within N differing fields of one, logging it as `near_reference` (`RejectionLog` counts `near_references`); the manifest gets `evaluation_only {d_team, near_references}` (optional, minor change within v1; the reader requires near_references <= draws and, with the config, one component). `trainingPool` refuses such a dataset (`TrainingDrawError`) and `datasetPoolDetail`/`datasetTeamPool` with a `GenerationError`; `readDataset`, `datasetValidationPool` and `--eval-dataset-dir` accept it. `loadFixtureInputs(engine, format, datasetDir?)` now picks, from `config/teams/<format>/generator*.json`, the config whose hash the dataset's manifest names, so a mixture or role-set dataset reads against its own config. Only COMMITTED generator configs can be read back this way (`generator.role-sets-eval.json`, role_sets 1000, is the pure config for EXP-M1's V_M*); a dataset from an uncommitted config fails to read with a config-hash mismatch. Tests: `pilot-gates.test.ts` (P3 against hand-worked toys, the gate arithmetic, cross-split counts on a hand-built split pair, a small mixture generation), `evaluation-only.test.ts` (Binner exclusion, a generation from the committed config, the readers and pools), `command.test.ts`.

**Parquet shards** (`packages/team-dataset/src/shards/`, ADR-013 §4, §6, §7, #862). A snapshot is immutable, content-addressed Parquet shards plus a `TeamSnapshotManifest`; shards live in R2 and Releases, never in git (`check_binary_files.py` rejects `.parquet`), so every test writes them to a temp dir. `columns.ts` is the column layout (parquet schema version 1: one column per record scalar, `labels` as a list of `{label_id, value}` structs, derived `stratum` and `label:<id>` columns) and the rebuild rule (`recordOf`: null, a missing or extra column, an int64 outside +-(2^53-1) or a derived column that differs from the labels refuses the shard). `layout.ts` is the layout (`S = ceil(R / 2^18)`, shard = first 8 bytes of HMAC-SHA256(`shard_key`, canonical hash) mod S, rows sorted by stratum then canonical hash, `cell_ranges`). `reader.ts` (`readShard`, hyparquet and hyparquet-compressors, both MIT, production dependencies that pass `make licences`) decodes, rebuilds, computes `record_hash` and checks row count, order, split, `logical_hash` and `cell_ranges` against the manifest; it never calls the engine. `snapshot.ts` builds the manifest (`buildSnapshotManifest`, checked by the bridge's `readTeamSnapshotManifest`), reads a snapshot shard by shard (`eachSnapshotShard`: file SHA-256 and size, one-to-one shard indices) and holds `checkFixtureAgreement` (a committed split's `hashes.json` and the shards of that split must list the same sorted `record_hash`es). `convert.ts` turns a dataset directory (`records.<split>.jsonl` or the committed `teams-<split>.jsonl`, plus `manifest.json`) into shards under `pub/v1/shards/<sha256>` and `snapshot.json` with bounded memory (one shard at a time) and reads every shard back once; `verifySnapshot` checks the shards against the dataset file byte for byte. The writer is the DuckDB command-line tool run as a child process (`duckdb.ts`): never a library import, never in a `package.json`, lockfile, Dockerfile or image (a test scans every tracked manifest), user-installed (`DUCKDB_BIN` or the PATH); `scripts/duckdb-pin.json` holds the version URL and SHA-256 of the zip and binary per platform, CI downloads and checks it and sets `ARENEON_REQUIRE_DUCKDB=1` so the real-tool tests cannot skip there (without the tool they skip, and a stand-in that writes through hyparquet-writer, a dev dependency, covers the conversion paths). The manifest's `writer` is read from the binary that ran. File bytes depend on the writer version and settings; `logical_hash` and `logical_snapshot_hash` do not (tests cross two DuckDB settings, and two versions when `DUCKDB_BIN_ALT` is set). Cross-architecture byte equality is not established: see ADR-013 §6. Measured rebuild is about 20 us per record (about 5 s per 2^18-row shard) against the ADR-013 d1 and section 5 budget of about 7.8 us (about 2 s); a read of one 250k-row shard peaks at 2.1 GB RSS (maxRSS, includes uncollected garbage), a write at 3.5 GB, while the decoded shard retains about 0.42 GB of heap after a forced GC (the ADR's 0.4 GB holds for the cache, not for the peak). The miss is to be recorded as an ADR erratum (#882). Publishing, the sampler and the working database are later slices.

**Private shards** (`packages/team-dataset/src/shards/private-shards.ts`, `private-reader.ts`, ADR-015 §2, #899): the d1 layout with the `PrivateTeamRecord` columns (`privateColumnsFor`: the record's scalars, `supersedes_record_hash` nullable, `tags` a list of strings, `holdout` a boolean, `labels`, the derived `stratum` and `label:<id>` columns; parquet schema version 1, `data_class` private, one split `owner`). It reuses `shardCount`, `shardAssigner` (HMAC of the canonical hash), `cellRanges`, the int64 rule and `recordHash` / `shardLogicalHash`. Differences: rows are ordered by stratum, canonical hash, then `team_id` (a retired record may share its team's canonical hash with an active one), cell ranges carry `active_rows` (`privateCellRanges`), and every refusal message is fixed text naming a layout column or a row position, never a value, a column name read from the file or library text (a canary test in `private-team-store`). `readPrivateShard` is engine-free and keyless like `readShard`, but the Parquet decode runs in a worker thread (`private-decode.ts`: `decodeIsolated`, an empty environment, a 4 GB heap limit, a wall-clock limit of 5 minutes by default, terminated afterwards; the worker is a `data:` module that imports the decode, so there is no separate worker script): hyparquet is written for trusted files and a damaged one can make it loop forever (a page header with a negative size), allocate without bound, or leave a rejected promise unhandled, which ends the process with library text; a seeded corruption fuzz (`snapshot-fuzz.test.ts`) found all three. Whatever the worker meets, the main thread sees the rows, a layout refusal's fixed message or `not a readable Parquet file`. The footer checks and the column-by-column read are `decodePrivateParquet` (tested in process, because the worker is not instrumented for coverage). The new column kinds (`NULLABLE_VARCHAR`, `BOOLEAN`, `VARCHAR_LIST`) are in `ColumnType`, and `DuckdbWriter.writeShard` takes `isolated: { dir, timeoutMs? }` for the private path: the tool runs with HOME, TMPDIR and cwd set to a scratch directory made inside `dir`, `DUCKDB_HISTORY=/dev/null` and nothing else in its environment (the `Runner` gained an optional cwd and timeout), the SQL starts with `SET autoinstall_known_extensions = false; SET autoload_known_extensions = false;` and locks the configuration (`SET lock_configuration = true;`) after the other settings and before the copy (a real-tool test reads the three settings back inside a child of the same shape), the tool is killed after `timeoutMs` (10 minutes by default), a failure is the fixed `WITHHELD_MESSAGE` (DuckDB's stderr can quote a paste and is never forwarded; a real-tool test provokes a DuckDB error that quotes the row and checks the public path forwards it and the private one does not), and the scratch directory is removed by `removeTree` (a best-effort chmod of leftovers, then a second try; it never throws, so cleanup cannot replace the original error; a removal that fails after a successful write is the fixed `CLEANUP_MESSAGE`). The public `writeShard` path is unchanged.

**Repo guards** (`scripts/`, Python): `check_no_pokepaste.py` rejects PokePaste-shaped files outside `fixtures/` (exact-path exemptions in `.pokepaste-guard-allow`). `check_data_class.py` (ADR-008) rejects committed JSON/JSONL with `"data_class": "private"` anywhere, and, outside `fixtures/`, any team-shaped object (`species` + `moves` + `evs`) without `"data_class": "synthetic"` at the document root; and (ADR-009 rule T2) any sealed evaluation Episode (an object with `"kind": "evaluation"` and `"sealed": true`) everywhere, `fixtures/` included, except the exact paths in `SEALED_MARKERS` (the shared corpus's synthetic markers: `valid/evaluation-sealed.json`, and the negative sample `invalid/collection-evaluation-no-seed.json`, under `fixtures/contracts/v1/episode/`) whose root says `"data_class": "synthetic"`; a new marker is an edit to that constant, under code-owner review. The guard checks structure and path, not content: a real Episode relabelled synthetic and placed at an allowlisted path is caught only by review and the O13 hash scan (evaluation-protocol §7), and stringified JSON inside a string is not inspected. A marker added by another PR means rebasing onto this constant (the pinning test fails until `SEALED_MARKERS` is edited); both run in `make team-guard`. A TeamRecord (a string `paste` and `team_hash`) and a team dataset or snapshot manifest (`dataset_id`, `generator`, `key`, `splits`; `snapshot_id`, `shards`, `cells`) must carry `"data_class": "synthetic"` on the object itself, `fixtures/` included, and a dataset manifest's `key` must be `{"kind": "public", ...}` (any other kind of key is a sealed or unknown one). Any object with a string `paste` member is team-shaped too (ADR-015 §6) and needs a synthetic root outside `fixtures/`. `PRIVATE_SAMPLE_MARKERS` (exact files, never directories, a code-owner-reviewed edit; today the `private-team-record` `valid/` and `reader-invalid/` samples, which a vitest test keeps equal to the files that carry the label and a pytest test requires to exist and to pass the guard as committed) lists synthetic samples of a private record whose root says `"data_class": "private"`: the guard itself requires at least one `paste` in the file and every `paste` to equal a paste in the committed synthetic team corpus (`fixtures/teams/**`: `.txt` files, and `paste` strings in its JSON and JSONL files). The root keys must equal `PRIVATE_RECORD_KEYS` (the PrivateTeamRecord shape of ADR-015 §2; both a pytest test and a vitest test pin it to the properties of `private-team-record.schema.json`, so a schema change edits that one constant), no other object may be team-shaped, and no other string or key may look like a paste (`Species @ Item`, `Ability:`, `- Move` lines), so real team text cannot ride in a marked file. Every value is type-checked against the PrivateTeamRecord (ADR-015 §2: `schema_version` 1, `data_class` `private`, 32-hex `team_id`, `revision` 1 to 2^31-1, null or 64-hex `supersedes_record_hash`, 64-hex hashes, `status` active or retired, at most 16 `tags` matching `[a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]{0,31}`, at most 64 `labels` that are exactly `{label_id, value}` ADR-013 token pairs, a boolean `holdout`); that the two hashes equal the paste's needs the engine's `Teams.import`, so the guard cannot check it; `private-records.test.ts` round-trips every marked paste through `importPaste` and requires its `teamHash` in `syntheticFixtureHashes`. An `invalid` PrivateTeamRecord sample cannot carry the private label (the guard checks a marked file's types), so it says `synthetic`, and a test flips the label back to show it stays invalid for its own fault. `PRIVATE_MANIFEST_MARKERS` (exact files, a code-owner-reviewed edit) lists the corpus samples of a `PrivateSnapshotManifest` that say `"data_class": "private"`; the guard accepts such a file only if it is exactly a manifest (`PRIVATE_MANIFEST_SPEC`, pinned to the schema by a test): the schema's keys at every level and no others (`parent_snapshot_hash` and `first` optional), every string a 64-hex hash, the 40-hex engine commit, a fixed word or an id token (`[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._:-]{0,127}`), every number an integer in the schema's range, and no `paste` or team-shaped object, so no team content or free text can ride in a manifest. The marker waives the root's private label only. The guard also refuses any JSON or JSONL file with a duplicate object key (a reader keeps the last value, the first stays in the committed bytes). The marker waives only the private label on the root object, not the sealed or unlabelled-team rules (a pinning test over the whole tracked tree fails on a marker without a file or a private-labelled file without a marker). `check_binary_files.py` (ADR-013 §8, ADR-015 §6) rejects a tracked Parquet (`.parquet`, also `.gz`, `.zst`, `.snappy` and `.bz2`), age, DuckDB (`.duckdb`, `.duckdb.wal`, `.ddb`), Arrow, Feather or SQLite (`.sqlite`, `.sqlite3`, `.db`, with `-wal`, `-shm` and `-journal` files) file by name, any tracked file that is not UTF-8 or holds a NUL byte (so a renamed binary is caught), the Parquet (`PAR1`, and `PARE` for an encrypted footer when the file also holds a NUL or non-UTF-8 byte), SQLite, age and DuckDB signatures, armored age text anywhere in a file, and a base64 run (wrapped or not, URL-safe or standard) that decodes to one of those signatures; its exact allowlist `ALLOWED` starts empty (a code-owner-reviewed edit), and `.gitignore` carries `*.duckdb`, `*.duckdb.wal`, `*.parquet` (with `!fixtures/**/*.parquet`), `*.age` and `.tmp/`. `.gitleaks.toml` extends gitleaks' default rules with `age-secret-key-pq` (the default rule misses hybrid `AGE-SECRET-KEY-PQ-1...` identities) beside the narrow dataset-key allowlist; the pre-commit hook and the CI `gitleaks` job read it from the repository root. These are not part of `make team-guard`. `tests/scripts/test_gitleaks_config.py` (in `make python`) builds keys in memory, never committed: its regex tests always run, its binary tests only when gitleaks is on PATH or named by `GITLEAKS` (the python job has none), so the CI `gitleaks` job runs the same positive and negative checks with the pinned binary (a generated PQ identity is reported; a generic key line outside the dataset fixtures is still reported; a 57-character PQ-shaped string is not). Both run in `make team-guard`. Private snapshots (ADR-015 §6) are never committed or published (no Release, public bucket, image or fixture): `data_class: private` is refused anywhere in committed JSON, Parquet is refused by the binary guard, and `.gitignore` keeps `private-data/`. A gitleaks test case with a bucket-credential-shaped pair (a 32-hex id and a 64-hex secret, which the default rules may miss) is planned with the import (#887). `check_licenses.py` fails closed on any production dependency licence outside the allowlist, for pnpm and, through `python_licences.py`, for the shipped Python packages' whole lockfile closure (an SPDX `License-Expression`, else an explicitly mapped trove classifier, else refused). `SHIPPED` in `python_licences.py` lists the shipped Python packages (`areneon-contracts`, `areneon-ml`). The allowlist includes `BSL-1.0` and `Zlib` (ADR-001 clarification); a package whose only licence metadata is the bare BSD trove classifier is admitted only through its entry in `scripts/licence_exceptions.json` (package, licence verified from its own licence text, version, evidence, date; code-owner reviewed; the classifier is never mapped). `ml/` resolves CPU `torch` from the `pytorch-cpu` uv index (root `pyproject.toml`, `explicit`, used by `ml/pyproject.toml` only). `check_cuda_separation.py` (in `make licences`, ADR-010 §5.2 C-1 to C-3) fails if the root `uv.lock` or a shipped closure holds `nvidia-*`, `cuda-toolkit`, `cuda-bindings` or `triton`, if the root workspace lists the GPU training project (convention: its own uv project in `ml/gpu-env/` with its own lock, never a workspace member or in `SHIPPED`), or if a Dockerfile, Containerfile or compose file refers to it. C-3 is a name-based check (case-insensitive, `-` and `_` ignored; `vendor/` skipped at the repo root only): that an image installs from the root lock only is enforced by the first `ml/` image's build test, not by this guard. A `licence_exceptions.json` entry holds only for the version it names (another installed version is refused, so a bump needs the licence text read again) and an entry for a package outside the shipped closure fails the check. Every `uv sync` now installs CPU torch (the venv grows from about 113M to 894M) and `osv` may report torch CVEs. No CUDA package or licence is allowlisted. `licences-selftest.sh` (`make licences-selftest`, CI only: it needs network) injects an AGPL dependency into a scratch copy, once for pnpm and once for Python, and an `nvidia-*` package into the shipped `ml` closure, and requires `make licences` there to fail each time. `.github/CODEOWNERS` puts the gates under code-owner review.

**Rules store** (ADR-007, #704): published rules snapshots and channel history live in PostgreSQL 18, append-only. `db/bootstrap.sql` (a superuser, once; role passwords from the environment) creates `rules_owner` (NOLOGIN, owns the tables), `rules_migrator` (runs dbmate), `importer` (INSERT/SELECT) and `areneon_reader` (SELECT) and the `rules` schema. `db/migrations/` (dbmate, pinned image) defines `rules_snapshot` and `rules_release` with named CHECKs (hash = sha256(body), columns agree with the payload, the channel enum equals `channel.schema.json`'s) and triggers that refuse UPDATE, DELETE and TRUNCATE.
