Publishing (ADR-007 §6): `.github/workflows/rules-publish.yml` runs on main after a rules change in two jobs. `plan` (read-only, no environment) re-runs the channel check on the merged commit, builds the artifacts not yet released (`src/rules-publish.ts`: the commit's snapshot `rules-snapshot-<hash>.json`, and each channel's current file as `rules-channel-<channel>-<commit that last changed it>.json`, validated by `packages/schema/rules/v1/channel-artifact.schema.json`) with a sha256 manifest as a workflow artifact, and outputs `publish=true` only if there are any, so a push with nothing new ends without asking for approval. `publish` (protected environment `rules-dev`) then checks the downloaded files against the manifest and against the plan made again (`verifyPlan`), writes the release set to `verified/` (skipping entries released meanwhile), and attests (build provenance) and releases only that verified set, exactly those bytes, snapshots first. A Release counts only if it is published and carries its file; runs are grouped per commit, so none is dropped. A channel must name this commit's snapshot or a released one; an older snapshot that was never released (before this workflow, or a cancelled run) is refused: re-run that commit's workflow, or revert the records in a PR. `packages/rules-store` tests it against a real Postgres, so the root `pnpm test` skips it: `make db-up db-test` locally, the `database` job in CI. `make db-bootstrap-test` (also in that job) runs `db/bootstrap.sql` against its own throwaway Postgres that logs every statement: it refuses missing and blank (empty or whitespace-only) passwords, which psql checks on the client, and requires each password in the server log only in its own `CREATE ROLE`. Never point these at the Coolify database.

**Rules importer** (`packages/rules-importer`, ADR-007 §6, #768), the last step of the rules pipeline (publish, then import): `GitHubReleaseSource` lists the non-draft `rules-snapshot-*` / `rules-channel-*` Releases through `gh` (an injected `CommandRunner` whose children see only an env allowlist), resolves each tag to its commit, downloads each asset into a private temp dir and keeps only the bytes `GhAttestationVerifier` returns. It skips a snapshot asset whose hash (in its name) is already stored; `planImport` then plans a snapshot a channel names by reference (`stored-snapshot`), and `applyImport` refuses the plan unless that row is stored with hash = sha256(body) (summary status `referenced`). A skipped asset is never used, so tampering with the Release of a stored snapshot is caught only by GitHub's reported digest; the stored row stays authoritative. `runImportCommand` (entry `src/cli.ts`) reads `DATABASE_URL` (never logged; a URL that sets a timeout or `options` is refused) and `RULES_IMPORT_{STATEMENT,LOCK,IDLE_IN_TRANSACTION}_TIMEOUT_MS`, requires the `importer` role, then `planImport` and `applyImport`, and prints one JSON line `{ok, stale, steps}`; exit 0 ok, 1 refused (the whole plan rolled back), 2 configuration. A `stale` channel step (seq at or below the stored maximum, no stored row at that seq) is skipped, never written, reported and counted in `stale`, and does not fail the run; a stored row at that seq with another hash or source commit is a fork and is refused. The planner stays fail-closed on a channel naming a snapshot that is neither released nor stored. `gh` needs `GH_TOKEN`. Its Dockerfile's default target is the production image (node, gh, CA bundle, `/app`); the `e2e` target alone adds a stand-in `gh` replaying `fixtures/rules-releases/`, and `FakeAttestationVerifier` lives only in the `./testing` export.

**Evaluation records and register** (ADR-010 §6, §7, §9 O1/O11, #846; code in `packages/showdown-bridge`, engine-free, Node built-ins only). Values live in reviewed data, never in code: `config/evaluation/evaluation-parameters.v1.json` (`EvaluationParameters`: §6, `maxTurns` included) and `training-gate-rule.v1.json` (`TrainingGateRule`: margins, alternatives, target power, the claim family). Ratios are integer ppm (`PPM` is only the unit). `evaluation-config.ts` reads each against its ADR-008 contract and then enforces what the profile cannot: alpha' = alpha / (m·B_sealed) is whole ppm, `b_boot` exceeds m·B/alpha, `r_cov` is at least 1/alpha', `cov_tol` is derived (never stored), sorted-and-unique lists, and, with both records (`readEvaluationConfig`, `checkGateConsistency`), the family size equals the claims, G2 claims name each baseline once, and each gate's `h` is at most what the target power allows (`maxHalfWidthPpm`, from computed normal quantiles; no z table). The measured entries (`n_teams_floor`, `n_teams`, `n_seeds`, `d_team`) are null in the committed v1 record, which is therefore *provisional*: `requireComplete` refuses to commit a set or register a plan on it. A record, entry, commitment or plan is referenced only by `domainHash(domain, value)` (SHA-256 of `areneon:<domain>:v1\n` plus its JCS bytes: a hash of one kind never stands for another); changing any value is a new version, never an edit. `evaluation-plan.ts` holds the `SealedSetCommitment` (L3: hashes and counts only, E1's stratum counts; `setCommitment`/`keyCommitment`/`checkReveal` compute and open the hashes, and no sealed content is ever a field) and the `EvaluationPlan` (I2) with `checkCommitment`/`checkPlan`, which tie both to the records they name. `evaluation-register.ts` is the register (I6, O1): an append-only JSON Lines file in the isolated store, each line the JCS bytes of an `EvaluationRegisterEntry` (`set_commitment`, `plan_registration`, `l2a_recheck`, `run_outcome`, `incident`, `set_retirement`) carrying the previous entry's hash. `verifyRegister(text, configs)` is pure and offline; given the records (the CLI defaults to the committed v1 files, repeatable `--parameters`/`--rule`) it re-checks every commitment and plan against them, ties their hashes to those files and takes the run budget from the parameters, and it refuses a changed, dropped, reordered or non-canonical line, a time that goes back, more plans than `b_sealed`, a second plan for a candidate, a run without a passing L2a re-check, and any result on a contaminated or retired set. The chain head (`EvaluationRegisterHead`: position and hash, no secret) is merged to `main` by PR as `evaluation/register-heads/<10-digit seq>.json` (or attested; a Redmine note is not a trusted timestamp); `checkHeads` (with `--heads-ref origin/main`, read from git, not the working tree) refuses a register shorter than a published head (rollback) or with another hash at a head's position (rewrite, fork), and `assertPlanPublished` gates a run on its plan's head: `append` of an `l2a_recheck` or `run_outcome` naming a plan is refused unless that head is published. Head files are add-only (`checkHeadsAddOnly`; the `typescript` CI job runs `heads-check --base HEAD^1`; CODEOWNERS covers `evaluation/register-heads/` and `config/evaluation/`). `init` is the only command that starts a register, `gen-secret` writes a 256-bit secret (mode 0600, never overwrites) and prints only its hash, and `checkReveal` also requires `team_count` teams whose strata equal `stratum_counts` (E1 minimum). `scripts/evaluation-register.ts` (logic in `src/evaluation-cli.ts`: `config-check`, `commit-set`, `register-plan`, `append`, `verify`, `head`, `heads-check`) is the owner's tool; `make ts` runs `heads-check`. The sample corpus covers every new contract (`src/evaluation-samples.ts`). The isolated store itself (a separate OS user plus a container, files mounted read-only only into the evaluation container) is created by the owner from `docs/runbooks/evaluation-store.md`; `scripts/check-evaluation-store.sh` (tested in `tests/scripts/test_check_evaluation_store.py` on a scratch directory) is the read-only check the owner runs as the store user (owner, modes, parents, `--code`, container mounts and user) and as the training user (`--probe-denied` needs EACCES, so a missing path fails). This code never creates a user, container or mount, and the evaluation runner and sealed team generation are other tickets.

**Sealed team generation** (`packages/team-dataset/src/sealed*.ts`, ADR-013 §2, protocol L1 to L5, #861). Runs only inside the isolated evaluation store (`docs/runbooks/evaluation-store.md` §9; `node packages/team-dataset/scripts/sealed.ts generate|reveal-hash|retire|verify`); no agent or CI job runs it against a real store, and the repository holds no sealed data and no key (tests use throwaway counter-derived keys and a temp store). `sealed-store.ts` is the file discipline: the store directory must be absolute, a real directory, owned by the invoking user, mode 0700, outside any git working tree and below no group- or world-writable parent (sticky accepted); directories are created 0700 and files 0600, never overwritten, and a loosened file is refused on read; JSON parse errors never quote the text. `sealed.ts`: the key and the salt are 256 bits from `generateSecret` (the OS CSPRNG; tests inject a source) and are written only to `<store>/sealed/<set_id>/{key,salt}`; they are never an option, a return value, a log line or an output. The set shares the public pool's generator, config, label set, vocabulary, version manifest and D20 strata (`sealedPlan` reads the public `TeamDatasetManifest`; it never merges again): `n_teams` is shared over the strata by pilot hits (largest remainder, at least `n_stratum_min`), the draw budget is the public `d_max` raised by the config's `sealed_margin_permille`, and a bin still short discards the whole generation (key, teams and rejections dropped; one `discards.jsonl` line of counts and the key's hash), then redraws a new key, up to `maxAttempts` (`SealedShortfallError` after that, nothing committed). L1 is the canonical hash, L2 is `NearIndex` (distance at most `d_team`, the fewest differing fields over all pairings of the sets' species, item, ability, nature, move set and Stat Points; an inverted index on whole sets finds candidates, then the exact distance), both against every `--known` team (public splits, released datasets, the training pool, retired sealed sets); `rejection-log.ts` gained the `near_duplicate` reason. The L3 commitment (`setCommitment`/`keyCommitment` over the teams `{k, paste, stratum}` in stratum then draw order) is checked with `checkCommitment`; the register CLI (not this module) commits it. Contracts: `SealedTeamSetManifest` (hashes, counts, plan and log hashes; no secret; `readSealedTeamSetManifest`) and `RetiredSealedManifest` (the reveal: key, salt, file hashes; `readRetiredSealedManifest`, `retiredManifestHash` is the register's `reveal_hash`), readers in `showdown-bridge/src/sealed-records.ts`. `SealedTeamHashes` is built only by `loadSealedTeamHashes` after the commitment is recomputed from the store, unions with the public hashes for stamping (`withPublic`), and refuses to serialise. `sealed-retire.ts`: `retireSealedSet` writes the reveal bundle (a new 0700 directory) only when the register logs `set_retirement` for the set with exactly that bundle's `reveal_hash` and the stored commitment; `verifyRetiredSealed` needs no store: it opens both commitments, runs `checkReveal` with the stratum from each team, regenerates the set from the revealed key against the reference pool (teams and the whole rejection log must be byte-equal, which replays every L1/L2 skip) and re-checks each team. `scripts/check_data_class.py` now rejects a reveal (`set_id` + `reveal.key`/`salt` + `files`) or a sealed-team line `{k, paste, stratum}` anywhere but the exact paths in `REVEAL_ALLOWED` (the corpus samples; a retired bundle is added by a reviewed edit). The index is in memory (the shard-scale build is a later slice).
**Sealed generation hardening** (#884, review P3s of #861): `sealed.ts verify` requires `--heads DIR` (and takes `--heads-ref REF`), reads the published chain heads with `readPublishedHeads` (exported from the bridge, shared with `evaluation-register.ts`) and runs `checkHeads` on the register it verifies, so a rollback or fork is exit 1 and a missing `--heads`, an absent or empty heads directory (or a ref with no head files, `requireHeads`; no override) or a malformed head file (fixed `EvaluationConfigError`, never the text) exit 2 (`evaluation-register.ts verify` keeps its no-head-yet path); `sealed.ts retire` takes the same `--heads`/`--heads-ref` and runs the same check (`checkPublishedHeads`, shared with verify) before the store is read, and `assertRetirementPublished` (`evaluation-register.ts`, beside `assertPlanPublished`) also requires the set's `set_retirement` entry to be at or before the latest published head, so a refused retire (rollback, fork, absent heads, unpublished retirement) writes nothing (#1242; tests: pass, rollback, fork, absent/empty/malformed/missing heads, unknown ref, unpublished retirement, no output on any refusal); the `no-console` lint covers `rejection-log.ts`, `draw.ts`, `dedupe.ts` and `binner.ts` beside `sealed*.ts`; `readStoredSet` returns a deeply frozen `StoredSet` (teams array and each team, `texts`, the set itself), so the `SealedTeamHashes.fromVerified` WeakSet gate cannot be bypassed by mutating a verified set; every JSON file and `--known` line the command reads goes through `parseSecretJson` (a usage error, exit 2, that names the file or line and never the text). Tests: `sealed-command.test.ts` (heads, malformed-JSON marker, retire/verify output canary with negative control) and `sealed.test.ts` (mutation throws).

**Private teams** (ADR-015, which supersedes ADR-014 and its encrypted store; the wording rules are in AGENTS.md; the owner's bucket, lock and token setup is `docs/runbooks/private-store.md`, #889). The owner's real teams are inputs to a separate private run after the Training gate (#878), never to public datasets, published checkpoints or Episodes. They are kept, once the bucket is set up (ADR-015; #889, #891), as plain `PrivateTeamRecord`s in Parquet shards (the ADR-013 d1 layout, `data_class: private`) in a private R2 bucket, written once per snapshot, as plain objects with no client-side encryption (R2 encryption at rest only); the owner accepts the residual token risk (ADR-015 section 1); on the owner's workstation they appear only under gitignored `private-data/` (`teams/` is staging, `snapshots/<snapshot_hash>/` the verified local copy, run outputs stay there). Two bucket-scoped tokens exist, a write token for the import only and a read token for readers; both come from the environment of one command on the owner's workstation, never from the repository, a log, a chat or a command line, and an agent never runs a command that holds one. Built (#898): the pure import planner in `packages/private-team-store/src` (`planImport` in `import-plan.ts`, `parseStagedFile` in `staged-file.ts`, typed refusals in `import-error.ts`). Built (#899): the snapshot writer and reader there (below). It takes the staged files as `{name, text}` items, the parent snapshot's `PrivateTeamRecord`s (or null for `--first`), the `--retire` team_ids, the engine and the resolved format, and ports for labels, the hold-out flag (the snapshot layer plugs in the keyed HMAC; the planner holds no key) and fresh team_ids; it returns the next record list (one record per `team_id`, sorted) and a summary of five counts (`new`, `duplicate`, `revised`, `unchanged`, `retired`), or throws an `ImportPlanError`. Header `#! team_id=<32 hex> tags=...` (either key optional, order fixed, tags sorted and unique, at most 16; an empty `tags=` clears the tags, an absent one keeps the parent's), any other `#` line refused; each team goes through `importPaste` and `validateTeam`; a `team_id` not in the parent is refused (never created). A file without an id whose canonical hash equals an active record's, or an earlier file's, changes nothing and counts as a `duplicate` (ADR-015 section 3), so re-importing the same staged folder against its own result is a no-op. An edit is revision +1; a staged record identical to its parent (paste and tags, active) is unchanged; a retired one staged with its id becomes active again; a team absent from staging is unchanged; retirement is only `--retire` (retiring a retired record is a no-op counted as unchanged); a revision or revival that takes another active record's team is `duplicate_canonical_hash`; a result that loses an active parent record is refused; parent records' hold-out flags must equal the hold-out port's value. Beyond the ADR text (to be listed in its erratum, #892): a tag-only edit and a Pokemon-order-only change each make a new revision. A refusal is a code plus a 1-based position that indexes the caller's list (staged files as passed, or the `--retire` list; the command slice maps it back to a file), never text from an input: no paste, file name, tag, header value, id, species or move, and an exception thrown by a port is replaced by a fixed `hook_failed`; the canary tests (`test/import-canary.test.ts`, which asserts every refusal code and has a negative control) enforce it. The package depends on `@areneon/showdown-bridge` as a `workspace:*` link (the packaging test accepts that form for `@areneon/*` only; no registry dependency was added). **Snapshot writer and reader** (#899, slice 3 of #887; `snapshot-writer.ts`, `snapshot-reader.ts`, `work-dir.ts`, `snapshot-io-error.ts`, `snapshot-plan.ts`; the package now depends on `@areneon/team-dataset` as `workspace:*`). `writePrivateSnapshot({records, holdoutKey, parentSnapshotHash | null, labelSet, manifest, vocabularyHash, shardKey, writer, pin, workDir, ...})` lays the records out in shards (`layoutPrivate`), writes each through the pinned DuckDB tool on the private path (`writer.requirePinned(pin)` first; the stand-in or real tool in tests), reads every shard back with the engine-free private shard reader, and returns `{manifest, snapshotHash, shards}` in memory (shard files by manifest index; the writer uploads nothing: that is `publishSnapshot`, below; the CLI, `export` and the collision count are #901). Shards are ordered by logical hash, each with its position as `index` (the HMAC placement index is a separate property the reader checks). The records must already carry their hold-out flags (the writer refuses a flag that differs from `holdoutFlag(key, team_id)`) and satisfy `checkPrivateRecordSet`; a stratum is the label set's base cell (`labelPlan`; no merged strata for owner teams). Intermediates (the rows file and the shard file, which hold pastes in the clear) exist only in a fresh 0700 directory inside `workDir`, which `assertWorkDir` accepts only if it is absolute, a real directory (no symbolic link), owned by the invoking user, mode 0700, no parent writable by group or other (a sticky bit is accepted, as in the sealed store) and every parent owned by the user or root, and either has a `private-data` path segment or is on tmpfs/ramfs (`statfs`); a Windows drive mounted in WSL (`/mnt/<drive>`) is accepted only on tmpfs, because a 0700 mode proves little there; the owner and file-system probes are a test seam of `assertWorkDir` only (the writer's input has no `probe`, and a test reads its source to say so). They are removed afterwards, on failure too; if the write succeeded but the removal did not, the call fails with `cleanup_failed`, and a write that failed keeps its own error. `readPrivateSnapshot({engine, manifest, shard(index), labelSet, expectedSnapshotHash, expected})` is what the import (after writing and after downloading) and #888-era readers run: `readPrivateSnapshotManifest`, a parent equal to the snapshot's own hash refused, the hash the manifest was fetched under (`expectedSnapshotHash` is REQUIRED: there is no option to read without an anchor), the version manifest and vocabulary hash the caller derives from the pinned engine and rules in `expected` (`version_manifest_mismatch`; REQUIRED like the hash: a reader of a snapshot made under another pin says `expected: INSPECT_ONLY` out loud, and `publishSnapshot` refuses that value), `label_set_hash` = `recordHash(labelSet)`, the Parquet layout version, no two shards with one `file_sha256`, `shardCount(rows)` = number of shards, each file's size and SHA-256, `readPrivateShard` (columns, order, placement, logical hash, ranges with active rows), no two shards on one layout index, each record through `readPrivateTeamRecord` (engine round trip), each hold-out flag recomputed from the manifest's key and ppm, and `checkPrivateRecordSet`; it returns the records sorted by `team_id`. The counts need no check of their own: the manifest reader ties `records` and `cells` to the shards' ranges and each shard read ties its ranges to the decoded rows. Every refusal is a `SnapshotIoError` with a code (`SNAPSHOT_IO_REFUSALS`), a fixed message and at most a shard index and row number; whatever a lower layer threw (DuckDB stderr, hyparquet, the engine, ajv) is dropped and not attached as `cause`. Tests: `snapshot.test.ts` (round trip, every code), `snapshot-canary.test.ts` (markers in a paste nickname, tag, label, column name, file bytes, the tool's stderr, a key, a path and a failing shard source; error text, stack, fields and process output scanned; negative controls show the scan flags a leaking message and the public writer path), `snapshot-forged.test.ts` and `snapshot-self-parent.test.ts` (consistent forgeries the writer cannot make). The real-tool case runs where `DUCKDB_BIN` is the pinned binary and cannot skip when `ARENEON_REQUIRE_DUCKDB=1` (CI sets it for `pnpm test`; no workflow change was needed). Not checked by the reader: that the manifest's hold-out block and key equal the parent's (needs the parent), and `vocabulary_hash` / the version manifest against the engine (the caller's). `manifest.regulation` stays `minLength 1` because `make contracts` requires every contract's `$defs/VersionManifest` to equal `version-manifest.schema.json`; a pattern needs a change of that shared definition (not done here). **Object store and publish** (#900, slice 4 of #887; `object-store.ts`, `memory-store.ts`, `r2-client.ts`, `r2-settings.ts`, `current-pointer.ts`, `local-copy.ts`, `snapshot-store.ts`, `publish.ts`). `ObjectStore` is the port: `putIfAbsent(key, body)` (`If-None-Match: *`; an existing object is `ObjectStoreError("exists")` and nothing is overwritten), `get`/`head` (null when absent), `list(prefix)` (only `snapshots/` or `snapshots/<hash>/`) and `overwriteCurrent(body)`, the only overwrite, which cannot reach any key but `current.json`; there is no delete. Keys are checked against the layout (`current.json`, `snapshots/<64 hex>/manifest.json`, `snapshots/<64 hex>/shards/<n>.parquet`): anything else, `snapshots/_lock-check/` included, can be neither written nor fetched, and `listSnapshotHashes` returns only the hashes that have a manifest object, so an unknown key under `snapshots/` is never a snapshot (a test seeds one); `fetchSnapshot` is the verification (`readPrivateSnapshot` on the downloaded bytes). `InMemoryObjectStore` (tests; `seed` puts an object where the port would refuse to, as the owner's lock-check object) and `R2ObjectStore` implement it; one contract suite runs against both (the R2 client on a fake bucket behind `fetch`, so no test opens a socket; `test/network-isolation.test.ts` requires that only `r2-client.ts` calls `fetch` or imports a network module). The R2 client is path-style (`<endpoint>/<bucket>/<key>`), signed with `aws4fetch` 1.0.20 (MIT, no dependencies, pinned exactly, ignored by Dependabot, imported by `r2-settings.ts` only; the `age-encryption`, `@noble/*` and `@scure/base` pins of the superseded design are gone) with the real SHA-256 of the body in `x-amz-content-sha256`, no redirects, no retries, a time limit; 412 and 409 on a conditional write are `exists`, 401/403 `denied`, 429/5xx and any transport failure `unavailable`, other statuses `failed`, a listing or head that is not in the expected form `response_invalid`; every message is fixed and carries no response body, URL, header, bucket or key (`ObjectStoreError`, no `cause`). `R2Settings.fromEnvironment(env, argv, bucket)` reads `AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID`, `AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY`, `AWS_ENDPOINT_URL` (https, an `*.r2.cloudflarestorage.com` host, nothing else in the URL) and `AWS_DEFAULT_REGION` (`auto`) from the environment only; a command line that names one of them (`AWS_*`, `--secret`, `--token`, `--endpoint`, `--account`, `--region`, ...) or contains a value (key id, secret, endpoint, the account id) is `credentials_on_argv` before the environment is read. The values live in `#` fields; the object prints as `[R2Settings]` to String, JSON and `util.inspect`, and it signs requests itself, so only the signing library sees the secret. The bucket name is an argument, not a credential. `test/credential-canary.test.ts` puts markers in all four variables and the bucket, scans errors (message, stack, fields, inspect) and process output on every settings, client and publish error path, including fake responses that echo the markers, checks that the secret appears in no request, and has negative controls. `publishSnapshot({store, engine, labelSet, written, expected, expectedCurrent, snapshotsDir, ...})` runs the ADR-015 section 3 sequence: the snapshot's parent must equal `expectedCurrent` (null for the first) and `current.json` must still name it; the local copy's place must be free and safe; every shard then the manifest (JCS bytes) uploaded with `If-None-Match: *`; every object downloaded again and verified with `fetchSnapshot` (required `expected`, the manifest bytes compared with the sent ones); the verified copy written to `private-data/snapshots/<hash>/` (`assertWorkDir` rules, directories 0700, files 0600, built in a hidden partial directory and renamed, never replacing a copy); `current.json` (`{parent_snapshot_hash, schema_version, snapshot_hash}`, strict reader) checked once more and overwritten last. The final check and the overwrite are a read and a write, not one atomic request: a second writer in between is not excluded (one owner, one write token, ADR-015 section 7). A failure before the last step leaves `current.json` as it was; uploaded objects stay (the lock keeps them; they are unreferenced), and a retry with the very same bytes meets `exists`, so the next attempt needs another `snapshot_id` (another hash). A 404 reads as "absent", so a wrong bucket name looks like an empty bucket until the first write is refused. Also carried from the #899 review: the decode worker settles on `exit` with the fixed message instead of waiting for its time limit. Planned, not built yet: the import and `export` commands around the writer and `publishSnapshot` (#887 slice #901: reading the bucket name and the env, the parent check against `current.json` and the local copy, a change set of staged `.txt` files, explicit retirement, the collision count), the snapshot-backed `PrivateTeamSource` (#888; consumers such as the bot client take the port, never the private package), the DuckDB read recipe for inspection (#890: read token only, no persistent secret, no history file, no UI on private data) and the first import (#891). The only private source in the code today is `PasteDirectorySource`. The `packages/private-team-store` package (#868) is a `private: true` pnpm workspace package that is never shipped and holds the import planner, the snapshot writer and reader, the object store port with its R2 client and the publish sequence so far; the import commands will live there too. `packages/private-team-store/test/packaging.test.ts` is the packaging rule: no Dockerfile, compose file or Coolify build file names the package, no other `package.json` depends on it, no Dockerfile copies a whole directory of packages or runs `pnpm install` without `--filter`, and the DuckDB CLI, the `age` CLI and OpenSSL appear in no `package.json`, `pyproject.toml` or Dockerfile. The Dockerfile checks parse `COPY`/`ADD` sources (explicit non-glob paths only) and pnpm commands, and `test/packaging-rules.ts` has table-driven bad and good cases for every check. A Coolify application configured in the UI is not visible to a repo test: it is an operator rule (README). "Owner's machines" means the owner's workstation only; keeping the read token and any private run off every other host is an operator rule, not a code guarantee (the code guards are `loadTrainingSet`, `stampTeam` / `readEpisode` and the provenance checker, above). `make licences` checks the dependencies like any production dependency.

**Owner-session guard, private output root and canary harness** (ADR-015 erratum E1, #1059; groundwork for the owner-run private commands of Epic #1058). `packages/showdown-bridge/src/owner-session.ts` and `ml/areneon_ml/private/owner_session.py` are one behaviour in two languages: every command that reads private teams calls `assertOwnerSession` / `assert_owner_session` before it reads any team. It refuses when `CLAUDECODE` or `AI_AGENT` is set (any non-empty value), unless stdin, stdout and stderr are all terminals, and unless `/dev/tty` opens (a tmux terminal is a terminal and passes). There is no override flag. A child process that a guarded driver starts has pipes for stdio and calls `assertGuardedChild` / `assert_guarded_child`, which checks the environment only. A refusal is an `OwnerSessionError` with a code (`agent_session`, `not_a_terminal`, `no_controlling_terminal`) and fixed words that name no path or value; `fixtures/private-guard/refusals.json` holds the words of both guards and of the root check, and a test on each side pins its constants to it. It is accident prevention, not a boundary. `team-inventory` uses the shared `terminalRefusal` and `openControllingTerminal` with unchanged messages. `private-root.ts` / `private_root.py` are the one rule for where a private command may write: `assertPrivateRoot` accepts a directory only if it is absolute, a real directory (no symbolic link in the path), owned by the invoking user, mode 0700, below no group- or world-writable parent (sticky accepted), every parent owned by the user or root, not on a Windows drive mount, and has a `private-data` path segment (the `assertWorkDir` rules, which also admit tmpfs for snapshot intermediates; an output root does not, and each failure has its own code). `uid` and `parentUid` are test seams. `privateRootFile` / `writePrivateFile` re-check the root, accept one plain file name only and create the file 0600 without overwriting. `packages/showdown-bridge/test/support/private-canary.ts` is the privacy canary harness for any later private command: `makeCanaryWorld` stages stand-in teams from `fixtures/teams/` (private-classed, each Pokemon given a marker nickname) in a throwaway 0700 `private-data` root; `observe` runs a command capturing stdout, stderr, the console, the exception (message, stack, fields, cause chain), extra surfaces the command returns, and every file or file name written outside the private root (and the modes inside it); `findLeaks` / `expectNoLeak` look for markers, species, items, abilities, moves, set lines, team hashes and pastes. `test/private-canary.test.ts` holds the negative controls that show each surface is flagged. Python tests of a later private driver use the same fixture teams and the guard's pseudo-terminal pattern in `tests/ml/private/`.

**Private test** (ADR-015 erratum E1 item 2, #1060; `docs/training-run.md` section 6). `python -m areneon_ml.loop private-test` (owner only, no override) measures a frozen pair of a committed generation on the owner's teams, both seats, counts and scores only. The Python top level (`ml/areneon_ml/private/private_test.py`) runs `assert_owner_session` first, then `assert_private_root` on `--out-root`, then verifies the run exactly as the progress evaluation does (`_source`, now with `allow_private` and `needs_dataset` keywords: a pair with private ancestry reads as `candidate_data_class: private`), and starts the Node child `packages/private-team-store/scripts/private-test.ts` (guarded by `assertGuardedChild`, environment only), which reads the teams, plays through `playPrivateTest` (`packages/showdown-bridge/src/private-test.ts`, built from the progress evaluation's seats: `rowOf`, `tallyRows`, `damageAwareBaseline`, `frozenPairClient`) and prints one line with the record. Python never holds a team; it re-reads the record with its own reader, ties it to the request, writes it (0600, never overwritten; an identical rerun is a no-op) and logs a look (`private-test.looks.jsonl` in the root, the validation look log's format). Sources: `staging` (`PasteDirectorySource`, subset `all`), `snapshot` (the verified local copy through `readPrivateSnapshot`, `src/test-source.ts`, subset `all` = active records or `holdout`) and `dataset` (`datasetValidationPool`). The pair is refused unless it has no private ancestry, or the source is a snapshot's `holdout` or the public dataset (`privateTestAllowed`; the staged directory and a whole snapshot are refused for a private-ancestry pair; the ticket text allows both a public dataset and holdout-only, read here as either). `seed`/pairing/commitment are keyed by a per-test 256-bit salt (`private-test-seed/1`, `private-test-pairing/1`, `private-test-seed.ts`; the salt file stays in the root; the commitment is SHA-256 of the JCS of `{salt, sorted canonical team hashes}`). Plan kind `disjoint-pairs` only; the `all-pairs` option of the ticket (bootstrap over teams) is not built. `PrivateTestRecord` (`packages/schema/contracts/v1/private-test-record.schema.json`, `data_class` const `private`, integers only, closed): the pair's ids and class, the test set (commitment, team and pair counts, source kind, snapshot or dataset hash, subset), the plan, and per match games, wins, ties, losses, turn-cap ties, forfeits by side and cause, pairs and a pair-score histogram (the bootstrap needs the pairs' scores, and a histogram of five counts holds them with no team). Readers in TS (`private-test-record.ts`) and Python (`private/private_test_record.py`) recompute the counts. The interval (`areneon_ml.progress.stats.pair_interval`) is seeded from the commitment and the match id (`private-test-bootstrap/1`), so it is derived from the record alone, never stored, and always labelled `private test set; not the Training gate; not validation`. The corpus samples that carry the private label are listed in `PRIVATE_TEST_RECORD_MARKERS` (`check_data_class.py`; the guard accepts them only in the strict `PRIVATE_TEST_RECORD_SPEC` shape). Every failure is a fixed `private-test: <code>` (and a 1-based team position); the child's stderr is forwarded only if it is exactly such a line. Tests: `packages/showdown-bridge/test/private-test.test.ts` (canary harness with marker nicknames and negative controls, determinism, every refusal), `packages/private-team-store/test/test-source.test.ts`, `tests/ml/private/test_private_test.py` and `test_private_test_record.py`. The real Python worker is not driven by these tests (stand-in clients), and no run ever reads real teams in CI.

**CI** (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`) runs `python`, `typescript` (submodule checkout + `make showdown`, and the locked CPU closure of `areneon-ml` so the real-worker test runs; the job fails if a CUDA package comes with it), `database` (rules store on Postgres 18), `importer-image` (`make importer-e2e`), `team-guard`, `licences`, `gitleaks`, `osv` and `dco`. The aggregate `ci` job is the required check on `main`, together with CodeQL.

**A2 reconnect (ADR-008 §3-5, ADR-005 gate 3, #895).** `session.ts` joins each connection's channel to the ones before it with `continueChannel` as it goes (`Battle.joined`, the fold of the kept channels; `#build` returns the joined channel, `#keepChannel` keeps the connection's own channel in `A2BattleRecord.channels` and folds it into `joined`), so after a reconnect the bot builds observations, decides and numbers `channel_request_seq` on the battle's one continuous channel, with the actor rule and the watchdog unchanged. A rejoin on another seat is a `SessionError`; one that does not continue the earlier channel (`ObservationError` from the join) is a fatal error like any channel that cannot be built. `a2Episode` folds `record.channels` with `continueChannel` (a refusal is an `A2EpisodeError` that quotes nothing) and stores that one channel; folding covers several reconnects. A decision first made on a rejoin with no timer snapshot (the snapshot went to the old connection; the server does not replay it) has no deadline record, and none is invented (ADR-008 Errata E1b, #904, no contract change): the session marks the `DecisionRecord` `rejoin_without_snapshot` and logs an `incident` (`rejoin_without_timer_snapshot`), the battle plays on and its record is kept, and `a2Episode` refuses it with an `A2EpisodeError` whose `code` is `rejoin_without_timer_snapshot` and whose fixed message quotes nothing. Such a battle is never an Episode or training data. `test/session.reconnect-episode.test.ts` drives the real session through the committed reconnect fixtures (`fixtures/a2/<format>/bot-vs-bot-3.json`: a reconnect after the choice, Episode equal to the A1 Episode in channel, decisions and observations; `-4.json`: no snapshot, record equal, no Episode), two reconnects in a row, and the negative controls (a rejoin that does not replay the room log, a spliced `rqid`, the wrong seat).

A2 (ADR-005) is a bot client on a local Showdown server, for local human sparring against the bot; `packages/bot-client/README.md` is the sparring guide (start, connect, challenge, stop). In `packages/bot-client`, `ProtocolReader` (`src/protocol.ts`) numbers each room's requests per channel: a room's `|init|battle` (sent on every join, before the replayed log, which holds no requests) resets its `request_seq`, open request and re-send identity, so the request re-sent after a rejoin is request 1 of the new channel, as `buildObservation` numbers it.
